# Editorial review of *HaDover Bi*

## How to read this report

- Main-text page numbers are the printed page numbers. The archive image number is included in parentheses.
- Front matter is unnumbered, so those entries use the archive image number only.
- **Fix** means a clear typo, grammatical error, punctuation error, duplicated wording, or broken construction.
- **Query** means the wording, source, factual point, or intended meaning should be confirmed by the author.
- **Consistency** means the manuscript needs a house-style decision applied throughout.
- **Bidi** means Hebrew and English directionality, punctuation, or line wrapping has produced a visible ordering problem.

## Front matter

### Haskamah, Rabbi Eliyahu Safran

- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** “long time friend” should be **“longtime friend”** when used as a compound adjective.
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** Add a comma in “his dear parents z”l, who …”.
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** Add a comma after “mensch” in “Dr. Bennett, a warm, kind, and personable mensch, always loves …”.
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** “a good vort, insight and challenging question” is not parallel. Suggested: **“a good vort, an insight, and a challenging question.”**
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** Add a comma after introductory “Now” in “Now, all who …”.
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** “a wellspring of wisdom, inspiration and lifetime of learning” is missing an article. Suggested: **“a wellspring of wisdom and inspiration, drawn from a lifetime of learning.”**
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** Add a comma after “With Dr. Bennett’s diagnosis”.
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** “would find that good thought and insight” needs an article, and “new refreshing Sefer” has awkward adjective order. Suggested: **“would find a good thought and insight in this refreshing new sefer.”**
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** Add commas around “however” in “Dr. Bennett, however, …”.
- **Archive p. 1, Fix:** The sentence beginning “He is filled not only with a lifetime of medical knowledge …” is grammatically broken and nonparallel. Suggested rewrite: **“He possesses not only a lifetime of medical knowledge and experience, but also the qualities of a respected ben Torah: beautiful middos, compassion, sensitivity, and a love of Torah learning.”**
- **Archive p. 1, Consistency:** “Baal ha’bait” and “Baal ha’bayit” appear on the same page. Choose one transliteration.
- **Archive p. 1, Consistency:** Standardize “haTorah” versus “ha’Torah”, and “olam haTorah” versus “olam ha’Torah”.
- **Archive p. 1, Consistency:** “Sefer” and “sefer” are capitalized inconsistently.

### Haskamah, Rabbi Menachem Genack

- **Archive p. 2, Fix:** “a true baal middos, who …” likely should not have a comma if the clause is restrictive.
- **Archive p. 2, Fix:** “useful both to baalei batim and rabbanim for the collection …” is awkward. Suggested: **“useful to both baalei batim and rabbanim because of its collection …”.**
- **Archive p. 2, Fix:** “culled from a wide range of sources and including his own insights” is nonparallel. Suggested: **“drawn from a wide range of sources and enriched by his own insights.”**
- **Archive p. 2, Consistency:** Replace spaced hyphens used as sentence breaks with a consistent dash or comma style throughout.

### Message, R’ Noach Isaac Oelbaum

- **Archive p. 3, Fix:** “Even though there’s a whole שאלה …, but according to most פוסקים …” uses both “even though” and “but”. Remove one.
- **Archive p. 3, Fix:** “as a רב, and someone involved …” has a faulty comma and weak parallelism. Suggested: **“as a rav and as someone involved …”.**
- **Archive p. 3, Fix:** “then that גוי becomes מתגייר” is grammatically mixed. Use **“then that גוי converts”** or **“then that גוי is מתגייר.”**
- **Archive p. 3, Fix:** “repeat this vort over” is redundant. Use **“repeat this vort”** or **“say this vort over.”**
- **Archive pp. 3–4, Fix:** “I want to wish you a lot of ברכה and הצלחה, and to continue …” is not parallel. Suggested: **“I want to wish you much berachah and hatzlachah, and I hope you continue …”.**
- **Archive pp. 3–4, Bidi/production query:** The sentence crossing the page break contains several Hebrew/Yiddish runs. Keep each run in an explicit right-to-left span and recheck punctuation after final pagination.

### Haskamah, Rabbi Peretz Steinberg

- **Archive p. 5, Production query:** The handwritten facsimile is low-contrast and difficult to read. Consider adding a typed transcription for accessibility and searchability while retaining the original image.

### Letter, Rabbi Paysach J. Krohn

- **Archive p. 6, Fix:** Add a comma after “In his casual, loving manner”.
- **Archive p. 6, Fix:** “This comes along with his personal stories …” has an unclear antecedent. Suggested: **“The sefer also includes his personal stories …”.**
- **Archive p. 6, Fix:** “He gives all of you, and anyone who reads it, original thoughts and clarifies …” is awkward and nonparallel. Rewrite so that both verbs take a clear object.
- **Archive p. 6, Fix:** “May his work be a role model …” uses the wrong subject. A work can **serve as a model**; a person can **be a role model**.
- **Archive p. 6, Fix:** Hyphenate **“Torah-true Jews.”**
- **Archive p. 6, Consistency:** Standardize capitalization and transliteration of “parshios” and “Yomim Tovim”.

### Haskamah, Yossi Bennett

- **Archive p. 7, Fix:** “To which Uncle Leslie replied …” is a sentence fragment. Attach it to the preceding sentence or write **“Uncle Leslie replied …”.**
- **Archive p. 7, Fix:** “And while many times there is Torah being shared, with Uncle Leslie …” is syntactically awkward and should be recast.
- **Archive p. 7, Fix:** “throughout my childhood (and I would assume until today)” should be **“throughout my childhood, and to this day”.**
- **Archive p. 7, Fix:** “experienced seeing him over Shabbos, only to have …” is awkward. Suggested: **“encountered him on Shabbos and had …”.**
- **Archive p. 7, Fix:** “enhanced my family’s as well” is missing a noun, likely **“enhanced my family’s Shabbos as well.”**
- **Archive p. 7, Fix:** “Perusing through this sefer” is redundant. Use **“Perusing this sefer”** or **“Paging through this sefer.”**
- **Archive p. 7, Query:** Consider removing scare quotes around “chiddushim” unless the distancing is intentional.
- **Archive p. 8, Fix:** The opening sentence about the title is overloaded and repeats “title/titled”. Rewrite for one clear subject and predicate.
- **Archive p. 8, Fix:** “hints to the inclusion” should be **“hints at the inclusion.”**
- **Archive p. 8, Fix:** “has devotedly stood by” is awkward. Use **“has stood devotedly by”** or **“has steadfastly supported.”**
- **Archive p. 8, Fix:** The list after “myriad accomplishments” is nonparallel: “raising their family, the medical profession, as a community askan …”. Rewrite all items in the same grammatical form.
- **Archive p. 8, Fix:** The sentence crediting Reb Ari Bennett is grammatically broken. Suggested structure: **“Tremendous credit is due to Reb Ari Bennett, who transcribed the recordings, reviewed each ma’amar, and used his technical expertise to bring the sefer to publication.”**
- **Archive p. 8, Consistency:** Standardize “Divrei Torah” versus “divrei Torah”.
- **Archive p. 8, Consistency:** Standardize tractate/source style, for example **“Yerushalmi, Rosh Hashanah”** rather than “Yerushalmi in Rosh Hashana”.

### About the Name

- **Archive p. 9, Fix/query:** The opening translation is “What are these, my Lord?”, but the later direct quotation reads “What is this?”. If the later wording is meant as a direct quote, make it match; otherwise identify it as a paraphrase.
- **Archive p. 9, Fix:** “This model of חינוך, of children asking questions …” has a clumsy double “of”. Suggested: **“This model of chinuch, in which children ask questions …”.**
- **Archive p. 9, Fix:** “And finally, one last reason …” is redundant. Use either “finally” or “one last reason”.
- **Archive p. 9, Fix:** “the act of communication, of transmitting wisdom” is smoother as **“communication and the transmission of wisdom.”**
- **Archive pp. 8–10, Consistency/query:** Verify the exact Hebrew spelling of the book title everywhere. It should match the source phrase and the cover/title treatment exactly.

### Preface, Zeidy

- **Archive p. 10, Fix:** “It required lots of work” has an unclear antecedent. Suggested: **“Recording these stories required a great deal of work.”**
- **Archive p. 10, Fix:** “find meaningful and practical thoughts to be מקיים והגדת לבנך” makes “thoughts” the thing fulfilling the mitzvah. Recast so the speaker is the one who is מקיים it.
- **Archive p. 10, Fix:** “Ari provided constant vigilance and encouragement” uses an awkward collocation. Suggested: **“constant oversight and encouragement”** or simply **“constant encouragement.”**
- **Archive p. 10, Fix:** Remove the commas around the parenthetical in “It was Ari’s thought, (and Ari’s alone), to publish …”. Use **“It was Ari’s thought (and Ari’s alone) to publish …”.**
- **Archive p. 10, Fix:** “Samples of his greatness are displayed …” is awkward. Suggested: **“Examples of his greatness are evident …”.**
- **Archive p. 10, Consistency:** Treat the title as **“Shabbos: The Gift of Olam Haba in This World”** or another consistent title style, rather than a comma construction.

### L’ilui Nishmas

- **Archive p. 11:** No clear copy error found on visual review.

### Foreword, Ari

- **Archive p. 12, Fix:** The heading is misspelled **“Foreward”**. It should be **“Foreword.”**
- **Archive p. 12, Fix:** “continued on” is redundant. Use **“continued.”**
- **Archive p. 12, Fix:** “Some I heard once, and many I was fortunate enough …” is not parallel. Suggested: **“Some I heard once, and many others I was fortunate enough …”.**
- **Archive p. 12, Consistency:** Italicize book titles consistently, including *My First Parsha Reader* and *The Little Medrash Says*.
- **Archive p. 13, Fix/style:** “where the Divrei Torah could be collected and searched” is clearer as **“where the Divrei Torah could be collected and made searchable.”**
- **Archive p. 13, Fix/style:** “presented them in a way with which he would be satisfied” is stiff. Suggested: **“presented them in a way that would satisfy him.”**
- **Archive p. 14, Fix:** “grateful for his giving me his time” is awkward. Use **“grateful that he gave me his time.”**
- **Archive p. 14, Fix:** “putting up with all my nudging, pestering, and generally being a nuisance” has clumsy coordination and should be recast.
- **Archive p. 14, Fix:** The final sentence is grammatically awkward: “will continue with his children … and, b’ezras Hashem, for generations …”. Suggested: **“will continue among his children, grandchildren, and great-grandchildren for generations to come, b’ezras Hashem.”**

### Contents

- **Archive p. 15, Consistency:** “Chayai Sara” and “Chaya Sara” are inconsistent. Choose one house spelling, ideally one used throughout the headings, contents, and URLs.
- **Archive pp. 15–20, Consistency:** Part-number placement is inconsistent, for example “Bereshis 5786 (1)” versus “Mishpatim (1) 5784”. Choose one pattern.
- **Archive p. 17, Query:** “Story 5785” is too vague and is inconsistent with the descriptive naming of other pieces. Consider **“Naso Story 5785”** or a descriptive title.
- **Archive p. 18, Consistency:** “Matos-Maasei” and “Matos-Massei” are inconsistent. Choose one spelling.
- **Archive p. 19, Consistency:** Standardize “Lag Baomer” to the chosen house style, for example “Lag BaOmer”.
- **Archive p. 19, Consistency:** Standardize “Rosh Hashana” versus “Rosh Hashanah”.
- **Archive p. 19, Query:** “How to do Teshuva” and “Rosh Hashana in Berditchev” lack years while surrounding entries include them. Confirm whether this is intentional.
- **Archive p. 20, Consistency/query:** “Hoshana Raba” is a nonstandard/variant spelling; confirm the desired form, commonly “Hoshana Rabbah”.
- **Archive p. 20, Consistency/query:** “Purim Katon” should be checked against the intended pronunciation/transliteration; “Purim Katan” or “Purim Koton” would be more internally predictable.
- **Archive p. 20, Consistency:** Use the same punctuation in the Shabbos title here and on the piece itself, preferably a colon rather than a spaced hyphen.

## Main text

### Bereshis 5784

- **Printed p. 1 (archive p. 21), Fix:** “which had not been there before, it’s a Latin term” is a comma splice. Use a period, semicolon, or dash before “it’s”.
- **Printed p. 1, Fix:** “The exact opposite of Aristotle who said matter is eternal, we say …” is grammatically malformed. Suggested: **“In contrast to Aristotle’s claim that matter is eternal, we say …”.**
- **Printed p. 1, Fix:** “Just because it was there before, the previous moment’s existence is absolutely no reason …” has a dangling/illogical opening. Suggested: **“The fact that it existed in the previous moment is no reason for it to exist in the next.”**
- **Printed p. 1, Fix:** “in our minds, in our eye” should likely be **“in our mind’s eye.”**
- **Printed p. 1, Fix:** “That’s called מנוחה. That’s called Shabbos, it’s called resting” contains a comma splice. Split the final two clauses.
- **Printed p. 2 (archive p. 22), Fix:** “I understand the great גדולים knew this” needs **“that”** after “understand”.
- **Printed p. 2, Fix:** “keep in mind, relearn, and emphasize” lacks clear parallel objects. Suggested: **“keep this in mind, relearn it, and emphasize it.”**

### Bereshis 5785

- **Printed p. 3 (archive p. 23), Fix:** “He said this so sweetly that it was unforgettable, as many of the things that he did” is awkward. Suggested: **“He said it so sweetly that it was unforgettable, as were many things he did.”**
- **Printed p. 3, Fix:** The source chain using “he … and he …” has an ambiguous pronoun. Repeat the relevant name or rewrite the sentence.
- **Printed p. 3, Fix/style:** The closing discussion repeats “our own light” and “illuminate” several times in one sentence. Tighten to avoid accidental repetition.

### Bereshis 5786 (1)

- **Printed p. 4 (archive p. 24), Fix:** “One of the מוסר … that he had was that he advised …” is wordy and awkward. Suggested: **“One of his teachings was that people should …”.**
- **Printed p. 4, Fix:** Use parallel prepositions: **“In one pocket …; in the other pocket …”.**
- **Printed p. 4, Fix:** “Since we cannot determine precisely when that moment when Shabbos comes in” duplicates **“when”**. Remove one occurrence.
- **Printed p. 4, Fix/style:** “have to perforce” is redundant. Use **“must.”**
- **Printed p. 4, Fix:** “Look how little we are, look how imprecise we are …” is a comma splice. Separate the independent clauses.
- **Printed p. 4, Fix:** “אפר is ashes, has no future” is missing a subject. Use **“אפר is ashes; it has no future.”**
- **Printed p. 4, Fix/query:** “recognize our humility” is an odd collocation. Likely **“recognize our limitations”** or **“cultivate humility.”**
- **Printed p. 5 (archive p. 25), Fix:** “The גאון again asks, what …?” should be punctuated as a direct question: **“The Gaon asks: What …?”**
- **Printed p. 5, Fix:** “We take a seed, we can crush it up …” is a comma splice. Suggested: **“We can take a seed, crush it into food, or plant it …”.**
- **Printed p. 5, Fix:** “plants which we can eat or feed them to our בהמות” has a pronoun mismatch. Remove **“them”**.
- **Printed p. 5, Fix:** “Hashem says, you rule the world, we have to do that job …” is a run-on. Set off the quotation and begin a new clause.
- **Printed p. 5, Fix:** “as part of our responsibility that Hashem gives us” is awkward. Use **“as part of the responsibility Hashem has given us.”**
- **Printed p. 5, Fix:** “this rulership of the world, we already got this …” is redundant. Suggested: **“we already received this dominion …”.**

### Bereshis 5786 (2)

- **Printed p. 6 (archive p. 26), Fix:** “a real difficult one” should be **“a really difficult one”** or **“a very difficult one.”**
- **Printed p. 6, Fix:** “After so many offers that ended in nothing, one can easily reach despair” shifts from “he” to generic “one”. Keep the subject consistent.
- **Printed p. 6, Fix:** In the narrated past, “It turns out that he was …” should be **“It turned out …”.**
- **Printed p. 6, Fix:** “There’s a חתן and כלה up there” needs a second article or a collective phrase: **“There’s a חתן and a כלה …”** or **“There’s a bride and groom …”.**
- **Printed p. 6, Fix:** “The man who had called me looked and said to me” should be **“looked at me and said”.**
- **Printed pp. 6–7, Fix:** “A few members of the bride’s family, it might have been the groom’s family, who knew, hummed along …” is grammatically tangled. Suggested: **“A few relatives, perhaps from the bride’s or groom’s family, knew the songs and hummed along.”**
- **Printed p. 7 (archive p. 27), Fix:** “the same man approached me and whispers” shifts tense. Use **“whispered.”**
- **Printed p. 7, Fix:** “who went far in his learning, עבודת השם, and in relation to his friends” is not parallel. Suggested: **“who excelled in learning, avodas Hashem, and his relationships with friends.”**
- **Printed p. 7, Fix:** “My words made a great impression on those present, and to be honest, myself too” should be **“and, to be honest, on me too.”**
- **Printed p. 7, Fix:** “How are you related to the groom?” followed by “he was interested” is an awkward dialogue tag. Use **“he asked.”**
- **Printed p. 7, Fix:** The sentence “What makes you think of proposing your sister’s daughter before you even know me? I asked him.” is missing a closing quotation mark before **“I asked him.”**
- **Printed p. 7, Bidi/punctuation:** The one-word Hebrew reply meaning “chesed” has visibly scrambled comma/period/quotation marks. Re-enter the whole reply as a protected RTL span and confirm it renders as **“חסד,” I told him.**
- **Printed p. 7, Fix:** “Since we are both in the same family situation towards this חתן and כלה” is awkward. Suggested: **“Since we have the same relationship to this חתן and כלה …”.**
- **Printed p. 7, Fix:** “suggest to you my sister’s daughter” should be **“suggest my sister’s daughter to you”** or **“suggest that you meet my sister’s daughter.”**
- **Printed p. 8 (archive p. 28), Fix/style:** “We both looked at each other. We both knew each other” is repetitive. Suggested: **“We looked at each other and knew we had met before.”**
- **Printed p. 8, Fix:** “you saved it with your appearance” likely means **“with your presence.”**
- **Printed p. 8, Fix:** “Don’t worry, Hashem has kept your promise, I received my prize” is a comma splice. Use a semicolon or period.

### Noach 5784 (1)

- **Printed p. 9 (archive p. 29), Fix:** Add a comma after introductory “So in פרשת נח”.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix:** “Not what you expected, we’re going to talk about …” is a comma splice. Use a period or semicolon.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix/style:** “Ain’t nowhere in the Torah” is a nonstandard double negative. Keep only if the deliberately colloquial voice is desired.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix:** “But as he was going into the fire and was being saved, they asked הרן …” has an ambiguous pronoun. Name Avram explicitly.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix:** “he wasn’t saved as he was doing it שלא לשמה. אברהם אבינו did it לשמה” needs clearer punctuation and parallel phrasing.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix/style:** “the whole אור כשדים מדרש, upon which we start our whole narrative …” repeats **“whole”** and is syntactically heavy.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix:** Add vocative commas in **“Thank you, Rashi, and thank you, pasuk …”**, or recast as **“Thanks to Rashi and the pasuk …”.**
- **Printed p. 9, Factual query:** “The Mexicans have José de Martín” appears wrong. José de San Martín is not the standard figure for Mexican independence, and the name itself is incomplete/misspelled here. Verify and replace the example.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix:** “Every nation, even the Russians … even the Ukrainians, certainly the French, everybody …” is repetitive and should be streamlined.
- **Printed p. 9, Fix:** “Avraham Avinu’s great brilliance, fantastic מסירות נפש … is also buried” has a compound subject with a singular verb and is missing **“and”**. Use **“his great brilliance and fantastic mesirus nefesh … are also buried”**, or recast.
- **Printed p. 10 (archive p. 30), Fix/style:** “look for it … look for it … effort to find it” repeats the same idea three times in one sentence.
- **Printed p. 10, Fix:** “We’re not בני נח, we’re בני אברהם, יצחק ויעקב” is a comma splice. Use a semicolon or period.

### Noach 5784 (2)

- **Printed p. 11 (archive p. 31), Fix:** Add a comma after “In פרשת נח”.
- **Printed p. 11, Fix:** “יפת’s שכר is after the מלחמת גוג ומגוג when they die, his offspring will …” has mismatched number and an unclear antecedent. Suggested: **“After the war of Gog and Magog, Yefes’s descendants who die will merit burial.”**
- **Printed p. 11, Fix:** “The Torah is specifically saying, ‘And he took,’ and it’s talking about two people, it should say …” is a comma splice. Split before “it should say”.
- **Printed p. 11, Fix/style:** “יפת only tagged along, he shlepped along” is both a comma splice and an accidental repetition of “along”.
- **Printed p. 11, Fix/query:** “the שכר is equally compensatory” is unclear and seems to contradict the following contrast. Likely **“correspondingly different”** or **“measure for measure.”**
- **Printed p. 11, Fix:** The paragraph shifts from third person (“יפת did”) to second person (“יפת, you did …”). Keep point of view consistent.

### Noach 5785

- **Printed p. 12 (archive p. 32), Fix:** “as perhaps a commemoration or symbol of some of my own background” is awkward. Suggested: **“as a commemoration of, and link to, my background.”**
- **Printed p. 12, Query/style:** “a scorecard of the chassidish masters” is an unusual metaphor. Confirm whether “lineage chart” or “map” is intended.
- **Printed p. 12, Fix:** “After that he became a תלמיד of the Maggid of Mezritch, as Reb Shmelke introduced him” is awkward and has an unclear time relationship. Suggested: **“After Reb Shmelke introduced him, he became a talmid of the Maggid of Mezritch.”**
- **Printed p. 12, Fix:** “there was going to be a lighting mechanism for the תיבה, they couldn’t sit there pitch black” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 12, Fix:** “either a window or it was a brilliant jewel” is not parallel. Use **“either a window or a brilliant jewel.”**
- **Printed p. 12, Fix/style:** “The word תיבה can mean ark, and it also can mean the word ‘word’” is repetitive. Suggested: **“The word תיבה can mean both ‘ark’ and ‘word.’”**
- **Printed p. 12, Fix:** “anyone who for 40 days consecutively did not speak …” has awkward modifier placement. Use **“anyone who did not speak … for 40 consecutive days”.**
- **Printed p. 13 (archive p. 33), Fix:** “work on our davening, our learning, and on our תהלים” is not parallel. Use “on” for all three or only once.
- **Printed p. 13, Fix:** “make our words illuminated and glorious, mean them and know what we’re saying” is a run-on and nonparallel. Suggested: **“illuminate our words, mean them, and understand what we are saying.”**
- **Printed p. 13, Fix:** “at least certainly פשוט טייטש” is redundant. Use **“at least the פשוט טייטש.”**
- **Printed p. 13, Fix/style:** “grace us with his very early coming” is an awkward collocation. Suggested: **“arrive speedily.”**

### Noach 5786

- **Printed p. 14 (archive p. 34), Fix:** “continues up until our very day” is awkward. Suggested: **“remains relevant to this day.”**
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** “Rashi says on these words” should be **“Rashi comments on these words.”**
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** “When after 120 every person faces …” needs reordered punctuation: **“When, after 120 years, a person faces …”.**
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** Add a colon after “Everyone will answer”.
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** “they’ll give the same answer ‘I said I spent my whole life …’” needs a colon, and **“I said”** appears accidental. Suggested: **“they will give the same answer: ‘I spent my whole life …’”**
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** “Nobody cares about themselves; they only care about their children, all of their עמל ויגיעה is for their children” contains a comma splice after “children”.
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** “He did not exert himself and put all his efforts in only for his children” has incorrect word order. Suggested: **“He did not devote all his effort only to his children.”**
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** “He was doing the efforts” is not idiomatic English. Use **“making the effort”** or **“working”.**
- **Printed p. 14, Fix:** The sentence beginning “Not only his children, not a bad thing …” is grammatically broken and needs a full rewrite.
- **Printed p. 14, Source query:** “I assume it is the בית הלוי” signals uncertainty in the published text. Verify the attribution before publication or move the uncertainty to a note.
- **Printed pp. 14–15, Fix:** “All of my life, I constantly yearned that I should never come to the phrase …” is awkward. Suggested: **“Throughout my life, I hoped never to need the phrase …”.**
- **Printed p. 15 (archive p. 35), Fix:** Add a comma or colon before the inner quotation in “when I come upstairs ‘I did what I was sent to do’”.

### Lech Lecha 5784

- **Printed p. 16 (archive p. 36), Query:** “the foundation of all three messages” has no clear antecedent. Identify the three messages or use “all three pillars”.
- **Printed p. 16, Fix/style:** “a small מעשה that arrived today” is awkward. Suggested: **“a short story I received today.”**
- **Printed p. 16, Fix:** “Each of these corresponding to one of the אבות” is a sentence fragment. Use **“Each corresponds to one of the Avos.”**
- **Printed p. 16, Fix:** “In the later generations, in which we are now participating, preceding the coming of משיח …” is syntactically tangled and should be rewritten.
- **Printed p. 16, Fix:** In “in the חותמה of גלות, (at the end of the גלות) It will …”, remove the comma before the parenthesis and add a period after it.
- **Printed p. 16, Query:** The Hebrew word “נבוך” in “make up for נבוך, our failures” appears to be the wrong word/spelling for the intended interjection “nebach”. Confirm and punctuate it as an aside.
- **Printed p. 17 (archive p. 37), Fix:** “Our existence in the world is tied with a קשר to הקדוש ברוך הוא” is redundant. Use **“is tied to”** or **“is connected to”.**
- **Printed p. 17, Fix/style:** “contains a ו, a ו החיבור, a connecting ו, that connects …” repeats the same word and explanation several times.
- **Printed p. 17, Fix:** “I’ve been saying אשרי all these years and never saw this” should be **“have never noticed this.”**
- **Printed p. 17, Fix/style:** “by the help of Hashem’s hand, with בעזרת השם יתברך” duplicates the idea of “with Hashem’s help”.

### Lech Lecha 5785

- **Printed p. 18 (archive p. 38), Fix:** “whose birthday is on פרשת …” is awkward. Use **“whose birthday falls during/on the week of Parshas …”.**
- **Printed p. 18, Fix:** “like every other Yiddish boy” likely should be **“like every other Yiddishe boy”** or **“like every other Jewish boy.”**
- **Printed p. 18, Fix:** “ספר בראשית has only three of all the תרי״ג מצוות” should be **“contains only three of the 613 mitzvos.”**
- **Printed p. 18, Fix/style:** “includes an incredible מעשה that took place involving …” is wordy. Use **“includes an incredible story involving …”.**
- **Printed p. 18, Query:** “performing a ברית מילה on a goy” may be imprecise terminology in a conversion story. Confirm whether “performing milah as part of the conversion” is intended.
- **Printed p. 18, Fix:** “a סכנה, not only for that town but as a threat of a nationwide pogrom” is not parallel. Suggested: **“a danger not only to that town but to Jewish communities nationwide.”**
- **Printed p. 19 (archive p. 39), Fix:** In past narration, “he must be מוסר נפש” should be **“he would have to be מוסר נפש.”**
- **Printed p. 19, Fix:** “This way, the goyim would believe …” is better as **“In this way …”.**
- **Printed p. 19, Clarity query:** “apply it to the ברית מילה” refers to the procedure rather than the wound/site. Consider **“apply it to the circumcision site.”**
- **Printed p. 19, Bidi:** The final Hebrew phrase is split across two lines in reverse semantic order. The intended ending appears to be **“משיח במהרה בימינו אמן.”** Keep the entire phrase in one protected RTL span or prevent the internal line break.

### Lech Lecha 5786 (1)

- **Printed p. 20 (archive p. 40), Fix/style:** The opening “פרשת לך לך. Hashem instructs …” is a fragment. Suggested: **“In Parshas Lech Lecha, Hashem instructs Avram …”.**
- **Printed p. 20, Fix:** “a series of penetrating and pointed questions which makes you think” should be **“questions that make you think.”**
- **Printed p. 20, Fix:** “The first question he asks is what’s the pasuk telling you?” mixes indirect and direct-question grammar. Use **“The first question is: What is the pasuk telling us?”**
- **Printed p. 20, Fix:** “if you would have left his age out” should be **“if you had left his age out.”**
- **Printed p. 20, Fix:** “we would say his nephew is a כל שכן” incorrectly makes the nephew the logical argument. Suggested: **“all the more so, his nephew would be included.”**
- **Printed p. 20, Fix/style:** The explanation repeats “superficial connection”, “his own purposes”, and the contrast between אתו and עמו several times. Tighten after the first clear definition.
- **Printed p. 20, Fix:** The final sentence has ambiguous pronouns: “aside from Sarah, who is not a יורש, he is his closest living …”. Name Lot and Avram explicitly.
- **Printed p. 21 (archive p. 41), Fix:** “There’s all the reason in the world” should be **“There’s every reason in the world.”**
- **Printed p. 21, Fix:** “Lot’s actual purpose was not because of his wanting to do what Avram does” is awkward and shifts tense. Suggested: **“Lot’s purpose was not a desire to do what Avram was doing.”**
- **Printed p. 21, Fix:** The sentence beginning “This pasuk started from the beginning of our creation of כלל ישראל … tells you …” is grammatically broken and needs a full rewrite.
- **Printed p. 21, Fix:** “רחום, חנון, does חסד” is a fragment with no clear subject. Suggested: **“Hashem is רחום וחנון and does חסד.”**
- **Printed p. 21, Fix:** “A literal translation of the words that we’ve always learned for these years” is a sentence fragment.
- **Printed p. 21, Fix:** “get up to that מדרגה, which אברהם אבינו was” should be **“reach the מדרגה that אברהם אבינו reached.”**
- **Printed p. 21, Fix/style:** “our own ulterior motives” is redundant. Use **“ulterior motives”** or specify **“self-serving motives.”**

### Lech Lecha 5786 (2)

- **Printed p. 22 (archive p. 42), Fix/style:** The opening “לך לך. I heard this vort …” is a fragment. Suggested: **“In Parshas Lech Lecha, I heard …”.**
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** There is no visible space after “question.” before the Hebrew word that follows.
- **Printed p. 22, Bidi/translation:** The English glosses are attached to the wrong Hebrew words. The page reads as though **וַאֲבָרְכָה** means “whoever blesses you” and **מְבָרְכֶיךָ** means “I will bless them.” The intended mapping is the reverse: **מְבָרְכֶיךָ, “those who bless you”; וַאֲבָרְכָה, “I will bless [them].”** This is likely an RTL/LTR reordering error.
- **Printed p. 22, Fix/style:** The two consecutive questions about כלל ישראל’s reaction to a curse and to an attack repeat the same setup.
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** “all of a sudden we are עם אחד, we get together, we unify” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** In past narration, “Avram noticed that Sarah is beautiful” should be **“was beautiful.”**
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** “because they were in a situation where it will become an issue, become something to deal with” has a tense error and repetition. Use **“would become an issue.”**
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** “Avram already recognized Sarah, he already knew of her beauty” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** “Why is Avram saying now …, he already knew that?” is a run-on. Introduce the quotation with a colon and make the final question a separate sentence.
- **Printed p. 22, Fix:** “A person can have beauty of various types” is awkward. Suggested: **“A person can possess different kinds of beauty.”**
- **Printed pp. 22–23, Fix:** The sentence contrasting recognition of Avraham Avinu’s greatness with Sarah Imenu’s greatness is grammatically tangled, especially “at least not as on the spot, not as instinctively”. Rewrite for one clear comparison.
- **Printed p. 23 (archive p. 43), Fix:** “Her beauty did not lie in her externality, her beauty was internal, it was part of her מהות” contains two comma splices.
- **Printed p. 23, Fix:** “Her majesty and beauty was” should be **“were.”**
- **Printed p. 23, Fix/style:** “beauty” and “beautiful” are repeated many times in a short passage; vary or condense the wording.
- **Printed p. 23, Fix:** The final sentence is overlong and has an unclear “which”: “at a tremendously high level of spirituality and majesty, which is also what our focus should be.” Split and state the takeaway directly.

### Vayairah 5784

- **Printed p. 24 (archive p. 44), Fix/style:** The opening “After אברהם אבינו prepares … placing יצחק אבינו …” is awkward. Suggested: **“After Avraham Avinu prepared for the Akeidah and placed Yitzchak Avinu on the mizbe’ach …”.**
- **Printed p. 24, Clarity query:** “the מלאך called אברהם twice” can be read as the doubled name “Avraham, Avraham” rather than the two separate calls later discussed. Clarify **“called to Avraham on two occasions.”**
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** “But in the second ברכה, it says it is given in the name of ה׳” is awkward. Suggested: **“The second blessing, however, is delivered explicitly in Hashem’s name.”**
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** The question “How could the מלאך say, “‘You did not withhold your son from me’?”” has an unnecessary nested opening quotation mark. Use one set of quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** “an answer that almost only the גר״א could say” has awkward word order. Suggested: **“an answer that perhaps only the Gra could have given.”**
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** “every time a person does a מצוה, it creates a מלאך” has an unclear “it”. Suggested: **“each mitzvah a person performs creates a malach.”**
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** In “ממני, from me”. the final period/quotation mark order is wrong. Use **“from me.”**
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** “This teaches us a profound מוסר” is nonidiomatic in formal English. Use **“a profound mussar lesson.”**
- **Printed p. 24, Fix:** “If we put more כוונה, more time … it translates into a greater מצוה” has an unclear singular pronoun. Suggested: **“Investing more time, kavanah, ahavas Hashem, and yiras Hashem results in a greater mitzvah.”**
- **Printed p. 25 (archive p. 45), Fix:** “and that way, they will be of the highest quality” should be **“and in that way …”.**
- **Printed p. 25, Bidi:** The closing Hebrew prayer is split across lines so that **ביאת** is separated from **גואל צדק** and the semantic order is difficult/wrong. Keep **ביאת גואל צדק במהרה בימינו אמן** in one protected RTL span.

### Vayairah 5785

- **Printed p. 26 (archive p. 46), Fix/style:** “contains within it” is redundant. Use **“contains”.**
- **Printed p. 26, Fix/style:** “Each and every detail … has a מוסר השכל and a תורה that we can learn from” is repetitive. Suggested: **“Every detail carries a Torah and mussar lesson.”**
- **Printed p. 26, Fix:** “has countless מפרשים, discussions, and even full ספרים written about it” is not a parallel list. Suggested: **“has been analyzed by countless mefarshim and discussed in entire sefarim.”**
- **Printed p. 26, Fix:** “one tiny קניטש … which I hadn’t seen before and thought was worth sharing” has a dangling subject. Add a second **“that”** or recast.
- **Printed p. 26, Fix:** “Rashi explains on the beginning of the עקדה” should be **“Rashi explains at the beginning of the Akeidah.”**
- **Printed p. 26, Fix/style:** “the same exact שמחה” is redundant. Use **“the same simchah”** or **“exactly the same simchah.”**
- **Printed p. 26, Fix:** The narration shifts from past to present: “waited below … They have no idea … They don’t inquire”. Keep one tense.
- **Printed p. 26, Fix:** “feel their ‘grass was greener’” misuses the idiom. Suggested: **“feel superior to others.”**
- **Printed p. 27 (archive p. 47), Fix:** “R’ Yoshe Ber … whom I was privileged to hear” is missing what was heard. Use **“whom I was privileged to hear speak/teach.”**
- **Printed p. 27, Fix/query:** “No one else calls him that, except R’ Hershel Schachter” contradicts the absolute “no one”. Use **“Almost no one else …”.**

### Vayairah 5786 (1)

- **Printed p. 28 (archive p. 48), Fix/style:** The opening “פרשת וירא. If we look …” is a fragment. Suggested: **“If we look at Parshas Vayairah in the context …”.**
- **Printed p. 28, Fix:** “Avram’s name is not even in the last pasuk, it’s in second-to-last pasuk” is a comma splice and is missing **“the”** before “second-to-last”.
- **Printed p. 28, Fix/style:** “ספר אוצר פלאות התורה, a wonderful ספר …” repeats “sefer”. Use **“a wonderful work”.**
- **Printed p. 28, Fix:** “If someone goes in to visit a חולה” should be **“goes to visit a choleh.”**
- **Printed p. 28, Fix:** “it’s very important to help keep the place clean and neat, a visitor should …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 28, Clarity query:** “he helped to clean him and clean the room” is awkward and potentially misleading. Confirm whether the intended meaning is **“attended to him and cleaned the room.”**
- **Printed p. 28, Bidi/punctuation:** The Hebrew reply after “the sick person said” displays the opening/closing quotation marks in the wrong order and appears to contain duplicated quote marks. It should render as a normal quotation, for example **the sick person said, “רבי, החייתני.”**
- **Printed p. 28, Fix:** “things you should do on a ביקור חולים visit” is awkward. Use **“during a bikur cholim visit.”**
- **Printed p. 28, Fix:** The paragraph shifts from “someone” to “you” in “if someone is מבקש רחמים … you don’t mention …”. Keep the subject consistent.
- **Printed p. 29 (archive p. 49), Fix:** “because that the גזר דין …” contains an extra **“that”.**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** The sentence beginning “Now, another fascinating aspect of this brought down from the Munkatcher Rebbe … and is mentioned …” is missing a clean main verb and has a broken appositive. Rewrite fully.
- **Printed p. 29, Bidi/punctuation:** The phrase naming the **מנחת אלעזר, זכותו יגן עלינו** renders with a comma immediately after the English article “the”. Rebuild the mixed-language appositive so it reads in the intended order without “the,”.
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “is that when we make the מי שברך …, not to say the word החולה” needs a finite verb: **“we should not say”.**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “we changed our מנהג to not say the word החולה, we don’t want to label …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “a person should always be מבקש רחמים that he should not get sick” is awkward. Suggested: **“a person should always pray not to become ill.”**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “we’re being מקיים that this is his status” is not idiomatic. Suggested: **“we are confirming/establishing that status.”**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “That’s not a זכות, that’s cementing him …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “Whoever’s a גבאי who hears that” is redundant. Use **“Any gabbai who hears this …”.**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “The point is a person’s name is that person” needs **“that”** after “is”: **“The point is that …”.**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “when a baby is born to give a name …” has a dangling construction. Suggested: **“It is important to give a newborn an appropriate name …”.**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “We don’t want that same name חס ושלום to be a חובה to him” has awkward word order. Set off the interjection and use **“become a chovah for him.”**
- **Printed p. 29, Fix:** “if חס ושלום a situation happens where someone gets sick” is wordy. Suggested: **“if someone becomes ill, chas v’shalom …”.**

### Vayairah 5786 (2)

- **Printed p. 30 (archive p. 50), Fix/style:** “looking at the end of וירא for a vort on the עקדה because it’s the עקדה” is tautological. State why the ending prompted the search.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** The sentence describing the dream is overloaded: “a גאון whose name was חזון נחום had, in which he saw the father of the חיד״א, his name was …”. Split it and identify each person unambiguously.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “The חיד״א writes that his father did not give him any answer, however, when he woke up, he himself came to his own conclusion …” is a comma splice with several ambiguous pronouns. Rewrite with names.
- **Printed p. 30, Logic query:** The paragraph says it was not necessary to recite הגומל, then says it “would be appropriate”. Clarify whether the ruling is “not required but permissible/appropriate”.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “It was being מקיים דבר המלך ודתו” is awkward English. Suggested: **“It fulfilled the King’s command.”**
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “who bentchs גומל when they recover” mixes singular “who” with plural “they”. Suggested: **“Who recites Hagomel after recovering from illness?”**
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “We don’t פסקן like this, but he says …” has an unclear “he”. Name the cited authority or work.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “if someone was deathly ill, was already a גוסס and he became well” needs a conjunction and smoother sequencing.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix/style:** Use a consistent range style for “20–30 years” rather than a spaced hyphen.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “near-death experiences, where …” should be **“near-death experiences in which …”.**
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “does not bentch גומל, rather makes …” should be **“does not bentch Hagomel, but rather recites …”.**
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** Remove the comma before “that” in “He brings down from …, that after …”.
- **Printed p. 30, Fix:** “Then יצחק got up and he said” is smoother as **“Then Yitzchak got up and said”.**
- **Printed p. 30, Bidi/punctuation:** In the quotation ending with **תחית המתים**, the comma is outside the closing quotation mark. Recheck the quote as one protected mixed-language span.

### Chayai Sara 5784

- **Printed p. 31 (archive p. 51), Fix:** “The מדרש on this רש״י and the entire episode is vast” is awkward. Suggested: **“The midrashic discussion of this Rashi and the entire episode is extensive.”**
- **Printed p. 31, Fix:** “she passed away from the shock of this revelation” is an odd collocation for news about the Akeidah. **“from the shock of this news”** is clearer.
- **Printed p. 31, Fix:** The sentence beginning “I often mention that my אלטע זיידע was a שוחט in פרמישלאן …” contains two comma splices. End the first sentence after the historical detail, then write: **“Whether this was during Rav Meir’s lifetime is irrelevant; we nevertheless have a deep connection …”.**
- **Printed p. 31, Fix:** “What is this - sour grapes?” should use the manuscript’s chosen parenthetical-punctuation style, for example **“What is this, sour grapes?”** or **“Is this sour grapes?”**
- **Printed p. 31, Fix:** In “If one regrets an עבירה, it almost entirely … cancels out …”, **“it”** has an uncertain antecedent. Use **“that regret”**.
- **Printed p. 32 (archive p. 52), Fix:** “he cried, but with a small כ - he didn’t cry excessively” should be recast with a semicolon, colon, or parentheses rather than a spaced hyphen.
- **Printed p. 32, Fix:** “it was a גורם for שרה’s death” should be **“it was a cause of Sarah’s death.”**
- **Printed p. 32, Date query:** The parenthetical “written during the Gaza war” will become ambiguous in a lasting book. Add a month/year or identify the specific war period intended.
- **Printed p. 32, Fix:** “all of us, our children, our soldiers, and civilians” is overlapping and uneven. Suggested: **“all of us, including our children, soldiers, and civilians.”**
- **Printed p. 32, Fix:** “May we merit a tremendous ישועת Hashem” is grammatically mixed. Use **“a tremendous yeshuah from Hashem.”**
- **Printed p. 32, Fix/style:** “take seriously what we need to do in the future” is vague. Suggested: **“take seriously the work that lies ahead.”**

### Chayai Sara 5785

- **Printed p. 33 (archive p. 53), Factual/style query:** “the first recorded שידוך in history” is too broad. **“the first shidduch recorded in the Torah”** is safer and more precise.
- **Printed p. 33, Fix/style:** “there was also an underlying חכמה, a deep wisdom and intellect, in this request” is redundant and awkward. Suggested: **“the request also reflected deep wisdom.”**
- **Printed p. 33, Fix:** “discern if” should be **“discern whether.”**
- **Printed p. 33, Fix:** “even worse, he might …” should be **“or, even worse, he might …”.**
- **Printed p. 33, Fix:** “given it might be contaminated” should be **“given that it might be contaminated.”**
- **Printed p. 33, Fix/style:** “This approach would show both שכל and דרך ארץ - respect …” should use a colon or parentheses, and the apposition should make clear whether “respect” translates only דרך ארץ or the entire phrase.
- **Printed p. 33, Fix/style:** “a clever ניסיון, a test” repeats the term and its translation. Use one integrated formulation.
- **Printed p. 34 (archive p. 54), Fix:** “we strive to show them not only by instruction but by example” is missing what is being shown. Suggested: **“we strive to teach these middos not only through instruction but also by example.”**
- **Printed p. 34, Fix:** “our children and grandchildren, and all our descendants” is redundant. Suggested: **“our children, grandchildren, and future descendants.”**

### Chayai Sara 5786

- **Printed p. 35 (archive p. 55), Fix/style:** The opening “חיי שרה. The pasuk says …” is a fragment. Suggested: **“In Parshas Chayei Sarah, the pasuk says …”.**
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** “The sefer אחת שאלתי - a sefer of R’ Yitzchok Zilberstein’s torah wisdom written by R’ Erez Chazani - discusses …” is awkward and repeats “sefer”. Suggested: ***Achas Sha’alti*, R’ Erez Chazani’s collection of R’ Yitzchok Zilberstein’s Torah insights, discusses …**
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** “the importance of how we should look upon our efforts at going to daven” is cumbersome. Suggested: **“how we should regard the effort to attend minyan.”**
- **Printed p. 35, Fix/query:** “quotes a Rabbi Markowitz” should normally be **“quotes Rabbi Markowitz”**, unless the indefinite article is intentional because the identity is uncertain.
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** “Part of his job … there’s a מנין every day, מעריב, מנחה, שחרית” is syntactically broken and lists the services in reverse conventional order. Suggested: **“Part of his job was to ensure that there was a daily minyan for Shacharis, Mincha, and Maariv.”**
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** “One of my מתפללים, we will call him Mr. Feinstein, came …” has a faulty appositive. Use **“One of my mispallelim, whom we will call Mr. Feinstein, came …”.**
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** “he never came on time, he always came exactly ten minutes late” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** “One day, at exactly seven o’clock on the dot, walks in Mr. Feinstein” shifts into present tense. Use **“Mr. Feinstein walked in …”.**
- **Printed p. 35, Fix/style:** “to what we could attribute the honor of having him there …” is excessively formal for the anecdote. Suggested: **“what had brought him on time.”**
- **Printed p. 35, Bidi/punctuation:** In the line beginning with **הקדוש ברוך הוא**, the opening quotation mark displays after the Hebrew phrase, producing **הקדוש ברוך הוא“ sent me …**. Rebuild the full mixed-language quotation as one protected span so the opening quote appears before the sentence.
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** In **“that was crystal clear”, he said**, the comma belongs inside the closing quotation mark under the manuscript’s otherwise American punctuation style.
- **Printed p. 35, Fix:** **“What’s the big deal of ten minutes here or there”, the fireman asked.** should end the quoted question with a question mark: **“What’s the big deal about ten minutes here or there?” the fireman asked.**
- **Printed p. 36 (archive p. 56), Fix:** “what I always had said” should be **“what I had always said.”**
- **Printed p. 36, Fix:** “To have respect and come on time to any meeting is very וויכטיגע and a very big מעלה, it’s a מידה טובה” is grammatically broken and contains a comma splice. Suggested: **“Coming to any meeting on time is important, a significant ma’alah, and a middah tovah.”**
- **Printed p. 36, Language query:** In the predicate construction “is very וויכטיגע”, verify the Yiddish form. **וויכטיג** rather than the inflected **וויכטיגע** may be intended.
- **Printed p. 36, Fix:** “To come to shul on time is showing respect” should be **“Coming to shul on time shows respect.”**
- **Printed p. 36, Fix:** “respect to the shul, to הקדוש ברוך הוא, and to how important we consider davening” is not parallel. Suggested: **“respect for the shul and for הקדוש ברוך הוא, and it demonstrates how seriously we regard davening.”**
- **Printed p. 36, Fix/style:** “the first ten מתפללים who come to shul, and they always come on a regular basis” repeats the idea of coming regularly.
- **Printed p. 36, Fix/style:** “they’ve gotten themselves trained … Anyone can train themselves …” is awkward and repetitive. Suggested: **“they have developed that habit, and anyone can do the same.”**

### Chaya Sara 5787

- **Printed p. 37 (archive p. 57), Consistency:** The heading uses **“Chaya Sara”**, while the nearby pieces use **“Chayai Sara”** and the conventional transliteration may be **“Chayei Sarah.”** Choose one spelling for headings, contents, URLs where editable, and index entries.
- **Printed p. 37, Fix/style:** “לכאורה, to my mind” duplicates the qualification. Use one or the other.
- **Printed p. 37, Fix/query:** “the first שבעה that was ever alluded to in the Torah” is awkward and categorical. Suggested: **“the Torah’s earliest allusion to shivah may be …”.**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** “The famous קאפיטל we say in תהלים of למנצח has within it a pasuk” is cumbersome. Suggested: **“The familiar kapitel of Tehillim beginning למנצח includes the pasuk …”.**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** The translation beginning “Don’t be afraid if a person is wealthy, that the כבוד …” is grammatically broken. Suggested: **“Do not fear when a person becomes wealthy and the glory of his house increases …”.**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** “this pasuk, which I have used in many, many שבעה calls” has an uncertain antecedent and repeated “many”. Suggested: **“this interpretation, which I have shared on many shivah calls”.**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** “it has been a נחמה for, if not hundreds of people, dozens of people” reverses the usual scale. Use **“for dozens, if not hundreds, of people.”**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** Add commas in **“What, after all, is ריכטיגע עשירות?”**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** “ממש exact opposite” is redundant and missing an article. Use **“ממש the opposite”** or **“the exact opposite.”**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix:** “It’s a tremendous נחמה for a person to hear such a word, that he knows …” is awkward. Suggested: **“It is a tremendous nechamah to know that …”.**
- **Printed p. 37, Fix/style:** “the מסורה will be ongoing as a שלשלת, as a link in the chain” mixes and repeats two versions of the same metaphor.
- **Printed p. 37, Bidi/production query:** The closing phrase **ביאת גואל צדק במהרה בימינו אמן** breaks after **ביאת**. Keep the Hebrew phrase together if possible so its semantic unit is not split across lines.

### Toldos 5784

- **Printed p. 38 (archive p. 58), Translation query:** The translation of Rashi as “A person who is not quick and unable to deceive others …” is redundant and likely imprecise. **מי שאינו חריף לרמות** is closer to **“one who is not clever/shrewd enough to deceive others.”** Verify against the intended edition.
- **Printed p. 38, Fix:** “a person has an obligation to hold his מידות in his hand and be מושל on them” is nonidiomatic. Suggested: **“a person must keep his middos under control and govern them.”**
- **Printed p. 38, Fix/style:** “in תנ״ך and literature” is vague. Identify the intended body of literature, for example **“elsewhere in Tanach and rabbinic literature”.**
- **Printed p. 38, Fix:** “an איש who was master of his תמימות” is smoother as **“an ish who mastered and controlled his temimus.”**
- **Printed p. 38, Source-style query:** The parenthetical citation **“(29:12 ע״ש רש״י)”** should be normalized to the manuscript’s chosen source format and checked to ensure it is clear which work has chapter/section 29:12.
- **Printed p. 39 (archive p. 59), Fix:** “we are responsible to maintain control” should be **“we are responsible for maintaining control”** or **“we are responsible for maintaining self-control.”**
- **Printed p. 39, Fix:** “use what we’ve been given לשם שמים and for עבודת ה׳” is not parallel. Suggested: **“use what we have been given l’shem Shamayim in our avodas Hashem.”**
- **Printed p. 39, Accuracy/tone query:** “There’s no concept in Judaism like ‘temporary insanity’ as an excuse” sounds like a categorical legal claim. Either source and qualify it, or restate it as the moral point being made rather than a statement about law.

### Toldos 5785

- **Printed p. 40 (archive p. 60), Consistency:** Capitalize **“Parshas”** consistently when it is used as part of a named parsha.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “a pasuk that, at first blush, presents profound philosophical and logical questions” is wordy. Consider **“a pasuk that raises profound questions.”**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix:** “most of you know that’s where I originally come from; Zeidy Bennett’s עלטערן came from פרמישלאן” is internally awkward. If the point is ancestry, use **“that is where my family comes from”.**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix:** “not to see these stories as פשוט פשט” is awkward and repetitive. Suggested: **“not to read these stories as simple narratives.”**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix:** “We cannot ascribe our own thoughts, motives, or intentions to the אבות, they are far beyond …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “this pasuk here” is redundant. Use **“this pasuk.”**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix:** “when משיח will come” should be **“when Mashiach comes.”**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “Both אברהם and יעקב, individually, respond …” is overqualified. Use **“Avraham and Yaakov each respond …”.**
- **Printed p. 40, Source/translation query:** Verify the force and translation of **ימחו על קדושת שמך**. “They should be destroyed to bring about a Kiddush Hashem” may be stronger or less precise than the source intends.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “when הקדוש ברוך הוא comes to יצחק אבינו, יצחק will sit down with הקדוש ברוך הוא” repeats both names. Condense the sentence.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “It’s worth looking up that Gemara as a homework assignment” is markedly more classroom-like and conversational than the surrounding prose. Confirm that this tone is wanted in the book.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix:** “Avraham is the paradigm of gemilus chasadim, Yaakov is the paradigm …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “That’s what our lives are - cards that we are dealt” is awkward and repeats the deck-of-cards metaphor. Suggested: **“Our lives are shaped by the cards we are dealt.”**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix:** “what cards was יצחק given?” should be **“what cards were Yitzchak given?”**
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/query:** In the mixed-language explanation **“a בן חוטא. He is a חוטא. He is חטא”**, the last phrase is not grammatical English and may confuse the noun **חטא** with the verb **חָטָא**. Translate or explain each form explicitly.
- **Printed p. 40, Fix/style:** “I was מוחל him. I forgave him.” repeats the same statement. Use one integrated translation.
- **Printed pp. 40–41, Fix:** “הקדוש ברוך הוא you, the רבונו של עולם, for whom סליחה, מחילה, and כפרה are in your hands …” is grammatically tangled. Suggested structure: **“You, הקדוש ברוך הוא, the Ribbono Shel Olam, in whose hands are סליחה, מחילה, and כפרה …”.**
- **Printed p. 41 (archive p. 61), Fix:** “through יצחק אבינו’s אהבה of עשו” should be **“through Yitzchak Avinu’s love for Esav.”**
- **Printed p. 41, Fix/style:** “complete and absolute defense” is redundant. Use **“a complete defense”** or **“an absolute defense.”**
- **Printed p. 41, Fix:** “Not what you and I might think” is a sentence fragment. Attach it to the preceding or following sentence.
- **Printed p. 41, Fix/style:** “something to answer back to הקדוש ברוך הוא with his mouth” is awkward. Suggested: **“an argument to present orally before הקדוש ברוך הוא.”**
- **Printed p. 41, Logic/fix:** “saving us from our עבירות” is not the apparent intended meaning. A defense would save כלל ישראל from punishment or judgment, not from the עבירות themselves.

### Toldos 5786

- **Printed p. 42 (archive p. 62), Fix/style:** The opening “פרשת תולדות. One of …” is a fragment. Integrate the parsha name into the sentence.
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** In “the ברכות of יצחק to יעקב אבינו, which he was able to snatch away from עשו”, **“which”** and **“he”** have ambiguous antecedents. Suggested: **“the berachos that Yaakov Avinu received from Yitzchak in Esav’s place, with his mother’s help.”**
- **Printed p. 42, Tone query:** “the ridiculous outfit he was wearing” is jarringly colloquial and dismissive. Use a neutral description of the goatskin disguise unless the tone is deliberate.
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “All the מפרשים ask why the מדרש is telling us that?” should be an indirect statement ending with a period, or a direct question: **“All the mefarshim ask: Why does the midrash tell us this?”**
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “it’s still the ידי עשו, the pasuk does not sound …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “both of those קול s are מלא” is a malformed English plural. Suggested: **“both instances of קול are written malei.”**
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “When the קול קול יעקב, then …” is missing a verb. Use **“When the קול קול יעקב is heard …”.**
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “The first קול is a חסר, it’s missing a ו” is a comma splice and misuses the adjective as a noun. Suggested: **“The first קול is written chaser; it is missing a ו.”**
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “Meaning, when we slack off …” is a sentence fragment.
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “the קול יעקב is getting weaker, it’s a חסר, then ידי עשו” is a run-on and needs a fully stated conclusion.
- **Printed p. 42, Fix:** “put in our full כוחות” is nonidiomatic. Use **“devote all our kochos”** or **“put forth our full effort.”**
- **Printed p. 42, Tone query:** “there’s no time for vacation” is categorical and may not express the intended mussar point accurately. Consider **“there is no time to abandon or neglect our avodah.”**

### Vayaitzei 5783

- **Printed p. 43 (archive p. 63), Bidi/word order:** The opening displays as **“In יעקב, פרשת ויצא first marries …”**. It should read **“In פרשת ויצא, יעקב first marries …”.** Protect the two Hebrew runs separately so the subject does not move before the parsha name.
- **Printed p. 43, Fix/style:** “the פסוק transitions into the story” should be **“the narrative then turns to the births of the shevatim.”**
- **Printed p. 43, Fix:** “The Belzer Rebbe … asks a powerful קשיא: What …” repeats the word “question” across two languages. Use one integrated formulation.
- **Printed p. 43, Fix:** Add a vocative comma in **“Keep it coming, הקדוש ברוך הוא!”**
- **Printed p. 43, Fix/style:** “the תפילה of … every Friday night” is smoother as **“the tefillah … each Friday night”.**
- **Printed p. 43, Consistency:** Standardize the capitalization and spelling of **“Halivai Veiter”** if the phrase recurs.

### Vayaitzei 5784

- **Printed p. 44 (archive p. 64), Fix:** “In this week’s פרשה, פרשת ויצא it says …” needs a comma after the parsha name and is smoother as **“In this week’s parsha, Parshas Vayaitzei, the Torah says …”.**
- **Printed p. 44, Fix:** “What does רש״י say on this? That …” uses a nonidiomatic preposition and leaves a fragment. Suggested: **“What does Rashi say about this? He explains that …”.**
- **Printed p. 44, Fix/clarity:** “another man who was within a year of his father’s פטירה” is awkward. If this means he was in the first twelve months of mourning, say so directly.
- **Printed p. 44, Consistency:** Standardize **“8 o’clock”** to the book’s chosen time style, for example **“8:00”** or **“eight o’clock”.**
- **Printed p. 44, Fix/style:** “The general rule is that someone with a יארצייט takes precedence, but a minyan regular is not displaced by a stranger” may be clearer if the two competing rules are stated separately before the question.
- **Printed p. 44, Fix/style:** “Could his son now act against his father’s approach?” is vague. Use **“Could the son yield when his father had opposed yielding during his lifetime?”**
- **Printed p. 45 (archive p. 65), Clarity query:** “When this was shared with רב חיים” does not identify what was shared or by whom. Restate the antecedent.
- **Printed p. 45, Consistency:** Italicize or otherwise style the listed sefer titles consistently, and separate them with punctuation that will not be disrupted by RTL order.
- **Printed p. 45, Fix:** In **“Hashem repaid him in full measure”.** the period belongs inside the closing quotation mark under American style.
- **Printed p. 45, Bidi/punctuation:** The next quotation begins visually as **אבא“ lived in her house …**, with the opening quote after the Hebrew word. It should display as **“אבא lived in her house …”.**
- **Printed p. 45, Consistency:** Replace the spaced hyphen in “it wasn’t extraordinary - it was simply …” with the chosen parenthetical punctuation.

### Vayaitzei 5785

- **Printed p. 46 (archive p. 66), Fix:** “a discussion brought down in … which comes out twice a year from England” is cumbersome. Suggested: **“a discussion in *Kol HaTorah*, a Torah journal published semiannually in England.”**
- **Printed p. 46, Bidi/spacing:** There is no visible space between the English word **“journal”** and the Hebrew title **קול התורה**. Insert a protected space or style the title as a separate span.
- **Printed p. 46, Fix:** “In this week’s Parsha, ויצא, it delves …” has an awkward pronoun and capitalization. Suggested: **“Its discussion of Parshas Vayaitzei addresses …”.**
- **Printed p. 46, Fix/style:** “Upon experiencing this” has a vague object. Name the vision or revelation.
- **Printed p. 46, Fix:** “This is the place of the בית המקדש” should be **“This is the site of the Beis Hamikdash.”**
- **Printed p. 46, Fix:** “The מפרשים raise an interesting question, brought down from a rebbi …” dangles the modifier. Suggested: **“The journal cites, in the name of R’ Aaron Goodman of Bnei Brak, a question raised by the mefarshim.”**
- **Printed p. 46, Fix/style:** “He explains, based on מדרשים, a critical הלכה relevant to us” is smoother as **“Based on several midrashim, he derives a relevant halachic principle.”**
- **Printed p. 46, Fix:** “choose a place that had עבודה זרה to house the בית המקדש” should be **“choose a site associated with avodah zarah as the site of the Beis Hamikdash.”**
- **Printed p. 46, Source correction:** The citation **(22:7)** beside **וירא את המקום מרחוק** appears wrong. That phrase is in Bereishis **22:4**. Verify and correct the reference.
- **Printed p. 46, Fix:** “אברהם אבינו was מקדש the mountain and was קונה it; it was given to him” is grammatically mixed and repetitive. Suggested: **“Avraham Avinu sanctified and acquired the mountain.”**
- **Printed p. 46, Fix/translation:** “a person cannot אסור דבר שאינו שלו” is malformed. Suggested: **“a person cannot render property he does not own forbidden (אין אדם אוסר דבר שאינו שלו).”**
- **Printed p. 46, Fix:** “fit for הקדוש ברוך הוא’s שכינה to dwell” is redundant. Use **“fit for the Shechinah to dwell there.”**
- **Printed p. 47 (archive p. 67), Fix:** “cause tremendous spiritual effects” is awkward. Use **“have a tremendous spiritual impact.”**
- **Printed p. 47, Fix:** “perform מצוות and מעשים טובים to be מזכה ourselves and all כלל ישראל” is nonidiomatic. Suggested: **“perform mitzvos and ma’asim tovim that bring merit to us and to all of Klal Yisrael.”**
- **Printed p. 47, Fix:** “and b’ezras Hashem, further the גאולה” should be **“and, b’ezras Hashem, help hasten the geulah.”**

### Vayishlach 5783

- **Printed p. 48 (archive p. 68), Fix/style:** “distasteful and disfavored” is redundant. Use one adjective.
- **Printed p. 48, Fix:** “this week’s פרשה, פרשת וישלח” is awkwardly repetitive. Suggested: **“this week’s parsha, Parshas Vayishlach”.**
- **Printed p. 48, Fix/style:** “the תפילה that יעקב אבינו davened” repeats the same concept. Use **“Yaakov Avinu’s tefillah”.**
- **Printed p. 48, Consistency:** Replace the paired spaced hyphens around “his large משפחה … accomplishments” with the manuscript’s chosen parenthetical punctuation.
- **Printed p. 48, Factual/fix:** “when דוד המלך sought to build the בית המקדש, שלמה המלך could not get the doors to open …” mismatches the subjects and event. The intended account appears to concern Shlomo Hamelech at the inauguration of the Beis Hamikdash, when the doors opened after he invoked Dovid Hamelech’s merit. Verify and rewrite.
- **Printed p. 48, Clarity query:** The question “how could יעקב imply that he merited all of these חסדים on his own?” does not obviously follow from **קטנתי מכל החסדים**. Add the interpretive step that creates the question.
- **Printed p. 48, Fix/style:** “acknowledging that he may have tried as hard as he could to earn it” is speculative and wordy. Clarify exactly what the cited interpretation says.
- **Printed p. 49 (archive p. 69), Fix/style:** “the זכות of the פריערדיקע generations, those who came before us” repeats the Yiddish term in English. Use either the Yiddish with a concise gloss on first appearance or the English alone.

### Vayishlach 5784

- **Printed p. 50 (archive p. 70), Fix/style:** The opening “פרשת וישלח. The מלאכים …” is a fragment. Integrate the parsha name into the sentence.
- **Printed p. 50, Source-style query:** The Rashi citation beside the three strategies is visually hard to parse because the chapter/verse and Hebrew abbreviation reorder around the parentheses. Normalize the citation in a stable LTR form.
- **Printed p. 50, Fix:** “יעקב was a highly capable warrior” is missing **“a”.**
- **Printed p. 50, Source query:** “כמעט all the מפרשים comment that עשו meant the kiss genuinely” is a broad claim in a famously disputed passage. Verify “almost all” or attribute the specific view more narrowly.
- **Printed p. 50, Tone/accuracy query:** “תפילה, which can accomplish anything” is absolute. Consider qualifying it to express the intended spiritual lesson without making an unlimited factual claim.
- **Printed p. 50, Fix:** “Many examples brought down in storybooks, or in מעשה ביכלעך as they say, illustrate this interesting concept” is awkward and repetitive. Suggested: **“Many stories in the ma’aseh bichlach illustrate this point.”**
- **Printed p. 50, Fix:** “the son of the famous ‘All for the Boss’ יעקב יוסף הרמן” treats a book title as an adjective for the person. Suggested: **“Rav Nachum Dovid Herman, son of Rav Yaakov Yosef Herman, the subject of *All for the Boss*.”**
- **Printed p. 50, Fix:** “I didn’t really fully grasp” is redundant. Use **“I didn’t fully grasp”.**
- **Printed p. 50, Fix:** “what opportunities were there” should be **“what opportunities were available.”**
- **Printed p. 50, Translation query:** **מוסר** is glossed as “a black belt troublemaker”. That is not a standard translation of *moser*, which generally means an informer or one who hands another Jew over to hostile authorities. Verify the intended description.
- **Printed p. 50, Fix/tone:** “Today we would call him a mafioso, really not a good guy” is a comma splice and is unusually colloquial. Suggested: **“Today we might call him a mafioso, a dangerous criminal.”**

### Vayishlach 5784, continued

- **Printed p. 51 (archive p. 71), Fix/style:** “for all of the רע things that he did to him” is awkward. Suggested: **“for all the harm he had caused him.”**
- **Printed p. 51, Fix/style:** “And the trick is, he meant it. He didn’t just say it, he meant it” repeats the same point three times. Condense to **“The key was that he meant it sincerely.”**
- **Printed p. 51, Fix:** “the profound power of being לכף זכות דן” should be rendered in the intended order as **דן לכף זכות**. Protect the Hebrew phrase from bidi reordering.
- **Printed p. 51, Fix:** “It’s a very good thing to adopt the following מנהג which I saw, that every morning … I ask …” is grammatically tangled and shifts from a general recommendation to first person. Suggested: **“I adopted the following minhag: every morning before davening, I ask …”.**
- **Printed p. 51, Fix:** “my dear uncle whom I never met but am named after” should be **“my dear uncle, whom I never met but after whom I am named”.**
- **Printed p. 51, Fix:** “This is from the אריז״ל, I heard it from Rabbi Oelbaum” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 51, Tone/query:** “since I mentioned it in his name, משיח should come any moment now” reads as an oral aside or joke. Confirm whether it belongs in the edited book.
- **Printed p. 51, Production/style query:** “I can even make you a copy if you want” addresses a live audience and is no longer actionable in print. Delete or replace with a note directing readers to the printed text or QR page.
- **Printed p. 51, Fix/style:** “some of the very big chassidish סידורים” is vague. Use **“several major/standard Chassidic siddurim”** if that is the intended claim.
- **Printed p. 51, Fix/style:** “go our way … go our way … go the right way” repeats the same phrase. Condense the contrast.
- **Printed p. 52 (archive p. 72), Hebrew grammar:** “May our עבודה be מקובל” has a gender mismatch. Because **עבודה** is feminine, the adjective should be **מקובלת**, unless the sentence is recast in English.
- **Printed p. 52, Consistency:** Standardize **“Im Yirtzeh Hashem”** versus **“im yirtzeh Hashem”**, punctuation around it, and capitalization throughout.

### Vayishlach 5785

- **Printed p. 53 (archive p. 73), Fix:** “In a wonderful ספר that I received … there is a … discussion” is an awkward existential construction. Suggested: **“A wonderful sefer I received from my grandson Ari contains …”.**
- **Printed p. 53, Word-choice query:** The fight between Yaakov and the malach is called a **מחלוקת** and then a **מעשה**. If the intended meaning is a struggle or battle, choose a more accurate term and use it consistently.
- **Printed p. 53, Fix:** “a מעשה with tremendous implications for us and much to teach” is not idiomatic. Suggested: **“an episode with tremendous implications and much to teach us.”**
- **Printed p. 53, Fix/style:** “the heavenly spheres, the heavenly bodies, the constellations” is partially repetitive. Condense or distinguish the categories.
- **Printed p. 53, Fix:** Remove the comma before the parenthesis in **“Our head, מוח (which we mention …)”.**
- **Printed p. 53, Fix:** “The מלאכים are entirely רוחניות” should be **“The malachim are entirely spiritual”** or **“are pure ruchniyus.”**
- **Printed p. 53, Fix/style:** “the tremendous דעת and wisdom” repeats the Hebrew term in English. Use one integrated gloss.
- **Printed p. 53, Fix/style:** “our ability to use our body to do all the things that we do” is tautological. Suggested: **“our ability to use our bodies”.**
- **Printed p. 54 (archive p. 74), Fix:** “Their fight that night was a רוחניות struggle” should be **“a spiritual struggle”** or **“a struggle in ruchniyus.”**
- **Printed p. 54, Fix:** “he struck him on his leg, his thigh” is redundant and may be anatomically imprecise. Choose the intended term, likely thigh or hip socket, based on the source.
- **Printed p. 54, Bidi/word order:** The page displays **“through his struggle with the יעקב, מלאך demonstrated …”**. It should read **“through his struggle with the מלאך, יעקב demonstrated …”.**
- **Printed p. 54, Clarity query:** “This became dominant in his relationship with הקדוש ברוך הוא” has no clear antecedent. State whether “this” means seichel, the head, conscious emunah, or something else.
- **Printed p. 54, Fix/style:** The explanation “the level of ראש … the top level of קדושה - the head” is hard to follow and repeats “head”. Recast the wordplay in one concise sentence.
- **Printed p. 54, Fix/style:** “things we sometimes take for granted. We shouldn’t take these things for granted” is an immediate repetition.
- **Printed p. 54, Fix:** “transform ourselves into a ראש” is grammatically mixed. Suggested: **“become people guided by the ראש/seichel”.**

### Vayishlach 5786

- **Printed p. 55 (archive p. 75), Fix/style:** The opening **“פרשת וישלח. (32:4) …”** is a fragment. Introduce the verse in a complete sentence.
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “on such a level of accomplishment” should be **“at such a high level”.**
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “I asked him when was the last time he had put on תפילין” should be **“I asked him when he had last put on tefillin.”**
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “I asked him if I brought him my תפילין, would he put them on” improperly combines indirect and direct question forms. Suggested: **“I asked whether he would put on my tefillin if I brought them.”**
- **Printed p. 55, Identification query:** “R’ Menachem Mendel, the last Lubavitcher Rebbe” should give the full name, Rabbi Menachem Mendel Schneerson. The word **“last”** may also be sensitive or unnecessary; confirm the preferred institutional wording.
- **Printed p. 55, Factual query:** “there’s no city in the world that does not have a Lubavitcher outpost” is obvious hyperbole when printed as fact. Qualify it, for example **“Lubavitch has outposts in cities around the world.”**
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “a story that I knew about Dr. Allen Goldstein” should be **“a story about Dr. Allen Goldstein.”**
- **Printed p. 55, Factual/source query:** “one of the world’s most prominent ear, nose and throat doctors” is a superlative claim. Source it or soften it.
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “he was deciding what to do as a specialty” should be **“he was deciding which specialty to pursue.”**
- **Printed p. 55, Factual/word-choice query:** The text calls his Public Health Service uniform an **“army uniform.”** If he served in the U.S. Public Health Service Commissioned Corps rather than the Army, use **“service uniform”** or the exact branch.
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “and dressed up as a military officer went down to 770” needs commas and a less costume-like phrase: **“and, dressed in his uniform, went to 770.”**
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “the חסידים took one look at him and thought he was a general, they took him right in” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** A medical specialty is not “a … position”. Use **“a demanding and dramatic specialty”.**
- **Printed p. 55, Fix:** “came to the conclusion that the Rebbe was right, changed his life course …” should include **“and”** before the final coordinated action or be split for clarity.
- **Printed p. 55, Fix/style:** “keeping his office open … with an office jammed with people” repeats “office”.
- **Printed p. 56 (archive p. 76), Fix:** “a colleague R’ Menachem Genack” is missing **“of”**: **“a colleague of R’ Menachem Genack”.**
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** “Dr. Goldstein examined him and told that he could assist him” is missing an object. Use **“told him that …”.**
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** “by operating on him, which he arranged” has an unclear “which”. Suggested: **“He arranged the operation.”**
- **Printed p. 56, Punctuation:** The quotation beginning **“Tell Rabbi Yosef …”** never closes at the end of the paragraph. Add the closing quotation mark after **“done.”**
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** “the surgery was quite successful, everything was accomplished” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** “give him many ברכות to show his הכרת הטוב” is awkward. Suggested: **“bless him warmly and express his gratitude.”**
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** “I want to give you a special ברכה, I’m going to send down …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** Add a vocative comma in **“Excuse me, Rabbi”.**
- **Printed p. 56, Bidi/Hebrew grammar:** The punch line’s Hebrew adjective-noun phrases display in reversed order, and **אשכנזי** is singular beside four angels. The intended wording is approximately **“four מלאכים אשכנזים … one מלאך ספרדי.”** Rebuild the entire line as protected RTL phrases.
- **Printed p. 56, Fix:** “We were regaled with laughter, it was so hilarious” is both a comma splice and a misuse of “regaled”. Suggested: **“We erupted in laughter.”**
- **Printed p. 56, Fix/style:** “they still can help us and they still do help us” repeats “they still”.
- **Printed p. 56, Clarity/theology query:** “assistance through מלאכים and through הקדוש ברוך הוא Himself” should be checked carefully for theological precision and rewritten so it is clear that assistance comes from Hashem, not from independent intermediaries.

### Vayaishev 5783

- **Printed p. 57 (archive p. 77), Fix:** “The pasuk uses a לשון of שנאה - hatred him” should be **“a language of sinah, hatred toward him.”**
- **Printed p. 57, Fix:** “The first dream represented that יוסף would be greater” should be **“represented/predicted that Yosef would become greater”.**
- **Printed p. 57, Fix/style:** “This is the תורה of פרנסה” is unclear. State the lesson explicitly.
- **Printed p. 57, Fix:** “when a person achieves מעלות of רוחניות, when he grows …” repeats “when”. Suggested: **“when a person attains spiritual heights through Torah, mitzvos, and ma’asim tovim …”.**
- **Printed p. 57, Fix/style:** “Their dependence was on his resources, not his person” is awkward. Use **“not on him personally.”**
- **Printed p. 58 (archive p. 78), Fix:** “be more kind” should be **“be kinder.”**
- **Printed p. 58, Clarity query:** In “speak and think positively about them”, **“them”** could refer to friends, neighbors, or families. Name the intended group.
- **Printed p. 58, Bidi/punctuation:** The exclamation point appears after the English words **“the ultimate!”** and before the closing Hebrew prayer. It should come after the complete phrase **גאולה אמיתית במהרה בימינו אמן**.

### Vayaishev 5784

- **Printed p. 59 (archive p. 79), Consistency:** Standardize the parsha heading spelling, **“Vayaishev”** versus the more common **“Vayeishev”**, across headings, contents, index, and URLs where editable.
- **Printed p. 59, Fix/style:** The opening **“ר’ מאיר מפרמישלאן - some of my ancestors …”** is a fragment. Make the source/person’s name part of a complete sentence.
- **Printed p. 59, Fix:** “In the times of ר’ מאיר” should be **“In R’ Meir’s time.”**
- **Printed p. 59, Fix:** “collect the payment at the rate for tobacco” should be **“collect payment at the tobacco rate.”**
- **Printed p. 59, Tone query:** “not a nice thing to do” trivializes deliberate fraud and potential communal danger. Use a more accurate description.
- **Printed p. 59, Fix:** “an inspector was coming to audit his transactions, certain to expose his fraud” has a misplaced modifier. Suggested: **“an audit that would certainly expose his fraud.”**
- **Printed p. 59, Fix:** “not someone who you could pull something over” misstates the idiom. Use **“not someone you could fool”** or **“not someone you could pull one over on.”**
- **Printed p. 59, Fix:** “imprisoned יוסף over an alleged crime” should be **“for an alleged crime.”**
- **Printed p. 59, Fix:** “The ראש הקהל wanted the ר’ מאיר to stop …” contains an extra **“the”.**
