- **Printed p. 59, Fix/style:** “relented, and continued the leining and stopped …” should lose the comma and avoid the repeated “and”.
- **Printed p. 60 (archive p. 80), Fix:** “his audit was postponed due to being reassigned” dangles the modifier. Suggested: **“the audit was postponed because the inspector had been reassigned.”**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix:** “which actually is appropriate” should be **“which is actually appropriate.”**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix:** “the טובה that יוסף gave him” is an awkward collocation. Use **“the favor/kindness Yosef had done for him.”**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix:** “This is a very good פסוק for people …, it can be used …” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 60, Fix/style:** “move on to a different focus” is awkward. Suggested: **“turn their attention elsewhere.”**
- **Printed p. 60, Typo:** “The best thing to **so** in an עת צרה” should be **“do”.**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix:** “is to keep in mind to אין עוד מלבדו” contains an extra **“to”.** Suggested: **“is to keep אין עוד מלבדו in mind.”**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix:** “I still believe and hold very shtark” is nonidiomatic. Suggested: **“I still believe strongly”.**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix/clarity:** “if הקדוש ברוך הוא chooses to use them as His שליח” uses a singular messenger for plural efforts. Use **“as His instruments.”**
- **Printed p. 60, Fix:** Add the second comma in **“and that, in turn, should cause …”.**

### Vayaishev 5785

- **Printed p. 61 (archive p. 81), Fix:** “the hero in פרשת וישב, in the very sad story … was יהודה” repeats “in” and is cumbersome. Suggested: **“The hero of the tragic sale-of-Yosef episode in Parshas Vayeishev was Yehudah.”**
- **Printed p. 61, Fix/style:** “and then all of a sudden … for the first time יהודה spoke up and got directly and actively involved” is wordy and redundant.
- **Printed p. 61, Tone query:** “future עבירות - thank God!” is a conversational aside. Confirm whether this interjection belongs in the edited prose.
- **Printed p. 61, Fix:** “We cannot act on the עתיד for something that he is not responsible for now” has awkward prepositions and an unclear pronoun. Suggested: **“We cannot punish a person now for a descendant’s future conduct.”**
- **Printed p. 61, Fix/style:** The explanation repeats **“the middah of Hashem”** and **“where he is now”** several times. Tighten after the principle is stated once.
- **Printed p. 61, Word-choice query:** **“blandishments”** is technically possible but highly archaic beside the otherwise conversational style. Consider **“temptations”.**
- **Printed p. 62 (archive p. 82), Fix/style:** “Maximize its potential. Utilize it to its fullest to fulfill your purpose” piles up near-synonyms. Suggested: **“Use each day fully to fulfill your purpose.”**

### Mikeitz 5783

- **Printed p. 63 (archive p. 83), Translation/source query:** Verify the gloss of **רהבים** as “magicians, magic, or other sources”. Use the translation intended by the cited midrash/commentary rather than a loose list.
- **Printed p. 63, Fix:** “an additional two years were added” is redundant. Use **“two years were added”.**
- **Printed p. 63, Fix/style:** “This מדרש is cited in רש״י. The בית הלוי notes that this מדרש …” repeats “this midrash” in adjacent sentences.
- **Printed p. 63, Grammar:** In the translation “Hashem **give** us a ברכה in all of our actions”, **“give”** should be **“gives”.**
- **Printed p. 63, Fix:** “people are not on the level to rely entirely on Hashem” should be **“not at the level of relying entirely on Hashem.”**
- **Printed p. 63, Missing word:** “The בית הלוי that יוסף הצדיק is described as …” is missing a verb, likely **“explains/notes”.**
- **Printed p. 64 (archive p. 84), Fix:** “work on our own בטחון to get to such a level; הלוואי, we should come even close” is awkward. Suggested: **“work on our own bitachon and, halivai, approach even a small part of that level.”**

### Mikeitz 5784

- **Printed p. 65 (archive p. 85), Fix/style:** “It’s repeated again, and יוסף repeats it one more time” is redundant and leaves **“it”** unclear. State that the dream is first narrated, then retold by Pharaoh, then interpreted by Yosef.
- **Printed p. 65, Consistency:** Replace paired spaced hyphens around the aside beginning “what might seem …” with the chosen punctuation style.
- **Printed p. 65, Hebrew typo:** The contrast paragraph says four verses begin **ויאמר פרעה**, but then incorrectly says verse 17 also begins **ויאמר פרעה**. It should say **וידבר פרעה**, as the verse and following question correctly indicate.
- **Printed p. 65, Fix:** “What does יוסף answer him?” should be **“How does Yosef answer him?”**
- **Printed p. 65, Word-choice/typo:** “He’s going out of his **beanbags** trying to get the answer” is not an English idiom. Likely **“going out of his mind”** or **“going to extraordinary lengths”.**
- **Printed p. 65, Fix:** “He’s going … trying to get the answer, why is he going …?” is a comma splice.
- **Printed p. 65, Fix/style:** “unconscionable in פרעה’s mind and unconscionable in our world today” repeats the adjective.
- **Printed p. 65, Punctuation:** The quotation **“Hashem is in charge”** needs terminal punctuation before **“Nobody wants to hear that.”**
- **Printed p. 65, Tone query:** “Not one גוי wants to hear that” is a sweeping statement about all non-Jews. Qualify or remove it.
- **Printed p. 65, Bidi/spacing:** The Hebrew verse begins immediately after **“Hashem is in charge.”** with no visible space. Separate the English sentence and Hebrew quotation.
- **Printed p. 66 (archive p. 86), Fix/style:** “switched his language to וידבר, a harsher tone” should be **“switched to the harsher verb וידבר.”**
- **Printed p. 66, Fix/style:** The long parenthesis gives two speculative explanations and calls each obvious/easy. Condense and attribute them clearly.
- **Printed p. 66, Duplicated phrase:** “that is our job: to make a kiddush Hashem” is repeated almost verbatim two sentences later. Keep one instance.
- **Printed p. 66, Consistency:** Replace the spaced hyphens around **“how the pasuk changes from ויאמר to וידבר”** with the chosen punctuation style.

### Mikeitz 5785

- **Printed p. 67 (archive p. 87), Fix:** “There’s more than meets the eye here with the פרשה. Many רמזים appear” is awkward. Suggested: **“The parsha contains many Chanukah allusions.”**
- **Printed p. 67, Fix:** “His first example is when the scraggly פרות and the שבלים consuming …” is missing a finite verb and mixes the two dream images. Suggested: **“His first example is the skinny cows consuming the fat cows and the thin ears consuming the healthy ears.”**
- **Printed p. 67, Fix:** After the colon in “an idea …: how do you connect …?”, capitalize the direct question or turn it into an indirect clause.
- **Printed p. 67, Fix:** “This פגישה sets up another momentous confrontation in פרשת ויגש: יהודה standing up …” leaves a fragment after the colon. Use **“in which Yehudah stands up …”.**
- **Printed p. 67, Fix:** “if we recognize they are His doing” is missing **“that”.**
- **Printed p. 67, Factual/source query:** The story says the Gerrer Rebbe **R’ Avraham Mordechai** died very young. Verify the identity and biographical detail; the well-known R’ Avraham Mordechai Alter lived to old age, so either a different person or a different name may be intended.
- **Printed p. 67, Fix/style:** “This is an interesting vort” appears twice within a few lines. Delete one.
- **Printed p. 68 (archive p. 88), Fix:** “he is considered like a תינוק” should be **“he is considered a tinok.”**
- **Printed p. 68, Fix/style:** “This is עולם האמת … these statements seem untrue” repeats the phrase and could be tightened.
- **Printed p. 68, Fix:** “grab a ספר, משניות, תהילים, or anything …” is not parallel. Suggested: **“pick up a sefer, learn Mishnayos, say Tehillim, or do anything else …”.**

### Vayigash 5783

- **Printed p. 69 (archive p. 89), Fix/style:** “the great פגישה, the great confrontation” repeats the translation. Use one integrated phrase.
- **Printed p. 69, Fix:** Remove the comma before the predicate in “this אדון in מצרים who had been challenging them, was יוסף.”
- **Printed p. 69, Fix:** “questions on this episode” should be **“questions about this episode.”**
- **Printed p. 69, Fix/style:** “Where is the place for this question?” is a Yiddish/Hebrew calque. Use **“Why is this question relevant here?”**
- **Printed p. 69, Fix/style:** “woe to us from the day of דין, and the day of judgment” translates **דין** and then repeats the translation. Distinguish **דין** from **תוכחה**, the contrast the paragraph is meant to explain.
- **Printed p. 69, Factual correction:** “יוסף was the youngest of the שבטים (of course, except for בנימין)” is self-contradictory. Use **“the second-youngest of the brothers”** or **“the youngest besides Binyamin.”**
- **Printed p. 69, Fix/style:** “his מוסר, his תוכחה, his reproof” repeats the Hebrew term in English more than once. Use one gloss.
- **Printed p. 69, Fix:** The last sentence on the page, beginning “Only after the second פסוק …”, is syntactically incomplete and must be joined cleanly to the continuation on p. 70.
- **Printed p. 70 (archive p. 90), Fix:** “This סתירה undermined every טענה יהודה had just made” should be **“every ta’anah that Yehudah had just made.”**
- **Printed p. 70, Consistency:** Capitalize **“Torah”** in “a seder in torah”.
- **Printed p. 70, Fix:** The closing wish is a long run-on: “I want to wish everybody …, and …, and …, we should …”. Break it into two or three sentences and keep the subjects consistent.
- **Printed p. 70, Consistency:** Standardize the transliteration/capitalization of **“Im Yirtzeh Hashem,” “gitta besuros,” “gezunt,” “shtark,”** and the Yiddish calque **“meet by simchos.”**

### Vayigash 5784

- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** "All the mefarshim discuss at great length this confrontation and reunion" has awkward adverb placement. Use **"discuss this confrontation and reunion at great length."**
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** The sentence about Yaakov's meeting with Pharaoh repeats "Yaakov" and shifts tense in "has severe consequences." Suggested: **"Another significant episode is Yaakov's meeting with Pharaoh, which, according to the cited teaching, led to severe consequences for him."**
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Source query:** "It is brought down in all the mefarshim" is an unnecessarily sweeping attribution. Name the source or soften to **"many mefarshim explain."**
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** "the 33 years by which Yaakov's life was shorter than Yitzchak's" is cumbersome. Use **"the 33-year difference between Yaakov's and Yitzchak's lifespans."**
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** "Why should Yaakov lose eight years for the eight words..." repeats "eight" heavily. A lighter construction would improve the sentence.
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** "his presentation and appearance demonstrated sorrow and negativity" is redundant and imprecise. Consider **"his demeanor conveyed sorrow."**
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** "better that somebody should show someone his white teeth rather than giving him a glass of milk" is not parallel. Suggested: **"better to smile at someone than to give him a glass of milk."**
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Query:** "Our faces are public property" reads as a direct quotation or coined maxim. Either source it, put it in quotation marks, or identify it as the author's formulation.
- **Printed p. 71 (archive p. 91), Fix:** "Beyond avoiding lashon hara or being a baal gaavah" is not parallel. Suggested: **"Beyond avoiding lashon hara and arrogance..."**
- **Printed pp. 71-72 (archive pp. 91-92), Fix:** The sentence continuing onto p. 72 appears to refer to "our responsibility as bnei Torah." Confirm that the Hebrew words survived the page break in the intended order and that the subject is stated explicitly.
- **Printed p. 72 (archive p. 92), Fix:** "increase our mitzvos, and use our mitzvos as a zechus" repeats "our mitzvos." Suggested: **"increase our mitzvah observance and thereby create a zechus."**
- **Printed p. 72 (archive p. 92), Fix/query:** "Emunah and bitachon will save us under every circumstance, and hopefully will save us from tzaar" repeats "save us" and makes a categorical claim. Consider **"Emunah and bitachon can sustain us in every circumstance and, we hope, spare us further tzaar."**
- **Printed p. 72 (archive p. 92), Style:** "And that's really what the message is here" is oral filler. Delete or replace with a direct transition.

### Vayigash 5785

- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "The very last words Yosef tells them are in the pasuk" is awkward. Suggested: **"Yosef's final words to them appear in the pasuk..."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "Rashi provides two pshatim, the first pshat is" is a comma splice and repeats "pshat." Use a colon or two sentences.
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "There are two parallel, beautiful divrei Torah" has awkward adjective order. Use **"two beautiful, parallel divrei Torah."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Bidi/punctuation:** The parenthesis identifying the *Aperyon* and Rav Shlomo Ganzfried is hard to parse because the Hebrew title, English explanation, and second book title run together. Rebuild the entire parenthetical with explicit RTL spans and balanced parentheses.
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "While Yaakov had left home under his parents' directive" should likely be **"at his parents' direction"** or **"under his parents' instructions."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "the din was still medukdak for tzaddikim, measuring them kechut hasa'arah" mixes passive and active constructions. Suggested: **"tzaddikim were still judged with exacting precision, kechut hasa'arah."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "they themselves will have to give a din on it" shifts into the future while describing a past realization and uses an unnatural idiom. Suggested: **"they understood that they themselves would be held accountable for it."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** In the translated Hillel quotation, remove the comma in **"those who killed you, will meet the same fate."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Source query:** "possibly the Maharatz Chayes" should be resolved before publication. A parenthetical guess at a source should not remain in finished copy.
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "the brother's noted" must be **"the brothers noted"** or **"the brothers' observation was..."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "Yaakov's 14 years spent learning Torah" is clumsy. Use **"the 14 years Yaakov spent learning Torah."**
- **Printed p. 73 (archive p. 93), Fix:** "a maven on the brothers' enormous capacity to scale the heights of torah scholarship" is colloquial and overextended. Also capitalize **"Torah."** Suggested: **"Yosef, who understood his brothers' exceptional capacity for Torah study..."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** The gloss "that the road should not be a danger to you, that you should not get lost" is repetitive. Suggested: **"so that the road would not endanger you or cause you to lose your way."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix/query:** "If two talmidei chachamim are walking and don't learn Torah, they have to be destroyed" is an unusually blunt translation. Verify the source wording and consider **"are liable to be burned"** or another precise rendering if that is what the Gemara says.
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "This directly contradicts what Yosef was telling them?" should be either **"Does this directly contradict...?"** or a declarative sentence ending with a period.
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Style:** "an additional vort, important to transmit to you as well" is transcript-like. Use **"The Brisker Rav offers an additional insight."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "Now, this 'Ani Yosef' was astounding to them" treats a quotation as a noun awkwardly. Suggested: **"The declaration 'Ani Yosef' astounded them."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "on the small chance they might possibly find Yosef" is redundant. Use either **"on the small chance"** or **"hoping they might possibly."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "he would be an eved avadim in some who-knows-where" is malformed and colloquial. Suggested: **"they assumed he would be an anonymous slave in some distant place."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "from the moment that they got there; it never entered their minds that this is Yosef" shifts tense. Use **"had never entered their minds that he was Yosef."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** Remove the comma before "that" in **"so astonishing, so overwhelming, so incredible, that..."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "let's say this happened to us?" should be **"Suppose this happened to us."** or **"What if this happened to us?"**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Style:** "knocks out all of our preconceived notions in one shot" and "get that under our belts" are mismatched colloquialisms. Use a more formal formulation such as **"overturns all our assumptions at once"** and **"absorb it."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "contemplating on it" should be **"contemplating it."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Query:** "We've got to sit down and learn better" is ambiguous. If the intended idea is greater depth or intensity, say **"learn more deeply"** or **"learn with greater diligence."**
- **Printed p. 74 (archive p. 94), Fix:** "in order that we should be able to be mechaper on our aveiros" is cumbersome. Suggested: **"so that our learning might bring kapparah for our aveiros."**
- **Printed p. 75 (archive p. 95), Fix:** After "our response should be what Hashem wants from us," use a colon rather than a spaced hyphen before "engaging in Torah and mitzvos."

### Vayechi 5783

- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Source/style query:** Verify the exact essay and book titles, author credit, and quotation status for "The First Jewish Grandfather" in *Man of Faith in the Modern World* by Rabbi Abraham Besdin. Use italics for the book title and quotation marks for the essay title consistently.
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Fix:** "Yaakov's names, Yaakov and Yisrael, became synonymous with Klal Yisrael" is repetitive. Suggested: **"The names Yaakov and Yisrael became names for the Jewish people as a whole."**
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Fix:** "Avraham and Yitzchak remained individual names, never raised to the status of all of Klal Yisrael" is grammatically awkward. Use **"never became collective names for Klal Yisrael."**
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Fix:** The quotation marks around the Hebrew title "hazaken" are doubled/misaligned. Rebuild that mixed-direction phrase.
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Source query:** The claims that Yaakov's grandchildren studied with him every day and that he listened, worked, played, and planned with them should be tied clearly to Rabbi Soloveitchik's essay or to a primary source.
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Fix:** "one who lectures with a detachment" should be **"one who lectures with detachment."**
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Style:** "co-searcher of emes" is unusual English. Consider **"a fellow seeker of truth."**
- **Printed p. 76 (archive p. 96), Fix:** "We journey with him, feeling his trials in Lech Lecha" is awkward because Lech Lecha is a parshah title, not the location of the trials. Recast as **"We journey with him through the trials described in Lech Lecha."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Quotation query:** The long passages attributed to R' Yoshe Ber appear highly polished or paraphrased. Verify every quoted word against the published source. If this is a paraphrase, remove quotation marks and introduce it as a summary.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** Add punctuation after **"For a minute he says"** before the quotation.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** In "Can I as an old man talk to these young children?" set off the parenthetical phrase: **"Can I, as an old man, talk to these young children?"**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Query:** "Indian summer" is a potentially confusing metaphor for old age. Retain only if it is verified as the source's wording; otherwise use **"late season of life."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Bidi/production:** The list of historical rabbinic figures is difficult to follow because Hebrew honorifics and English articles reverse around punctuation. Rebuild the list item by item in a single, verified order.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** "All of a sudden all of the doros come together" repeats "all." Use **"Suddenly, all the doros come together."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** "all are operating with the same categories that all of the previous doros utilized" repeats "all" and sounds bureaucratic. Suggested: **"all use the conceptual language of earlier generations."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** "spanning the antiquity of the middle ages and our times today" is redundant and historically muddled. Suggested: **"spanning the Middle Ages and the present day."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Unresolved placeholder:** The bracketed text **"[he mentions it with a saying of Rabbi Akiva]"** is an editorial note left in the published prose. It must be replaced with the actual citation or deleted.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Bidi/punctuation:** The closing Hebrew prophecy beginning with "Veheshiv lev avos..." is inserted into an English quotation with unstable punctuation. Rebuild the line and make clear where the English quotation resumes.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** Hyphenate **"a two- or three-hour shiur."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Duplicated idea:** "the students look exhausted! The students are exhausted from this exercise" repeats the same statement back to back. Delete one.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** "Hands ... embrace warm and supple hands together" is repetitive and physically awkward. Suggested: **"Dry, wrinkled hands clasp warm, supple ones in a common bond."**
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Fix:** "through our connection with all of you together" is unclear and redundant. Specify the intended antecedent, likely earlier generations or grandchildren.
- **Printed p. 77 (archive p. 97), Bidi/clarity:** The final sentence ends with a Hebrew phrase meaning approximately "the mesorah until the coming of the righteous redeemer," but the English syntax does not accommodate it. Recast the complete sentence in one language or set the Hebrew phrase off cleanly.

### Vayechi 5784

- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Fix:** "the two berachos that we repeat each and every week, giving them to our kinderlach" is wordy and has an unclear participle. Suggested: **"the two berachos we recite each week when blessing our children."**
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Redundancy:** "each and every week" can be **"each week."**
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Query/tone:** Calling kinah and gaavah "two machalos" is rhetorically strong. Confirm that "maladies" is intended rather than simply "traits" or "failings."
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Fix:** "A tzaddik can achieve something through ratzon, as we say... On a higher level this is accomplished through tefillah" has an unclear "this." State exactly what is achieved.
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Transcript cleanup:** Delete or recast **"even though you guys are going to see this in Vayechi, I'm recording this on Chanukah."** It is recording-room commentary, not finished-book prose.
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Fix:** "A candle's flame comes from the fire, its fuel is the shemen" is a comma splice. Use a semicolon or split the sentence.
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Factual/translation query:** **"Keshet" means a bow, not a bowstring.** Revise "the keshet, which is a bowstring" and check whether the analogy depends on the bow or the string.
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Fix:** The long closing sentence joins several independent wishes with commas. Divide it into two sentences and set "Im Yirtzeh Hashem" off with commas.
- **Printed p. 78 (archive p. 98), Bidi:** The final Hebrew prayer breaks around the English punctuation, with the exclamation point appearing before "Amen." Keep the full prayer in one RTL span and place punctuation after the span.

### Vayechi 5785

- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Fix:** "This Parshas Vayechi is a very special parshah" repeats both "this" and "parshah." Suggested: **"Parshas Vayechi is especially meaningful to us..."**
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Style:** "the extremely happy occasion" is overmodified. Use **"the joyous occasion."**
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Redundancy:** "all of the family, all of the einiklach, ur-einiklach and everyone" repeats the same inclusive idea. Condense the list.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Redundancy:** "Almost everyone, with few exceptions" says the same thing twice. Use one phrase.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Fix:** Hyphenate **"second-greatest rebbi."**
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Bidi/clarity:** In the survey answers, the Hebrew word "Rebbi" sits next to "Rabbi Yehudah Hanasi" in a way that can look like two separate answers. Rebuild the sentence so each answer is unmistakable.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Fix:** "How do we judge his greatness? It's not a contest, but we aim to understand who is chashuv and why they should be considered great" shifts from singular "his" to plural "they." Keep the pronouns consistent.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Style:** "little children often bring home projects from yeshiva" may be inaccurate for girls or for general school projects. Use **"from school"** unless yeshiva is specifically intended.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Fix:** The translation after Rashi begins **"Who will come from Ephraim? Yehoshua..."** The question-and-answer order is choppy. Suggested: **"Yehoshua, who would apportion Eretz Yisrael and lead the nation, would descend from Ephraim."**
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Source query:** Verify the spelling and identification of **R' Zev Klag/Klagsbald of Antwerp**, the title *Minchas Chen*, and the journal *Kol HaTorah*.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Query:** "When Mashiach comes, it is said that bechorim will return to perform the avodah..." should name a source, particularly because it is central to the comparison.
- **Printed p. 79 (archive p. 99), Style:** The bracketed aside **"[I hope so because I am a bechor!]"** is conversational and interrupts the argument. Consider moving it to a parenthetical sentence without square brackets or deleting it.
- **Printed p. 80 (archive p. 100), Comma splice:** "From the moment a bechor is born, his status is set, it requires no effort on his part." Use a semicolon or split into two sentences.
- **Printed p. 80 (archive p. 100), Fix:** "striving for and achieving greatness through personal effort" is slightly repetitive because striving already implies effort. Consider **"greatness achieved through sustained personal growth."**
- **Printed p. 80 (archive p. 100), Repetition:** The three consecutive sentences repeat **"put on tefillin"** and **"the first time."** Condense while preserving the point.
- **Printed p. 80 (archive p. 100), Fix:** "with real great concentration" should be **"with great concentration"** or **"with genuine intensity."**
- **Printed p. 80 (archive p. 100), Fix:** "we should see nachas and gezunt" uses "see" unnaturally with gezunt. Suggested: **"we should enjoy nachas and gezunt."**
- **Printed p. 80 (archive p. 100), Fix:** "may we see all of our children ... be successful" should be **"may we see all our children ... succeed"** or **"may we see that they are successful."**

### Shemos 5783

- **Printed p. 81 (archive p. 101), Fix:** "The questions are 1) What did Klal Yisrael do? And 2) Why Hashem do this" should be **"The questions are: (1) What did Klal Yisrael do? and (2) Why did Hashem do this...?"**
- **Printed p. 81 (archive p. 101), Consistency:** Standardize source naming and capitalization in phrases such as "sefer Hoshea," "Midrash Rabbah," and "Tehillim."
- **Printed p. 81 (archive p. 101), Fix:** "The act of being mefer the bris milah" is an awkward hybrid construction. Suggested: **"Their violation of bris milah did not mean that they stopped performing it."**
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Fix:** "a declaration of separateness, of being the am hanivchar" has a clumsy repeated "of." Use **"a declaration that they were separate, the am hanivchar."**
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Conditional tense:** "If Klal Yisrael would not maintain their separateness" should be **"If Klal Yisrael did not maintain their separateness"** or **"If they would not..."** depending on intended register.
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Fix:** "the whole purpose of giving the Mitzriyim this sinah" is awkward. Suggested: **"the purpose of causing this hostility among the Egyptians."**
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Fix:** "to keep us as the am hanivchar" should be **"to preserve us as the am hanivchar."**
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Punctuation:** Add a comma before **"especially bris milah"** in "keep doing all of our mitzvos especially bris milah."
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Clarity:** "We should have many more of them in our family" has an unclear antecedent. Say **"many more brissim"** if that is intended.
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Comma splice:** "We should have many more of them in our family, we should have a lot of simchah" needs a conjunction, semicolon, or sentence break.
- **Printed p. 82 (archive p. 102), Bidi:** The final Hebrew prayer and exclamation point are visibly reversed. Put the full prayer, including "Amen," in one RTL span and punctuate after it.

### Shemos 5784

- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Comma splice:** "We start out with Yetzias Mitzrayim and conclude with the Mishkan. That's the entire Sefer Shemos in one sentence - a tremendous geulah" is logically compressed and uses a transcript-like afterthought. Recast as one clear thesis.
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Fix:** "There are many reasons we were zoche to this geulah" should be **"zocheh to this geulah"** in transliteration or **"merited this geulah"** in English.
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Word choice:** "an enigmatic portion of Parshas Shemos" is vague. Use **"an enigmatic passage."**
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Language/factual query:** The aside claiming that Yiddish **"eיניקל"** derives from the Hebrew phrase meaning "he is not" appears to be folk etymology and should be verified by a reliable Yiddish etymological source. Delete if unsupported.
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Transcript cleanup:** The imagined dialogue beginning "Sure, Hashem, I'm glad to go" and the repeated "No" sequence is highly oral. Tighten and add a comma after the vocative **"Hashem."**
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Style:** "This goes on and on, pasuk after pasuk" is oral filler. Use **"Moshe continues to object over several pesukim."**
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Fix:** "Hashem doesn't get angry, but anger, כביכול, flares up" is internally contradictory in English. Clarify that the phrase is anthropomorphic or that it signals punishment rather than literal anger.
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Style:** "But, as always, there's a machlokes" is casual and overgeneralized. Use **"R' Shimon bar Yochai disagrees."**
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Clarity:** "We know Aharon is not referred to as a Levi" is imprecise because he is from Shevet Levi. Specify that the verse calls him "Aharon your brother, the Levi" rather than "the Kohen," if that is the point.
- **Printed p. 83 (archive p. 103), Terminology query:** "When nisuch hashemen was performed during Aharon's inauguration" appears to use the wrong term. The inauguration involved anointing with the shemen hamishchah, not "nisuch hashemen." Verify and correct the Hebrew term.
- **Printed p. 84 (archive p. 104), Fix:** "From this Midrash, R' Yehoshua ben Korchah's position becomes clear" reads better as **"R' Yehoshua ben Korchah's position becomes clear from this Midrash."**
- **Printed p. 84 (archive p. 104), Fix:** "especially when we consider the kinah seen among Yosef's brothers and the praise bestowed upon Menashe and Ephraim by their lack of kinah" is nonparallel and awkward. Suggested: **"especially in contrast to the jealousy among Yosef's brothers and the harmony between Menashe and Ephraim."**
- **Printed p. 84 (archive p. 104), Style:** The sequence "First, by... Second, by... Third, by..." consists of fragments. Either retain as a deliberate numbered list or turn it into one complete sentence.
- **Printed p. 84 (archive p. 104), Comma splice:** "In that zechus, we will have a geulah, all our kinderlach and all of Klal Yisrael will have a tremendous yeshuah" needs a conjunction, semicolon, or sentence break.
- **Printed p. 84 (archive p. 104), Date query:** "written during the war in Gaza" will become ambiguous. Supply the specific year or conflict/date.
- **Printed p. 84 (archive p. 104), Fix:** "Here in Chutz La'aretz, we are also facing nisyonos, being ridiculed and threatened" is loose. Suggested: **"Here in Chutz La'aretz, we also face nisyonos, including ridicule and threats."**

### Shemos 5785

- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Fix:** "a very chashuvah inyan" has incorrect Hebrew gender/order. Use **"an inyan chashuv"** or simply **"an important practice."**
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Word choice:** "Despite his immense schedule" should be **"Despite his demanding schedule."**
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Source query:** Verify the reported order in which R' Chaim Kanievsky read shnayim mikra v'echad targum. The sentence gives **mikra, targum, mikra** and should match the cited source exactly.
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Fix:** "Names hold a very chashuvah place" uses the wrong adjective form. Use **"Names hold an important place"** or **"a makom chashuv."**
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Run-on:** "there's a small inyan of ruach hakodesh involved when parents choose a name for their child, parents have to ask for siyata dishmaya..." is a comma splice and repeats "parents." Divide and tighten.
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Pronoun:** "Rav Meir ... would ask for a person's name before interacting with them" shifts from singular to plural. Use **"before interacting with that person."**
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Bidi/punctuation:** The parenthetical ending with the Yiddish phrase "stam azoi a vertl" has reversed punctuation around the closing parenthesis. Rebuild the entire parenthesis.
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Style:** "Both the Chazon Ish and the Steipler were shtark on this inyan" is very colloquial. Consider **"both emphasized this point strongly."**
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Query:** "These are secrets of ruach hakodesh" may use the wrong category; the preceding discussion concerns the Zohar and mystical name combinations. Verify whether **"sodos haTorah"** or **"Kabbalah"** is intended.
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Word choice/query:** "naming a child after a deceased relative had no yichus or benefit" uses **"yichus"** in an unclear sense. Verify the intended word, perhaps "effect," "zechus," or "benefit."
- **Printed p. 85 (archive p. 105), Article:** "it was simply a zikaron of the niftar" should likely be **"a zikaron for the niftar."**
- **Printed p. 86 (archive p. 106), Faulty comparison:** "approach all our activities with the same seriousness and chashivus as we do when naming a child" should be **"with the same seriousness and chashivus with which we approach naming a child."**
- **Printed p. 86 (archive p. 106), Logic query:** "the names that we have chosen for ourselves" conflicts with the piece's repeated point that parents choose a child's name. If this is metaphorical, clarify it.
- **Printed p. 86 (archive p. 106), Fix:** "be a nachas for Klal Yisrael" is not idiomatic. Use **"be a source of nachas to Klal Yisrael."**

### Vaeira 5783

- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Clarity:** "For so many millions of Jews throughout history ... it tragically was a physical experience" has an unclear "it." Suggested: **"For millions of Jews, mesirus nefesh tragically required literal physical sacrifice."**
- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Fix:** "this important and chashuvah concept" is redundant and uses the wrong Hebrew adjective form. Use **"this important concept"** or **"this inyan chashuv."**
- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Fix:** "I accept upon myself the willingness" should be **"I accept upon myself a willingness"** or **"I resolve to be willing."**
- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Repetition:** "The third is the mitzvah of Krias Shema ... fulfilling the mitzvah of Krias Shema itself" repeats the full phrase. Condense.
- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Fix:** "who were not commanded in kiddush Hashem" should be **"who were not commanded to sanctify Hashem's name"** or **"were not commanded in the mitzvah of kiddush Hashem."**
- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Source query:** Verify the story that the Gra was seven years old when he gave this answer and that the Shaagas Aryeh kissed him. Cite a source or soften "according to the story."
- **Printed p. 87 (archive p. 107), Tone/query:** "Each frog could have avoided the oven and chosen a bed or another location" anthropomorphizes the frogs in a way that may be faithful to the derashah but reads comically in English. Confirm the intended tone.
- **Printed p. 88 (archive p. 108), Style:** "While I have your attention" is transcript residue. Delete and begin directly with the second question.
- **Printed p. 88 (archive p. 108), Redundancy:** "a famous kashya related to Pharaoh, unrelated to the above" repeats "related/unrelated." Use **"a separate, well-known question about Pharaoh."**
- **Printed p. 88 (archive p. 108), Style:** "a monumental machlokes" and "a fantastic teretz for this very fundamental kashya" are inflated and conversational. Consider a more neutral register.

### Vaeira 5784

- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Fix:** "This is the point up until which I, Hakadosh Baruch Hu, perfect the world" has awkward tense and word order. Suggested: **"This is the point to which I, Hakadosh Baruch Hu, have perfected the world."**
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Tone:** "You, tiny little basar vadam" is needlessly belittling in English unless it is a verified quotation. Consider **"Human beings must now continue..."**
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Fix:** "the job of achieving shleimus, of continuing the process" is a loose double "of." Use parallel infinitives: **"to pursue shleimus and continue the process."**
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Repetition:** "This is what Hashem wants from us. This is what Avraham, Yitzchak, and Yaakov did" can be combined.
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Sentence fragment:** "That the Avos understood the message" is not a complete sentence. Attach it to the preceding sentence: **"The pasuk means that the Avos understood the message."**
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Fix:** "Their avodas Hashem was the ultimate perfection, the shleimus of their time prior to Matan Torah" is awkward. Suggested: **"Their avodas Hashem represented the highest shleimus attainable before Matan Torah."**
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Theological/source query:** The explanation of "Hakol bidei Shamayim chutz miyiras Shamayim" personifies *Shamayim* as a plural decision-maker that "can say yes or no" and "must say yes." Attribute this carefully to the named speaker, verify the wording, and avoid presenting it as the plain meaning of the Gemara.
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Agreement:** "Shamayim has bechirah. They can say..." shifts from singular to plural. Choose one grammatical treatment.
- **Printed p. 89 (archive p. 109), Fix:** Remove the comma in **"I was so taken by it, that every single day..."**
- **Printed p. 90 (archive p. 110), Source/name query:** Verify the spelling and identification of **R' Zev of Strickov** and the attribution of the explanation of Tehillim 19:10.
- **Printed p. 90 (archive p. 110), Logic query:** The second group did not bring their servants and livestock inside, yet the prose says "even if we are technically fulfilling the dvar Hashem." Clarify how that conclusion follows from this group, which appears not to have fulfilled the command.
- **Printed p. 90 (archive p. 110), Redundancy:** "each and every day" can be **"each day."**
- **Printed p. 90 (archive p. 110), Punctuation:** Set off **"Im Yirtzeh Hashem"** with commas in the final sentence.

### Vaeira 5785

- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Fix:** "Hashem promises Moshe Rabbeinu He will do everything necessary" is missing **"that."** Use **"Hashem promises Moshe Rabbeinu that He will do..."**
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"the four leshonos of geulah, and all of Hashem's promises, are contrasted..."**
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Fix:** "we find a yichus sefer by second aliyah" is an unnatural description. Suggested: **"we encounter a genealogical account in the second aliyah."**
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Fix:** "Moshe and Aharon, as great as they were, were people" repeats "were." Suggested: **"Despite their greatness, Moshe and Aharon were human beings."**
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Tone/source query:** "They were not gods, as other religions often portray their heroes" is a sweeping characterization of other religions. Attribute this precisely to Rav Hirsch or recast more narrowly.
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Tone:** "these were not lunatics claiming, 'I am a Mashiach, I will save you!'" is jarring and pejorative. Verify whether this is a direct quotation; otherwise use a neutral formulation.
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Fix:** "individuals of great yichus and segulos, holy and good people, with the qualifications..." is not parallel. Recast the list into one grammatical form.
- **Printed p. 91 (archive p. 111), Fix:** "The Be'er Mayim Chaim offers a tremendous insight, providing a haskel mussar for us" is redundant and mixed-register. Use **"offers a powerful mussar lesson."**
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Fix:** "The kedushah of Aharon and Moshe remained exactly the same, that the tumah of Pharaoh had zero effect on them" is grammatically broken. Suggested: **"Their kedushah remained unchanged; Pharaoh's tumah had no effect on them."**
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Style:** "Not even kemelo nima, a hairbreadth" is a fragment. Integrate it into the preceding sentence.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Hyphenation:** Use **"deal-making"** as a compound noun.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Major syntax/bidi error:** The sentence about R' Yosef Chaim Sonnenfeld is visibly malformed: **"was bitterly criticized by the other members of certainly the anti-dati, but even the dati, to 'give a little bit', but..."** The English and Hebrew modifiers have become reordered, "but" appears twice, and the intended groups are unclear. Reconstruct this sentence from the source recording.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Fix:** "we have to not compromise" should be **"we must not compromise."**
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Repetition:** The paragraph repeats "our principles," "a little," and "we have to" several times. Tighten before publication.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Tone/query:** The examples "daven a little faster," "come a little bit later," and "don't wear the things that we used to wear" make a broad judgment without defining the principle at issue. Clarify the intended standard or remove the vague clothing reference.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Redundancy:** "untarnished and uncompromised" can be reduced to one adjective.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Fix:** "for our dor doros, our children and our grandchildren" is redundant and grammatically loose. Use **"for future generations, including our children and grandchildren."**
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Clarity:** "continue our segulos, our yeshuos, and our shalsheles" combines unlike concepts and does not convey a clear meaning. Confirm the intended nouns.
- **Printed p. 92 (archive p. 112), Bidi:** The closing Hebrew prayer is reversed around the exclamation point. Keep the complete prayer and "Amen" in one RTL span.

### Bo 5783

- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Punctuation:** Add a comma in **"for some of them Pharaoh received a warning, but for choshech he did not."**
- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Comma splice:** "This darkness was unlike any other, it was so intense and thick..." should be two sentences or use a semicolon.
- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Tone:** "to take a look at all their goodies" is too casual. Use **"to see where the Egyptians kept their possessions."**
- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Possessive:** Use **"the Egyptians' houses,"** not "the Mitzriyim houses."
- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Repetition:** "if you don't see your friend when he's having difficulty, he's having his own tzaar" repeats the same idea. Condense.
- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Fix:** "When a person ignores and totally is uninterested in his friend's difficulty" has faulty adverb placement. Suggested: **"When a person ignores a friend's distress and shows no interest in helping..."**
- **Printed p. 93 (archive p. 113), Source/tone query:** The categorical prediction that such a person will experience "exactly the same issues" should be clearly attributed to the Chidushei HaRim rather than stated as an editor's factual assertion.

### Bo 5784

- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Punctuation:** The sentence introducing the three difficult topics uses a semicolon where a comma or colon is needed before "until Hakadosh Baruch Hu showed him each one."
- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Fix:** "the fundamentally divergent concepts of our olam of gashmiyus and the Torah" is awkward. Suggested: **"the apparently divergent realms of gashmiyus and Torah."**
- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Redundancy:** "the world itself is sustained and only exists" can be **"the world exists only in the zechus of Torah."**
- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Hebrew grammar:** "sheratzim, which are tamei" should use a plural adjective or be rendered in English: **"sheratzim, which are impure."**
- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Source/context query:** The statement that the nations "are not called adam" should retain the exact Yevamos citation and enough context to prevent an overbroad or misleading reading.
- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Fix:** "Each of these concepts shares a common theme" is fine, but the following question and answer overstate that all three are explained "only" by zechus haTorah. Make clear that this is the cited sefer's homiletic synthesis.
- **Printed p. 94 (archive p. 114), Tone:** "not through superior intellect, scientific achievements, or Nobel Prizes" is polemical and dated. Confirm that this contrast is intentional.

### Bo 5785

- **Printed p. 95 (archive p. 115), Query:** "in an actual, physical sense" does not fit the spiritual "inspiration and enlightenment" described immediately afterward. Verify whether **"experiential"** or **"tangible"** is intended.
- **Printed p. 95 (archive p. 115), Transcript cleanup:** "We recently experienced it during Chanukah, and since I'm recording this just after Chanukah, it's fresh in my mind" should be recast or removed from permanent book prose.
- **Printed p. 95 (archive p. 115), Source query:** The claims that the berachah generates the Ohr HaGanuz in the Chanukah candles and that many gedolim perceived this hidden force should be sourced specifically.
- **Printed p. 95 (archive p. 115), Translation error:** "like an eagle who spreads over his wings over his little eagles, the little babies" repeats "over" and mistranslates the image. Suggested: **"like an eagle awakening its nest and hovering over its young."**
- **Printed p. 95 (archive p. 115), Fix:** "the sefer *Aperyon*, written the Baal Kitzur Shulchan Aruch" is missing **"by."** Use **"written by the author of the *Kitzur Shulchan Aruch*, R' Shlomo Ganzfried."**
- **Printed p. 95 (archive p. 115), Fix:** "in a big, fast hurry" is redundant and colloquial. Use **"in great haste."**
- **Printed pp. 95-96 (archive pp. 115-116), Halachic query:** The discussion of matzah made with fruit juice, wine, oil, honey, or milk concerns practical halachah and should be checked by a qualified halachic reviewer. In particular, distinguish "does not become chametz" from whether it may be used for the mitzvah at the Seder.
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Repetition:** "because that's how they did it in Mitzrayim. Therefore, that's what the zecher... is" repeats the same causal phrase. Condense.
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Bidi/grammar:** The sentence beginning "We davka use mayim" is visually unstable around the Hebrew words. Ensure the English verb remains in the correct position after layout.
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Punctuation:** "It would have been much better to do it without any problem of chametz?" is a statement with a question mark. Use **"Would it not have been better...?"**
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"the same elevation in our spiritual status, that they had in Mitzrayim."**
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Tone:** "What a gigantic chiddush!" is very conversational. Consider **"This yields a striking chiddush."**
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Word choice:** "No matter how poor our mitzvah observance is" sounds dismissive. Consider **"however imperfect our observance may be."**
- **Printed p. 96 (archive p. 116), Repetition:** "the proper way, which is the way that our earlier generations did them" should be **"in the manner transmitted by earlier generations."**

### Beshalach 5783 (1)

- **Printed p. 97 (archive p. 117), Word choice:** "the once-in-history nes" is not idiomatic. Use **"the singular miracle"** or **"the once-in-history event."**
- **Printed p. 97 (archive p. 117), Parallelism:** "The first purpose was yeshuas Yisrael - allowing... The second purpose was to drown..." mixes a noun phrase and an infinitive. Make both purposes grammatical equivalents.
- **Printed p. 97 (archive p. 117), Word choice:** "water splitting is an extraordinary, unnatural event" should be **"the splitting of water is..."**
- **Printed p. 97 (archive p. 117), Fix:** "the yad hagedolah of Hashem was demonstrated through what happened to Mitzrayim, that the waters returned" needs a colon or "namely." Suggested: **"was demonstrated in what happened to Egypt: the waters returned and drowned the pursuers."**

### Beshalach 5783 (2)

- **Printed p. 98 (archive p. 118), Production error:** A period appears alone on a line between the quoted pasuk and "different methods of explaining a dvar Torah." Remove it.
- **Printed p. 98 (archive p. 118), Fix:** "uses this pasuk as a remez to a beautiful middah" should be **"as a remez for a beautiful middah."**
- **Printed p. 98 (archive p. 118), Redundancy:** "allowing one's mouth the freedom to speak freely" repeats "free." Suggested: **"before allowing oneself to speak without restraint."**
- **Printed p. 98 (archive p. 118), Style:** "A good idea" is an abrupt oral aside. Delete or integrate it into the conclusion.

### Beshalach 5784

- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Fix:** "contains many important topics, with a highlight being Shirat HaYam" is clumsy. Suggested: **"contains many major events, foremost among them Shirat HaYam."**
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Style:** "This cryptic chiddush demands elucidation" is inflated. Use **"This statement requires explanation."**
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Fix:** "it was considered that Moshe Rabbeinu did an aveirah" should be **"it was considered an aveirah on Moshe Rabbeinu's level."**
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Vagueness:** "somehow, the 'az' that Moshe said..." signals an unresolved thought. State the proposed mechanism directly.
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Preposition:** "There are two perspectives of his praise" should be **"two perspectives on his praise."**
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Syntax:** The sentence beginning "Their primary joy was the realization that through their suffering and redemption..." needs commas around the intervening phrase or a full recast.
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Comma splice:** "when he said 'az' then, it was not a complaint, rather, he was thanking Hashem" should be split or use a semicolon and **"rather."**
- **Printed p. 99 (archive p. 119), Word choice:** "through the modality of shibud Mitzrayim and geulas Mitzrayim" is unnecessarily technical. Use **"through both the enslavement and the redemption."**
- **Printed p. 100 (archive p. 120), Punctuation:** "Hashem, I am praising You for my inuy, for the suffering that I had, Why?" needs a period before **"Why?"**
- **Printed p. 100 (archive p. 120), Preposition:** "giving You thanks both on the inuy... and the yeshuah" should be **"giving thanks for both the suffering and the salvation."**
- **Printed p. 100 (archive p. 120), Transcript style:** "Are we on such a madregah? I don't know about you, but I am not" is conversational. Recast in a more durable register if the book aims for edited prose.
- **Printed p. 100 (archive p. 120), Duplicated word:** "absolute bedrock emunah that that is the truth" should have only one **"that."**

### Beshalach 5785

- **Printed p. 101 (archive p. 121), Repetition:** "all these tapes, all the topics, and the entire Jewish year... all emanating" repeats "all" four times. Tighten.
- **Printed p. 101 (archive p. 121), Source query:** Verify the spelling of **R' Beryl Weisbord**, the title of the Chanukah sefer published by Yeshivas Ner Yisrael, and the precise attribution of the vort.
- **Printed p. 101 (archive p. 121), Fix:** "the last three seem to be natural outcomes we would expect when engaging in Torah and mitzvos, that Hashem will give us hatzlachah" has a dangling "that." Suggested: **"the last three seem like expected spiritual outcomes of Torah and mitzvos rather than miracles."**
- **Printed p. 101 (archive p. 121), Style:** "R' Beryl brings down a Ramban" can be tightened to **"R' Beryl cites the Ramban."**
- **Printed p. 101 (archive p. 121), Broad claim/query:** "Klal Yisrael are not natural warriors" and "any battle ... is merely a means to escape" should remain explicitly attributed to the cited interpretation.
- **Printed p. 101 (archive p. 121), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"everything that we do our entire lives, is a nes."**
- **Printed p. 102 (archive p. 122), Repetition:** The opening sentence repeats "milchamah" and "the fact of the matter." Recast to **"Although the rescue occurred through battle, victory came not from human strength..."**
- **Printed p. 102 (archive p. 122), Word choice:** "working with swords, bows and arrows" should be **"fighting with swords, bows, and arrows."**
- **Printed p. 102 (archive p. 122), Bidi:** The sequence quoting the final clauses of *Al HaNissim* is difficult to parse because the Hebrew pairs and the surrounding English reorder. Set each pair in its own RTL span or present them as a short vertical list.
- **Printed p. 102 (archive p. 122), Missing word:** "The pasuk is telling that the teva of gravity..." should be **"telling us that..."**
- **Printed p. 102 (archive p. 122), Agreement/wording:** "the teva that water drowns people is exactly the same nissim niflaos as lemaalah min hateva" is grammatically mismatched. Suggested: **"ordinary gravity and buoyancy are no less expressions of divine power than overt miracles."**

### Yisro 5783

- **Printed p. 103 (archive p. 123), Fix:** "The Mechilta uses a very specific lashon, a mamonis lashon" repeats "lashon" and uses awkward adjective order. Suggested: **"The Mechilta uses the language of ownership."**
- **Printed p. 103 (archive p. 123), Source query:** The halachic principle that an unlimited undertaking does not take effect, "even if it is a neder," should include the exact Rambam citation and be reviewed for precise scope.
- **Printed p. 103 (archive p. 123), Punctuation/bidi:** The parenthetical about the finite number of words in the Chamishah Chumshei Torah and an extra word invalidating a Sefer Torah is crowded and visually unstable. Recast it as a separate sentence.
- **Printed p. 103 (archive p. 123), Question form:** "According to halachah, accepting an undefined obligation is not binding?" should be **"How, then, could an undefined obligation be binding?"**
- **Printed p. 103 (archive p. 123), Fix:** "I accept upon myself to become Reuven's eved" should be **"I accept upon myself the status of Reuven's eved"** or **"I agree to become..."**
- **Printed p. 103 (archive p. 123), Hebrew agreement:** With the plural subject "the nations," use the plural form **"meshubadim,"** not "meshubad."

### Yisro 5784

- **Printed p. 104 (archive p. 124), Sentence fragment:** The opening Hebrew verse is followed by **"possibly the most beautiful pasuk in the Torah"** without a verb. Add **"is."**
- **Printed p. 104 (archive p. 124), Tone:** "Even a simple bank teller like Rav Kehati" sounds patronizing. Suggested: **"Rav Pinchas Kehati, who worked as a bank clerk, demonstrates..."**
- **Printed p. 104 (archive p. 124), Word choice:** "an enlightened teaching" should likely be **"an illuminating teaching."**
- **Printed p. 104 (archive p. 124), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"the malachei hashareis, who serve before Hashem, complained"** unless the clause is deliberately parenthetical.
- **Printed p. 104 (archive p. 124), Translation error:** The angels' question is translated as **"coming here amongst them."** It should be **"among us."**
- **Printed p. 104 (archive p. 124), Wrong verb:** "Moshe Rabbeinu does an astounding thing to Hashem" should be **"says something astounding to Hashem"** or, better, state the response directly.
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Tone:** "We don't buy it" is too colloquial for imagined speech by the malachim. Use **"We do not accept that answer."**
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Source query:** The extended imagined dialogue between Moshe and the malachim goes beyond the quoted Gemara. Mark clearly what is direct text, what is Midrash, and what is explanatory paraphrase.
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Pronoun clarity:** "when they promised tinokos shel beis rabban" has no clear antecedent. Name Klal Yisrael or the relevant speakers.
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Style:** "allowed us to get the Torah" should be **"allowed the Torah to be given to us."**
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Missing object:** "The tinokos shel beis rabban are the answer to the malachim and enable to receive the Torah" should be **"enable us to receive the Torah."**
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Redundancy:** "return back" should be **"return."**
- **Printed p. 105 (archive p. 125), Tone:** "Let us all be kinderlach" is highly colloquial. Consider **"May we recover a childlike acceptance..."**

### Yisro 5785

- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Relative pronoun:** "something which we should not have" reads more naturally as **"something that we should not have."**
- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Fix:** "The first is from the Ibn Ezra, that by cultivating..." is grammatically broken. Suggested: **"The first, from Ibn Ezra, teaches that by cultivating..."**
- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Wrong idiom:** A princess is not beyond the farmhand's **"ken"** (knowledge); she is beyond his **"station"** or **"reach."**
- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Run-on sentence:** "Imagine someone driven by a strong taavah, he really wants to do something..." is a comma splice followed by a long string of alternatives. Divide and condense.
- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Transcript cleanup:** The square-bracketed aside about New York weather and relatives in warmer climates should be removed from the finished essay.
- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Typo:** "A pathway to achieve an inkling into Hashem's infinite greatness **it** to recognize..." should be **"is to recognize."**
- **Printed p. 106 (archive p. 126), Source/quotation query:** The direct quotation placed in Yisro's mouth about witnessing Pharaoh's secret plotting should be verified against its Midrashic source or presented as paraphrase.
- **Printed p. 107 (archive p. 127), Clarity:** "a lesson for us in yiras Shamayim, the purpose of the Aseres HaDibros" makes the final phrase appear to define yiras Shamayim as the sole purpose of all Ten Commandments. Clarify the intended relationship.

### Mishpatim 5783

- **Printed p. 108 (archive p. 128), Preposition:** "Yisro's arrival to join Klal Yisrael" should be **"Yisro's arrival among Klal Yisrael"** or **"his decision to join..."**
- **Printed p. 108 (archive p. 128), Redundancy:** "the true climax is actually Parshas Mishpatim" needs only one intensifier.
- **Printed p. 108 (archive p. 128), Style:** "important ones, certainly" is a transcript-like aside. Integrate or delete it.
- **Printed p. 108 (archive p. 128), Grammar:** "two children were becoming bar mitzvah the same Shabbos" should be **"were becoming bar mitzvah on the same Shabbos."**
- **Printed p. 108 (archive p. 128), Source query:** Verify the well-known story attributed to R' Chaim Kanievsky, including the identities, hospital circumstances, and whether the boy switched from Yisro to Mishpatim.
- **Printed p. 109 (archive p. 129), Quotation punctuation:** Write **"'However,' he said, 'now I am stuck...'"** The comma belongs inside the first quotation mark, and "now" is lowercase after the dialogue tag.
- **Printed p. 109 (archive p. 129), Quotation punctuation:** The period in **"prepare Parshas Mishpatim instead".** belongs inside the closing quotation mark under the manuscript's prevailing US style.
- **Printed p. 109 (archive p. 129), Duplicated word/idea:** "Her condition worsened and worsened" should be **"continued to worsen."**
- **Printed p. 109 (archive p. 129), Typo:** "the rav approached the boy to thank him for his **leaning**" should be **"leining"** or **"Torah reading."**
- **Printed p. 109 (archive p. 129), Sentence fragment:** "All because a young boy was ready to forgo his due..." should be attached to the preceding sentence or made complete.
- **Printed p. 109 (archive p. 129), Run-on/repetition:** "I'm happy to relate it to those of you who have not heard before, and those who have heard it before, it's a great idea to hear it one more time" repeats "heard" and joins two independent clauses with a comma. Suggested: **"It bears retelling for both new and familiar audiences."**

### Mishpatim (1) 5784

- **Printed p. 110 (archive p. 130), Category mismatch:** "the halachos of Bava Metzia, Bava Kama, Nezikin, and Bava Basra" mixes tractates with the broader seder. Use **"the masechtos of Seder Nezikin, including Bava Kama, Bava Metzia, and Bava Basra."**
- **Printed p. 110 (archive p. 130), Number agreement:** "One of the classic halachos" or **"one classic halachah,"** not "one of the classic halachah/halachos" if the current wording is singular.
- **Printed p. 110 (archive p. 130), Style:** "none of us are going around stealing beheimos and shechting them" is conversational. Use **"Although this law does not describe ordinary contemporary conduct..."**
- **Printed p. 110 (archive p. 130), Logic query:** The Torah's reduced payment for a stolen sheep is explained here as reflecting the thief's embarrassment or burden, but the application suddenly calls this a **"lack of joy in his aveirah."** Explain the inferential step or preserve the source's actual language.
- **Printed p. 110 (archive p. 130), Repetition:** "That is the tochachah. That is the mussar" can be one sentence.
- **Printed p. 111 (archive p. 131), Malformed sentence:** "we should be grabbing them, collecting like that the jewels that they are" is not grammatical. Suggested: **"we should seize these opportunities and treasure them like jewels."**
- **Printed p. 111 (archive p. 131), Repetition:** The next sentence repeats both **"collecting"** and the **"jewels"** metaphor. Keep one formulation.
- **Printed p. 111 (archive p. 131), Source/wording query:** "he lit the menorah with the same enthusiasm each time" is an interpretive expansion of *shelo shinah*. Attribute that emotional reading to its source rather than presenting it as the verse's plain meaning.

### Mishpatim (2) 5784

- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Typo:** "did not **abandoned** him" should be **"did not abandon him."**
- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Source query:** The claim that Aharon was permitted to ascend and voluntarily remained behind out of sensitivity to the elders needs an exact source. The peshat of Shemos 24:1-2 distinguishes among several levels of ascent, so this interpretation should not be stated without attribution.
- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Word choice:** "visit a mortal king" sounds social rather than confrontational. **"Approach a mortal king"** or **"go before a mortal king"** is more precise.
- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Fragmented rhetoric:** "So why didn't he? Who decided he shouldn't go up? Aharon himself" reads like a transcript. Combine it into one edited sentence.
- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Sentence fragments:** "What an incredible act of gadlus. What remarkable chesed" should either be made complete or joined to the following sentence.
- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Repetition:** The final paragraph uses **"leader"** four times and repeats **"feel/feelings"** several times. Tighten the paragraph.
- **Printed p. 112 (archive p. 132), Word choice:** A leader does not necessarily **"command respect"** merely by holding a title. **"receive deference"** may better fit the contrast being made.

### Mishpatim 5785

- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Grammar:** "If a person is a mezid" turns an adjective into a person-label. Use **"If a person acts intentionally"** or **"If the killing was intentional."**
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Run-on sentence:** The sentence beginning "if he runs, as the famous Gemara says" contains the verse, the Yoav narrative, and an explanation in one long chain. Divide it.
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Wrong comparison:** Yoav did not hold the mizbeach **"like a sanctuary city."** He grasped its horns in an attempt to claim sanctuary.
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Euphemistic wording:** "take him away and take care of him" is vague and colloquial. The verse means that he is removed from the altar to be put to death; state that precisely if appropriate for the audience.
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"The Aperyon, from R' Shlomo Ganzfried, brings out..."** Suggested: **"The Aperyon by R' Shlomo Ganzfried offers..."**
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Repetition:** *Lefi aniyus daati* is quoted, repeated in brackets, and then translated as **"in my small and humble opinion."** One quotation plus one translation is enough.
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Pronoun/register:** "why did the Torah have to give **you** a separate... warning" shifts into second person. Use **"give a separate warning"** or **"warn the kohanim separately."**
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Overlong question:** The paragraph asking why a ramp was necessary despite the bigdei kehunah runs for more than six lines and repeats **"cover/covered."** Condense it.
- **Printed p. 113 (archive p. 133), Category/logic query:** "all evidence, eidim, and accumulated data" treats valid testimony and general data as interchangeable. In a discussion of dinei nefashos, use the source's exact evidentiary categories.
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Mixed-language grammar:** "Don't rush to dan him to misa" should be **"Do not rush to judge him guilty"** or **"Do not rush to sentence him to death."**
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Major transcript cleanup:** The full parenthetical beginning "Now what's fascinating" is a spoken aside rather than finished prose. It repeats **"So," "I think,"** and the ramp question, contains several fragments, and should be substantially recast or omitted.
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Source accuracy:** In the account of the kohanim racing, verify the tractate, the precise injury, and the sequence leading to the lottery. The current paraphrase, "he knocked him down and he hurt him," is too loose for a cited Gemara.
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Number agreement:** "to pick which kohanim did which avodah" should be **"to determine which kohen performed each avodah."**
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Mixed-language grammar:** "Always be dan l'chaf zechus" should be **"Always judge others l'chaf zechus"** or fully translated.
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Punctuation/bidi:** In the child's dialogue, the comma and quotation marks around **"Shabbos"** are displaced by the Hebrew-English direction change. Rebuild the quotation with isolated RTL text.
- **Printed p. 114 (archive p. 134), Tone:** "and you will be happy you did" is a generic slogan. A more specific closing would better match the essay's argument.

### Teruma 5783

- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Calendar/source query:** Clarify when **Shovavim Tat** includes Teruma and Tetzaveh. In common usage, the additional *Tat* weeks are associated with a leap year, so the current wording may overgeneralize.
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Awkward hybrid:** "a yesodosdik Ramban" mixes registers. Suggested: **"a foundational Ramban."**
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Repetition:** "The purpose of that was that Har Sinai was..." should be **"Har Sinai was a singular event..."**
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Missing word:** "Why isn't Parshas Teruma immediately after Parshas Yisro?" needs **"placed"** if the question concerns sequence: **"Why isn't Parshas Teruma placed immediately after...?"**
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Tone:** "in his usual fantastic Brisker style" is promotional. Name the analytical move instead.
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Tense:** "whoever wanted to give... **has** to give" should be consistently past, present, or modal.
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Overstatement/query:** "giving tzedakah is meaningless" is too categorical unless that is the source's exact claim. A stolen contribution may be invalid or tainted; explain the halachic point precisely.
- **Printed p. 115 (archive p. 135), Source query:** Verify the formulation that a stolen lulav means the person **"does not fulfill the mitzvah"** in every relevant circumstance, and cite the exact rule of *mitzvah haba'ah b'aveirah* being applied.
- **Printed p. 116 (archive p. 136), Bidi/punctuation:** The closing Hebrew prayer is attached directly to the English sentence without a clear separator. Put it on its own RTL line or introduce it with a colon.

### Teruma 5784

- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Repetition:** "Parshas Teruma is the famous parsha that discusses" repeats *parsha*. Suggested: **"Parshas Teruma describes..."**
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Comma splice:** "the main quality of the Mishkan was nedivas lev, every single piece..." needs a semicolon, period, or relative clause.
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Vague wording:** "All institutions that we build on generosity like that fall into that category" does not identify the category or explain the practical implication.
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Transcript style:** "I want to do something unusual" and "in the next Rashi he goes on the word" should be recast as direct exposition.
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Tone:** "those pesukim have the mazal" and "the end is not so mazaldik" are cute but imprecise. Explain that end-of-parsha verses receive less attention.
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Redundancy:** "all instruments and all equipment, utensils" lists three overlapping terms. Use the source's specific categories.
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Sentence fragment:** "Not only that, the hammers as well, and pins..." needs a main verb.
- **Printed p. 117 (archive p. 137), Repetition:** "Their very name gives away and reveals" uses two synonyms for the same action.
- **Printed p. 118 (archive p. 138), Transcript residue:** "That's what I thought was quite fascinating" and "Look it up" style commentary should be removed or converted into direct prose.
- **Printed p. 118 (archive p. 138), Unresolved author note:** **"(This is my vort, I hope I'm not wrong)"** cannot remain in a finished volume. Verify the interpretation, attribute it as a suggestion, or omit it.
- **Printed p. 118 (archive p. 138), Factual/source query:** Verify which Mishkan vessels were ultimately used in the Beis Hamikdash. The aron, menorah, and kiyor did not necessarily share the same history, and the broad claim needs qualification.
- **Printed p. 118 (archive p. 138), Theological overstatement:** "anything given for Hashem gains eternity automatically" should be attributed as a homiletic conclusion rather than stated as a literal rule.
- **Printed p. 118 (archive p. 138), Word choice:** "Hashem's projects" sounds corporate. Use **"sacred purposes"** or name the specific mitzvah.

### Teruma 5785

- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Word choice:** "the initiation of the entire story" should be **"the beginning of the account."**
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Sequence/punctuation:** The list **"Teruma, Tetzaveh including Vayakhel, Ki Sisa and Pekudei"** is confusing and not in Torah order. Punctuate and order the parshiyos deliberately.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Transcript residue:** "We've had that concept of the Ramban before" should be replaced by the concept itself or by a precise cross-reference.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Vague attribution:** "The mefarshim say they represent Creation" needs a named source and an explicit antecedent for **"they."**
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Imprecision:** "about 30 or 40 years ago" is unnecessarily vague. Give the relevant date, say **"decades ago,"** or omit the time marker.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Wrong noun form:** "a beautiful sefer that illustrates the keilim" should likely be **"a beautifully illustrated sefer about the keilim."**
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Repetition:** The paragraph announcing two points says **"two," "one," "second," "both,"** and **"unrelated"** in quick succession. Condense.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Stacked superlatives:** "the most important, most holy feature" should be one carefully chosen description.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Antecedent error:** The sentence says the *luchos* "were visited once a year by the Kohen Gadol." It was the Kodesh HaKodashim that he entered, not the tablets that he visited.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Transcript brackets:** Both bracketed comments explaining that Hashem has no fingers are spoken asides. Integrate one concise qualification into the prose and remove the brackets.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Apparent transcription error:** "as Casey said, you could look it up" is unexplained and almost certainly does not belong in the final text. Check the recording for the intended name or phrase.
- **Printed p. 119 (archive p. 139), Colloquial analogy:** "like an Erector set" dates the prose and may distract. Use **"from separately fashioned pieces"** unless the analogy is essential.
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Wrong idiom:** "here comes the bomb kashya" should be **"the central difficulty is..."**
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Colloquial/anachronistic wording:** The keruvim are described as being **"screwed onto"** the kapores. Use **"attached separately"** for the hypothetical construction.
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Repetition:** "made by themselves by a nes... made by itself by a nes" should be rewritten once, with correct number agreement.
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Grammar:** "The keruvim had a demus of two tinokos, a b'feirush Gemara" is malformed. Suggested: **"The Gemara in Sukkah 5b explicitly describes the keruvim as having the appearance of children."**
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Overstatement:** "The Torah is meramez to us in this tremendous accomplishment of making this kli" is abstract and overloaded. State the inferred lesson directly and attribute it to the cited sefer.
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Repetition:** "by themselves" appears three times within two sentences in the child-rearing application.
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Sentence fragment/repetition:** "Only after tremendous effort, amal rav, with tremendous tefillos..." lacks a main clause and repeats **"tremendous."**
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Awkward fragment:** "A famous proof of this, and this was what sealed the greatness of this vort" needs a complete sentence and a less self-congratulatory formulation.
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Grammar:** "the locale know as Shittim" should be **"the location known as Shittim."**
- **Printed p. 120 (archive p. 140), Source query:** The claim that the water at Shittim itself drew people toward immorality needs an exact Midrashic citation and careful wording.
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Repetition:** "He creates the refuah before He creates the makkah" repeats **"creates"** unnecessarily.
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Punctuation:** Add a question mark after **"where Rashi will translate a word into French."**
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Name/source check:** Verify the spelling and title of **Rav David Olewski** and the chain of attribution from the Shem MiShmuel to the Chidushei HaRim.
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Sensitivity/source warning:** The claim that French is inherently immoral and that French culture is "steeped in tumah" is a sweeping national stereotype. Quote and contextualize the source exactly, or omit the generalization.
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Grammar/word choice:** "can transform dirt into clean" should be **"can transform impurity into purity."**
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Unsupported intention:** "Rashi sought to be metaher" states Rashi's motive as fact, although the paragraph first presents it as **"perhaps."** Keep the speculation consistently qualified.
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Vague wording:** "people who use language which is unbelievable" should name the problem, for example **"coarse or degrading language."**
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Redundancy:** "I shrink and I shudder" uses two near-synonyms.
- **Printed p. 121 (archive p. 141), Logic:** "learn from them how to be better people" appears to say that the speakers of offensive language are the model. Suggested: **"Let the contrast teach us to speak and act more carefully."**

### Tetzaveh 5783

- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Clarity:** "Any time a person goes up to daven" is ambiguous. If this means approaching the amud, say so; if it means entering shul, use that phrase.
