- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Overmodified list:** "a great zechus, a tremendous obligation, and a remarkable opportunity" mixes three abstractions and two superlatives. Tighten.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Awkward question:** "which mitzvah, if you had to choose just one... includes all the other mitzvos?" should be recast as one direct question.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Source/title query:** Verify the exact title and role of **Hakosev** in the introduction to *Ein Yaakov*, and verify the names and wording of the three opinions.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Bracketed prose:** The full Rashi explanation is set in square brackets although it is part of the main argument. Either integrate it normally or label it as an editorial note.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Redundancy:** "Morning and evening, every single day" repeats the daily idea.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Triple synonym:** "represent, signify, and declare" should be reduced to one verb.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Sweeping claim/query:** "the ultimate purpose of all mitzvos" should be attributed to the cited interpretation and not presented as an uncontested definition.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Category mismatch:** "Shabbos, Yom Tov, mamonos, nezikin and korbanos" mixes days, legal categories, and offerings. Use parallel categories or explain the range.
- **Printed p. 122 (archive p. 142), Overgeneralization:** Dividing every life into a first half of ascent and a second half of descent, followed by a broad claim that age necessarily brings physical decline, needs attribution and gentler wording.

### Tetzaveh (1) 5784

- **Printed p. 124 (archive p. 144), Word choice:** "one of the rare parshiyos" should be **"one of the few parshiyos."**
- **Printed p. 124 (archive p. 144), Calendar query:** "almost always coincides with 7 Adar" should be checked against the calendar record and qualified if necessary.
- **Printed p. 124 (archive p. 144), Unresolved research note:** "I haven't counted how many times" should not remain. Count, omit, or say simply **"the word recurs throughout the parsha."**
- **Printed p. 124 (archive p. 144), Repetition:** The discussion repeats *ve'atah*, **"constant,"** and **"every single"** in close succession.
- **Printed p. 124 (archive p. 144), Parenthetical transcript:** The bracketed aside beginning "in his cute way" is conversational, repeats itself, and should be removed or rewritten as one sentence.
- **Printed p. 125 (archive p. 145), Word-choice query:** *Lehavdil* is unnecessary in a simple comparison between Hebrew and English uses of **"constant."**
- **Printed p. 125 (archive p. 145), Redundancy:** "no breaks, no moments of stepping away" says the same thing twice.
- **Printed p. 125 (archive p. 145), Source/logic query:** The explanation moves from *ner tamid* to the claim that "Hashem's light is upon us at all times." Make the exegetical step explicit and attribute it.
- **Printed p. 125 (archive p. 145), Apparent contradiction:** Yom Kippur is called both **"once a year"** and **"a constant avodah."** Clarify that its obligation recurs consistently from generation to generation.
- **Printed p. 125 (archive p. 145), Excessive repetition:** **"constant/constantly/consistently"** appears many times in the final two paragraphs. Vary or condense.
- **Printed p. 125 (archive p. 145), Awkward closing:** "successful in that l'dor doros" should be recast, for example **"May we sustain that constancy for generations."**

### Tetzaveh (2) 5784

- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Overmodified phrase:** "a fantastic, beautiful simcha" needs one adjective.
- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Repetition:** The introduction says **"connection"** and **"simcha"** twice in two sentences.
- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Unclear exposition:** "just as the yonah, in various ways, brings light" does not explain the dove image. State the source's specific association.
- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Unnecessary personal aside:** The bracket about being asked whether the author is related to R' Benet interrupts the argument and should be removed from the edited piece.
- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Scientific overstatement:** Oil does not mix readily with water, but **"does not intermingle or mix with any other liquid"** is too broad.
- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Translation/source error:** The Gemara's phrase about a kallah whose eyes are beautiful is glossed as **"meaning she is satisfied and content."** That is an interpretation, not a literal translation; present the derivation and cite the source accurately.
- **Printed p. 126 (archive p. 146), Comma splice/repetition:** "If Hashem sees that you are satisfied, that you're content, then the Shechinah will rest upon you and rest upon the house" repeats the same ideas. Tighten and fix the clause structure.
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Word choice:** "done every Shabbos by the eishes chayil" should be **"performed each Shabbos by the woman of the household"** or another precise formulation.
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Causal/source query:** "with olive oil, then there will be even more shalom bayis" needs a source and should not read as an automatic causal guarantee.
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Duplicated phrase:** **"a great lesson"** is repeated within the same paragraph.
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Intentional but excessive repetition:** "more and more and more" and the repeated **"happy"** may work orally but should be reduced in print.
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Colloquialism:** "happy with what we've got" should be **"content with what we have."**
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Quotation punctuation:** The period belongs inside the closing quotation mark in **"I just want a little bit more".** under the manuscript's prevailing US style.
- **Printed p. 127 (archive p. 147), Unclear antecedent:** "a lot of berachos from all of them" does not clearly identify who **"them"** refers to.

### Tetzaveh 5785

- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Repetition:** "Parshas Zachor... coincides with Parshas Tetzaveh. Tetzaveh is a parsha..." repeats both title and category.
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"This connects to, Haman, Amalek, and Purim."**
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Local transcript aside:** The bracketed note about who leins Zachor in the author's shul should be omitted unless the book intentionally preserves local context.
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Redundancy:** "an annual mitzvah which we observe year after year" repeats the meaning of **"annual."**
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Overstatement:** "Who is Amalek? We don't know" needs nuance. The halachic and historical identification issues are more complex than complete ignorance.
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Loaded wording:** "hold onto our enmity" is stronger than the mitzvah's language of remembering Amalek's attack. Use exact source language and explain the distinction from personal revenge.
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Factual/source check:** Identify Yochanan Kohen Gadol precisely and include the Gemara's stated duration of service if that detail is part of the argument.
- **Printed p. 128 (archive p. 148), Broad causal claim:** The bracketed aside says anti-Semitism disappears when Jews repent and that all evil stems from Jewish sins. This is theologically and ethically sensitive, can sound victim-blaming, and must be either removed or explicitly attributed and carefully contextualized.
- **Printed p. 129 (archive p. 149), Source query:** The claim that Esav saw or understood Yaakov's actual angels yet remained undeterred needs an exact source beyond Rashi's phrase *malachim mamash*.
- **Printed p. 129 (archive p. 149), Quotation accuracy:** Lavan's imagined words, especially **"I can barely hold myself back,"** are not the verse's direct wording. Mark them as paraphrase.
- **Printed p. 129 (archive p. 149), Missing preposition:** "to last minute" should be **"until the last minute."**
- **Printed p. 129 (archive p. 149), Source query:** The deathbed story and the instruction to say Shema so observers will think the person died righteous require a traceable source.
- **Printed p. 129 (archive p. 149), Bidi:** The two Hebrew imperatives in the closing paragraph are visually difficult to associate with their English explanation. Put each Hebrew term in an isolated RTL span or on its own line.
- **Printed p. 129 (archive p. 149), Wrong preposition:** "cause Klal Yisrael a teshuah gedolah" should be **"bring Klal Yisrael a great salvation."**

### Ki Sisa 5783

- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Transcript opening:** "Happy Purim, everybody!" is event-specific spoken language. Retain only if the collection deliberately preserves the original delivery.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Biographical/source check:** Verify R' Simcha Sheps's title at Torah Vodaath, the exact title of his sefer, and the tractate/page cited for the statement about wealthy people being frugal.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Promotional language:** "a splendid sefer" and "a tremendously interesting ha'arah" can be made more neutral.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Repetition:** "everybody had to give... everybody had to give exactly the same amount" should be stated once.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Halachic wording query:** The text calls giving more than a half-shekel an **"issur."** Verify whether the source means a formal prohibition, a limit within this specific collection, or simply a command to give the prescribed amount.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Overgeneralization:** "when it comes to tzedakah, the more a person gives, the better" ignores halachic limits and competing obligations. Qualify it.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Class stereotype:** "rich people are often frugal" and the extended imagined arrogance of the wealthy donor should remain explicitly attributed and be phrased carefully.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Punctuation:** Add a comma after **"in the eyes of Hashem"** in the sentence concluding that all are equal.
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Transcript question:** "Who was the one giving over this mitzvah?" should be **"Who transmitted this command?"**
- **Printed p. 130 (archive p. 150), Source/logic query:** The paragraph asks why the wealthy person could not simply learn humility from Moshe. The next paragraph must make the causal answer explicit rather than merely beginning a separate discussion of the veil.
- **Printed p. 131 (archive p. 151), Comma splice:** "the true measure of greatness is anavah, knowing how to project leadership" needs a colon, semicolon, or relative clause.
- **Printed p. 131 (archive p. 151), Source/logic query:** The veil is interpreted as concealing Moshe's humility so that he could project royal stature. This is a novel inference and should be tied explicitly to its source rather than stated as the plain reason for the veil.
- **Printed p. 131 (archive p. 151), Transcript closing:** "Though you may be hearing this after Purim, that's okay" is tied to the original delivery date and should be removed or reframed for a book.
- **Printed p. 131 (archive p. 151), Repetition:** The final sentence uses **"always strive"** twice within two lines.

### Ki Sisa 5784

- **Printed p. 132 (archive p. 152), Wordiness:** The opening sentence lists **"daily, weekly, monthly, or yearly"** and then repeats the idea with **"our whole lives."** Condense.
- **Printed p. 132 (archive p. 152), Personal praise/style:** The parenthetical praise of Rabbi Oelbaum interrupts the thesis. Keep a concise attribution and move on.
- **Printed p. 132 (archive p. 152), Source clarity:** Explain the calendar case more precisely: when Yom Tov begins on Saturday night, the havdalah incorporated into Kiddush omits besamim.
- **Printed p. 132 (archive p. 152), Question sequence:** The three consecutive questions about the neshamah yeseirah on Yom Tov can be combined into one clear problem.
- **Printed p. 132 (archive p. 152), Colloquialism:** "here comes his bomb chiddush" should be **"the Sfas Emes offers a striking answer."**
- **Printed p. 132 (archive p. 152), Theological/source query:** "kedushah that comes through our ma'aseh never leaves" is a broad claim. Give the exact Sfas Emes citation and state the scope of the teaching.
- **Printed pp. 132-133 (archive pp. 152-153), Quotation accuracy:** The imagined exchange between Turnus Rufus and Rabbi Akiva, including **"Oh, good answer!"**, reads as dramatized dialogue. Verify it against the Midrash or mark it as paraphrase.
- **Printed p. 133 (archive p. 153), Typo in citation:** **"(31:134)"** should be **"(31:13)."**
- **Printed p. 133 (archive p. 153), Repetition:** The page repeats **"kedushah"** and **"source of kedushah"** unusually often. Some instances can be replaced with specific referents.
- **Printed p. 133 (archive p. 153), Overstatement:** "generate kedushah on our own" should preserve the paragraph's premise that this capacity is granted by Hashem, not independent of Him.

### Ki Sisa 5785

- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Vague wording:** "He wanted us to become better" should identify the intended repentance or repair after the eigel.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Repetition:** "wants to have rachmanus... wants to be kind, gracious, and generous" stacks near-synonyms.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Pronoun clarity:** "She relates that when she was a little girl" should name Rebbetzin Kanievsky again, because several family members are introduced in the same sentence.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Family/source check:** Verify the precise relationship between R' Elyashiv and the Leshem and the exact family tradition concerning Tehillim 121.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Stereotype/tone:** The bracketed comment that belief in segulos is **"fascinating to hear from a Litvak"** is a broad stereotype and an oral aside. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Source query:** The report that twenty bochurim all found shidduchim within two months after adopting the practice needs a traceable source and an exact citation to the Rama MiPano.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Unresolved citation:** The parenthetical reference to the Rama MiPano is fragmented and not bibliographically usable. Supply sefer, ma'amar, chelek, perek, and edition as applicable.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Personal digression:** The discussion of the author's own three perakim, running late, and catching Kedushah is much longer than the teaching it illustrates. Shorten drastically or move it to a personal note.
- **Printed p. 134 (archive p. 154), Awkward joke:** "the minhagim of earlier generations, which I now have become" is grammatically and logically unclear.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Colloquialism:** "smack dab in the middle" should be **"during"** or **"in the midst of."**
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Transcript style:** "I want to refer back to a previous vort I shared" and **"And guess what?"** should be converted to direct exposition.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Translation accuracy:** *Rosh besamim* is rendered as **"the number one most beautiful spice."** Use a standard translation such as **"choice spices"** and explain the derash separately.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Wrong source citation:** The derivation of Mordechai from *mor deror*, translated by Onkelos as *mira dachya*, is associated with **Chullin 139b**, not Megillah 13a. Verify and correct the citation.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Vague wording:** "apparently known to those people at that time" is speculative and unnecessary.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Colloquial/disparaging wording:** Chelbenah is called **"the crummy smelling spice"** and then **"a bitter, horrible, terrible spice."** Use the traditional description, a foul-smelling ingredient, without piling on insults.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Missing preposition:** "have rachmanus and everybody" should be **"have rachmanus on everybody"**, although the source may more precisely teach inclusion of sinners in communal prayer.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Source/logic query:** The comparison between chelbenah and Haman combines a gematria, the inclusion of sinners, and a denial of compassion for Haman. Clarify which points come from which sources.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Tone/sensitivity:** The prayer for the quick downfall of **"all of the Amalekim in this world"** is broad and potentially reads as directed toward living groups. Use exact liturgical or source language and avoid ambiguous contemporary application.
- **Printed p. 135 (archive p. 155), Bidi/punctuation:** The closing Hebrew prayer and **"Amen"** appear in reversed visual order. Put the prayer in a separate RTL paragraph.

### Vayakel 5784

- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Title consistency:** The manuscript uses **"Vayakel"** here and elsewhere. Decide whether the book's transliteration will be **"Vayakhel"** or **"Vayakel"** and apply it consistently in titles, URLs, and the index.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Wrong verse citation:** *Vayakhel Moshe es kol adas Bnei Yisrael* is **Shemos 35:1**, not 35:3.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Repetitive question:** "What do we learn from this? What do we gain from knowing..." asks the same thing twice.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Overstatement:** "single-handedly brought about kapparah" overlooks Hashem's role and the people's repentance. Use the source's more precise formulation.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Transcript rhetoric:** The imagined line **"I worked so hard, now I need a vacation"** and the direct **"So what do you do?"** are conversational. Recast if the collection aims for polished essays.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Unsupported superlative:** "Yom Kippur is great, but the day after Yom Kippur is even greater" needs attribution or qualification as a rhetorical lesson.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Overbroad halachic wording:** "You can only do teshuvah... if you go around and make up with all the people" oversimplifies the requirements for interpersonal repentance. State apology, restitution, and appeasement accurately.
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Awkward repetition:** "We have to all work together to be together" should be **"We must continue building communal unity."**
- **Printed p. 136 (archive p. 156), Historical/source query:** "its keilim were used until Shlomo Hamelech built the Beis Hamikdash" treats all vessels alike. Verify the history of each class of vessels or qualify the claim.
- **Printed p. 137 (archive p. 157), Vague antecedent:** "The promise given to us at Har Sinai remains alive" should identify the promise or covenant meant.
- **Printed p. 137 (archive p. 157), Awkward closing:** "continue what we were meant to do with a Beis Hamikdash" should name the avodah or spiritual function being continued through Shabbos.

### Vayakel 5785

- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Wordiness:** "one aspect that was not done as perfectly as it could have been" should be **"one aspect was deficient."**
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Source nuance:** Rashi says the nesi'im were deficient because they delayed; calling them simply **"lazy"** may flatten the source. Quote and translate *nis'atzlu* carefully.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Biographical check:** Verify R' Reuven Grozovsky's positions and the exact title **Peninim MiShulchan Gavoha**.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Citation quality:** "quoted in a TorahAnytime issue (Bo 5785)" is not a stable citation. Give the article or recording title, author, date, and URL in the source notes.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Disability-sensitive wording:** "wasn't really capable of doing so on his own" can sound patronizing. Describe the accommodation respectfully and focus on supported participation.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Quotation-mark error:** The long quotation closes with an opening-style curly single quote. Use a proper closing double quotation mark and standardize nested quotations.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Theological overstatement:** "Hashem does everything" and human action only creates an **"appearance"** of accomplishment should be attributed as the mashal's message, not stated as the only view of human agency.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Sweeping claim:** "A person's tafkid... is not to fix the world" is too absolute. Clarify the distinction between outcomes beyond human control and the obligation to act.
- **Printed p. 138 (archive p. 158), Bidi:** The Hebrew maxim about not letting a mitzvah become chametz is broken across the English sentence. Isolate and typeset the full phrase as one RTL unit.
- **Printed p. 139 (archive p. 159), Halachic review required:** The conclusion that one should immediately wear shatnez tzitzis rather than wait for a non-shatnez garment is highly technical and potentially misleading. Have a competent halachic reviewer verify the Tosafos, the conditions of *aseh docheh lo sa'aseh*, whether donning the garment is obligatory, and whether both commands can be fulfilled without conflict.
- **Printed p. 139 (archive p. 159), Internal contradiction:** The hypothetical calls the available tzitzis both a permitted case under *aseh docheh lo sa'aseh* and something less than **"100% kosher."** Use legally precise terminology.
- **Printed p. 139 (archive p. 159), Number agreement:** "his tzitzis contains... replace **them** with a new **one**" shifts between plural and singular. Use **"his garment contains... replace it."**
- **Printed p. 139 (archive p. 159), Transcript residue:** "I hope we don't have that specific problem... You can look it up" should be removed.
- **Printed p. 139 (archive p. 159), Source verification:** Check that Shevuos 3a, Tosafos beginning *ve'al hazakan shtayim mikan*, actually supports the practical ruling attributed to it.

### Pekudei 5784

- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Bracketed aside:** The comment that Pekudei has the **"zechus of having its very own Shabbos"** in a leap year is a spoken joke. Remove or integrate without brackets.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Unsupported ranking:** "The greatest of these tasks was placing the luchos into the aron" needs a source or a more neutral phrasing.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Meta-commentary:** The aside that **"climax"** is overused and the **"ta-da moment"** language distract from the teaching.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Number/context query:** The Baal Haturim connects eighteen repetitions to the original eighteen berachos of Shemoneh Esrei. Explain why the prayer is still called Shemoneh Esrei although the weekday text now has nineteen berachos.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Agreement:** "the Mishkan, and later the Beis Hamikdash, **was**" should be **"were"** if both are the subject.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Overstatement:** "Shemoneh Esrei replaces the Mishkan" and is **"exactly the same thing"** as Temple service are strong theological claims. Attribute the analogy and avoid literal equivalence unless the source says so.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Repetition:** **"a whole new character, a whole new perspective"** and **"not just our little... not just our little..."** can be tightened.
- **Printed p. 140 (archive p. 160), Run-on ending:** The final sentence strings together effort, heart, kavanah, accomplishment, and the weekly parsha. Divide it.

### Pekudei 5785

- **Printed p. 141 (archive p. 161), Bidi:** The opening verse is visually scrambled by mixed Hebrew and English. Set the complete verse as an RTL block, then translate it separately.
- **Printed p. 141 (archive p. 161), Source-title check:** The Hebrew title of R' Yitzchok Zilberstein's sefer is not legible in the English flow. Standardize the transliterated title and include a precise citation.
- **Printed p. 141 (archive p. 161), Logic:** If the scoffers would not trust Moshe's accounting, the answer should explain what moral or procedural purpose the accounting served, not merely repeat that it followed Moshe's instructions.
- **Printed p. 141 (archive p. 161), Informal introduction:** "Here is the story from a Yid" should be replaced by a sourced introduction.
- **Printed p. 141 (archive p. 161), Quotation punctuation:** Several quotations close with the wrong curly quotation mark. Standardize all dialogue marks.
- **Printed p. 141 (archive p. 161), Halachic wording query:** "an onen... is not allowed to do mitzvos" is too broad without qualification. State the precise exemptions and restrictions from Yoreh De'ah.
- **Printed p. 142 (archive p. 162), Grammar:** "The question that was asked to R' Zilberstein was did the Rosh Yeshiva act correctly?" should be **"R' Zilberstein was asked whether the Rosh Yeshiva had acted correctly."**
- **Printed p. 142 (archive p. 162), Source citation:** Identify the verse as **Bamidbar 32:22** rather than saying only **"the pasuk says in Bamidbar."**
- **Printed p. 142 (archive p. 162), Ethical/halachic query:** The proposed solution, standing with the minyan and repeating unrelated verses so observers would think he was davening, raises issues of deception and aninus. Quote the responsum accurately and have the ruling reviewed.
- **Printed p. 142 (archive p. 162), Repetition:** The personal anecdote repeats **"learned," "halachos aninus," "Yoreh De'ah,"** and **"Aruch Hashulchan"** many times. It can be reduced by more than half without losing the point.
- **Printed p. 142 (archive p. 162), Overstatement:** "I became a baki in halachos aninus" after one night's study sounds exaggerated. Use **"I studied the relevant halachos."**
- **Printed pp. 142-143 (archive pp. 162-163), Promotional aside:** "It is a phenomenal sefer... you will benefit from it enormously if you ever pick it up" reads like an unscripted recommendation and is unrelated to the parsha's core lesson.
- **Printed p. 143 (archive p. 163), Bidi:** The closing blessing is unreadable in mixed direction. Place each Hebrew/Yiddish item in the intended order in a separate RTL line.

### Vayakel-Pekudei 5783

- **Printed p. 144 (archive p. 164), Title consistency:** Standardize **"Vayakel-Pekudei"** against the spelling selected elsewhere.
- **Printed p. 144 (archive p. 164), Factual check:** Verify that this combined reading with Parshas Hachodesh is indeed the year's second-longest leining, and identify what comparison is being used.
- **Printed pp. 144-145 (archive pp. 164-165), Permissions/source issue:** The piece reproduces a long first-person story attributed to Rabbi Dr. Abraham Twerski. Supply the original publication and determine whether quotation permission is required; otherwise summarize it.
- **Printed p. 144 (archive p. 164), Factual definition error:** The bracket says **"anathema means poison."** It does not. *Anathema* means something accursed, detested, or utterly opposed to one's values.
- **Printed p. 144 (archive p. 164), Editorial note left in text:** The bracketed definition of *anathema* should not sit inside the quoted speaker's words.
- **Printed p. 144 (archive p. 164), Typo:** "I do as **Iam** told" needs a space: **"I am."**
- **Printed p. 144 (archive p. 164), Quotation punctuation:** Closing quotation marks and punctuation alternate between US and non-US styles. Standardize the full nested quotation.
- **Printed p. 145 (archive p. 165), Capitalization/pronoun style:** In reference to Hashem, decide whether the book capitalizes pronouns consistently; **"a benevolent Being, who... from his dictates"** currently mixes choices.
- **Printed p. 145 (archive p. 165), Word choice:** **"dictates"** sounds coercive in this context. **"commands"** is more natural.
- **Printed p. 145 (archive p. 165), Transcript wording:** "And it says everywhere, through the whole parsha" should be replaced with a specific recurring formula and one or two citations.
- **Printed p. 145 (archive p. 165), Medical/generalization warning:** "An alcoholic will never recover if he relies on his own intellectual understanding" is categorical and can be read as clinical advice. Keep it clearly within the quoted recovery story and avoid universal claims.
- **Printed p. 145 (archive p. 165), Comma splice:** "The drives that lead a person to improper behavior are not logical, we cannot reason with them" needs a semicolon or two sentences.
- **Printed p. 145 (archive p. 165), Redundancy:** "we will be successful and... have hatzlachah" says the same thing twice.

### Vayikra 5783

- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Transcript opening:** "We're up to Parshas Vayikra" should be replaced by a direct opening.
- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Edition-specific factual check:** Verify the claim that Vayikra is the only book occupying two volumes in the cited Hirsch Chumash edition; this may vary by language and publisher.
- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Source attribution:** The small aleph explanation and Moshe's wish to write *vayikar* should be attributed to the correct commentary rather than introduced as **"Everybody knows."**
- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Anthropomorphic wording:** Calling the small aleph **"the compromise"** between Hashem and Moshe is casual. Use the source's formulation.
- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Missing verb:** "This language of the Tanchuma **a question**" should be **"raises a question."**
- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Colloquialism:** "Kriyas Yam Suf is pretty impressive" should be stated respectfully and analytically.
- **Printed p. 146 (archive p. 166), Source/logic query:** The piece moves from the saying that matchmaking is as difficult as splitting the sea to the conclusion that the bond between Hashem and Israel is **greater** than splitting the sea. Explain the source and logical step.
- **Printed p. 147 (archive p. 167), Broken dedication sentence:** The opening sentence has multiple possessives and an unattached **"and in whose zechus."** Divide it and identify each person clearly.
- **Printed p. 147 (archive p. 167), Repetition:** "I was looking for a devar Torah to find to say" should be **"I sought a devar Torah to share."**
- **Printed p. 147 (archive p. 167), Awkward wording:** "her job in life" should be **"her life's work"** or a more specific description.
- **Printed p. 147 (archive p. 167), Bidi:** The Tehillim phrase used in the memorial application is broken into visually reversed fragments. Set the verse as one RTL quotation.

### Vayikra 5784

- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Overstatement:** "All of our davening today is to replace the korbanos" should be qualified. The prayers correspond to and in part stand in place of offerings, but prayer has additional functions.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Source query:** The custom of beginning young children with Vayikra and its stated reason should be tied to the Midrash or traditional source.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Category wording:** "One of the korbanos... refers to the nasi" is imprecise. The passage gives the law of the nasi's sin offering.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Categorical rhetoric:** "It is clear, obvious, and expected that the nasi will commit aveiros" is stronger than the derash and unfairly treats corruption as inevitable. Attribute and moderate.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Repetition:** "Power brings corruption to leaders" repeats the quotation about absolute power immediately above it.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Broad claim:** "We see this every day, everywhere in the world" is unsupported and unnecessary.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Pop-culture aside:** **"[not a bad song]"** is an editorial/transcript aside and should be removed.
- **Printed p. 148 (archive p. 168), Vague application:** "know when it should be our way and when it should not" needs a concrete ethical criterion.

### Vayikra 5785

- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Broken syntax:** "not only despite that but specifically because of that" should be **"precisely because the subject feels remote to many readers..."**
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Edition/source check:** The statement that Hirsch devotes six volumes to the five books, with two to Vayikra, is edition-dependent. Name the edition or omit the count.
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Repetition:** "a world of learning and a world of Torah" should be reduced to one phrase.
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Promotional interruption:** "a tremendous gaon" is not needed between the author's name and the argument.
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Hype:** Gematriyos are called **"deep mathematical concepts."** Use **"a series of gematriyos"** unless actual mathematical analysis follows.
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Transcript aside:** "I will try my best... if I do not succeed..." and "which I had never heard of, though that is not unusual" should be deleted.
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Logic:** A sefer being quoted in the name of mekubalim does not by itself prove that every following statement is Kabbalah rather than peshat. State only what the source says.
- **Printed pp. 149-150 (archive pp. 169-170), Theological precision required:** Claims that Hashem's name **"consists of"** the 613 mitzvos, that a neshamah is **"a part of Himself,"** and that a portion is identical to the whole are highly sensitive and can sound corporeal or pantheistic. Quote the source exactly, distinguish metaphor from doctrine, and obtain rabbinic review.
- **Printed p. 149 (archive p. 169), Citation:** Identify the verse as **Shemos 3:15** and present the gematria in a clean equation or note rather than embedding many Hebrew fragments in a paragraph.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Misapplied verse/translation:** Devarim 32:9, *ki chelek Hashem amo*, means that Hashem's portion is His people; it does not literally say that each soul is a portion of Hashem. The phrase *chelek Eloka mima'al* comes from a different textual tradition and must not be conflated with this verse.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), False generalization:** "A sample of something is identical to the whole" is not generally true. Present it only as a specifically defined metaphysical principle from the cited source.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Anthropomorphism:** "given to us... a part of Himself" should be replaced by the source's carefully qualified language.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Vague source:** "a vast amount of discussion, especially from the Beis Halevi" needs an exact work and location.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Order mismatch:** The explanation of final letters lists them in a different order from *nefesh, ruach, neshamah*. Keep first and final letters aligned so readers can verify the total 613.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Citation needed:** Identify Iyov 10:11 for the verse about skin, flesh, bones, and sinews.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Doctrinal overstatement:** "Hashem's name... expresses His essence" should be reviewed and likely recast; classical theology treats divine names with much greater precision.
- **Printed p. 150 (archive p. 170), Repetition:** The conclusion repeats three times that a part is exactly the same as the original.
- **Printed p. 151 (archive p. 171), Halachic scope:** "if there is a mitzvah that a person is obligated to do but refuses, we coerce him" is too broad. State the particular legal contexts in which *kofin oso ad sheyomar rotzeh ani* applies and cite the source.
- **Printed p. 151 (archive p. 171), Source conflation:** The explanation of coerced consent is associated especially with Rambam, Hilchos Gerushin 2:20. Distinguish that source from the applications offered by Hirsch, the Aperyon, and the Beis Halevi.
- **Printed p. 151 (archive p. 171), Citation:** Identify Pirkei Avos 2:4 and quote the full phrase in a stable Hebrew block; it is visually broken in the mixed line.
- **Printed p. 151 (archive p. 171), Theological precision:** The language of *Adam Elyon*, the soul as *chelek Eloka mima'al*, and physical existence as merely a covering continues the doctrinally sensitive discussion from the prior pages. Preserve exact attribution and avoid unqualified claims that a human soul is literally part of God.
- **Printed p. 151 (archive p. 171), Repetition:** "deepest ratzon," "innermost feelings," "deepest aspirations," and "our essence" stack four versions of the same idea.

### Tzav 5783

- **Printed p. 152 (archive p. 172), Repetition:** The opening calls Shabbos Hagadol **"the great Shabbos"** before a **"wonderful Yom Tov"** and repeats *gadol* several more times. Tighten.
- **Printed p. 152 (archive p. 172), Historical/source query:** The annual derashah on Hilchos Pesach is presented as the reason the day became known as Shabbos Hagadol. Give the source and distinguish origin theories from later custom.
- **Printed p. 152 (archive p. 172), Quotation/source query:** Moshe's protest that the Egyptians would kill Bnei Yisrael for taking the sheep, and Hashem's direct answer beginning **"I swear"**, should be traced to the exact Midrash and not presented as Torah dialogue.
- **Printed p. 152 (archive p. 172), Word choice:** "within the great panoply of miracles" is ornate and out of register with the surrounding prose.
- **Printed p. 152 (archive p. 172), Scientific wording:** The sea's return is attributed specifically to **"gravity."** Use **"the ordinary laws of nature"** unless the source itself names gravity.
- **Printed p. 153 (archive p. 173), Typo:** "even in simply **typing** the sheep to the bedpost" should be **"tying."**
- **Printed p. 153 (archive p. 173), Overgeneralization:** "All of these difficulties and stresses are designed to help us" should be softened and attributed as a faith perspective, not a knowable explanation for every hardship.
- **Printed p. 153 (archive p. 173), Transcript transition:** "An additional point to mention, since we are in Parshas Tzav and can't overlook it" should be replaced by a direct transition.
- **Printed p. 153 (archive p. 173), Liturgical accuracy:** The text says Mizmor L'Sodah is recited every day except Shabbos, then separately notes Pesach. Standard Ashkenaz practice also omits it on Yom Tov and on additional specified days. State the minhag accurately for the intended audience.
- **Printed p. 153 (archive p. 173), Repetition:** "Thank you, thank you, thank you" is oral emphasis. One formulation will read better in print.
- **Printed pp. 153-154 (archive pp. 173-174), Source query:** The Chasam Sofer explanation that gratitude to Egypt prevented Bnei Yisrael from fighting their pursuers is striking and should have an exact citation.
- **Printed p. 154 (archive p. 174), Redundancy:** "receive berachah and hatzlachah" can be reduced to one term or differentiated.

### Tzav 5784

- **Printed p. 155 (archive p. 175), Grammar:** "which continues to **discusses** more details" should be **"continues to discuss."**
- **Printed p. 155 (archive p. 175), Time-sensitive prose:** References to the IDF, **"our enemies,"** an upcoming aufruf, and a yahrzeit in **"the next week or two"** preserve the delivery moment rather than functioning as edited book prose. Date or contextualize them, or shorten the opening.
- **Printed p. 155 (archive p. 175), Repetition:** "ever-growing and ever-improving family" and the later **"ever-growing family"** repeat a promotional phrase.
- **Printed p. 155 (archive p. 175), Source/citation:** Identify the exact Rashi or Midrash containing the maidservant-and-calf parable; the Hebrew citation is unreadable in the present bidi flow.
- **Printed p. 155 (archive p. 175), Unsupported superlative:** Calling the eigel **"the greatest sin in history"** requires a source or should be moderated.
- **Printed p. 155 (archive p. 175), Tone:** The extended dirty-diaper analogy, including the invented dialogue **"Ugh, that stinks! Take him away!"**, is vivid but undignified and may distract from the cited parable. Condense.
- **Printed p. 156 (archive p. 176), Theological overstatement:** The parah adumah is said to provide a **"constant opportunity for kapparah, no matter how severe the sin."** That is broader than the ritual's halachic function; keep the statement within the mashal's homiletic scope.
- **Printed p. 156 (archive p. 176), Sweeping cultural claim:** "family values are under attack from all directions, across all societies and cultures" and **"the world seeks to undermine"** are unsourced culture-war generalizations. Replace them with the specific positive value the piece wants to affirm.
- **Printed p. 156 (archive p. 176), Polarizing wording:** "We recognize how wrong the world is and how right Hashem is" creates an unnecessary us-versus-them contrast.
- **Printed p. 156 (archive p. 176), Repetition:** **"family"** appears repeatedly within a short closing paragraph. Vary or condense.

### Tzav 5785

- **Printed p. 157 (archive p. 177), Repetition:** "completed by Revi'i. Then, in Revi'i" should be stated once, and aliyah boundaries should be omitted unless important to the argument.
- **Printed p. 157 (archive p. 177), Factual error:** The Ohel Moed itself was not approximately 50 by 100 amos; those are the dimensions of the Mishkan courtyard. The tent structure was much smaller. Correct the object and dimensions before building the crowding question.
- **Printed p. 157 (archive p. 177), Unverified estimate:** "three million people" should be identified as an estimate based on the census and families, not a Torah count.
- **Printed p. 157 (archive p. 177), Theological/tone problem:** The comparison between Hashem and Og, especially **"Hashem can fit into a mouse hole"** and **"fit all of Klal Yisrael into a suitcase,"** is irreverent and conceptually confused. Replace it with Rashi's language that a small space miraculously held the many.
- **Printed p. 157 (archive p. 177), Wrong term:** The inauguration of the Mishkan is called **"chanukas habayis."** **"Chanukas haMishkan"** is more precise.
- **Printed pp. 157-159 (archive pp. 177-179), Source/permissions issue:** The long October 7 story is reproduced as a near-complete quotation attributed secondhand to Rabbi YY Jacobson through TorahAnytime. Locate the primary recording or publication, verify the painter's name and events, and secure permission or summarize.
- **Printed p. 158 (archive p. 178), Factual verification needed:** Claims that painted shelters allowed rescuers to locate people and **"helping save countless lives"** are concrete historical assertions. Document them with reliable sourcing before publication.
- **Printed p. 158 (archive p. 178), Date punctuation:** Write **"October 6, 2023"** with a comma.
- **Printed p. 158 (archive p. 178), Hyphenation:** **"life saving system"** should be **"life-saving system."**
- **Printed p. 158 (archive p. 178), Quotation punctuation:** The period belongs inside the quotation in **"I'm just a simple painter, nothing special".**
- **Printed p. 159 (archive p. 179), Hyperbole:** The claim that no IDF, Mossad, Shin Bet, government, or police official could locate the shelters and that only one person in Israel could do so is implausibly absolute. Verify or temper it.
- **Printed p. 159 (archive p. 179), Citation:** Give the exact Sanhedrin citation for *bishvili nivra ha'olam* and do not say one anecdote **"proves"** the teaching.
- **Printed p. 159 (archive p. 179), Invented divine quotation:** "Hashem tells each of us, 'I need you...'" is the author's application, not a scriptural quotation. Remove the quotation marks or identify it explicitly as paraphrase.

### Shmini 5783

- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Title consistency:** Standardize **"Shmini"** versus **"Shemini."**
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Wrong verse citation:** *Vayidom Aharon* is Vayikra **10:3**, not 3:10.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Speculative motive:** Nadav and Avihu are described as acting **"in their enthusiasm and kedushah."** Chazal offer multiple explanations; avoid assigning a single motive without attribution.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Inexact wording:** "they paid the ultimate price and were burnt up" is colloquial and may conflict with Midrashic descriptions of what the fire consumed. Use the verse's wording.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Source nuance:** The laws of intoxicated service are addressed directly to Aharon, but the claim that this was his reward for silence should be explicitly attributed to Rashi/Chazal.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Halachic wording:** "an onen is forbidden to do the avodah" needs precise scope and should distinguish ordinary kohanim from a Kohen Gadol.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Quotation accuracy:** Moshe's admission **"You're right, I forgot"** should either quote the source exactly or be marked as paraphrase.
- **Printed pp. 160-161 (archive pp. 180-181), Textual-count query:** Kiddushin 30a identifies *darosh darash* as the midpoint in words, but modern Masoretic counts do not line up straightforwardly. Explain the traditional statement rather than calling it an independently verified exact midpoint.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Sefer title:** Use the full title **Degel Machaneh Ephraim** consistently.
- **Printed p. 160 (archive p. 180), Sensitive doctrinal claim:** The declaration that one who rejects even one rabbinic derivation is a *kofer b'Toras Moshe* needs exact quotation, context, and rabbinic review.
- **Printed p. 161 (archive p. 181), Repetition:** The closing repeats **"half of the Torah"** and **"complete/unified Torah"** several times. Condense.

### Shmini 5784

- **Printed p. 162 (archive p. 182), Awkward opening:** "In Parshas Shmini, the sefer Aperyon... brings down" is interrupted by two appositives. Move the author's credentials to a note or shorten the sentence.
- **Printed p. 162 (archive p. 182), Pronoun:** "It connects this idea to Bereshis" has no clear antecedent. Name the Aperyon or Midrash.
- **Printed p. 162 (archive p. 182), Source citation:** Identify Bereshis 3:8 for the verse about hearing Hashem's voice in the garden, and cite the Midrash that maps seven sins and seven tzaddikim to the seven firmaments.
- **Printed p. 162 (archive p. 182), Bidi:** The seven generations and seven tzaddikim are difficult to follow in a mixed-direction paragraph. Use a short numbered or semicolon-separated list with isolated RTL names.
- **Printed p. 162 (archive p. 182), Anthropomorphic wording:** The Shechinah is described as having a **"deep desire"** and Hashem's **"deepest ratzon."** Preserve the source's language and avoid intensifying it in translation.
- **Printed p. 162 (archive p. 182), Sweeping application:** "through every wedding... we contribute to bringing the Shechinah back into this world" is a homiletic extension and should be attributed, especially in a family-dedication context.

### Shmini 5785

- **Printed p. 163 (archive p. 183), Wrong verse citation:** The kosher-animal verse is Vayikra **11:4**, not 10:4.
- **Printed p. 163 (archive p. 183), Zoological/source precision:** "only four [animals] have one sign of kashrus" should be framed as the Torah's four named exceptional categories and attributed to Chazal, not as an unsupported survey of every animal species.
- **Printed p. 163 (archive p. 183), Source check:** Verify the exact Torah Tavlin issue, column title, author spelling, and the R' Avraham Pam story.
- **Printed p. 163 (archive p. 183), Wordiness:** The story repeats that the student was capable, not reaching potential, later improved, and became excellent. Tighten the outcome.
- **Printed p. 164 (archive p. 184), Overgeneralization:** "Harsh criticism rarely works" is presented as a universal behavioral rule. Soften to **"often provokes defensiveness"** or keep it as practical counsel.
- **Printed p. 164 (archive p. 184), Redundancy:** **"colleagues"** and **"co-workers"** duplicate one another.
- **Printed p. 164 (archive p. 184), Sentence fragment:** "A wise lesson for each and every one of us..." needs a verb or should be joined to the preceding sentence.

### Tazria 5784

- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Grammar:** "the beginning of the two parshiyos that discuss negaim" should be **"the first of two parshiyos..."**
- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Overstatement:** "All the mefarshim explain that we no longer experience negaim today" is too broad. Name the sources and distinguish halachic non-practice from claims about whether the phenomenon can occur.
- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Medical terminology:** It is helpful to say tzaraas is not ordinary leprosy, but avoid calling it a **"skin condition"** and then a direct diagnosis of sin without source qualification.
- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Citation clarity:** The letter rearrangements *nega/oneg* and *tzaraas/atzeres* should be displayed cleanly, with Yeshayahu 58:13 and Bamidbar 29:35 cited in English as well.
- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Colloquialism:** "sweat the details" and **"an easy-pickings situation"** do not fit the surrounding register.
- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Redundancy:** "we are able to have the ability" should be **"we gain the ability."**
- **Printed p. 165 (archive p. 185), Source query:** The Avigdor Miller statement about every blade of grass and apple seed needs an identifiable source if quoted as a famous teaching.
- **Printed pp. 165-166 (archive pp. 185-186), Sweeping interfaith claim:** "This is what differentiates us from all the other religions of the world" is an unprovable generalization and may be needlessly disparaging. State the positive Jewish teaching without comparing every other religion.

### Metzora 5784

- **Printed p. 167 (archive p. 187), Awkward syntax:** "continues the halachos, which are many and intricate, of tzaraas" should be **"continues the many intricate laws of tzaraas."**
- **Printed p. 167 (archive p. 187), Source/order query:** The house-clothing-body progression should be attributed to the relevant Midrash or mussar source; it is not simply the narrative order of the parshiyos.
- **Printed p. 167 (archive p. 187), Transcript locator:** "right after Revi'i" depends on a particular leining division. Use chapter and verse only.
- **Printed p. 167 (archive p. 187), Repetition:** "a very interesting and unusual lashon, unusual phrase" should be reduced to one description.
- **Printed p. 167 (archive p. 187), Halachic distinction:** The verse says *kenega nirah li* because only a kohen can formally declare tzaraas, even when a scholar recognizes the signs. The personal-bias lesson may be homiletic, but the primary halachic reason should not be obscured.
- **Printed p. 167 (archive p. 187), Source citation:** Verify whether *ein adam mesim atzmo rasha* is being cited from Kesubos or another tractate, and give the exact page.
- **Printed p. 168 (archive p. 188), Story source:** The Chafetz Chaim story, including the stranger striking him and the lesson about self-directed lashon hara, needs a traceable source; it is often circulated in varying forms.
- **Printed p. 168 (archive p. 188), Halachic overreach:** "you're also not allowed to speak lashon hara about yourself" should not be stated as settled law solely on the basis of the anecdote.
- **Printed p. 168 (archive p. 188), Source:** The instruction to view oneself and the world as evenly balanced comes from Kiddushin 40b/Rambam Hilchos Teshuvah 3:4; cite it rather than calling it simply a famous idea.
- **Printed p. 168 (archive p. 188), Weak closing:** "It's a very good lesson to learn" is generic and can be deleted.

### Tazria-Metzora 5783

- **Printed p. 169 (archive p. 189), Comma splice:** "This should humble us, placing us in our proper place and a little hachna'ah goes a long way" needs a period or semicolon.
- **Printed p. 169 (archive p. 189), Contradictory transition:** The first paragraph emphasizes humans coming after animals as a humbling message; the next begins **"Once again, we see the superiority of human beings."** Explain how the two readings coexist.
- **Printed p. 169 (archive p. 189), Halachic overstatement:** "The concepts of tumah and taharah are unique to Klal Yisrael" is too broad. Non-Jews and their possessions have complex statuses in these laws; state the narrower point intended.
- **Printed p. 169 (archive p. 189), Technical accuracy:** A mother does not simply **"remain tahor"** for the following thirty-three or sixty-six days. Explain *d'mei tohar* and the Temple restrictions accurately for the historical halachic context.
- **Printed pp. 169-170 (archive pp. 189-190), Source/theological query:** The explanation that childbirth creates tumah because an extra soul leaves the mother's body, and that a girl creates twice the tumah because she can one day bear more souls, is a homiletic theory. Give its exact source and do not present it as the definition of tumah.
- **Printed p. 170 (archive p. 190), Dismissive language:** "a certain faction of modernistic people" is vague and contemptuous. Address the possible concern directly and respectfully.
- **Printed p. 170 (archive p. 190), Combative aside:** **"punkt farkert!"** reads as a spoken retort. Replace it with a reasoned transition.
- **Printed p. 170 (archive p. 190), Gender essentialism/sensitivity:** The argument equates female spiritual status with future childbearing and calls this the unique greatness of the **"Yiddishe Mama."** This excludes women and girls who do not or cannot bear children. Preserve the source narrowly and avoid making fertility the measure of female worth.
- **Printed p. 170 (archive p. 190), Potentially misleading hierarchy:** Saying the resulting tumah is **"proportionately greater"** can sound as though a baby girl creates greater impurity. Explain that this is the source's symbolic account of differing durations, not a judgment on the child.

### Tazria-Metzora 5785

- **Printed p. 171 (archive p. 191), Category/wording:** The opening calls tzaraas both a severe tumah and an *av hatumah*. Have a halachic editor verify the exact status being assigned to the metzora, the lesion, and the related objects.
- **Printed p. 171 (archive p. 191), Overstatement:** "it has nothing to do with the physical properties of the world" ignores the physical signs by which many tumah statuses are determined. The intended point appears to be that tumah is a halachic-spiritual status, not a medical contaminant.
- **Printed pp. 171-172 (archive pp. 191-192), Source check:** Verify the story that the Vilna Gaon offered this derashah at age six and give the exact Yoma citation. The pregnant-women passage is Yoma 82b in standard editions.
- **Printed p. 171 (archive p. 191), Names/transliteration:** Standardize **R' Yochanan** and **Shabbesai Otzar Peiri**; the latter name is visually and phonetically unstable in the present text.
- **Printed p. 172 (archive p. 192), Serious halachic/ethical problem:** The woman who required food on Yom Kippur is later blamed for eating and for bearing a wicked child. In the Gemara, feeding a pregnant woman when the danger does not subside is mandatory pikuach nefesh. Do not imply that obeying that ruling was a moral failure.
- **Printed p. 172 (archive p. 192), Internal contradiction:** "The woman who ate did not do anything wrong" is immediately followed by "it was her" because she ate. The disclaimer does not cure the blame assigned by the paragraph.
- **Printed p. 172 (archive p. 192), Source distinction:** The roof-parapet principle, that a death decreed from Heaven need not occur in one's house, should be attributed to Rashi/Chazal on Devarim 22:8 and not treated as proof of prenatal moral causation.
- **Printed p. 172 (archive p. 192), Transcript residue:** "go back, look it up, and tell me what you think" should be removed.
- **Printed p. 172 (archive p. 192), Halachic review:** The personal account of tefillin with the shin facing "the other way," bought accidentally and then adopted for generations, needs a knowledgeable sofer or posek to identify exactly which feature and valid minhag are meant. *Eilu v'eilu* is not enough explanation for a potentially invalid construction.
- **Printed p. 172 (archive p. 192), Unsupported providential claim:** "Hashem wanted us to wear tefillin this way" states divine intention without a source.
- **Printed pp. 172-173 (archive pp. 192-193), Pregnancy sensitivity:** Broad statements that every maternal choice has a tremendous effect on the child can impose unwarranted guilt and are not established by the cited story. Narrow the lesson.
- **Printed p. 173 (archive p. 193), Dismissive comparison:** "I'd like to see anyone do that today" is an unnecessary put-down of contemporary children and scholars.
- **Printed p. 173 (archive p. 193), Quotation punctuation:** In US style, write the Boy Scout motto as **"Be prepared."** with the period inside the quotation mark.
- **Printed p. 173 (archive p. 193), Vague attribution:** "the Gemara constantly tells us that the harder we try, the more we will succeed" needs either a source or a less categorical formulation.

### Achrei Mos 5784

- **Printed p. 174 (archive p. 194), Contradiction:** "Invariably (with major exceptions)" should be **"As a general rule, subject to important exceptions."**
- **Printed p. 174 (archive p. 194), Terminology:** Standardize the plural of *machlokes*; **"machlokesim"** is awkward in English prose.
- **Printed p. 174 (archive p. 194), Source citation:** Mishnah Eduyos explicitly discusses why minority opinions are recorded. Quote or summarize the relevant mishnayos rather than asking the question without the canonical answer.
- **Printed p. 174 (archive p. 194), Source precision:** The statement that Beis Hillel conceded whenever Beis Shammai convinced them should be tied to the specific cases and wording in Eduyos.
- **Printed p. 174 (archive p. 194), Source query:** Give the exact Yerushalmi or Midrash for the comparison between Yom Kippur's atonement and the death of tzaddikim.
- **Printed pp. 174-175 (archive pp. 194-195), Source query:** Verify the chain of attribution from R' Chaim Kanievsky to R' Yitzchok Elchonon Spector and the Ketubos account of the bas kol at Rebbi's funeral.
- **Printed p. 175 (archive p. 195), Novel theology:** The explanation that Hashem temporarily honored Rebbi's minority view so everyone at his funeral received Olam Haba is a chiddush, not the Gemara's plain meaning. Preserve attribution throughout.
- **Printed p. 175 (archive p. 195), Malformed sentence:** "the power of a daas yachid was so great that it was able to be... all those" is grammatically broken. Suggested: **"the merit of Rebbi's minority view could benefit everyone present."**
- **Printed p. 175 (archive p. 195), Misleading application:** "we must listen to all of the daas yachid opinions" could imply following rejected minority rulings. Use **"study and understand why minority opinions were preserved."**
- **Printed p. 175 (archive p. 195), Punctuation:** Add a comma before the final item in **"chachamim, teachers, and leaders."**

### Kedoshim 5784

- **Printed p. 176 (archive p. 196), Source attribution:** Rashi's statement that this parsha was said *behakhel* because most foundational Torah principles depend on it comes from Toras Kohanim. Do not label it generically or incorrectly as Midrash Rabbah.
- **Printed p. 176 (archive p. 196), Repetition/hype:** "majority, the essence, the rov" and "so many fantastic and important" overexplain and oversell.
- **Printed p. 176 (archive p. 196), Translation:** *Hochei'ach tochiach* means **"you shall surely rebuke/reprove"**, not simply **"give mussar."**
- **Printed p. 176 (archive p. 196), Exegetical clarity:** The text calls collective responsibility the **"simple peshat"** of *velo sisa alav chet* and then gives Rashi's embarrassment reading. Identify which interpretation belongs to which source.
- **Printed p. 176 (archive p. 196), Characterization:** "a non-religious Israeli girl" is reductive. Describe the relevant conduct, for example **"an Israeli woman who was not observing the fast."**
- **Printed pp. 176-177 (archive pp. 196-197), Historical error:** The bracketed claim that the Aruch Hashulchan was *the* posek hador **"before the Mishnah Berurah for over a hundred years"** is chronologically impossible; the works and authors substantially overlap. Rewrite with accurate publication dates and influence.
- **Printed pp. 176-177 (archive pp. 196-197), Long bracketed interruption:** The Aruch Hashulchan discussion interrupts the story for nearly a page. Move it to a footnote and provide the exact siman/se'if.
- **Printed p. 177 (archive p. 197), Halachic query:** Verify the rule for one who ate, intentionally or accidentally, on Tisha B'Av and whether extending the fast into the night is appropriate or merely part of the anecdote.
- **Printed p. 177 (archive p. 197), Quotation punctuation/bidi:** The Hebrew name of the fast sits outside the closing quotation mark. Rebuild the sentence with isolated RTL text and standard US punctuation.
- **Printed p. 177 (archive p. 197), Sentence fragments:** "Look at that." "A tremendous accomplishment." and "A mussar for all of us." are transcript fragments. Combine them.
- **Printed p. 177 (archive p. 197), Typo:** "Do it **ina** way" should be **"in a way."**
- **Printed p. 177 (archive p. 197), Repetition:** "a sweet way, a kind way" can be one phrase.

### Achrei Mos-Kedoshim 5783

- **Printed p. 178 (archive p. 198), Bidi:** The opening verses and their English explanations are severely reordered. Set each verse as a separate RTL block before discussing the sequence.
- **Printed p. 178 (archive p. 198), Awkward wording:** "the proper qualification of an outstanding leader" should be **"a defining quality of an effective leader."**
- **Printed p. 178 (archive p. 198), Unsupported social taxonomy:** The piece divides Jews into elite tzaddikim, **"middle-class people"**, and people at **"the edge of the abyss."** *Baalei batim* does not mean middle class, and the hierarchy is both inaccurate and demeaning.
- **Printed pp. 178-179 (archive pp. 198-199), Disparaging language:** "busy doing nothing," "wasted their lives," "don't accomplish anything," and "attracted to the lowest forms of aveirah" should not be used to describe the audience a leader hopes to reach. Recast in terms of differing levels of observance or connection.
- **Printed p. 179 (archive p. 199), Self-congratulatory phrasing:** "a particularly brilliant proof... something you might not notice unless you're very clever" should simply state the textual observation.
- **Printed p. 179 (archive p. 199), Repetition:** "enough power, enough kedushah, enough influence" and repeated **"proper"** can be condensed.
- **Printed p. 179 (archive p. 199), Abrupt subject shift:** "This idea isn't only true for Moshe Rabbeinu. Rashi too..." suddenly changes from leadership to the accessibility of Rashi's commentary. Explain the analogy or omit it.
- **Printed p. 179 (archive p. 199), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"Rashi too, was able..."**
- **Printed p. 179 (archive p. 199), Diminutive wording:** "the smallest little kid" should be **"a young child."**

### Achrei Mos-Kedoshim 5785

- **Printed p. 180 (archive p. 200), Source identification:** The "famous vort" connecting *bezos* with the gematriyos of *tzom, kol,* and *mamon* needs an author and primary citation.
- **Printed p. 180 (archive p. 200), Bidi/layout:** Display *teshuvah, tefillah, tzedakah* and the three corresponding words in a two-column list; their present mixed order is hard to verify.
- **Printed p. 180 (archive p. 200), Exegetical scope:** In Vayikra 16:3, *bezos* refers in context to the prescribed offerings and service. The 408 gematria is a derash and should not replace the verse's plain meaning.
- **Printed p. 180 (archive p. 200), Guarantee:** "so that... our tefillos can be answered" should not imply that performing three practices guarantees a desired outcome.
- **Printed p. 180 (archive p. 200), Invented afterlife dialogue:** "After 120 years... we will say..." is imaginative rhetoric, not a source quotation. Remove quotation framing or attribute the mashal.
- **Printed p. 180 (archive p. 200), Awkward list:** "a good yeshuah, a heartfelt tefillah, and a favorable gmar din" mixes an outcome, an act, and a judgment.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Transcript opening:** "we have two parshiyos this week" and **"I'd like to quote again"** should be edited out or dated.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Invented divine speech:** "Hashem says, 'Pay attention! What am I really saying here?'" is the author's paraphrase. Remove quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Source query:** The Third Temple descending in fire is a specific tradition. Cite it and avoid presenting one eschatological model as uncontested detail.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Transcript rant:** The discussion of when children may use **"awesome"** and the dismissal of modern usage as *narishkeit* distracts from the verse.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Halachic error/overstatement:** A synagogue is a *mikdash me'at*, but it does not have **"the same kedushah as the Beis Hamikdash."** Correct the degree and legal consequences.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Factual wording:** The Kotel is not literally **"the holiest site in the world"** in the hierarchy of sacred spaces; the Temple Mount and former Temple precincts have higher sanctity. Use a careful formulation.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Tone:** "He thought he was back in Boro Park" makes a community the punchline for disrespectful shul behavior. Remove the geographic jab.
- **Printed p. 181 (archive p. 201), Unclear word:** "bring the same shmatas... into the Third Temple" appears to use *shmatas* for habits or casual behavior. Verify the recording and replace the unexplained Yiddish.
- **Printed p. 182 (archive p. 202), Source query:** Verify the Semak and Kav Hayashar citations about a synagogue as a miniature Temple and kissing its walls.
- **Printed p. 182 (archive p. 202), Halachic/personal-practice warning:** The author's practice of never speaking while wearing tefillin is offered as advice. Distinguish personal stringency from formal law and cite the applicable halachos.
- **Printed p. 182 (archive p. 202), Bidi:** The final verse phrase and blessing are visually reversed around the English sentence. Put the blessing on its own RTL line.

### Emor 5783

- **Printed p. 183 (archive p. 203), Sweeping historical claim:** "a concept that was not understood by the people of the ancient world" is far too broad; many ancient philosophies discussed time and divine transcendence.
- **Printed p. 183 (archive p. 203), Oversimplified calendar structure:** Dividing the entire Jewish year into a Pesach-Shavuos half and a High Holidays-Sukkos half needs a source and clearer handling of the intervening months.
- **Printed p. 183 (archive p. 203), Unclear practice:** "We count days until Shabbos each week" is not a universal formal count. Explain whether this refers to naming weekdays in relation to Shabbos.
- **Printed p. 183 (archive p. 203), Source query:** The claim that the midpoint of Sefirah bridges the two halves of the year is a novel interpretation and needs attribution.
- **Printed p. 183 (archive p. 203), Textual-count nuance:** Masoretic notes identify traditional midpoints in letters, words, and verses, but modern counts differ. Do not state the vav of *gachon* as a simple independently verified exact midpoint without explanation.
- **Printed p. 183 (archive p. 203), Tone:** "Yom Kippur is the fabulous day" is too casual. Use **"the sacred day"** or a precise description.
- **Printed p. 184 (archive p. 204), Untranslated word:** Explain *a zitter* or replace it with **"a palpable trembling."**
- **Printed p. 184 (archive p. 204), Source query:** Give the source for R' Yosef Chaim Sonnenfeld's eighteen-week countdown and for the parallel practice attributed to the Arizal.
- **Printed p. 184 (archive p. 204), Incomplete explanation:** The "simple explanation" of Yom Hameyuchas as merely sandwiched between important days is a popular quip, not the only or necessarily primary explanation. Qualify it.
- **Printed p. 184 (archive p. 204), Transcript address:** "Many of you are aware" and the weekly announcement dialogue preserve oral delivery. Recast in narrative form.
- **Printed pp. 184-185 (archive pp. 204-205), Repetition:** The conclusion repeats **"part of the year," "opposite end of the year,"** and **"entire year"** several times.

### Emor 5784

- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Typo:** "is **arather** famous one" should be **"is a rather famous one."**
- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Parenthetical overload:** The aside about the transmission pairs in Pirkei Avos interrupts the Hebrew quotation. Put background in a note.
- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Translation accuracy:** Check the rendering of Avos 1:3; the mishnah contrasts serving in order to receive reward with serving not in order to receive reward, rather than two unrelated servant types.
- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Source/permissions:** Verify the Baal Shem Tov story in Irving Bunim's *Ethics from Sinai* and determine whether the long retelling requires attribution beyond a passing mention.
- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Source query:** "the mesorah teaches that whatever we give in tzedakah Hashem repays tenfold" needs an exact source and may overstate the promise.
- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Logical tension:** The first answer says the Baal Shem Tov expected a multiplied reward, while the third says he did not act for reward. Explain the progression rather than leaving an apparent contradiction.
- **Printed p. 186 (archive p. 206), Weak transition:** The move from the mikvah story to the laws of kohanim is abrupt. State the shared theme of service without personal reward.
- **Printed p. 187 (archive p. 207), Typo:** **"Ray Aharon Kotler"** should be **"Rav Aharon Kotler."**
- **Printed p. 187 (archive p. 207), Agreement:** "every Jew, especially talmidei chachamim, **represents**" mixes singular and plural. Recast.
- **Printed p. 187 (archive p. 207), Superiority tone:** "a higher moral and spiritual standard than those around us" is better as **"a heightened responsibility because our conduct reflects on the community and Torah."**
- **Printed p. 187 (archive p. 207), Source/name query:** Identify the Alter of Slabodka's son and cite the dowry-and-food story.
- **Printed p. 187 (archive p. 207), Untranslated Yiddish:** Explain or replace *gutskeit*.
- **Printed p. 187 (archive p. 207), Awkward closing:** "serve Hashem and be successful in it" should be **"serve Hashem sincerely."**

### Emor 5785

- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Pop-culture intrusion:** "that Wile E. Coyote yetzer hara got smart" is jarring, comic, and theologically unsupported. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Unsupported substitution claim:** The Sanhedrin's prayer against the urge for idolatry does not establish that the urge was **replaced** by lashon hara. Present that only as the speaker's analogy, if retained.
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Colloquialism:** Calling rechilus lashon hara's **"sidekick and buddy"** trivializes serious prohibitions.
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Broad admission:** "We are all nichshal more or less" should be **"Many people struggle with..."**
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Source verification:** Check the Chovos Halevavos chapter, the exact dialogue, the Tefillah of R' Elimelech wording, and the source of the Levushei Serad story.
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Patronizing language:** "even if they seem less capable or intelligent than us. Perhaps they are... far greater" still frames others by presumed inferiority. State the lesson positively.
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Class insult:** "the wealthiest man is not necessarily the smartest man. That happens all the time" is gratuitous and demeaning.
- **Printed p. 188 (archive p. 208), Overstatement:** "parnassah comes directly from Hashem, not from our wily business instincts" creates a false either-or between providence and responsible effort.
- **Printed p. 189 (archive p. 209), Halachic clarity:** A kohen may give a get to a wife while she is a niddah; the first rav's statement was a delaying ruse. The text must say plainly that this was not the law, or readers may learn a false halachah.
- **Printed p. 189 (archive p. 209), Terminology:** Describe the wife's status as *niddah* rather than repeatedly calling her simply **"tamei/not tahor."**
- **Printed p. 189 (archive p. 209), Ethics query:** The story celebrates a rabbi's false legal claim to prevent a divorce. Preserve the source's framing and discuss the ethical/halachic basis rather than endorsing deception without comment.
- **Printed p. 189 (archive p. 209), Grammar:** "Another person came in and took his place. I'll use the words..." is followed by a garbled Hebrew quotation and an English sentence with missing subjects. Rebuild from the source.
- **Printed p. 189 (archive p. 209), Quotation punctuation:** Put the period inside **"I want to remarry my wife."**
- **Printed p. 189 (archive p. 209), Misquotation/application:** *Chachamim, hizaharu bedivreichem* means **"Sages, be careful with your words"**, not simply **"Think carefully what you do."**
- **Printed p. 190 (archive p. 210), Broken wording:** "you should certainly be very careful before an am haaretz" is incomplete. The intended preposition or warning must be recovered from the recording/source.
- **Printed p. 190 (archive p. 210), Long unrelated parenthesis:** The practical questions about kohanim at Rashbi's grave and Me'aras Hamachpelah form a separate halachic essay and interrupt the moral conclusion.
- **Printed p. 190 (archive p. 210), Halachic review required:** The categorical rulings about kohanim entering Kivrei Tzadikim and Me'aras Hamachpelah require exact responsa citations, current site-specific facts, and competent rabbinic review before being offered as guidance.
- **Printed p. 190 (archive p. 210), Transcript recommendation:** "If you're a kohen, I think it'll be worth your while..." should be omitted or replaced by a formal source note.

### Behar 5784

- **Printed p. 191 (archive p. 211), Classification/tone:** Lag BaOmer is described without qualification as **"a joyous Yom Tov."** Because its formal status and observances differ from an ordinary Yom Tov, use **"a joyous day"** or explain the intended colloquial usage.
- **Printed p. 191 (archive p. 211), Awkward wording:** "Klal Yisrael finding itself with yeshuos venechamos" is not idiomatic English. Suggested: **"a time associated with yeshuos and nechamos for Klal Yisrael."**
- **Printed p. 191 (archive p. 211), Overstatement:** "A shidduch is the ultimate [relationship]" is too absolute and can be read as diminishing other relationships. State that marriage is **one especially demanding example** of mutual commitment.
- **Printed p. 191 (archive p. 211), Mistranslation:** *Ani ledodi vedodi li* means **"I am my beloved's, and my beloved is mine."** "What can I do for you, and what can you do for me?" is an interpretive application, not a translation. Label it as such.
- **Printed p. 191 (archive p. 211), Oversimplified halachic wording:** Shemittah is characterized as "sitting back and doing nothing," and its produce as something "the poor can come in and take whatever they want." Both formulations need careful revision. Shemittah contains affirmative laws, and the status and permitted use of produce are more specific than unrestricted taking.
- **Printed p. 191 (archive p. 211), Grammar:** "a widow who had a daughter was undergoing treatment" should be **"a widow whose daughter was undergoing treatment."**
- **Printed pp. 191-192 (archive pp. 211-212), Source/permission query:** The long Rachashei Lev story is credited only to *The Weekly Vort*. Give the issue/date and original source, verify names and medical details, and confirm permission if the account is not public-domain material.
- **Printed p. 192 (archive p. 212), Tone:** "an adorable story, which I could not resist but share" sounds conversational and sentimental. Suggested: **"a moving story that illustrates the point."**
- **Printed p. 192 (archive p. 212), Missing transition:** The story demonstrates kindness and providential matchmaking, but the connection to *ani ledodi* and Shemittah is not stated explicitly at the end. Add one concise sentence tying the example back to mutual responsibility.

### Bechukosai 5784

- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Transcript style:** "This week's parsha is the first of the two tochachos" and the explanation of why it is read before Shavuos retain oral-delivery phrasing. Tighten the opening and define *tochachah* once.
- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Redundant definition:** The paragraph defining a contract repeats **"contract"** and **"agreement"** several times. Condense to one sentence.
- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Theological shorthand:** "the Torah, which we are about to reaccept" should be framed as a customary description of Shavuos, not as though acceptance lapses annually.
- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Source identification:** Cite the tractate and page for the R' Chanina ben Dosa rain story and identify the R' Chaim Kanievsky sefer being quoted.
- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Overgeneralized conclusion:** "A person should always daven for whatever he needs and trust that no one will be negatively affected" is much broader than the cited episode supports. Recast as a lesson in sincere prayer and divine wisdom, not a guarantee that requested outcomes cannot harm others.
- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Questionable inference:** The Kohen Gadol's prayer concerning travelers does not by itself prove that a traveler's prayer against needed rain is a **legitimate** request. It shows that such prayer could be spiritually consequential. Revise the logic and cite the exact source.
- **Printed p. 193 (archive p. 213), Translation/source:** State that the interpretation of *im bechukosai teleichu* as toil in Torah is Rashi's derash, not the phrase's plain lexical meaning.
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Likely wrong word:** "proficient in the powers of tumah and **blackmail**" is almost certainly intended to be **"black magic."** Verify against the recording/source.
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Folklore presented as history:** The account of a corpse sitting up to learn, a church sinking into the ground, and anti-Jewish clergy wielding supernatural powers needs a primary source and clear attribution as a traditional story rather than unqualified historical narrative.
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Loaded language:** "the goyish population," "anti-Semites," and descriptions of priests injecting idolatrous thoughts into Jews should be revised for precision and restraint.
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Typo/name query:** Verify whether **"kfarim"** is the intended term. It may be a mistranscription of *kefarim*, *kofrim*, or another word.
