- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Missing article:** "a miracle of epic proportions" needs **"of"** if the text presently reads "a miracle epic proportions." More importantly, replace the hype with **"an extraordinary miracle, according to the account."**
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Tense/grammar:** "We certainly could not possibly imagine that we were able to pull off such a feat" should be **"We certainly cannot imagine being able to perform such a feat."**
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Source chain:** "I heard this from Rabbi Oelbaum on a tour of Ukraine" is not enough for a publication-level citation. Identify the underlying written or oral tradition if available.
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Broken construction:** "The more you learn, the greater level your kedushah" should be **"The more you learn, the higher the level of kedushah you can attain."**
- **Printed p. 194 (archive p. 214), Untranslated Yiddish:** Translate *poel zayn ales git* or give an immediate English gloss.

### Behar-Bechukosai 5783

- **Printed p. 195 (archive p. 215), Verse citation:** The promise answering "What will we eat?" and describing the sixth-year blessing is Vayikra **25:20-22**, not 25:19-21. Correct the range.
- **Printed p. 195 (archive p. 215), Historical overclaim:** "We have been keeping shemittah for thousands of years, and we have seen it work" compresses a complicated history of exile, limited agricultural observance, and modern practice. Qualify and document the claim.
- **Printed p. 195 (archive p. 215), Apologetic overstatement:** The argument is called a conclusive proof that Hashem created the world and authored the Torah. At most, the cited promise is being offered as one faith-based argument; it does not logically establish every stated conclusion without additional premises.
- **Printed p. 195 (archive p. 215), Source query:** Attribute the idea that asking "What will we eat?" reduces the form of the blessing and that not asking leads to *ochel kima umisbarech b'me'av*. The latter phrase is associated with Rashi on Vayikra 26:5 and should not be presented as the plain promise in the Shemittah passage without explanation.
- **Printed p. 196 (archive p. 216), Flippant application:** "You will never have to go on a diet" and the description of the blessing as **"super food"** trivialize the source and introduce a modern health claim. Remove both.
- **Printed p. 196 (archive p. 216), Speculative burden:** The statement that a three-year crop would create an onerous storage problem is the author's practical conjecture. Present it as such rather than as the reason the alternative blessing was superior.
- **Printed pp. 195-196 (archive pp. 215-216), Repetition:** The conclusion repeats **"proof," "authenticity," "absolute emunah,"** and the divine authorship claim. One restrained closing paragraph will be stronger.

### Behar-Bechukosai 5785

- **Printed p. 197 (archive p. 217), Date error:** Shemittah 5719 was in 1958-1959. It was about **66 years** before 5785/2024-2025, not **75 years** before. Revise the relative-date sentence or give the Hebrew year only.
- **Printed p. 197 (archive p. 217), Bibliographic detail:** Identify the exact title and edition of the R' Chaim Kanievsky work prepared by Rabbi Shai Graucher, and cite the Chazon Ish passage in *Shevi'is*.
- **Printed p. 197 (archive p. 217), Unsupported immediacy:** "Mashiach is coming" and "when Mashiach will come very quickly" should be expressed as prayer or hope, not a dated prediction. Suggested: **"in anticipation of the geulah, may it come soon."**
- **Printed p. 197 (archive p. 217), Historical generalization:** "Very few people were keeping shemittah" needs a geographic and documentary frame. Specify the community or agricultural sector being discussed.
- **Printed p. 197 (archive p. 217), Polemical phrasing:** "They did not look for any heterim" and "did not hold of the heter mechirah" imply that recognized halachic positions are evasions. State the community's practice neutrally.
- **Printed pp. 197-198 (archive pp. 217-218), Source verification:** Give a primary citation for the Komemiyut locust account, including the date, the species/plague area, the border claim, and who witnessed it.
- **Printed p. 197 (archive p. 217), Name inconsistency:** The rabbi's surname appears in forms resembling **Mendelson/Medelson**. Verify and standardize, likely Rabbi Binyamin Mendelson.
- **Printed p. 198 (archive p. 218), Overstated comparison:** Calling the locust boundary **"like Kriyas Yam Suf"** is rhetorical hyperbole. Attribute it to the storyteller or replace it with a factual description.
- **Printed p. 198 (archive p. 218), Source/factual query:** The statement that locusts have never entered Egypt since the biblical plague needs the Ramban's exact wording and careful distinction between no locust presence, no destructive swarm, and a theological tradition. Do not present it as an independently verified zoological fact.
- **Printed p. 198 (archive p. 218), Hyperbole:** "Every media outlet" came to see the field is unlikely to be literally documentable. Use **"members of the press"** unless a complete list supports the claim.
- **Printed p. 198 (archive p. 218), Speculation:** "Who knows, perhaps the publicity stimulated growth" is unsupported and distracts from the sourced account. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 198 (archive p. 218), Redundancy:** "have the zechus to merit" duplicates the same concept. Use either **"have the zechus"** or **"merit."**

### Bamidbar 5783

- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Nonstandard label:** "Shabbos Derech Eretz" is not a familiar calendrical name. Make clear that this is a homiletic designation attributed to the Chiddushei Harim, and give the source.
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Transcript aside:** The quiz format and "everyone looks glazed" comment belong to a live talk, not a polished essay. Remove them.
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Grammar:** "Why is it specifically Parshas Bamidbar comes before Shavuos?" should be **"Why is Parshas Bamidbar specifically read before Shavuos?"**
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Translation nuance:** *Se'u es rosh* literally introduces a census, while "lift up each person" is a homiletic reading. Label the latter as interpretation rather than direct translation.
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Patronizing hierarchy:** Contrasting "the person with the least knowledge, an am ha'aretz" with "the greatest talmid chacham" is unnecessary and demeaning. State that the census affirms the value of every individual.
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Awkward wording:** "an incentive aspire" needs **"an incentive to aspire."**
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Transcript correction:** "not the end of the parsha, the end of the haftorah" should be recast as **"The haftorah concludes with..."**
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Overgeneralization/exclusion:** "All boys thirteen and over instantly recognize" the Hosea verses is neither universal nor inclusive. Suggested: **"Many men recognize these verses from the custom of reciting them while wrapping the tefillin strap around the finger."**
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Citation:** Identify the verses as Hoshea 2:21-22 and set the three Hebrew lines in correct RTL order with one reliable translation.
- **Printed p. 199 (archive p. 219), Source query:** Cite the commentators who explain the use of *erusin* rather than *nisuin* as preserving anticipation and excitement.
- **Printed pp. 199-200 (archive pp. 219-220), Repetition:** "excitement," "enthusiasm," "looking forward," and the marriage analogy are repeated several times. Consolidate the idea before the memorial paragraph.
- **Printed p. 200 (archive p. 220), Awkward memorial sentence:** The sentence explaining that this was the author's father's bar mitzvah parsha and haftorah repeats **"which"** and obscures the antecedents. Split it into two sentences.
- **Printed p. 200 (archive p. 220), Register:** "the ultimate goal of all Torah" is a major theological conclusion. Attribute it to the cited verse/commentator rather than state it as an uncontested definition.

### Bamidbar 5784

- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Bidi:** The opening verse, its attribution, and the question about *rosh beis avosav* are visually difficult to follow because Hebrew and English fragments reverse around punctuation. Set the verse in its own RTL block, then translate it in a separate sentence.
- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Unclear question:** "Who is this person who is considered the head of the family? Is it the bechor? Is it someone with yichus?" needs a clearer explanation of what textual ambiguity is being resolved.
- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Source identification:** *The Short Vort* is cited generically. Supply author, edition/date, page or issue, and the underlying source for the teaching.
- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Overstatement:** "Everybody is born into circumstances that can shape and support" is plausible, but the next sentence divides people into those with and without "any particular yichus." Avoid implying that family pedigree determines access to a relationship with Hashem.
- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Talmudic citation/translation:** Give the exact citation for the statement that the prayers of a *tzaddik ben tzaddik* take precedence over those of a *tzaddik ben rasha*, and distinguish it from the separate saying about the place attained by *baalei teshuvah*.
- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Logical gap:** The piece asks why Rivkah's prayer was not preferred because of her difficult upbringing, then answers that Yitzchak forged his own path. Explain how this directly resolves the precedence question rather than merely praising Yitzchak.
- **Printed p. 201 (archive p. 221), Terminology:** "Each of the avos had his own unique method" should acknowledge that the discussion includes Rivkah and the imahos or remain specifically about the avos.
- **Printed pp. 201-202 (archive pp. 221-222), Gendered universal:** "unique to himself" and "Every Yid is a star of his own" should be rewritten in inclusive singular or plural form.
- **Printed pp. 201-202 (archive pp. 221-222), Cliché/repetition:** "forge a path," "own unique method," "own derech," and "do the work yourself" recur many times. Reduce to one formulation.
- **Printed p. 202 (archive p. 222), Offensive joke risk:** The duchaning joke calls a man "not-so-religious," emphasizes that he barely attends shul, and reveals only at the end that his male ancestors duchaned, implying that he is a kohen. The joke depends on the rabbi initially refusing a qualified kohen and makes a less-observant Jew the punchline. Remove it or rewrite with a coherent premise.
- **Printed p. 202 (archive p. 222), Halachic incoherence:** If the man's father and paternal grandfather were kohanim, he is presumptively a kohen as well; donating money is irrelevant. As written, the story cannot illustrate the stated lesson without explanation.
- **Printed p. 202 (archive p. 222), Unclear closing:** "the title of ish rosh l'veis avosav would be conferred" treats a descriptive biblical phrase as a formal title. Explain the intended homiletic claim.

### Bamidbar 5785

- **Printed p. 203 (archive p. 223), Duplicated word:** "the pesukim **that that** we all say" should be **"the pesukim that we all say."**
- **Printed p. 203 (archive p. 223), Grammar:** "he knew were he to be caught carrying tefillin" should be **"he knew that, were he to be caught carrying tefillin, he would be put to death."**
- **Printed p. 203 (archive p. 223), Repetition:** "Unexpectedly and unfortunately" is cumbersome. One adverb is enough.
- **Printed p. 203 (archive p. 223), Holocaust terminology/tone:** Repeatedly reproducing the officer's slur **"Dog"** is not necessary. Quote it once, if source fidelity requires it, and summarize later abuse.
- **Printed pp. 203-205 (archive pp. 223-225), Source and historical verification:** The dramatic account titled "Tefillin in Dachau" requires a full citation to Rabbi Yosef Wallace's article, verification of Judah Wallace's identity, dates, camp details, the second witness, and the ketubah provenance. Preserve distinctions between testimony, family tradition, and later retelling.
- **Printed p. 204 (archive p. 224), Typo:** "because this was **such as an** extremely painful form of death" should be **"because this was such an extremely painful form of torture"** or, more directly, **"because the punishment was so painful."**
- **Printed p. 204 (archive p. 224), Factual wording:** Twenty-five lashes to the head are described as a "form of death," although the account says Judah survived. Use **"method of torture intended to kill him"** if that is what the source states.
- **Printed p. 204 (archive p. 224), Awkward sequence:** "He was about to be dragged to a pile of corpses and burned in a ditch. When another Jew saw him..." should clarify whether he had already been placed with bodies or was still at the punishment site.
- **Printed p. 204 (archive p. 224), Romanticized trauma:** "The rest is history" is too breezy after an account of torture, bereavement, and a marriage proposal immediately after liberation. Use a restrained transition.
- **Printed p. 204 (archive p. 224), Honorific/bidi corruption:** The honorific following "Klausenberger Rebbe" is visibly scrambled. Re-enter the entire Hebrew/Yiddish honorific as a protected RTL span and verify whether it is appropriate for the date described.
- **Printed p. 204 (archive p. 224), Historical/source query:** Verify the statement that the Klausenberger Rebbe wrote a ketubah from memory and performed this couple's marriage, including location and date.
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Bidi/quotation mark:** The opening quotation begins with a reversed character, rendered like **"T am certain"**. It should begin **"I am certain."**
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Causation claim:** Mr. Olasky's belief that wearing the tefillin protected him and gave him long life should remain clearly attributed to him, not endorsed as demonstrable causation.
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Overclaim:** One late witness who recalls borrowing tefillin corroborates part of the broader story; he does not necessarily **"validate"** every detail of the attempted hanging and beating. Use **"corroborate an important part of my father's account."**
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Source/date query:** Identify the publication presently rendered as garbled Hebrew, its issue/date, Mr. Olasky's full name, and the organization of which Rabbi Wallace was director.
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Bidi breakdown:** The paragraph connecting the first and last haftorah verses to the tefillin verses is severely reordered. Separate each biblical verse, its citation, and its English explanation into distinct blocks.
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Grammar/logic:** "if we behave and Moshiach comes through our..." is incomplete in the extracted/visible text. Recover the intended sentence from the recording or source rather than copyediting the damaged line.
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Anthropomorphic/marital metaphor:** "Hashem tells us He will marry us forever" is an interpretation of Hoshea's metaphor. Attribute it and avoid moving directly from historical testimony to a guaranteed personal outcome.
- **Printed p. 205 (archive p. 225), Repetition:** "What a commitment. What a blessing" and the ensuing exhortation can be reduced to one dignified concluding sentence.

### Naso 5783

- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Factual nuance:** Naso is called the longest parsha by verse count, then said to be "not really the longest" because many offerings repeat. Verse count is still the relevant metric; say that the repetition makes it less difficult for a bar mitzvah reader than the count suggests.
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Diminutive/exclusion:** "for a little bar mitzvah boy" is patronizing. Use **"for a bar mitzvah student"** or **"for the reader."**
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Unclear maxim:** "the ikar of learning is Torah" is tautological in English and likely reflects a damaged Hebrew phrase. Verify whether the intended word is *chazarah* or another concept; the following sentence stresses repetition.
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Repetition:** "over and over again," "repeated over and over," and "the lesson of chazarah" restate the same point.
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Bibliographic verification:** Give the exact titles, spellings, and publication data for R' Shlomo Zalman Sonnenfeld's sefer, R' Yosef Engel's sefer, and the work on which the latter question is based. Several Hebrew titles are unreadable in mixed OCR and should be checked against title pages.
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Punctuation:** The question **"He could have picked any other number?"** should be **"Why did he choose eighty rather than another number?"**
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Quotation punctuation:** The dialogue has doubled opening quotation marks before **"What happened?"** and before the dream account. Normalize quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Typo:** **"Tl tell you"** should be **"I'll tell you."**
- **Printed p. 206 (archive p. 226), Unclear referent:** "the matter had indeed become perfectly clear" is acceptable, but the text should identify the difficult passage sufficiently for the anecdote to be meaningful and verifiable.
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Repetitive source formula:** "The sefer ... brings down divrei Torah ... brings down a Targum Yonasan" repeats **"brings down."** Use **"quotes"** or **"cites."**
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Translation/source:** Verify the exact Targum Yonasan wording and whether the three clauses are correctly mapped to wealth, mitzvos, and peace. The current Hebrew-English order is difficult to verify.
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Grammar:** "How can he have that and I don't?" should be **"How can he have that when I don't?"** or **"Why does he have that and I do not?"**
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Hebrew phrase/source:** The maxim intended as "Who is rich? One who is happy with his lot" should be cited to Avos 4:1 and translated accurately.
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Awkward blessing:** "You should always think that you have a lot of money and that you don't have enough yiras Shamayim" is clearer as **"May you feel that you have enough materially, yet never be complacent in yiras Shamayim."**
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Repetition:** Satisfaction, calmness, mitzvos, and peace are explained in several nearly identical sentences. Condense the causal chain.
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Guarantee:** "there cannot possibly be anything more shalom than that" overpromises that spiritual focus necessarily produces peace. State it as an aspiration.
- **Printed p. 207 (archive p. 227), Excessive personal aside:** "I got this from a very chashuv friend of mine... a wonderful fellow" interrupts the argument. Put the source in a concise note.
- **Printed p. 208 (archive p. 228), Duplicated phrase:** "kohanim **who are who are** duchaning" should be **"kohanim who are duchaning."**
- **Printed p. 208 (archive p. 228), Halachic review required:** The conclusion that an Israeli kohen visiting outside Israel may duchan on the local second day of Yom Tov is practical halachic guidance. Give the exact responsum, define all conditions and minhagim, and have a competent rav review the ruling before publication.
- **Printed p. 208 (archive p. 228), Confusing rationale:** The explanation moves from work-related distraction to simchah and then to the traveler's inability to work, but does not clearly distinguish the halachic status of the visitor from the local congregation. Reconstruct the argument from the cited source.
- **Printed p. 208 (archive p. 228), Untranslated conclusion:** The final Hebrew formulation is left without a reliable English gloss and is visually disordered. Provide a complete translation after the source quotation.

### Naso 5784

- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Bidi/source citation:** The verse from Tehillim and its parenthetical citation are reversed around the English sentence. Put the citation after a complete RTL verse block.
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Translation error:** The verse "He brought them out with silver and gold, and there was no stumbling one among His tribes" does not literally say that **"nobody was poor."** That is a derash on *koshel*, and it should be labeled and sourced.
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Grammar:** "only after receive money" should be **"only afterward receive money"** or **"receive money only afterward."**
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Repetition:** The opening asks twice why protection follows money. State the question once.
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Agreement:** "one person may not be a blessing for another person" should be **"what is a blessing for one person may not be a blessing for another."**
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Punctuation:** There is an extra closing quotation mark in **"one size fits all.""**
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Narrow definition:** "what his desires are" can imply that every desire should be fulfilled. Use **"what is truly appropriate for that person's needs and growth."**
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Invented celestial dialogue:** The dream report quotes a malach and a response in heaven as though the exchange were literal transcript. Clearly attribute the entire scene to R' Mendel of Rimanov's reported dream and cite the source.
- **Printed p. 209 (archive p. 229), Typographical corruption:** The sentence beginning **"The Rebbe cried out"** ends in badly reordered Hebrew/Yiddish and an apparent mistranscription of a name or phrase. Recover it from the source; it cannot be safely copyedited as printed.
- **Printed p. 210 (archive p. 230), Awkward closing:** "what's appropriate for him and in that brachah" needs punctuation and a clearer antecedent. Suggested: **"May each person receive the blessing appropriate for that individual, enabling all of us to fulfill the mitzvos..."**
- **Printed p. 210 (archive p. 230), Faulty comparative:** "to the best of our ability, and even greater" should be **"to the best of our ability, and continue to grow."**

### Naso 5785

- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Awkward opening:** "Naso has a great brachah to be the longest parsha" personifies the parsha oddly. Suggested: **"Naso is the longest parsha by verse count."**
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Source query:** The claim that the first post-Shavuos reading is deliberately long to demonstrate love of Torah needs attribution. It is not self-evident from the calendar arrangement.
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Transcript repetition:** "an important connection and an important lesson" should be tightened.
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Halachic/source detail:** Identify the source and exact wording of the formula recited during Birchas Kohanim after a troubling dream. The instruction depends on communal practice and timing and should not be summarized loosely.
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Unsupported equation:** Birchas Kohanim is called **"a real nevuah"** and then treated as sixty times stronger than a dream because a dream is one-sixtieth of prophecy. This mathematical construction does not follow from the cited statements. Present it only as a sourced derash, with the full source.
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Numerological non sequitur:** "one-sixtieth plus sixty is sixty-one" does not explain cancellation unless the intended rule is *batel beshishim* or a gematria. Reconstruct the argument from the source rather than leave the arithmetic as though it were proof.
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Repetition:** "through the koach of being a nevuah, of being mevatel" repeats **"of being."**
- **Printed p. 211 (archive p. 231), Audience aside:** "You can ask this of all your friends and neighbors" should be removed from a written essay.
- **Printed pp. 211-212 (archive pp. 231-232), Source identification:** Give the exact R' Chaim Kanievsky sefer, siman/page, and the name of the son whose siyum is described.
- **Printed p. 212 (archive p. 232), Agreement:** "R' ... **teach** us" should be **"teaches us"** if the subject is singular; verify the name obscured by the mixed-direction line.
- **Printed p. 212 (archive p. 232), Overstatement:** "It goes up to infinity" is not a precise consequence of Torah increasing peace. Suggested: **"its potential is not fixed by a measurable quota."**
- **Printed p. 212 (archive p. 232), Bidi:** The chain of Gemara citations and the final phrase about Torah and peace reverse around punctuation. Put each source in a separate RTL block and give a complete English paraphrase afterward.
- **Printed p. 212 (archive p. 232), Colloquial close:** "We have to get going and start doing our portion" should be **"Each person should devote sustained effort to learning Torah."**

### Story 5785

- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Vague title:** **"Story 5785"** gives readers no subject and is hard to distinguish in the contents. Use a descriptive title such as **"The Unfinished Masechta, 5785."**
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Source chain:** The account passes from R' Avi Wiesenfeld to R' Moshe Oelbaum to the author, without a publication, date, survivor's name, camp, or original testimony. Supply the earliest verifiable source and identify where details are unknown.
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Missing article:** "Amidst the horrors of **Holocaust**" should be **"amid the horrors of the Holocaust."**
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Generic wording:** "one such story is about a man" and repeated references to **"the man"** make the narrative difficult to follow. Name the survivor and his fellow prisoner, or explicitly state that the source withholds their names.
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Euphemism/ambiguity:** "the next day, the man was gone" does not tell readers whether he was transferred, selected, or killed. Preserve only what the source actually establishes and say so plainly.
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Faulty correlative:** "he eventually **not only acquired** [the masechta], **he mastered** it" needs **"not only ... but also."**
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Quotation punctuation:** The survivor's words after **"At the siyum he said"** should begin after a colon and be presented as one properly punctuated quotation.
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Repetition:** "worth sharing," "worth hearing," and "worth repeating" frame the piece three times. Keep one restrained editorial sentence.
- **Printed p. 213 (archive p. 233), Bidi:** The masechta title and Shas references are visually reversed in several places. Verify the intended masechta, likely *Niddah*, from the original source and protect each Hebrew title as RTL text.

### Behaloscha 5783

- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Bidi:** The two opening verses and citations are split into scrambled fragments. Re-enter each verse as a complete RTL block, followed by its citation and translation.
- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Factual wording:** The people barred from the korban were explicitly *temei'im lenefesh adam*. "For whatever reason, their tumah was at a level..." is vague and implies uncertainty the verse does not contain.
- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Theological hierarchy:** The assertion that seeking Hashem is a **greater relationship** than the Shechinah dwelling among the people needs a source and qualification; it should not be presented as a settled scale of divine intimacy.
- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Misidentified/misretold source:** The well-known exchange about whether divine or human works are greater is between Turnus Rufus and Rabbi Akiva. In the classic midrash, the comparison involves natural produce and prepared food, and it is tied to circumcision. The printed "Kaiser," cakes, pebbles, sand, and threat about denouncing Rabbi Akiva appear to conflate or embellish the source. Rebuild the story from the primary text.
- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Name/title consistency:** Standardize **R' Aharon/Ahron Soloveichik** and cite the lecture in which this version was given.
- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Loaded language:** "the Kaiser, who was a rasha" and the comic tasting of "sand and shmutz" lower the register and distract from a source that already needs careful historical framing.
- **Printed p. 214 (archive p. 234), Broken conclusion:** "You are wise... Had you answered that God's achievements are greater, I would have denounced you for doing..." is not intelligible as printed. Recover the missing Hebrew concept and explain the circumcision argument explicitly.
- **Printed p. 215 (archive p. 235), Repetition:** "We want to come to Hashem" is repeated several times in adjacent sentences.
- **Printed p. 215 (archive p. 235), Grammar:** The quoted sentence should close as **"We want to come to Hashem."** The period is currently outside the closing quotation mark.
- **Printed p. 215 (archive p. 235), Oversimplification:** Pesach Sheni is described as **"another chance"** granted because the petitioners wanted to do more. Cite the source for that moral formulation and distinguish it from the actual legal question, *lamah nigara*.
- **Printed p. 215 (archive p. 235), Comparison query:** Nadav and Avihu are used as the counterexample of doing more "outside the boundaries." This is a major interpretive claim; cite the commentator and avoid reducing their episode to a generic cautionary slogan.
- **Printed p. 215 (archive p. 235), Unsupported claim:** "I'm going to add an extra Yom Tov in the Torah in your merit" is invented divine speech and overstates Pesach Sheni's status. Paraphrase and attribute the derash.
- **Printed p. 215 (archive p. 235), Bidi:** The identities proposed for the impure men, the phrase *lamah nigara*, and the concluding Hebrew are visibly displaced. Set each as protected RTL text.

### Behaloscha 5784

- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Terminology:** The marks around *Vayehi binsoa* are inverted nuns, not ordinary "upside-down letters" without qualification. Use the conventional term and note that their exact form varies among manuscripts.
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Overstatement:** "Nothing else in the Torah is remotely similar" should be **"This marking is unique in the Torah scroll tradition."**
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Source/citation:** Cite Shabbos 115b-116a for the discussion of the section's placement and the opinion that it forms a separate book. Identify the secondary sefer whose title is obscured in the line.
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Conflated opinions:** The paragraph moves from the section being out of place, to its future relocation, to separating two punishments, without signaling that these are distinct statements/opinions. Separate and attribute them.
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Numerology/source query:** The claim that the two nuns, totaling 100, protect against one hundred curses requires an exact source. Devarim 28 is commonly counted as ninety-eight curses, with any added two requiring explanation.
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Guarantee:** "We shouldn't worry about them at all. The two nuns are there to protect us" turns a homiletic interpretation into a promise of immunity. Recast as symbolism.
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Time-sensitive reference:** "As I'm taping this, it is the day that the hostages were rescued" should identify the date and the four hostages rescued, or be removed from a timeless print piece. "The hostages" incorrectly suggests all hostages.
- **Printed p. 216 (archive p. 236), Bidi:** The page has long source quotations, reversed citations, and isolated letters that can be mistaken for the numeral 1. Rebuild the discussion with the Hebrew on separate lines.

### Behaloscha 5785

- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Bibliographic detail:** Verify the full name R' Yosef Shlomo Goldstein, the title of his sefer, the Gateshead affiliation, and the *Kol HaTorah* issue/page.
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Bidi:** The verse from Bamidbar 8:19 and the Rashi quotation are visually reordered. Set each in a standalone RTL block before translating.
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Dismissive aside:** "which is not usual for Rashi, it's more like the Baal HaTurim" is an unnecessary comparison and reads as criticism of Rashi's method.
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Source accuracy:** Rashi's five repetitions of *Bnei Yisrael* are an expression of affection and correspond to the five books of Torah. Verify that both elements are quoted completely rather than blending the source with later interpretation.
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Grammar:** "a vessel full of precious jewels and precious gems that kings put away in their treasure **house.,**" has redundant adjectives and malformed punctuation. Use **"a precious object kept in a royal treasury."**
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Awkward enumeration:** "number one... and number two, equally as important if not perhaps even more so" should be rewritten as a clear parallel pair.
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Speculative psychology:** The claim that the rest of Klal Yisrael might feel **"a little bit of a twinge"** when the Leviim were selected is invented motivation. Attribute it to the cited commentator.
- **Printed p. 217 (archive p. 237), Anthropomorphic scale:** "Hashem's ahavah scale" is overly casual and suggests ranked divine affection. Replace it with precise language about distinct roles and shared covenantal value.
- **Printed p. 218 (archive p. 238), Counting error:** The text says there are **three crowns** and then lists Torah, kehunah, malchus, **and** a good name. Avos 4:13 names three crowns and then says the crown of a good name rises above them. Punctuate and explain accordingly.
- **Printed p. 218 (archive p. 238), Contradictory superlative:** The page says the crown of Torah is "greater than all of them," while the cited mishnah explicitly elevates a good name above the three crowns. Clarify whether "greater" means universally accessible rather than highest.
- **Printed p. 218 (archive p. 238), Source identification:** Cite Rambam, Hilchos Talmud Torah 3:1, or the actual source for the teaching that the crown of Torah is available to every Jew.
- **Printed p. 218 (archive p. 238), Invented quotation:** "Hashem says, 'I love you all...'" is a paraphrase, not a biblical quotation. Remove quotation marks or cite the source wording.
- **Printed p. 218 (archive p. 238), Repetition:** The closing repeats **"love," "each and every," "zechus,"** and **"all"** several times.

### Shlach 5783

- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Transcript opening:** The comment that grandparents are "routinely accused" of turning everything into a lesson is conversational throat-clearing. Begin with the Rashi connection.
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Run-on:** "They fell into the same trap, they didn't learn mussar, they did not heed..." is a comma-spliced list. Split or coordinate the clauses.
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Vague wording:** "be on guard and be a little bit better" should identify the actual lesson, guarding speech after Miriam's punishment.
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Sectarian aside:** "from, of all people, a chassidish Rebbe" is gratuitous and suggests surprise that a source crosses communal lines. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Name style:** "Avraham Yehoshua Heshel **from** Apt" should be **"of Apt"** or **"the Apter Rav, R' Avraham Yehoshua Heshel."**
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Extraordinary claim/source:** The assertion that resurrection will occur alphabetically, with humble people moved to the front, needs the exact *Yalkut* or Apter Rav citation and should be identified as a homiletic tradition.
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Overliteral speculation:** The discussion that adding a yud to Hoshea's name would push him five places later in the resurrection queue is presented as mechanics rather than derash. Attribute and soften it.
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Guarantee:** Yehoshua's humility is said to **"guarantee him priority."** Use **"would, in this teaching, place him among the humble who rise first."**
- **Printed p. 219 (archive p. 239), Quotation mark:** The description of the Amora begins with no opening quotation mark before **"[He] was humble and modest..."** Reconstruct the quote and identify the Talmudic source.
- **Printed pp. 219-220 (archive pp. 239-240), Source/name verification:** The Amora's name and tractate are difficult to identify because of bidi corruption. Verify them from R' Chaim Kanievsky's original text rather than infer from the broken page.
- **Printed p. 220 (archive p. 240), Missing article:** "until the end of **block**" should be **"until the end of the block."**
- **Printed p. 220 (archive p. 240), Repetitive aside:** "and then he would walk back, doctor's orders" repeats information already stated.
- **Printed p. 220 (archive p. 240), Source query:** Cite the story of the Chazon Ish being told to recite Tehillim. Verify that the added practice and wording are not later embellishments.
- **Printed p. 220 (archive p. 240), Awkward fragment:** "We get very, 'You're telling me what to do?'" is grammatically broken. Suggested: **"We may react defensively: 'Who are you to tell me what to do?'"**
- **Printed p. 220 (archive p. 240), Unsupported generalization:** "This is how true gedolei Torah conducted themselves" extrapolates from one anecdote. Use **"The story portrays the Chazon Ish as..."**

### Shlach 5784

- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Jargon:** "The parsha is multi-factorial, multi-tasking" is clinical/business jargon and does not describe a text naturally. Use **"The parsha weaves together several themes."**
- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Flattery:** "the Chachamim in their genius understood" should simply identify the commentators and their explanation.
- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Casual phrasing:** "sad news, we flunked on our mission" is too flippant for the episode of the spies.
- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Pronoun/time shift:** The wilderness generation is told that it will die, but the reassurance concerns the nation and next generation entering the land. "We are indeed going to get Eretz Yisrael" blurs those subjects; explain the continuity accurately.
- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Translation overstatement:** *Korban* can legitimately be translated "sacrifice" in many contexts. "Universally translated... a mistranslation" is false. Explain that **"offering"** better preserves the root-based homiletic idea of drawing near.
- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Grammar:** "no matter how much effort he invested, these sugyos were..." is a comma splice. Use a semicolon or new sentence.
- **Printed p. 221 (archive p. 241), Sarcastic parenthesis:** "All these stories always happen, somebody was listening in. That's how we get the story" breaks the tone and undercuts the anecdote's credibility. Remove it.
- **Printed pp. 221-222 (archive pp. 241-242), Missing source:** Name the avreich, scholar, Rebbe, and original publication if known. A promise about livelihood, children, and health should not circulate as anonymous fact.
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), Sweeping promise:** "you will be spared from shedding tears over parnassah, your children's shidduchim, and health issues" is a reported blessing, not a general consequence of crying over Torah. Keep it inside clearly attributed quotation and avoid implying a guarantee.
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), Doubled quotation mark:** **""That's nice"** has two opening quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), Punctuation:** Remove the comma in **"17 words, which correspond, to the 17 times..."**
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), Halachic review required:** The proposed combined wording in *Atah chonen* changes the Amidah's nusach. Give R' Chaim Kanievsky's exact written ruling and context, and obtain competent halachic review before presenting it as permission.
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), Hype:** "a phenomenal response" should be replaced by the response itself.
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), Unsupported maxim/source:** "The entire world is not worth even one amen" and the trillions-and-quadrillions comparison need a source and explanation. As written, the value scale appears as an uncited absolute claim.
- **Printed p. 222 (archive p. 242), False guarantee:** "If you really want to learn, Hashem will send you all of the help that you need" is too categorical. Use aspirational language.
- **Printed p. 223 (archive p. 243), Narrow application:** Material requests are reduced to helping a person sit and learn. If this is the cited speaker's teaching, attribute it rather than defining the purpose of every prayer for health or livelihood.
- **Printed pp. 221-223 (archive pp. 241-243), Bidi:** The verses, nusach variants, formula, and citations are among the most directionally unstable pages in this section. Keep the two prayer versions in a labeled comparison rather than interleaving them with English.

### Shlach 5785

- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Major factual error:** The written Torah does not contain **320,000 words**. The number is closer to 80,000 words; approximately 304,805 is the traditional letter count. The text likely confuses letters with words and also gives the wrong rounded figure.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Repetition after factual error:** "those 320,000 words" repeats the incorrect number. Correct both occurrences.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Source identification:** Cite the exact lecture or publication by R' Mordechai Becher and the *Beis Halevi* passage used to explain Torah shebe'al peh.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Inaccurate Temple description:** The Aron contained the luchos, and traditions discuss other contents; saying simply **"What is in the Aron? The Torah"** needs clarification if it is foundational to the metaphor.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Broad doctrinal claim:** "our minhagim... are not merely commentary on the Torah, but a part of the Torah itself" requires careful distinctions among binding Oral Torah, rabbinic enactment, interpretation, and communal custom.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Citation/translation:** The verse cited as Jeremiah 31:33 should be checked against Jewish verse numbering and translated in full. It speaks of Torah placed within and written on the heart; it does not explicitly say that human forgetting will cease.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Unproven premise:** "At the time of Mashiach, there will no longer be any forgetting" needs a source. Do not derive it solely from the Jeremiah verse.
- **Printed p. 224 (archive p. 244), Exegetical overreach:** The conclusion that *ledoroseichem* was written specifically to teach that tzitzis remains required in the messianic era is a derash and needs R' Chaim's exact source and wording.
- **Printed p. 225 (archive p. 245), Repetition:** "a profound thought, a very beautiful thought" and repeated **"realize"** should be tightened.
- **Printed p. 225 (archive p. 245), Awkward phrase:** "one of the physical, tangible ways" is enough; the Hebrew-English hybrid preceding **"ways"** appears redundant.

### Korach 5783

- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Repetition:** "gaavah, not just gaavah but real..." repeats the same term rather than sharpening it.
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Speculative premise:** Applying "familiarity breeds contempt" to Korach is an interpretive proposal. Attribute it and avoid claiming that proximity naturally caused him to imagine equality with Moshe.
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Superlative/source:** "Moshe was far beyond anyone who ever lived" should cite the biblical basis and be framed in the relevant domain, prophecy and leadership.
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Faulty parallelism:** "We give them respect for two reasons: A) for their level... and B) even if..." should be recast as **"We honor accomplishment, and we also owe every person basic dignity regardless of what we know about that person's accomplishments."**
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Doubled quotation mark:** The complaint begins **""We're all going to die..."** with two opening quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Source query:** Cite the teaching that Aharon returned immediately to report the plague's end and the reported comments of the Chazon Ish and R' Moshe Feinstein about people failing to report good news.
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Logical simplification:** The ketores had previously been associated with the deaths of unauthorized offerers, but the lesson is not merely that "ketores could save lives, not endanger them." Explain that its effect depends on divine command and proper service.
- **Printed p. 226 (archive p. 246), Guarantee:** "their tefillos should reach Hashem and... bring about a yeshuah" should not promise a desired outcome. Say that people request prayer and counsel.
- **Printed p. 227 (archive p. 247), Punctuation:** **"January 28,1986"** needs a space after the comma: **"January 28, 1986."**
- **Printed p. 227 (archive p. 247), Historical verification:** Identify the supposed Jewish day-school teacher who was a Challenger finalist and supply a reliable source. Without a name and contemporaneous documentation, the account reads like an urban legend.
- **Printed p. 227 (archive p. 247), Insensitive framing:** The messages saying the teacher was **"lucky"** not to have been chosen and the child's explanation that Hashem would have saved the shuttle for one good teacher can imply that the seven people killed were less worthy. This theology is harmful and should not be used as a feel-good proof of judging favorably.
- **Printed p. 227 (archive p. 247), Contradictory moral:** The piece asks readers to assume a charitable meaning behind a cruel sentence, but the charitable meaning still rests on an offensive comparison with the victims. Replace the anecdote or discuss its problem explicitly.
- **Printed p. 227 (archive p. 247), Style:** "Challenger shuttle" should be **"Space Shuttle Challenger."**
- **Printed p. 227 (archive p. 247), Bidi:** The Hebrew maxim and final blessing are reversed around the English text. Set the maxim, translation, and blessing separately.

### Korach 5784

- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Grammar:** "the parsha that Zeidy Weiss had his aufruf, as well as myself" should be **"the parsha of Zeidy Weiss's aufruf, as well as mine."**
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Wordiness:** "two different ideas, both of which are fundamental and I think are worthwhile to give over" should be **"two related, foundational ideas."**
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Source query:** The interpretation that Korach "wanted to take everything for himself" and monopolize the merit of avodah needs attribution.
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Character absolutism:** "the paradigm of someone who was a selfish person and ruins everything for everyone" is colloquial and sweeping. Describe the specific rebellion and its consequence.
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Confusing tradition:** The claim that chassidim pray for Korach, that he is called **"heilige Korach,"** and that he rises from Gehinnom each Rosh Chodesh requires exact sources. Bava Basra 74a describes the swallowed group proclaiming Moshe and his Torah true; it should not be freely merged with later traditions.
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Repetition:** "we see from here a source here" duplicates **"here."**
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Source citation:** Cite Nedarim 39b for the derivation concerning bikur cholim and distinguish a biblical narrative model from an explicit command.
- **Printed p. 228 (archive p. 248), Historical/aggadic qualification:** The statement that Yaakov was the first person ever to become ill before death is an aggadic teaching and should be attributed, not presented as medical history.
- **Printed p. 229 (archive p. 249), Source query:** Verify the claim that the main purpose of visiting the sick is to help the patient do teshuvah. Standard discussions include practical help, prayer, and relieving suffering; this formulation needs the cited work's exact wording.
- **Printed p. 229 (archive p. 249), Theological severity:** The argument says Korach's group was deliberately denied illness so that they could not repent. This is a striking claim and may conflict with traditions about warning and opportunity for teshuvah. Attribute it carefully and avoid stating it as settled fact.
- **Printed p. 229 (archive p. 249), Grammar:** "The highest form of teshuvah **as** R' Avigdor Miller points out" should be **"The highest form of teshuvah, as R' Avigdor Miller points out, is..."**
- **Printed p. 229 (archive p. 249), Trivializing example:** The detailed watermelon-and-pits image distracts from the serious point about repenting during good times. Condense it and cite the original sefer accurately.
- **Printed p. 229 (archive p. 249), Mixed metaphor:** A tzaddik is compared to an overflowing wine cup, then learning and accomplishment are said to spill into "little cups." Identify the source and avoid implying that surrounding people are lesser vessels.
- **Printed p. 229 (archive p. 249), Bidi:** The mishnah, verse, sefer names, and final blessing are directionally unstable. Verify them against the sources before typesetting.

### Korach 5785

- **Printed p. 230 (archive p. 250), Opening syntax:** "topics, two of which are the [Hebrew] and the [Hebrew] in the latter part" is hard to parse because the Hebrew labels reverse. Name the two topics in English after a clean RTL phrase.
- **Printed p. 230 (archive p. 250), Lettered list:** The priestly role is introduced as **"twofold: A)... and B)..."** Apply the book's chosen list style consistently and make the second item grammatically parallel to the first.
- **Printed p. 230 (archive p. 250), Anthropomorphic premise:** "How can we give a gift to the Almighty?" is a homiletic question about *avodas matanah*. Cite Kedushas Levi or the exact R' Levi Yitzchok source rather than paraphrasing the verse as divine need.
- **Printed p. 230 (archive p. 250), Leadership generalization:** "Every member of Klal Yisrael has a responsibility to be a leader... in the broader world" needs a source or more modest wording about influence and responsibility.
- **Printed pp. 230-231 (archive pp. 250-251), Story verification/privacy:** Cite the original account involving R' Aharon Leib Shteinman. Verify whether the family details, planned intermarriage, cigarette use, school episode, and conversations were published with permission.
- **Printed p. 230 (archive p. 250), Stigmatizing phrase:** "a child who went off the derech" and **"the wayward son"** reduce a person to religious status. Use neutral narrative wording.
- **Printed p. 230 (archive p. 250), Quotation punctuation:** The sequence **"Would you like to come to shul? “No”. “Okay”."** has mismatched quotation marks and periods. Standardize all dialogue punctuation.
- **Printed p. 231 (archive p. 251), Typo:** **"I'1I tell you why"** uses numeral ones. It should be **"I'll tell you why."**
- **Printed p. 231 (archive p. 251), Inconsistent nested quotations:** The long childhood recollection alternates curly double and single quotation marks incorrectly. Reformat as a block quotation or use one consistent nesting scheme.
- **Printed p. 231 (archive p. 251), Faulty generalization/ethnic disparagement:** "When a goy does something, he gets paid for the results... By us... we pay for effort" stereotypes non-Jews and falsely makes one educational principle an ethnic divide. Preserve the teaching about valuing effort without contrasting Jews and non-Jews.
- **Printed p. 231 (archive p. 251), Grammar:** "I saw you put **an effort in**" should be **"I saw how much effort you made"** or **"I saw you put in effort."**
- **Printed p. 231 (archive p. 251), Contradictory insult:** A story meant to teach respect ends by calling struggling people **"those who can't make the grade, what we generically call a loser."** Delete **"loser"** and rewrite the conclusion around recognizing effort and dignity.
- **Printed p. 231 (archive p. 251), False dichotomy:** "The leader is not the one who deals with the winner" implies that successful people do not need or deserve leadership. Suggested: **"Leadership includes recognizing and supporting people whose efforts are easily overlooked."**
- **Printed p. 232 (archive p. 252), Source/theological review:** The teaching about elevating *nitzotzos* worldwide and inspiring conversion needs the exact Kedushas Levi citation. Define the kabbalistic terms and avoid presenting a metaphysical mechanism as empirically observable.
- **Printed p. 232 (archive p. 252), Repetition:** "the entire world, the sparks around the entire globe of the world" repeats **"entire/world"** three times.
- **Printed p. 232 (archive p. 252), Pronoun inconsistency:** "a non-Jew... that spark within **them**... that is **his** response" shifts number and gender. Use a consistent singular or plural construction.
- **Printed p. 232 (archive p. 252), Disparaging hierarchy:** Non-Jews and prospective converts are called **"people who are not up to our level."** This should be removed. It is demeaning and especially inappropriate in a paragraph praising conversion.
- **Printed p. 232 (archive p. 252), Invented divine response:** "Hashem will say thank you" is colloquial imagined speech. Use **"the service is described as a gift to Hashem."**
- **Printed p. 232 (archive p. 252), Comma splice:** "that is our gift to Hashem, Hashem looks at us..." needs a period or conjunction.

### Chukas 5784 (1)

- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Bidi:** The opening verse, Rashi, and quoted midrash are broken into left-to-right fragments. Set Numbers 19:2 and Rashi's comment in separate RTL blocks with source and translation.
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Redundant question:** The opening asks three times why the cow must be brought "to Moshe." State the textual question once.
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Source identification:** "Torah Treasures" is not a sufficient citation, and the sefer/acronym quoted after it is unclear. Give author, title, volume, and page.
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Technical concept:** The instruction to intend all the intentions of the אנשי כנסת הגדולה when one does not understand the prayers applies to prayer text, then is transferred to Moshe's intention in preparing the parah adumah. Explain the analogy and cite both sources.
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Historical absolutism:** "Moshe was the only person in the entire history of the world" to know the reason for parah adumah needs the exact midrashic source and qualification; other traditions discuss what was revealed to Moshe and what remains a chok to others.
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Comma splice:** "This particularly harsh decree left many Jews destitute, they had no parnassah" needs a period or conjunction.
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Dangling construction:** "On one such mission, it took him to the town of Bolin" should be **"One such mission took him to the town of Bolin."**
- **Printed p. 233 (archive p. 253), Name/source verification:** Identify the Alter Rebbe by his standard name and verify the identity and location of the Baal Shem Tov's grandson, apparently R' Baruch of Medzhybizh. The present place/name spellings are difficult to confirm.
- **Printed pp. 233-235 (archive pp. 253-255), Folklore versus history:** The extended tale about national ministering angels, Poland's admission of Spanish exiles, a supernatural bargain over the kavannah of *Echad*, and later expulsions is a chassidic story. Frame it explicitly as such and cite the earliest text rather than narrating it as documentary history.
- **Printed p. 234 (archive p. 254), Major historical error:** Jews expelled from Spain in 1492 were accepted in multiple places, especially the Ottoman Empire and North Africa. "Not one country was willing to take them in" is false.
- **Printed p. 234 (archive p. 254), Historical chronology:** Poland had established Jewish communities and legal protections well before 1492. The assertion that a Polish king admitted the Spanish exiles only after a bribe must not be presented as history without evidence.
- **Printed p. 234 (archive p. 254), Unsupported detail:** "a pile of money as high as a mounted Cossack holding a spear" is vivid but implausibly precise. Verify it in the source or omit it.
- **Printed p. 234 (archive p. 254), Loaded metaphysics:** The angel of Poland is said to seek increased **"impure power."** Define the symbolic framework and avoid treating other nations as inherently impure in the editorial voice.
- **Printed p. 234 (archive p. 254), Repetition:** "They built shuls, they built batei midrash..." and repeated statements that they said Shema without proper kavannah can be condensed.
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Melodramatic phrasing:** "The noose was tightened around the throat of European Jewry" is excessive and historically imprecise in a passage already blending legend and chronology.
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Name typo:** **"Rabbi Oelbuam"** should be **"Rabbi Oelbaum."** It appears twice.
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Bidi/quotation:** The three proposed kavannos for *Echad* are visually reversed, with quotation marks landing on the wrong side of the Hebrew. Put them in a numbered RTL list and translate each one.
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Personal-practice query:** The author presents private mystical intentions for the Shema and says readers may follow them. Verify the wording and appropriateness with a competent rav, and distinguish a personal kavannah from normative instruction.
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Transcript challenge:** "You can follow it or not follow it. You can hear what I have to say or not" is defensive oral language. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Grammar:** "That's why it's called **of Moshe Rabbeinu**" should be **"That is why the parah is associated with Moshe Rabbeinu."**
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Overgeneralization:** "all generations will not have the proper kavannah" should be **"later generations would rely, according to this teaching, on Moshe's original intention."**
- **Printed p. 235 (archive p. 255), Weak analogy:** The final comparison between the Baal Shem Tov teaching kavannah for *Echad* and Moshe's intention for parah adumah is asserted rather than explained. Add the shared principle in one explicit sentence.

### Chukas 5784 (2)

- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Transcript opening:** "This is an addendum... Rabbi Frand always has something worthwhile... I thought it would be nice to share" can be reduced to a source line and thesis.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Halachic precision:** Being under the same roof as a corpse is one form of tumas meis, not simply identical in every respect to direct contact. The purification description must also include immersion and the completion of the prescribed process, not only two sprinklings.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Verse summary:** Numbers 19:19 says that the tahor person sprinkles on days three and seven, after which the impure person launders clothes, bathes, and becomes pure in the evening. "Then he goes into the mikvah" is incomplete and potentially assigns the action unclearly.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Source reconstruction:** The Tiferes Yisrael/Mishnah quotation about one pure person sprinkling many impure people and the phrase "storehouse of Yavneh" are too garbled to verify. Give the exact Mishnah, commentator, and Hebrew.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Name/title style:** Identify R' Meir Shapiro once by full name and title; the mixed Hebrew honorifics presently reverse around his name.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Historical nuance:** The Yavneh request is a Talmudic account involving Rabban Yochanan ben Zakkai and Vespasian. Cite Gittin 56b and avoid calling Yavneh simply a remote city chosen to leave "a few talmidei chachamim" alive.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Counterfactual overclaim:** "Had they been wiped out, Torah would have been forgotten" and "literally saved the world of Torah" need attribution. The survival of Torah cannot be reduced historically to one institution without qualification.
- **Printed p. 236 (archive p. 256), Logic:** The source demonstrates the influence of one center or group, but the analogy shifts between one sprinkler, one individual, several sages, and hundreds of recipients. Keep the numerical comparison consistent.
- **Printed pp. 236-237 (archive pp. 256-257), Hyperbole:** "revolutionized the world" appears repeatedly for Daf Yomi and several gedolim. Use specific descriptions of their contributions.
- **Printed p. 237 (archive p. 257), Chronology inconsistency:** "We have seen this in our own lifetime" is followed by the Vilna Gaon and other figures who did not live in the author's lifetime. Change to **"Jewish history offers many examples."**
- **Printed p. 237 (archive p. 257), Unclear list:** Several rabbinic names are printed only as bidi-damaged acronyms. Spell them out so readers can identify whom the paragraph means.
- **Printed p. 237 (archive p. 257), Repetition:** "One person can make a difference" appears twice within a few lines.

### Chukas 5785

- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Date/context required:** The piece says it was recorded during war with Iran and the day after a major American strike. Give the exact date and identify the event in neutral factual language, or remove the time-sensitive opening.
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Political/editorial voice:** "we're all deeply grateful... for that" assumes a uniform political and moral judgment on a military operation. If retained, mark it as the speaker's personal response rather than the book's collective voice.
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Dangerous metaphor:** The verse *zos haTorah, adam ki yamus ba'ohel* is described as requiring a person to be willing to die for Torah. Make clear that this is a metaphor for devotion and self-sacrifice, not encouragement of physical danger or neglect of health.
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Bibliographic verification:** Give the exact title of Zev Wolf Zicherman's sefer, the cited responsum/commentary, and the identity of the authority whose acronym appears repeatedly as unreadable RTL text.
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Source/historical verification:** The story about a son of the Duke of Heidelberg, a non-Jewish friend of the Jews, and the phrase used after his death needs the original responsum and a reliable identification of the people involved.
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Grammar:** "it would preferable to say" should be **"it would be preferable to say."**
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Citation accuracy:** Verify the verse and wording of David's lament for Yonasan, and the second episode quoted as a counterexample. The Hebrew and parenthetical source are reordered and cannot be trusted as typeset.
- **Printed p. 238 (archive p. 258), Halachic framing:** "a halachah we don't follow nowadays" is too vague. State whether this is a rejected opinion, a context-specific wording preference, or an obsolete circumstance, and cite the ruling.
- **Printed p. 239 (archive p. 259), Mistranslation:** The Temple-era miracle in Avos 5:5 is that no person said, **"The place is too cramped for me to lodge in Jerusalem."** "No one ever said it was uncomfortable for them to sleep over" is an imprecise paraphrase.
- **Printed p. 239 (archive p. 259), Derash versus plain meaning:** Reading *tzar li hamakom* as "God caused me pain" is a wordplay on *HaMakom*, not the mishnah's contextual meaning. Label it clearly as a homiletic interpretation.
- **Printed p. 239 (archive p. 259), Verse translation:** Eichah 3:38 is a rhetorical question about good and adversity proceeding from the Most High. It does not literally say **"nothing bad ever truly comes from Hashem."** Supply an accurate translation before the theological application.
- **Printed p. 239 (archive p. 259), Absolutist theodicy:** "Every matter, every action, every molecule" is good, and people in Temple times never perceived pain, is an enormous theological claim. Attribute and soften it, especially in a book containing accounts of suffering.
- **Printed pp. 239-240 (archive pp. 259-260), Likely fabricated or badly garbled story:** The anonymous claim that an IDF officer deployed Iron Dome in Ukraine, observed a 50% rate there versus more than 95% in Israel, and concluded that kollel learning explains the difference requires independent, named documentation. Israel did not simply operate an equivalent Iron Dome system in Ukraine as the story describes. Do not publish this as fact on the strength of an unattributed forwarded story.
- **Printed p. 239 (archive p. 259), Technical inaccuracy:** Iron Dome's published success percentages concern rockets assessed as threatening populated areas, not **all incoming rockets**, and rates vary by period and methodology. Avoid unsupported exact percentages.
- **Printed p. 239 (archive p. 259), Unnecessary detail/privacy:** The 1,500-shekel cart, "high-ranking" officer, neighborhood, Central Command, and Iron Dome installation details make an anonymous story sound sourced without permitting verification. Either identify the source or remove the embellishments.
- **Printed p. 240 (archive p. 260), Causation claim:** Torah learning and prayer may be presented as religious sources of merit, but the story states that they explain a weapon system's measured performance. Frame this as belief, not technical causation.
- **Printed p. 240 (archive p. 260), False substitution:** "That's the real Iron Dome" can read as dismissing the engineers, soldiers, and material system. Suggested: **"The story expresses the belief that spiritual merit accompanies human efforts at defense."**
- **Printed pp. 238-240 (archive pp. 258-260), Bidi:** The verse, lament, halachic phrases, mishnah, and closing blessing all show displaced punctuation. Re-typeset source text independently of the English paragraphs.

### Balak 5784

- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Promotional aside:** "a wonderful sefer which I encourage everyone to get (if it's available)" interrupts the essay. Supply a normal bibliographic citation instead.
- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Grammar:** "Rashi asks why is the Gemara giving this distinction" should be **"Rashi asks why the Gemara singles out Parshas Bilam."**
- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Source identification:** Cite Bava Basra 14b for "Moshe wrote his book, the section of Bilam, and Iyov," then identify the Rashi and *Or Gedalyahu* passage used to explain it.
- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Redundant question:** The puzzle that Moshe wrote every word of Torah, including Bilam, is asked in several forms. State it once.
- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Consent/text nuance:** "Finally Hashem said, 'If they tell you to go, go'" omits the tension between permission and Bilam's motive. Quote Numbers 22:20 accurately and distinguish permission from approval.
- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Technical metaphor:** *Aspaklaria* is translated as "a piece of glass." Use **"lens"** or **"glass through which one sees,"** and distinguish *aspaklaria hame'irah* from *she'einah me'irah* with a source.
- **Printed p. 241 (archive p. 261), Doctrinal precision:** The claim that every prophet's message was colored by the prophet's moral character and that Bilam expected to distort prophecy into a curse requires exact attribution. Do not make the mechanism sound like the prophet could falsify divine speech.
- **Printed p. 242 (archive p. 262), Bracketed editorial intrusion:** "We are [speaking of himself and Aharon] nothing" should be integrated into a grammatical translation rather than leave square-bracket commentary inside the sentence.
- **Printed p. 242 (archive p. 262), Loaded language:** "Bilaam was the lowest of the low" is repetitive invective. Describe the cited moral contrast without editorial piling-on.
- **Printed p. 242 (archive p. 262), Source accuracy:** The teaching that no prophet arose in Israel like Moshe, but among the nations Bilam did, comes from a specific midrash/Sifrei. Cite it and explain in what respect the comparison applies.
- **Printed p. 242 (archive p. 262), Theological query:** The essay says Bilam was temporarily given Moshe's exact level of prophecy, while simultaneously calling his soul a distorting channel. Explain how the source resolves that contradiction.
- **Printed p. 242 (archive p. 262), Major doctrinal/source query:** "Moshe took the prophecies of Avraham, Yitzchak, Yaakov... and transmitted them through his level" is not a conventional description of Torah revelation. It requires precise quotation and rabbinic review before publication.
- **Printed pp. 242-243 (archive pp. 262-263), Misleading authorship language:** Saying Moshe merely **"copied"** the Bilam section from what Bilam had already received can suggest an independent textual source rather than divine dictation. Rephrase according to the cited commentator's actual theological claim.
- **Printed p. 243 (archive p. 263), Repetition:** "perfectly... almost perfectly... it shows" and repeated **"inkling"** should be condensed.
- **Printed p. 243 (archive p. 263), Self-undermining close:** "We don't even know about it" follows several pages claiming to explain the process. Use **"The concept remains beyond our full understanding."**
- **Printed p. 243 (archive p. 263), Broken construction:** "keep on perfecting... that which Hashem gave us as a gift" needs a clear object, likely **"continue refining our character and spiritual receptivity."**

### Balak 5785

- **Printed p. 244 (archive p. 264), Grammar:** "even by the prophet of the goyim" should be **"even through Bilam, a prophet among the nations."**
- **Printed p. 244 (archive p. 264), Faulty correlative:** "not just every blade of grass and not every atom, but every single thing" should be **"not only every blade of grass and every atom, but every aspect of creation."**
- **Printed p. 244 (archive p. 264), Flattery:** "masterfully, as only the [author] can" is promotional rather than editorial. Identify the commentator and present the idea.
- **Printed p. 244 (archive p. 264), Dialogue accuracy:** The retelling compresses Bilam's two delegations and divine responses. Check every quotation against Numbers 22 and do not put paraphrases in quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 244 (archive p. 264), Grammar:** "if you cannot do something small, certainly you cannot do something big" needs the linking clause that turns the obvious statement into a textual question.
- **Printed pp. 244-245 (archive pp. 264-265), Source reconstruction:** The two images for the word placed in Bilam's mouth, a bridle and a hook, need their exact Rashi/midrash text. The present explanation about pulling a horse backward and a fish forward is long, colloquial, and may reverse which image illustrates which control.
- **Printed p. 244 (archive p. 264), Mechanical wording:** "a fish catches it in his mouth" should be **"a fish bites the hook."**
- **Printed p. 245 (archive p. 265), Kabbalistic/source review:** The account of Bilam attempting to pronounce divine names associated with judgment, and God transforming their letters into names of mercy, must be quoted exactly from the source. Divine names should follow the book's reverent typesetting policy.
- **Printed p. 245 (archive p. 265), Graphic embellishment:** "Hashem would chop my tongue off" is not a neutral description of the wordplay. Verify whether this is the source's language; otherwise use **"prevent the utterance."**
- **Printed p. 245 (archive p. 265), Bidi:** The letter transformations are impossible to verify in the current mixed line. Display each intended name and transformed form in a table or separate RTL equations.
- **Printed p. 245 (archive p. 265), Misquotation/application:** Tehillim 130:3 asks who could stand if God preserved a record of sins. "Hashem has to save us from our own aveiros" is an application, not the verse's translation.
- **Printed p. 245 (archive p. 265), Desert language:** "Hashem is constantly giving us what we deserve" sits uneasily beside the cited verse about mercy despite sin. The intended word may be **"need"** rather than **"deserve"**; verify.
- **Printed p. 245 (archive p. 265), Absolute theodicy:** "Hashem... wants to do only good for us" should be attributed to the theological teaching and worded sensitively in light of suffering.

### Chukas-Balak 5783

- **Printed p. 246 (archive p. 266), Transcript setup:** The desert-island book quiz, "What would you suggest?", "99% of us," and "he said nope" preserve a live-room exchange. Replace with a concise statement about the recommended siddur and *Kitzur Shulchan Aruch*.
- **Printed p. 246 (archive p. 266), Patronizing description:** Saying the *Kitzur* is so clear that readers might think "a very simple person wrote it" insults both clarity and ordinary writers. Use **"Its accessible style conceals extraordinary learning."**
- **Printed p. 246 (archive p. 266), Bibliographic verification:** Spell out R' Shlomo Ganzfried's other sefer, apparently *Aperyon*, and the title/name of R' Tzvi Hirsch Heller's work. Acronyms are not enough for readers.
- **Printed p. 246 (archive p. 266), Wrong biblical book:** "I said I would be wise, but it was far from me" is **Koheles 7:23**, not Mishlei 7:23. Correct the book and citation.
- **Printed p. 246 (archive p. 266), Translation/derash:** The midrash's association of Koheles 7:23 with parah adumah should be identified as interpretation, not the verse's explicit subject.
- **Printed pp. 246-247 (archive pp. 266-267), Overstatement:** "Parah adumah is completely beyond human logic" and every other chok has a plausible rationale is a sourced theological position, not an objective taxonomy. Attribute it.
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Duplicated words:** **"the apikorsim or the or Reform Jews"** contains an accidental **"or the or."**
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Uncertain attribution:** "one of my rabbeim told me, I think it was Rav Moshe Tendler" is not publishable attribution. Verify the speaker or describe the example without attaching an uncertain name.
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Group stereotyping:** "the apikorsim or Reform Jews said Moses was a great hygienist" attributes one simplistic argument to entire groups. Identify an actual writer or say **"a modern rationalizing argument claims..."**
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Scientific imprecision:** Trichinosis is not simply "a particular kind of worm that pigs are known to carry," nor does modern husbandry eliminate all religious or health considerations. The argument can be summarized without shaky zoology.
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Hostile quotation:** "May Hashem shut their mouths" is inflammatory. Quote only if indispensable to source fidelity, with context; otherwise omit it.
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Capitalization:** **"mitzvos of the torah"** should be **"mitzvos of the Torah."**
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Repetition:** "whether or not we understand... whether or not there's a reason... even those we think..." can be condensed substantially.
- **Printed p. 247 (archive p. 267), Awkward comparative:** "one of Bilam's not most famous berachos, but a pretty famous berachah" should be **"one of Bilam's less frequently discussed blessings."**
- **Printed pp. 247-248 (archive pp. 267-268), Verse/source accuracy:** Distinguish Bilam's "a people that dwells alone" from Haman's "one people, scattered and dispersed." Give Numbers 23:9 and Esther 3:8 explicitly.
- **Printed p. 248 (archive p. 268), Contradiction:** The page says Jews **"are always unified"** and **"even when living together, they don't pick fights,"** then immediately admits that there has always been machlokes. Replace the idealized factual claim with an aspiration.
- **Printed p. 248 (archive p. 268), Bidi/explanation failure:** The letter-pair derash on ה and נ is unreadable as printed. Explain that letters are paired to total ten or one hundred, leaving ה and נ without partners, then show the pairs in a clear table.
- **Printed p. 248 (archive p. 268), Transcript language:** "Boy, what a brachah we could use," "I tell everybody, look away," and repeated "all the time" should be edited into formal prose.
- **Printed p. 248 (archive p. 268), Vague counsel:** "look away" could imply ignoring wrongdoing. If the lesson is judging favorably and declining needless conflict, say that precisely.

### Pinchas 5783

- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Scribal terminology:** Describe the *vav ketiah* using an authoritative masoretic/halachic source. "Normally any letter... with a split is pasul" is too broad without distinguishing the prescribed form from an accidental break.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Source identification:** Give the exact *Kesef/Chas?* sefer and Gemara Zevachim citation for the 380 kohanim gedolim descended from Pinchas. The titles are difficult to read in the current bidi layout.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Major numerical error:** The First Temple is traditionally described in Yoma 9a as having **eighteen** kohanim gedolim, not **eighty**. The manuscript appears to transpose **18** into **80**.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Historical/aggadic overstatement:** "They died every year on Yom Kippur because they didn't make it through the avodah" is not an accurate summary of Yoma 9a. The passage contrasts long tenures in the First Temple with the rapid turnover of many Second Temple high priests; do not claim each died during the Yom Kippur service.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Unsupported exoneration:** The text says the Second Temple candidates sought the office not from prestige but from zeal for holiness. This contradicts sources that criticize purchase and corruption of the office unless a specific commentator supports the reinterpretation.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Bidi/layout:** The *vav ketiah* transformation and gematria 380 cannot be followed in running prose. Show the altered spelling and arithmetic on separate RTL lines.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Name consistency:** Verify **Rav Kormornick** and the exact title *A Short Vort/The Short Vort*; the book is cited under varying English titles elsewhere.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Redundant question:** "from where do we learn out all of the halachos of making a kinyan?" and the next two sentences repeat the same question. State the *kicha-kicha* derivation once and cite Kiddushin 2a.
