- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Incomplete legal explanation:** Marriage law is not generally derived from a burial's emotional "opposite." The Talmud derives the use of money through the shared word *kicha*. Explain the textual gezeirah shavah accurately.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Dubious currency conversion:** "adjusted properly... four million dollars" needs a method, date, and source. Ancient shekel values cannot be converted to a modern dollar figure with that apparent precision.
- **Printed p. 249 (archive p. 269), Loaded sales language:** "sale of the century," "tiny piece of land," and "got the better deal" turn the purchase of Me'aras Hamachpelah into a comic real-estate bargain. Use a more respectful register.
- **Printed p. 250 (archive p. 270), Negative marriage framing:** "The chassan and kallah know their faults and shortcomings" is a bleak foundation for the analogy. Suggested: **"Each recognizes the other's value and feels fortunate to build a life together."**
- **Printed p. 250 (archive p. 270), Transactional theology:** Repeating **"deal"** and **"best investment"** for a covenantal relationship with Hashem makes worship sound financial. Preserve the reciprocal gratitude without the investment language.
- **Printed p. 250 (archive p. 270), Universal wording:** "which every one of us does, whether man, woman, or child, whether materially or spiritually" needs to explain what concrete act is meant; the phrase "make a deal with Hashem" is too vague.

### Pinchas 5784

- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Awkward opening:** “a number of massive topics, some more well-known than others” is imprecise, and “doesn’t get as much attention or isn’t learned as much” repeats the same idea. Suggested: **“The parashah contains several major themes; one less frequently discussed is Moshe’s request for a successor.”**
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Verse/source layout:** Numbers 27:15-23 and Rashi’s comment are interwoven with English in a way that displaces punctuation and obscures which words belong to which verse. Set the complete Hebrew passages as separate right-to-left blocks and cite the relevant verse and Rashi precisely.
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Source identification:** Give the exact source in the name of the Dubno Maggid/Vilna Gaon for the large becher surrounded by smaller bechers. At present the attribution is too indirect to verify.
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Repetition:** The large-cup/small-cup image is explained several times, including the contrast between overflowing and pouring. State the metaphor once, then move directly to its application.
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Register:** “trickle-down effect” carries modern economic associations that distract from the Torah image. Use **“overflowing influence”** or **“an effect that spreads naturally to others.”**
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Grammar:** “if a person wants to give others, he will not succeed” should be **“if a person wants to give to others...”** Better still, clarify what is being given: knowledge, inspiration, or spiritual influence.
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Source/numerical query:** The familiar Talmudic account names **five** later students through whom R’ Akiva rebuilt Torah after the deaths of his earlier disciples. The manuscript says **seven**; verify whether a different source or list is intended.
- **Printed p. 251 (archive p. 271), Historical overstatement:** “one single person turned Bnei Brak into what Bnei Brak is” attributes a community’s development to the Chazon Ish alone. Cite the statement if it is a quotation, or soften it to acknowledge his exceptional influence without erasing other institutions and leaders.
- **Printed p. 252 (archive p. 272), Bibliographic detail:** Supply the edition/page for the story about the *Bnei Yissaschar*, R’ Tzvi Elimelech of Dinov, and the Chozeh of Lublin. The sefer title and personal names should be consistently transliterated and styled.
- **Printed p. 252 (archive p. 272), Tradition versus genealogy:** The Chozeh’s conclusion that a special yearning for Chanukah indicated a soul-root in Shevet Yissachar is a chassidic tradition, not ordinary genealogical proof. Introduce it explicitly as a reported spiritual insight.
- **Printed p. 252 (archive p. 272), Logic/source query:** The reasoning “I am not a kohen, so I cannot descend from the Chashmonaim” and the subsequent identification with members of the beis din need the original wording. Verify whether the story concerns biological descent, a soul-root, or spiritual affinity.
- **Printed p. 252 (archive p. 272), Overclaim:** “living proof” is inappropriate for an anecdotal chassidic story. Use **“a vivid illustration”** or **“the story illustrates the idea.”**
- **Printed p. 252 (archive p. 272), Hyphenation:** Use **“self-improvement,”** not “self improvement.”
- **Printed pp. 251-252 (archive pp. 271-272), Structural transition:** The first teaching concerns a leader whose inner fullness influences others; the second concerns a personal yearning associated with Chanukah. Add a sentence explaining the connection, rather than presenting them as a single uninterrupted argument.
- **Printed pp. 251-252 (archive pp. 271-272), Bidi:** Hebrew names, quotations, and the book title repeatedly acquire English punctuation on the wrong side. Re-typeset them independently from the surrounding English.

### Pinchas 5785

- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Awkward list:** “the first one, of course...” sounds like a spoken introduction and leaves the reader waiting for a numbered sequence. Either number the teachings clearly or remove the aside.
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Verse/Rashi formatting:** Numbers 27:12-13 and Rashi’s comment should be set in separate RTL blocks. The current mixed line makes it difficult to tell where the verse ends and the explanation begins.
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Interpretive precision:** Rashi says Moshe desired a death like Aharon’s, traditionally understood as *misas neshikah*. Explain that concept rather than vaguely saying he wanted to die “in the same manner.”
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Attribution query:** Verify whether the teaching is from the Chasam Sofer, Ksav Sofer, or another member of that school, then give the exact work and location. The current source name is not clear enough to cite.
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Wrong source/translation:** David’s charge, “I am going the way of all the earth; be strong and be a man,” is **I Kings 2:2**, not Tehillim. “The way of all creatures” is also less accurate than **“the way of all the earth.”**
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Repetitive question:** The question about why David adds the apparently obvious phrase is restated several times. State it once, quote the source accurately, and proceed to the answer.
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Source needed:** The contrast between a human being as a *mehalech* and an angel as an *omed* should be tied to its biblical/rabbinic source, often associated with Zechariah 3:7 and later commentators.
- **Printed p. 253 (archive p. 273), Analogy/source review:** The distinction between a fixed *matzeivah* and an ascending *mizbe’ach*, attributed to R’ Moshe Feinstein, needs the exact source. As phrased, the physical descriptions and spiritual symbolism are too compressed to assess.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Halachic/aggadic nuance:** “After death a person is free from mitzvos” should cite the source and distinguish inability to perform new commandments from posthumous merit generated by one’s children, students, or prior actions.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Source and scope:** *Bara mezakeh abba* is presented as an automatic, unlimited rule “no matter when or where.” Give its source and explain the conditions and limits of the concept rather than promising a guaranteed transfer of merit.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Narrative attribution:** The description of Aharon watching Elazar receive the priestly garments, and being satisfied that his son would continue him, should cite Numbers 20 and the relevant Rashi/midrash. Do not present inferred emotions as explicit biblical narration.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Sensitive wording:** “Moshe did not have a son who was *ra’ui*” can sound like a gratuitous judgment on Gershom and Eliezer. State only what the source says: Moshe requested a successor, and Hashem selected Yehoshua.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Derash labeling:** The claim that “you too” promised Moshe posthumous continuity through Yehoshua is a homiletic reading. Attribute it clearly rather than presenting it as the plain meaning of the verse.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Source needed:** The idea that a student is like a child, and that a teacher thereby continues after death, should cite the relevant rabbinic teaching.
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Hyperbole:** “millions of people whom he was able to teach” needs evidence or should be replaced with **“generations of students and readers.”**
- **Printed p. 254 (archive p. 274), Clumsy repetition:** “grab the opportunity to do the teaching, do the living, and do the mitzvos” sounds improvised. Suggested: **“Use every opportunity to teach, live purposefully, and perform mitzvos.”**
- **Printed p. 255 (archive p. 275), Structural problem:** The “P.S.” about crossing the street reads like an appended speaking note rather than a finished ending. Make it a clearly labeled personal sidebar or integrate it into the essay’s discussion of inherited practice.
- **Printed p. 255 (archive p. 275), Safety/overgeneralization:** The father’s instruction never to run across a street is a personal safety practice, not a universal religious rule. Word it as family guidance and acknowledge that pedestrians must follow local signals and immediate safety conditions.
- **Printed pp. 253-255 (archive pp. 273-275), Bidi:** Biblical quotations and rabbinic phrases have unstable word order and punctuation. Rebuild each Hebrew citation as a complete RTL unit.

### Matos-Maasei 5783

- **Printed pp. 256-257 (archive pp. 276-277), Piece-name consistency:** This heading uses **“Matos-Maasei,”** while neighboring pieces use **“Matos-Massei.”** Choose one transliteration throughout the book.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Calendar qualification:** “Matos and Maasei always come out during the Three Weeks” is too categorical, especially when speaking of the combined reading. Say that these parshiyos **typically** fall during the Three Weeks in the annual cycle.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Theological phrasing:** “compensate for its loss” suggests that added learning mechanically offsets the destruction being mourned. Attribute the idea and explain it more carefully.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Judgmental generalization:** The claim that summer *bein hazmanim* gives everyone spare time that is “frittered away on narishkeit” assumes a uniform schedule and disparages ordinary rest. Focus on the positive opportunity for voluntary learning.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Date/source verification:** “5762/2001” spans two civil years. Give the specific civil date or year of R’ Chaim Kanievsky’s medical episode and cite a reliable biography or firsthand account.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Medical and ethical framing:** A story about studying through a stroke, paralysis, hospitalization, and inability to concentrate can be read as praising neglect of urgent medical recovery. Make explicit that medical care and rest take priority, and present the account only as a biographical report.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Internal tension:** “He could not even concentrate” conflicts with “he never missed one day” and the claim that he completed eight blatt that night. Clarify the chronology and what “never missed” means.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Unqualified superlative:** “He knew Shas by heart” should be attributed to a named source rather than asserted as measurable fact.
- **Printed p. 256 (archive p. 276), Colloquial overgeneralization:** “The beginnings of every parsha have more mazal than the ends,” “that’s just the way it is,” and the contrast between what “everyone” and “almost no one” knows read like live commentary. Rewrite as a measured observation about familiarity.
- **Printed pp. 256-257 (archive pp. 276-277), Source verification:** Check the Masoretic mnemonic for the parashah’s 132 verses and the exact phrase involving Machlah. The current Hebrew is vulnerable to bidi corruption, and the numerical note needs a textual source.
- **Printed p. 257 (archive p. 277), Wordplay versus translation:** The claim that *Machlah chalah* means “Machlah danced” appears to be a wordplay connected with *machol*. Present it as R’ Chaim’s interpretation and cite the original, not as the literal meaning of the form.
- **Printed p. 257 (archive p. 277), Mishnah wording:** Taanis 4:8 speaks of the **daughters of Jerusalem** going out in borrowed white garments and dancing in the vineyards. “Young girls would go out... so that they could make shidduchim” is a loose, colloquial paraphrase; quote or translate the mishnah accurately.
- **Printed p. 257 (archive p. 277), Fawning register:** “Just incredible bekius,” “I was totally blown away,” “this little, little pintzele,” and “what he was” preserve excited oral delivery. Replace with a concise sentence explaining what the example demonstrates.
- **Printed p. 257 (archive p. 277), Word choice:** “duplicate even one little fraction of his work” should be **“emulate even a small measure of his diligence.”**
- **Printed pp. 256-257 (archive pp. 276-277), Bidi:** The mnemonic, name, and Hebrew proof cannot be reliably read in the mixed lines. Place the Hebrew on separate RTL lines and explain the wordplay beneath it.

### Matos-Maasei 5784

- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Verse layout:** Numbers 30:2 and the repeated Hebrew formulas are split by English punctuation. Set the verse, Rashi, and each key phrase as independent RTL blocks.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Structural clarity:** The page announces “two significant ways” in which the passage differs, but the second point becomes a claim about word order. Number the two observations explicitly and separate textual fact from interpretation.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Bibliographic verification:** Give the exact *Oznayim LaTorah* citation for R’ Zalman Sorotzkin’s teaching and confirm the yahrtzeit reference if it is relevant to the printed piece.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Redundancy:** “the pinnacle and highlight” uses two near-synonyms. Choose one.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Derash versus syntax:** The reading that rearranges *leimor, zeh hadavar* into a theological statement is homiletic. Do not imply that it is the only grammatical parsing of the verse.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Unnecessary food list:** “shrimp and bugs, treifos...” is grammatically rough and distractingly vivid. One clear example of a Torah prohibition is sufficient.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Repetition:** “Exactly in the same way” and the comparison between intrinsic and vow-created prohibitions recur several times. Condense the contrast into one paragraph.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Halachic precision:** “If I say *konam alai*, then it becomes assur to me” is incomplete because the formula must identify an object or benefit. Use a valid example and have the passage reviewed by a halachic authority.
- **Printed p. 258 (archive p. 278), Unsupported spiritual claim:** “Basar chazir can be mazik a person’s neshamah” and the proposed equivalence to a vow-created prohibition require a source. Attribute the spiritual comparison rather than stating it as a self-evident mechanism.
- **Printed p. 259 (archive p. 279), Stray punctuation:** “What’s the lesson to be learned from that?)” contains an unmatched closing parenthesis.
- **Printed p. 259 (archive p. 279), Capitalization/house style:** If divine pronouns are capitalized elsewhere, “Hashem in his kindness” should be **“Hashem, in His kindness,”** with commas as well.
- **Printed p. 259 (archive p. 279), Source/logic query:** The claim that Moshe’s designation of the tribal heads made them holy enough to understand the power of speech needs an exact source and a clearer causal explanation.
- **Printed p. 259 (archive p. 279), Halachic review required:** The statements that one grain can become terumah, that a non-kohen who eats it is categorically liable to death, and that saying *harei zu olah* transforms an animal into a korban omit essential legal conditions. These examples should be rewritten and checked by a competent halachic reviewer.
- **Printed p. 259 (archive p. 279), Overstatement:** “change the entire world from the lowest to the highest” is inflated. State the narrower point that speech can alter an object’s halachic status.
- **Printed p. 259 (archive p. 279), Cliché/translationese:** “respectful of the power of the tongue” is awkward. Suggested: **“The passage calls for disciplined, responsible speech.”**
- **Printed pp. 258-259 (archive pp. 278-279), Bidi:** The vow formula, terumah example, and korban designation are not visually dependable in running English. Re-typeset and proof each formula independently.

### Matos-Massei 5785

- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Piece-name consistency:** This heading changes from **“Matos-Maasei”** to **“Matos-Massei.”** Standardize the transliteration book-wide.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Statistical claim:** “the greatest number of pesukim in any one...” is incomplete and should be verified against the intended comparison, such as longest combined weekly reading by verse count.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Reader assumption:** “the section with which we are all familiar” excludes readers and adds nothing. Name the cities-of-refuge passage directly.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Legal summary:** Use **“a person who killed unintentionally”** rather than “people who killed b’shogeg,” and distinguish arrest, trial, exile, and the protection offered within an ir miklat.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Incomplete law:** If the exile died before the kohen gadol, he was buried in the city of refuge, but his bones could later be returned after the kohen gadol’s death. Verify Makkos 11b; “he was never taken home” is too absolute.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Scope of protection:** “The goel had no authority” is true only within the protected area and under the applicable rules. Clarify the legal boundaries rather than implying that the avenger had no role anywhere.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Source wording:** Cite Makkos 11a for the mothers of the kohanim gedolim providing food and clothing so the exiles would not pray for the high priest’s death. Distinguish the Mishnah’s wording from the Gemara’s explanations and textual variants.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Moral/legal overstatement:** The teaching that the kohen gadol’s prayer should have prevented accidental death concerns spiritual communal responsibility; it does not make him legally or personally “at fault” for every tragedy. Attribute and qualify it.
- **Printed p. 260 (archive p. 280), Loaded causation:** Saying an ordinary person’s prayer can “make or break” the kohen gadol’s life, and that his mother had to “bribe” the exile, sensationalizes an aggadic teaching. Use the source’s sober language.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Missing identification:** Identify the speaker who compared the yeshivah’s wartime prayers with the kohen gadol’s prayer in the Holy of Holies, and give the original source. The present biographical clues are insufficient.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Theological attribution:** The claimed equivalence between a yeshivah’s prayer and the kohen gadol’s Yom Kippur prayer should be presented as that speaker’s exhortation, not an objective measurement.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Date punctuation:** “June 12,2025” should be **“June 12, 2025.”**
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Grammar:** “At that night” should be **“That night.”**
- **Printed pp. 261-262 (archive pp. 281-282), Source/permission:** The Y. Y. Jacobson account about a musician, a bar concert in Poughkeepsie, a dream involving Rabbi Leibel Groner, and an email from a woman in the audience needs a traceable publication, recording, date, and permission where appropriate. Identify the musician and correspondent, or state clearly that they are anonymous.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Supernatural claim:** A dream in which a deceased rabbi allegedly explains the military effect of a woman’s candle lighting is a reported dream, not independently verifiable fact. Mark it as such at every stage of the story.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Naming/style:** “the last Lubavitcher Rebbe” is an awkward designation. Use the person’s accepted title/name and explain Rabbi Groner’s relationship to him if relevant.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Hyphenation:** Use **“longtime,”** not “long-time,” when it appears before a noun in this context, subject to house style.
- **Printed p. 261 (archive p. 281), Unsupported causation:** The narrative says one mitzvah directly fortified the Israeli Air Force’s protection and success. Frame this as the storyteller’s spiritual interpretation, not a demonstrated causal fact.
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Quotation punctuation:** In “I was at the place where he performed”, she wrote, the comma belongs inside the closing quotation mark under standard American style: **“performed,” she wrote.**
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Hyperbole:** “Every mitzvah reverberates through the destiny of humanity” is grandiose. Attribute it or use a more focused conclusion about unseen influence.
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Military-source verification:** The report of a jet over Iran developing a fuel problem, receiving tanker support, and avoiding an emergency landing needs a reliable, named IDF or journalistic source. “Jettison over Iran” is almost certainly the wrong verb; determine whether the intended action was ejecting, diverting, or making an emergency landing.
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Absolute factual claim:** “Every single plane came back” requires the exact operation, date, and official source.
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Causal overclaim:** “This lady did it. All our tefillos did it” erases the pilots, planners, maintainers, and technical response while asserting an unverifiable spiritual cause. Suggested: **“The storyteller understood the safe return as accompanied by the merit of mitzvos and prayer.”**
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Technical imprecision:** Prayer does not keep “Iron Dome moving”; Iron Dome is a specific ground-based air-defense system and is unrelated to the described aircraft refueling incident. Use a general phrase such as **“Israel’s defenses”** only if that is the intended metaphor.
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Distorted source:** The kohen gadol’s Yom Kippur prayer concerning travelers and rain, discussed in Yoma 53b, does not establish that his prayer mechanically “knocks off” another Jew’s prayer. Quote the source and explain the tension without a power contest metaphor.
- **Printed p. 262 (archive p. 282), Hyperbole/caricature:** “the greatest person in the world, in the greatest place, at the greatest time” and the imagined traveler complaining, “Why are You making it rain? I’m getting wet,” turn a nuanced teaching into parody. Replace with a respectful, sourced explanation.
- **Printed pp. 260-262 (archive pp. 280-282), Bidi:** Talmudic phrases and prayers show punctuation and word-order problems. Rebuild each source quotation in RTL and translate it separately.

### Devarim 5783

- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Awkward phrasing:** “words of tochacha in an interesting, diplomatic lashon” should be recast as **“rebuke conveyed indirectly and with sensitivity.”**
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Chronology/source:** Verify the statement that Moshe began this address five weeks before his death and cite the calculation or traditional source.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Source identification:** Give Rav Guttman’s full name, the exact *Torah Tavlin* volume/issue, and the original source of the teaching.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Overgeneralization:** “each and every one knows exactly what tremendous aveiros he has” presumes a uniform inner state. Suggested: **“Each person can identify areas requiring honest self-examination.”**
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Halachic review required:** The obligation to rebuke, and the rule not to say what will not be heard, are highly nuanced. “Not giving tochacha is an even bigger aveirah unless one can be sure the words will be accepted” may reverse or oversimplify the relevant sources. Cite Yevamos 65b and applicable halachic authorities, then have the paragraph reviewed.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Inference presented as fact:** Tanach does not explicitly say Avraham withheld rebuke from Sodom because the residents would mock him. Attribute this reading and distinguish it from the narrative itself.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Verse citation:** Cite Mishlei 9:8 accurately: “Do not rebuke a scoffer, lest he hate you; rebuke a wise person, and he will love you.”
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Speculative psychology:** “Avraham did not allow himself to be mocked because it would take away his credibility” attributes a strategic motive not stated in the biblical text. Present it only as the cited commentator’s explanation.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Generalization:** “They will get the message” about Jews receiving rebuke needs qualification; reactions vary, and the preceding halachic discussion emphasizes that very point.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Time-sensitive prose:** “right now, during the Three Weeks” dates the transcript. Replace with wording that remains accurate whenever the book is read.
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Anthropomorphic intensity:** “Hashem is begging and pleading” may be intended rhetorically, but it should be tied to a source or softened to **“the prophetic message urgently calls us to return.”**
- **Printed p. 263 (archive p. 283), Bidi:** The opening verse references and rabbinic phrases should be set separately in RTL.

### Devarim 5784

- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Personal aside:** The birthday/leining comment reads like a live dedication. Move it to a prefatory note or remove it from the edited essay.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Citation/layout:** Give Isaiah 1:27 explicitly and set *Tziyon b’mishpat tipadeh v’shaveha bitzedakah* as a complete RTL quotation with an accurate translation.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Anthropomorphic simplification:** “The Beis Hamikdash did not do any aveirah” is a childlike way to state the contrast. Suggested: **“The physical sanctuary is not morally culpable; the people’s return requires moral repair.”**
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Promise versus exhortation:** “If we do teshuvah, Hashem will return the people and the Temple” should be tied to the cited teaching and framed as a religious hope, not a simple predictive guarantee.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Overhype:** “a fascinating problem” is unnecessary. State the difference between the liturgical formulations directly.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Nusach verification:** Check the exact wording of *Boneh Yerushalayim* in bentching and the relevant phrases in Shemoneh Esreh across the minhagim being discussed. Do not imply one universal text where liturgical variants exist.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Identification/source:** Spell out the Vilna Gaon/Gra on first mention and cite the source for omitting or retaining *berachamav*. “No less than the Gaon himself” is rhetorical rather than informative.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Citation check:** The prophecy *shavti liYerushalayim berachamim* appears to be Zechariah 1:16. Verify and print the source, rather than leaving a garbled parenthetical reference.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Derash labeling:** The contrast between rebuilding the Temple through *mishpat* and returning to Jerusalem through *rachamim* is a homiletic synthesis of several texts. Do not present it as the only literal meaning of Isaiah or the prayers.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Awkward ethical categories:** “all our dealings... between man and friend, even between man and place” should use standard terms, for example **“both interpersonal obligations and duties toward Hashem.”**
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Repetition:** *Tzedakah*, *mishpat*, *rachamim*, teshuvah, and tefillah are restated several times in the closing without adding distinctions. Tighten the conclusion.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Seasonal dating:** “We’re heading into Elul, into the new year” is tied to the original delivery date. Either identify the date in a heading or make the sentence evergreen.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Untranslated close:** Translate the final Yiddish blessing or provide an English gloss.
- **Printed p. 264 (archive p. 284), Bidi:** The Isaiah verse, liturgical formulas, and prophetic quotations need independent RTL typesetting and proofing.

### Devarim 5785

- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Redundancy:** “each and every year” should be **“each year.”**
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Chronology:** “Moshe’s final speech lasted many days” needs a specific textual basis and clearer date range.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Vague euphemism:** “the opposite... consequences” obscures the actual covenantal warnings being discussed. Name the contrast accurately and sensitively.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Doctrinal/source review:** The statement that whatever Moshe says must be fulfilled by Hashem because of Moshe’s righteousness requires the exact midrash and careful wording. It should not imply that a human compels God.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Verse sequence:** The appointment of judges appears in Deuteronomy 1:9-15, while the quoted criteria are in 1:13. Do not collapse Moshe’s burden, the people’s selection, and God’s instruction into one quotation.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Unsupported characterization:** Calling the delegation of leadership a “compromise” or “defeat” is not the plain sense of the passage. Attribute that question to a commentator or remove the judgment.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Garbled argument:** The move from the word *anashim* to the episode of infant Moshe refusing an Egyptian nurse, and then to the claim that every Jewish child can become Moshe Rabbeinu, lacks a visible logical chain. Reconstruct the source and explain each step.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Source citation:** Moshe’s refusal to nurse from Egyptian women and the explanation that a mouth destined to speak with the Shechinah should not nurse from them appears in Sotah 12b. Cite and translate it accurately.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Essentializing claim:** “Through holiness in a mother’s milk as well as a mother’s Torah” makes a biological mother the exclusive source of a child’s spiritual potential and may alienate adoptive families and other caregivers. Preserve the source’s idea without universalizing it.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Hyperbole:** “become a Moshe Rabbeinu” is much stronger than the likely lesson that each child has extraordinary potential for Torah and leadership.
- **Printed pp. 265-266 (archive pp. 285-286), Bibliographic verification:** Give the exact source for the R’ Leib Chasman/Chafetz Chaim/Shalom Aleichem story, including whether it appears in *Ohr Yahel* or in a report by R’ Sholom Schwadron. Verify every name and institutional affiliation.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Comma splice:** The sentence explaining the Chafetz Chaim’s sensitivity joins independent clauses with a comma. Divide it into two sentences or use a conjunction.
- **Printed p. 265 (archive p. 285), Quotation/source:** “The malachim are not hungry; they can wait” is memorable but should be identified as the story’s reported wording, not an authenticated direct quotation unless the source supports that form.
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Loaded label:** “How far can we change customs without being a ‘reformer’?” uses a denominational label as a pejorative. Frame the substantive question as the relationship among halachah, minhag, and sensitivity to a guest.
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Broad disparagement:** “The Reform Jews and all the Hellenists and all people throughout history wanted to change everything...” turns diverse historical movements and people into one hostile caricature. Remove the group attack and discuss the specific principle at issue.
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Strawman:** “wanted to change everything to make it better for us” is an unsupported account of entire groups’ motives. It does not belong in an edited teaching without a defined historical claim and source.
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Halachic nuance:** The difference between changing a minhag for a hungry guest and altering halachah cannot be reduced to “that’s what makes a gadol.” Explain what was discretionary in the case and cite an authority.
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Transcript/self-undermining aside:** “I wouldn’t do it; I would give the guest a nosh” shifts from the exemplar to the speaker’s improvisation and weakens the point. Remove or clearly label it as personal reflection.
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Guarantee:** “we can all become gedolim” should be **“we can all grow in judgment and sensitivity.”**
- **Printed p. 266 (archive p. 286), Casual metaphor:** “Moshe did not have a monopoly on leadership” is modern business language. Use **“leadership was not to remain concentrated in one person.”**
- **Printed pp. 265-266 (archive pp. 285-286), Bidi:** The verse, Talmudic passage, and liturgical phrases need complete RTL reconstruction.

### Vaeschanan 5783

- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Congregational generalization:** The claim that people bid large sums for the Aseres Hadibros and Shema aliyos may describe one community but is not universal. Identify it as a local custom or remove the monetary aside.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Source/citation:** Cite the rabbinic source for *zachor veshamor bedibur echad* and distinguish the wording in Exodus 20:8 from Deuteronomy 5:12.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Theological overstatement/joke:** “Only Hashem can say two things at the same time. Nobody else can,” followed by “talking out both sides of one’s mouth,” turns a teaching about simultaneous revelation into a joke about dishonesty. Remove the joke and state the source precisely.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Bibliographic verification:** Give the exact R’ Yaakov Kamenetsky story source and the full title of R’ Peretz Steinberg’s sefer.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Textual oversimplification:** Asking whether the luchos contained *zachor* or *shamor* requires care: the Torah records different formulations in the two Decalogues, while tradition teaches they were uttered together. Do not imply that synagogue depictions settle the textual question.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Liturgical proof:** Identify the exact Shabbos-morning line from which the child inferred *shemiras Shabbos* and verify its wording. The current retelling assumes readers know the prayer.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Diminutive register:** “little fellows” and *kinderlach* may suit oral delivery but feel patronizing in edited prose. Use **“children”** unless the affectionate tone is intentional throughout.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Prediction/source:** The claim that R’ Yaakov predicted the child would become a major talmid chacham should be tied to the story’s precise source and outcome, if known.
- **Printed p. 267 (archive p. 287), Mixed metaphor:** Material possessions are dismissed as not truly “precious,” then Shabbos is called a prized “possession.” Say instead that Shabbos is a sacred trust to be guarded.
- **Printed pp. 267-268 (archive pp. 287-288), Repetition:** Guard, keep, take care of, and watch are repeated with little distinction. Consolidate the moral into one focused paragraph.
- **Printed pp. 267-268 (archive pp. 287-288), Halachic review required:** The ruling about adding raw scallions to hot chicken soup depends on temperature, vessel, whether the ingredient is previously cooked, the category of *kalei habishul*, and communal practice. Cite Rabbi Oelbaum’s exact psak and do not publish a universal “not allowed” without full conditions.
- **Printed p. 268 (archive p. 288), Terminology:** Explain *kli shlishi* for general readers and standardize its transliteration.
- **Printed p. 268 (archive p. 288), Anecdote versus authority:** “Now I don’t do it” records the speaker’s practice but does not establish the law. Keep personal practice separate from the legal ruling.
- **Printed p. 268 (archive p. 288), Cliché:** “The things that are little down here are big up there, and the things that are big down here are nothing” is sweeping and unsourced. Tie the conclusion specifically to careful Shabbos observance.
- **Printed pp. 267-268 (archive pp. 287-288), Bidi:** *Zachor*, *shamor*, the prayer text, and halachic terms need separate RTL/italic treatment rather than mixed-direction running lines.

### Vaeschanan 5784

- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Bibliographic verification:** Give the exact sefer, page, and source chain for the teaching attributed through R’ Chaim Leibish Rottenberg and the Belzer Rebbe. The current oral attribution cannot be independently checked.
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Source citation:** The tradition that Moshe prayed 515 times, based on the gematria of *Vaeschanan*, should cite its standard midrashic source, often Devarim Rabbah 11:10.
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Extraordinary claim:** The description of the Temple as corresponding to four “walls” built by Hashem and the patriarchs, with Moshe as the “roof,” is a homiletic structure, not architecture or history. Quote and attribute the exact midrash/commentator.
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Casual anthropomorphism:** “Hashem does not need a contractor to build” is jokey and unnecessary. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Vague citation:** “That’s what the medrash says” is not a citation. Name the work, section, and wording.
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Biblical references:** Verify and print the verses behind Avraham’s *har* (Genesis 22:14), Yitzchak’s *sadeh* (Genesis 24:63), and Yaakov’s *bayis* (Genesis 28:17).
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Explain the arithmetic:** The reader needs to see the base value 103 and why *har*, *sadeh*, *bayis*, and *Vaeschanan* are treated as successive multiples. A small table would be clearer than running prose.
- **Printed p. 269 (archive p. 289), Derash labeling:** Calling Moshe’s 515 prayers the “fifth multiple” and therefore the roof is numerological interpretation. Present it as the cited author’s derash, not a necessary meaning of the gematria.
- **Printed p. 270 (archive p. 290), Structural inconsistency:** The piece first treats Moshe’s contribution as the roof, then adds peace as the foundation. Explain how the two extensions fit the original four-wall model rather than simply attaching another building part.
- **Printed p. 270 (archive p. 290), Gematria correction/clarity:** *Ish* equals 311 and *ishah* equals 306, totaling **617**. The stated **618** is reached only by adding the *kolel* value of one. Say that explicitly and explain why that convention is being used.
- **Printed p. 270 (archive p. 290), Source verification:** Verify the divine-name value of 103 and the intended sixfold calculation before publication. Divine names also require the book’s chosen reverent typesetting policy.
- **Printed p. 270 (archive p. 290), Talmudic precision:** Sotah 17a teaches that if husband and wife merit, the Shechinah is between them; if they do not, fire consumes them. It is not simply a generic claim that “if there is no peace, they lose the Shechinah.” Quote and explain the letter-play accurately.
- **Printed p. 270 (archive p. 290), Conclusion word choice:** Check whether the final promise of *nachas* follows from the source; it appears to be a generic closing rather than the result established by the derash.
- **Printed pp. 269-270 (archive pp. 289-290), Bidi/design:** The gematrios are nearly impossible to audit in mixed-direction prose. Present every Hebrew term, numerical value, multiplication, and total in a clean table with RTL text in its own cells.

### Vaeschanan 5785

- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Major citation error:** The verse *vihyisem nekiyim meHashem umiYisrael* is Numbers **32:22**, not “Matos 22:2.” Correct both the English citation and the bidi-damaged parenthetical.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Redundancy:** “blameless and spotless” gives two English equivalents for *nekiyim* without explaining a distinction. Choose one, or translate the verse conventionally and discuss reputation separately.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Brand/name check:** “in the Torah Anytimes” should likely be **“on TorahAnytime.”** Verify R’ Moshe Donnebaum’s spelling and provide the recording title/date.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Sweeping judgment:** “almost no one is mekayem the pasuk” is impossible to substantiate and needlessly condemns the readership. Suggested: **“The demand to be above suspicion before both God and people is exceptionally difficult.”**
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Argument from silence:** The fact that Torah does not record Aharon praying to enter Eretz Yisrael does not by itself prove that he never prayed. Present the conclusion only as the *Megaleh Amukos*’s interpretation.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Source/doctrinal query:** The suggestion that Aharon refrained because people might accuse him of seeking *matnos kehunah* is striking and potentially disparaging. Quote the source exactly, explain which priestly gifts are meant, and avoid adding speculative motive beyond it.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Abrupt subject change:** The piece moves from Aharon’s unrecorded prayer to Moshe’s 515 prayers without a transition. Add a sentence contrasting their situations.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Inflated metaphor:** “Moshe Rabbeinu’s torrent of tefillos” is melodramatic. Use **“Moshe’s repeated prayers.”**
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Source precision:** Identify the Gemara/midrashic dispute concerning when Moshe prayed and cite Rashi on Numbers 31:5 for the Israelites being delivered to the army against their will because Moshe’s death depended on the Midian campaign.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Wording:** “refused to send out the soldiers” sounds like organized insubordination. Use the source’s more precise description that the men had to be handed over or conscripted despite their reluctance.
- **Printed pp. 271-272 (archive pp. 291-292), Source citation:** Cite Avos 2:16 for *lo alecha hamelachah ligmor* and identify the collection that supplies the day-laborer parable.
- **Printed p. 271 (archive p. 291), Demeaning aside:** “one of the workers, who was not terribly bright” is unnecessary. The mistaken reasoning can stand without insulting the character.
- **Printed p. 272 (archive p. 292), Biographical clutter:** “the Satmer Rebbe of my youth,” “many of you may not have heard,” “a bit more familiar,” and the running explanation of acronyms preserve oral delivery. Introduce R’ Yekusiel Yehuda Teitelbaum, the Bach, and the Taz once, with full names and works.
- **Printed pp. 272-273 (archive pp. 292-293), Story-source verification:** The heavenly prosecution, the young messenger, the manuscript of *Bayis Chadash*, and the prediction that returning it would lead to the Bach’s death require a traceable source. Label every supernatural element as part of the reported story.
- **Printed p. 272 (archive p. 292), Legal slogan:** *Din perutah kedin me’ah* means that a small monetary case requires the same judicial seriousness as a large one; “there’s no such thing by frum Yidden as a small claims court” is inaccurate and imports a modern institutional term.
- **Printed p. 272 (archive p. 292), Currency ambiguity:** The dispute is described first as three versus ten *zehuvim* and then as an abstract “10 versus 3.” Retain one clear unit and explain it only if necessary.
- **Printed pp. 272-273 (archive pp. 292-293), Medical-safety concern:** The Yetev Lev’s reported refusal of an expert physician because he felt his time had come can be dangerous if read normatively. State explicitly that the anecdote is not guidance to refuse appropriate medical care.
- **Printed p. 273 (archive p. 293), Awkward wording:** “give up my life and my nefesh” duplicates the same idea. Translate or transliterate once.
- **Printed p. 273 (archive p. 293), Life-denying formulation:** “Our purpose in life is not to spend time down here” can sound as though embodied life has no value. Suggested: **“Our purpose is not merely to prolong life, but to use it in service of our mission.”**
- **Printed p. 273 (archive p. 293), Invented divine dialogue:** “You have fulfilled your tafkid... now is the time for you to go to Olam Haba” is an interpretive paraphrase, not a biblical quotation. Remove quotation marks or cite a source.
- **Printed p. 273 (archive p. 293), Theological guarantee:** Doing one’s role does not permit the writer to guarantee Olam Haba or claim that readers thereby make Moshe’s neshamah “infinite.” Attribute and soften the conclusion.
- **Printed pp. 271-273 (archive pp. 291-293), Bidi:** Nearly every source line mixes Hebrew, English, acronyms, and punctuation. Rebuild the verses, names, book titles, and rabbinic phrases as discrete RTL units.

### Eikev 5783

- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Generalization/gender:** “man thinks he can control everything” should be **“people can come to believe that technology gives them complete control.”**
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Verse translation:** Genesis 1:28 does not simply command humanity “to control and run the world.” Translate *kivshuha* accurately and discuss dominion together with responsibility/stewardship.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Citation:** Identify Deuteronomy 8:17 explicitly for the warning, “My strength and the power of my hand made me this wealth.”
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Tone/source:** The Titus-and-gnat episode should cite Gittin 56b. “Oh yeah? Get onto land...” is an invented, flippant divine quotation and should be replaced with a respectful paraphrase.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Pandemic theology:** “Hashem sends a tiny little virus called Covid” assigns direct causation and purpose to a mass-casualty event without attribution. It is also time-bound and potentially painful. Frame it, if retained, as the speaker’s reflection on human vulnerability.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Sweeping cultural claim:** “everything belongs to us and we believe we’re in charge of everything” caricatures an entire culture. Narrow the statement to the temptation toward self-sufficiency.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Wrong idiom:** “limit our vista” should be **“recognize the limits of our control”** or **“adjust our perspective.”**
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Internal contradiction:** “With Your help, I can do anything” repeats the limitless-control claim the paragraph is trying to reject. Use **“I can make responsible efforts while recognizing that outcomes are not wholly mine to control.”**
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Passage identification:** Name Deuteronomy 11:13-21 as the second paragraph of Shema and give the exact verse under discussion.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Grammar/source:** The claim that the verse “would be more grammatically correct” without *es* should be attributed to a commentator, not presented as a correction to Torah grammar.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Excessive praise:** “our teacher, our guide, our ultimate all-time rebbi” before Rashi is slangy and repetitive. **“Rashi explains”** is sufficient.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Scientific wording:** Desalination does not “make water out of saline water” in a magical sense; it removes salt from seawater or brackish water. The modern example also needs no national aside to make the point.
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Derash labeling:** Reading *es* as spanning alef through tav and therefore all reality is homiletic. Cite and label it rather than saying the word literally “represents everything.”
- **Printed p. 274 (archive p. 294), Repeated domination language:** “control the world, conquer the world and take care of it” combines ethically different ideas. Choose language of cultivating and caring for creation.
- **Printed pp. 274-275 (archive pp. 294-295), Bidi:** The Genesis, Deuteronomy, and Shema quotations are not reliably punctuated in their mixed lines. Re-typeset and translate each separately.

### Eikev 5784

- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Page-number production check:** The OCR sees the printed number as “216.” Visually verify that the book consistently prints **276** and that no source file carries a mistaken folio number.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Terminology:** Introduce *shivah denechemta* as the seven haftaros of consolation after Tisha B’Av; the phrase “the first pesukim... are a dialogue” needs a source or a more limited description.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Citation:** Identify Isaiah 49:14 for Zion’s cry and Isaiah 51:2 for the call to look to Avraham and Sarah.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Translation:** “who started you off, the foundation” is not a clean translation of the Hebrew. Supply a faithful translation first, then the homiletic application.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Bibliographic reconstruction:** The sefer names, R’ Aharon ben Chaim of Izmir, and the quoted author are difficult to recover from the mixed-direction line. Give full titles, names, and locations.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Rule/source verification:** The proposed rule that every biblical *ein lah* means “she lacks it now but will have it later” is very broad. Verify every example and present it as the commentator’s pattern, not a universal rule of Hebrew.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Numbering/layout:** The three examples are jammed into running bilingual lines and cannot be audited. Use a numbered table with verse, phrase, later fulfillment, and citation.
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Colloquial translation:** “I benched them” should be **“I blessed them.”**
- **Printed p. 276 (archive p. 296), Comparative suffering:** “We are in an even more bitter situation” invites an unhelpful competition between generations’ suffering. State the contemporary feeling of hopelessness without ranking it.
- **Printed p. 277 (archive p. 297), Dated/current-events insert:** The Iranian-government announcement and predicted attack need an exact date and reliable source. Because the threatened date has passed, revise the piece retrospectively rather than preserving a live prediction.
- **Printed p. 277 (archive p. 297), Combative transcript language:** “I immediately fired back to anyone who got my text message” is not polished prose. Use **“I responded with the analogy...”**
- **Printed p. 277 (archive p. 297), Analogy needs explanation:** Haman’s choice of Adar is linked to Moshe’s death and birth; the manuscript jumps from Tisha B’Av to 7 Adar without making that analogy explicit.
- **Printed p. 277 (archive p. 297), Failed-prophecy risk:** “Tisha B’Av is going to become the biggest day of yeshuah” was expressed as hope before the date. It should not remain as a factual prediction after publication without an editorial note on the outcome.
- **Printed p. 277 (archive p. 297), Transcript residue:** “When you hear this podcast, this talk...” should be removed or converted into a dated author’s note.
- **Printed pp. 276-277 (archive pp. 296-297), Bidi:** The haftarah verses and all three prooftexts need independent RTL composition.

### Eikev 5785

- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Verse citation:** Verify that the Malachi quotation is 3:6, *Ki ani Hashem lo shanisi*, and print the Hebrew and English cleanly.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Logical overreach:** Mathematics being expressed through divine-name gematrios does not make mathematical truths temporary, bendable, or less “black and white.” Separate the mystical symbolism of numbers from the validity of arithmetic.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Kabbalistic attribution:** The claims that all calculations are permutations of divine names and every Torah verse is a permutation of God’s Name require exact Zohar/commentary citations and careful theological wording.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Gematria clarity:** *Cheshbon* equals 366, as does the phrase *shem Havayah* (340 + 26), not the four-letter name by itself. Show the arithmetic explicitly and follow a reverent divine-name policy.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Bracketed digression:** The medical-journal-cover/genizah anecdote interrupts the argument and remains inside square brackets. Move it to a note or omit it.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Halachic/source review:** Whether a journal cover bearing a printed divine name requires genizah depends on the exact text and printing. Identify what appeared, cite the applicable ruling, and avoid implying a universal obligation based on the anecdote alone.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Bibliographic opacity:** The several sefarim and authors in the paragraph are unreadable in transliteration and RTL. Supply full names, titles, dates, and page references.
- **Printed p. 278 (archive p. 298), Numerical verification:** The counts of 1,820 appearances of the divine name in Torah and 93 in Eikev must be checked against a defined Masoretic text and counting method. Manual anecdote is not enough for a printed statistic.
- **Printed pp. 278-279 (archive pp. 298-299), Game-show register:** “my batting average is 100%,” “no one has gotten it right,” and “I’ve asked some pretty... people” turn source study into a quiz. Condense to the verified result.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Dating:** “I am 77 years old” fixes the piece to one year and will become inaccurate in a collected volume. Remove or date the original delivery.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Verse translation:** Deuteronomy 7:15 says God will remove illness from Israel and place it upon those who hate them; it does not say “all of the illnesses of the world.” Translate accurately.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Derash versus lexicography:** The distinction that *simah* is temporary while *nesinah* is permanent is a commentator’s homiletic pattern, not a dependable rule of Biblical Hebrew. Attribute it and address counterexamples.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Source/citation:** Give exact references for the verses about destroying idolatry, the Mishkan, rain, Torah, and divorce rather than stacking uncited Hebrew fragments.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Legal overstatement:** A get does not prove that every *nesinah* is eternally irreversible; divorce can be conditional, invalid, or followed by remarriage. Have the legal example checked before using it as linguistic evidence.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Unsupported universal:** “When Hashem gives us something, we must appreciate that it is forever” does not follow from the selected examples and is contradicted by many temporary gifts or conditions.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Major halachic wording problem:** “I’m giving you My name; use it, hear it, say it” can be read as permission to pronounce the Tetragrammaton. That is not acceptable practical guidance. Rephrase in terms of noticing, studying, and revering the divine name, and obtain rabbinic review.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Unclear original theory:** The association between Eikev’s alleged 93 occurrences, the consolation haftaros, and the divine attribute of mercy is the speaker’s own speculation. Mark it clearly and do not put invented words in God’s mouth.
- **Printed p. 279 (archive p. 299), Transcript jokes:** “He didn’t have that in his sefer, I thought of that myself,” “I listen to everybody’s opinion including my own,” and “sometimes I’m right” should be removed from an edited essay.
- **Printed p. 280 (archive p. 300), Orphaned close/layout:** The final blessing occupies only a few lines on an otherwise blank page. Pull it back to p. 279 if possible, or rebalance the preceding pages.
- **Printed pp. 278-280 (archive pp. 298-300), Bidi/design:** The divine-name calculations and quoted distinctions require a table; the current running text is particularly high-risk for reversal and accidental irreverent display.

### Re’eh 5783

- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Opening joke/source:** The claim that “everything in life needs mazal,” including a rolled Torah scroll because the person performing *pesichah* chooses one, is an oral joke. Either source it and explain the custom or remove the setup.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Capitalization:** Use **“Sifrei Torah,” “Torah,”** and **“Aron Kodesh”** consistently; several instances appear in lowercase.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Anthropomorphism:** “every Torah wants to be used for leining” is whimsical. It should not be presented as a factual explanation of *mazal*.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Untranslated Yiddish:** “a velt of Torah” needs an English equivalent, such as **“a world of interpretation.”**
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Audience-specific role:** “in my medical practice, and even in my ruchniyus practice” is unclear. A physician has a medical practice; define what the second “practice” means or say **“in conversations about spiritual growth.”**
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Fragment/rhetoric:** “What is one of the biggest brachos... Today.” should be rewritten as a complete sentence.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Cliché:** “Every day is a gift; that’s why it’s called the present” is a familiar English pun and may feel out of register in a Torah volume.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Comma splice:** “So we should not lose it, we shouldn’t say...” needs a period, semicolon, or conjunction.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Pronoun shift:** “do something with the day which I have today, and from that we’ll get...” shifts from “we” to “I” and back. Keep one point of view.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Theological simplification:** “Hashem always gives us brachos” sits beside a verse explicitly offering blessing and curse based on choice. State the intended distinction rather than resolving it with a slogan.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Source identification:** Give exact details for *Ma’ayanah Shel Torah* and *Niflaos Chadashos*, and identify the source of the acrostic on *ukelalah*.
- **Printed pp. 281-282 (archive pp. 301-302), Acrostic layout:** The initials of *vehakeren kayemes lo le’olam haba* should be shown in a compact table or with the initial letters bolded. As printed, the right-aligned list is detached from the explanation.
- **Printed p. 281 (archive p. 301), Redundant diminutives:** “only the peiros, only the small little parts” should be **“only the fruits, while the principal is reserved...”**
- **Printed p. 282 (archive p. 302), Grammar:** “*Vehakeren kayemes lo le’olam haba* is the roshei teivos represented by *ukelalah*” reverses the relationship. Suggested: **“The letters of *ukelalah* are read as an acrostic for...”**
- **Printed p. 282 (archive p. 302), Repetition:** “continue to grow and do more and more and get all of those brachos” is wordy and transactional. End with one focused sentence about using the present day well.
- **Printed p. 282 (archive p. 302), Severe pagination issue:** A single short paragraph sits at the top of an otherwise blank page. Reflow pp. 281-282 so the piece occupies one balanced page or uses the second page meaningfully.
- **Printed pp. 281-282 (archive pp. 301-302), Bidi:** The acrostic and verse need purpose-built RTL treatment; do not rely on a bilingual running line.

### Re’eh 5784

- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Repetition:** “Time is a gift,” “an invention,” “given to us as a gift,” “to utilize it,” and “another means” restate the same idea several times. Condense the opening by at least half.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Philosophical source:** “There was no such thing as time before creation” is a metaphysical claim. Cite the relevant Jewish philosophical/midrashic source and avoid presenting one formulation as scientifically demonstrable.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Passage identification:** Re’eh includes the command to count seven weeks in Deuteronomy 16:9. Cite it directly rather than calling Sefiras HaOmer vaguely “the mitzvah most involved with time.”
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Unsupported superlative:** “probably the mitzvah... most involved with time” is subjective and invites obvious alternatives. Say simply that counting makes time itself part of the mitzvah.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Family-only references:** “Bobbi’s grandfather, Papa” and “after I joined the family” require a family tree to follow. Identify the person by full name or move the reminiscence to a family note.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Halachic review required:** Counting the Omer during the following morning after missing the night, and counting ten times daily, require clarification about timing, wording, and use of a berachah. State that only the first proper count carries a blessing and cite the exact Munkatcher practice.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), False guarantee:** Repeating the count ten times does not literally “guarantee” that a person will not miss a day. Use **“was intended to reduce the chance of forgetting.”**
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Bracketed aside:** “[I consider myself included in that category]” is a spoken joke left inside editorial brackets. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Preposition:** “our time on this world” should be **“our time in this world.”**
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Missing word:** “you never know if will have a chance” should be **“you never know if you will have another chance.”**
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Repetitive maxim:** “grab the opportunity... whenever it arises” repeats a recurring formula from several other pieces. Vary the language or cross-reference rather than reproducing the same exhortation.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Unrelated close:** The general blessing for healing is not connected to the argument about time or Sefirah. Either create a meaningful transition or omit it.
- **Printed p. 283 (archive p. 303), Bidi:** The Omer phrases, family practice, and Munkatcher source need separate RTL styling.

### Re’eh 5785

- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Verse translation:** Deuteronomy 15:7-8 commands opening one’s hand and lending sufficient for the person’s lack. “Extend to him any credit he requires” sounds commercial and does not capture the verse’s charitable obligation.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Name consistency:** “Rav Kormornick” appears elsewhere as **Rav Kormornick**. Verify the person’s name and standardize the title *A Short Vort*.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Redundant question:** The closed-heart/closed-hand question is stated, paraphrased, and then asked again. State it once.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Source citation:** Give the precise Vilna Gaon source for the open-hand image; do not rely on a secondary quotation alone.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Gendered default:** Repeated “him” can follow the verse, but the application should acknowledge recipients of any gender.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Malapropism:** “a tremendously heartbreaking and critically impressive example” is awkward; **“a moving example”** is sufficient.
- **Printed pp. 284-285 (archive pp. 304-305), Historical/source verification:** The story about Rebbetzin Rivkah Holtzberg, the imprisoned traveler, a bribe, a Shabbos dress, and a diamond ring requires a reliable published source and careful chronology. The Mumbai attacks and the shivah should be identified accurately.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), Vague/blaming language:** “looking to get away from everything,” “connected with bad people,” and “found myself in trouble” insinuate fault without useful information. Either quote a source accurately or remove the moralizing ambiguity.
- **Printed p. 284 (archive p. 304), National generalization:** “You cannot begin to imagine how horrible a place that is in India” paints an entire country through one reported jail experience. Name the specific detention conditions, if sourced, without disparaging India generally.
- **Printed pp. 284-285 (archive pp. 304-305), Legal/safety query:** The account of bribing a guard and evading inspection at a border describes potentially illegal and dangerous conduct. It needs exact sourcing and should not be framed as a model for readers.
- **Printed p. 285 (archive p. 305), Quotation punctuation:** In American style, the comma belongs inside the quote: **“on your finger,” she said.** The nested single/double quotation marks also need systematic correction.
- **Printed p. 285 (archive p. 305), Repetition:** “This happened in our time, not thousands of years ago, this was now” is a comma splice and repeats “our time/now.” Suggested: **“This modern account makes the principle immediate.”**
- **Printed p. 285 (archive p. 305), Abstract praise:** “a tremendous ability to give something beyond what we can easily comprehend” says little. Name the sacrifice: giving valuable clothing and jewelry tailored to another person’s need.
- **Printed pp. 284-285 (archive pp. 304-305), Bidi:** The verse and concluding Hebrew labels need complete RTL typesetting.

### Shoftim 5783

- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Verse/source:** Cite Deuteronomy 17:11 and identify Rashi’s dependence on Sifrei. Translate the verse before discussing “right and left.”
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Loaded paraphrase:** “blindly follow the rabbis” is not neutral and prejudges the very interpretation being defended. Use **“defer to the authorized court even when its ruling conflicts with one’s own perception,”** subject to halachic nuance.
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Generalization:** “As Jews, we are trained to be independent thinkers” and “renowned for our critical eye” are flattering stereotypes. They can be condensed to the individual question about judgment.
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Source verification:** Give the exact R’ Yitzchak Berkovits source for the orientation explanation. Make clear that this is his homiletic resolution, not necessarily Rashi’s plain institutional meaning.
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Overstatement:** Right and left can be relative to orientation, but the verse is not literally giving street directions. Avoid saying Rashi’s whole point is simply “turn around.”
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Passage transition:** “In a similar vein, in an unrelated pasuk” is self-contradictory. Explain the shared theme of fixed versus developing perspective, or begin a second teaching.
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Citations:** Give Deuteronomy 16:21-22 for the asherah and matzeivah prohibition and the exact Exodus verse used for a stone altar. Check whether the intended citation is Exodus 20:22.
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Grammar:** “R’ Moshe adds to this and bring a pasuk” should be **“brings a pasuk.”**
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Definition:** A *matzeivah* is a standing pillar or single-stone monument; a modern grave marker is an analogy, not the exhaustive definition.
- **Printed p. 286 (archive p. 306), Source/derash:** Plural *avanim* does not literally mean “steps.” Attribute the movement symbolism to R’ Moshe Feinstein and cite the exact work.
- **Printed p. 287 (archive p. 307), Insensitive description of death:** “that’s as far as he got... more or less, hopefully more” sounds dismissive of a deceased person’s life. State the static-symbolism idea without judging the person.
- **Printed p. 287 (archive p. 307), Subject-verb agreement:** “*Avanim* represent” and “the Gemara brings,” not “represents” and “bring.”
- **Printed p. 287 (archive p. 307), Talmud citation:** Cite Berachos 64a for *lech leshalom* versus *lech beshalom* and translate the distinction accurately.
- **Printed p. 287 (archive p. 307), Casual/morbid idiom:** “keep on trucking,” “It’s all over,” and “what cemeteries are full of” undermine the seriousness of the teaching.
- **Printed pp. 286-287 (archive pp. 306-307), Bidi:** The verse, Rashi, altar texts, and *lech leshalom* wordplay require separate RTL lines.

### Shoftim 5784

- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Topic identification:** Name the law of *edim zomemim*, cite Deuteronomy 19:19 and Makkos 5b, and explain *ka’asher zamam velo ka’asher asah* before discussing the dispute.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Group-name accuracy/tone:** “The apikorsim, of course, didn’t know how to learn; they only know Written Torah” is hostile and likely names the wrong historical group. Identify the Sadducees/Boethusians or other source-specific group precisely and remove the insult.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Legal complexity:** The rule that conspiring witnesses receive the plotted punishment only before, not after, it was carried out is counterintuitive and highly technical. Do not paraphrase it without the Talmudic conditions and rabbinic review.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Source identification:** The commentator whose acronym is rendered unreadably must be named in full, with the exact explanation of *la’asos le’achiv*.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Cross-reference:** Give the exact Ki Seitzei/yibbum verses where *ach* refers to a deceased biological brother. The current proof consists of garbled Hebrew fragments.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Derash versus ontology:** The claim that a deceased fellow Jew is no longer one’s “brother” because he cannot perform mitzvos is a homiletic distinction, not a license to deny enduring kinship or dignity. Attribute and explain it with exceptional care.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Source:** Cite the rabbinic phrase *bametim chofshi* for release from mitzvah performance after death, and distinguish it from posthumous reward or influence.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Transcript repetition:** “Grab them, man. Grab them,” “It is not forever,” and the aside about the speaker being older should be rewritten into one dignified exhortation.
- **Printed p. 288 (archive p. 308), Grammar/spacing:** “Ifhe leaves behind” needs a space: **“If he leaves behind.”**
- **Printed pp. 288-289 (archive pp. 308-309), Posthumous-merit review:** The claim that one can transfer the merit of every prayer to deceased relatives needs a source and careful theological wording. A personal dedication practice is not automatically a universal mechanism.
- **Printed p. 289 (archive p. 309), Trivializing metaphor:** “I’m sending them care packages every single day” makes merit for the deceased sound like parcels. Use **“I pray that the merit benefit their souls.”**
- **Printed p. 289 (archive p. 309), Family terminology:** “the Zeidys and the Bobbis” is intimate but imprecise in a general book. Identify relatives or use **“my grandparents and other deceased family members.”**
- **Printed pp. 288-289 (archive pp. 308-309), Bidi:** The legal formula, yibbum verses, and dedication prayer are too directionally unstable to edit in place.

### Shoftim 5785

- **Printed pp. 290-291 (archive pp. 310-311), Substantial duplication:** The right/left-orientation teaching repeats the central idea of **Shoftim 5783** on pp. 286-287. Decide whether both annual pieces belong in the collection; if retained, cross-reference and avoid repeating the same setup.
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Institutional scope:** Deuteronomy 17:11 concerns the authorized court and halachic decisors, not an unlimited command to obey anyone called “our leaders.” Define whose ruling is binding, under what process, and with what halachic limits.
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Vague adjective:** “This is a firm pasuk” should be replaced by a precise statement about the prohibition against deviating from the court’s ruling.
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Grammar:** “He explain” should be **“He explains.”**
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Duplicated wording:** “depends on your point of your view” should be **“depends on your point of view.”**
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Awkward coinage:** “the right-sidedness of the bakery” should be **“whether the bakery is on the right.”**
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Source details:** Identify the Torah journal, its year/issue, R’ Pesach Eliyahu Falk’s exact sefer, and the original wording of the teaching.
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Unsupported scientific anecdote:** The account of animal bones in a permanently frozen northern ice cap supplies no location, species, date, excavation, or scientific source. Animals do live in polar regions, and fossils can reflect past climates; “scientists were perplexed” is a strawman without documentation.
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Source/science conflict:** The claim that after the Flood equatorial regions were moved north and northern regions shifted south must be quoted exactly from the cited Torah source and not presented as established geophysics.
- **Printed p. 290 (archive p. 310), Overclaim:** “The scientists could not understand, but the Torah already told us” dismisses scientific fields without identifying an actual unresolved finding.
- **Printed p. 291 (archive p. 311), Major scientific error:** Evolutionary biology does **not** teach that humans descended from modern apes; it proposes common ancestry among primates. Correct the description even if the author rejects the theory.
- **Printed p. 291 (archive p. 311), Category error:** The aggadah that some members of the generation of dispersion were transformed into apes does not establish that all ape species biologically originated from those humans. Present it as aggadah, not zoology.
- **Printed p. 291 (archive p. 311), Polemical absolutism:** “patently false,” “mistaken theory,” and “totally false” substitute assertion for argument and are out of place in a passage that misstates the scientific position.
- **Printed p. 291 (archive p. 311), Medical-source verification:** The neurosurgical story attributed to the Chazon Ish needs a reliable biography, the patient/procedure context, and a warning that expert medical care is not replaced by textual intuition.
- **Printed p. 291 (archive p. 311), Unsupported universal:** “Everything is there in the Torah” is a theological maxim. It does not demonstrate that operative diagrams can safely be derived without medical training; qualify the claim and avoid medical misinformation.
- **Printed pp. 290-291 (archive pp. 310-311), Bidi:** Rashi, journal titles, midrashic sources, and the medical story’s names all require RTL/source reconstruction.

### Ki Seitzei 5783

- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Statistical/source:** Ki Seitzei is commonly counted as containing 74 mitzvos, the largest number in one parashah. State the number and counting tradition instead of only saying “the most.”
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Hyperbole:** “each one... deserves a lifetime of study” and “precision and exactitude” are inflated/redundant. One sentence about the parashah’s legal density is enough.
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Verse citation:** Give Deuteronomy 32:4 and a faithful translation of *haTzur tamim pa’alo*; the current Hebrew-English line is too garbled to support the claim.
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Halachic review required:** Deuteronomy 23:20-21 and the laws of interest are considerably more complex than “to a goy you can charge interest.” Distinguish Jew-to-Jew *ribbis*, dealings with a non-Jew, commercial institutions, and applicable civil law.
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), False rationale/stereotype:** The manuscript says Jews may charge non-Jews interest because “the goy charges interest” and “that’s what they do to us.” That is not the Torah’s stated legal rationale and stereotypes all non-Jews through banks. Remove this argument.
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Collective language:** “Throughout our history... the goyim had this as a constant complaint against us” needs an actual historical source and should not reduce diverse polemics to one collective voice.
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Invented quotation:** “Hashem says, ‘Don’t do that to your brother...’” is a paraphrase. Quote the verse or remove quotation marks.
- **Printed p. 292 (archive p. 312), Vague praise:** “this incredible, exact cheshbon and thought process” does not explain the legal principle. Replace it with a precise conclusion about reciprocal covenantal responsibility.
- **Printed pp. 292-293 (archive pp. 312-313), Legal terminology:** Explain *muchzak* and *ra’ui* accurately: the firstborn’s double portion generally applies to property already held by the father, not every asset merely expected later. The law has details and exceptions that require rabbinic review.
- **Printed p. 293 (archive p. 313), Awkward finance language:** “incoming fund/incoming funds” should be **“property not yet in the estate”** or another legally precise phrase.
- **Printed p. 293 (archive p. 313), Letter-derash layout:** The spelling *bekhor* without a vav, and the doubling pattern ב/א, כ/י, ר/ק, should be displayed in a table with numerical values. Running bidi prose makes the proof nearly impossible to verify.
- **Printed p. 293 (archive p. 313), Source identification:** *Torah Treasures* “in the name of the Gra” needs an original Gra source or a clear statement that only the secondary anthology was located.
- **Printed p. 293 (archive p. 313), Fawning repetition:** “so beautiful,” “classic Gra,” “genius,” “astonishing and breathtaking” should be reduced to one measured sentence.
- **Printed p. 293 (archive p. 313), Vague exhortation:** “maybe we’ll get a little bit closer to what he was able to accomplish” should name the attainable practice, such as close textual attention.
- **Printed pp. 292-293 (archive pp. 312-313), Bidi:** The verses, legal categories, and letter arithmetic all need separate RTL composition.

### Ki Seitzei 5784 (1)

- **Printed p. 294 (archive p. 314), Talmudic accuracy:** Sanhedrin 71a records a dispute about whether a *ben sorer umoreh* ever existed or will exist; R’ Yonasan says he saw one and sat on his grave. “The Gemara says there has never been one and never will be” presents only one opinion as unanimous.
