- **Printed p. 347 (archive p. 367), Defensive overstatement:** “No one argues with this, it’s a halachah pesukah” is unnecessary and may not capture differences in application. State the rule with its citation.
- **Printed p. 347 (archive p. 367), Grammar/code-switching:** “Don’t be metza’er the meisim” is not grammatical English. Use **“Do not cause disrespect to the dead.”**
- **Printed p. 348 (archive p. 368), Fragment:** “Especially our friends and family, we must be sensitive to them” should be joined to the preceding sentence or rewritten as **“We must be especially sensitive to friends and family.”**
- **Printed p. 348 (archive p. 368), Quotation-mark typo:** In the quoted will, “when it’s big.‘ But” ends with an opening single quotation mark. Replace it with the correct closing mark and repunctuate the nested quotation.
- **Printed p. 348 (archive p. 368), Internal contradiction:** The source models loving correction, but the paraphrase says “Don’t criticize people.” Use **“Do not criticize harshly or for self-satisfaction; offer necessary correction with care.”**
- **Printed p. 348 (archive p. 368), Awkward metaphor:** “A tear in their behavior” is forced. Use **“a developing problem in their conduct.”**
- **Printed p. 348 (archive p. 368), Repetition:** Three consecutive “Let’s learn...” sentences can be combined, and “each and every day ... day by day” repeats the same phrase.
- **Printed pp. 347-348 (archive pp. 367-368), Bidi:** The acrostic, Psalm, halachic phrase, long declaration before giving mussar, and final blessing need isolated RTL lines. Several quote marks and commas are already visibly displaced.

### Lag Baomer 5785

- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Wrong source type:** The Abba Umna/Abaye account is from the Gemara, Ta’anis 21b, not simply “a Midrash.” Cite it correctly.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Historical terminology:** Abba Umna is generally described as a bloodletter/healer. Calling him a modern “doctor” or “physician” without explanation can mislead; translate the occupation accurately.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Missing substance:** The source describes concrete practices that protected patients’ privacy and dignity. “Unbelievable measures of tznius” is vague hyperbole; summarize the actual conduct.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Unsupported exaggeration:** “The mesorah would have been lost without [Abaye]” and “You can barely find a daf in Gemara without Abaye” are rhetorical overstatements. Describe his central role without unverifiable absolutes.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Unfair inference:** The story does not establish that Abaye “could not excel” in Abba Umna’s area. Say that Abba Umna possessed a distinctive merit, not that Abaye was deficient.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Judgmental wording:** “Some [people] even appear to have many bad middos” asks readers to imagine moral defects before urging them not to judge. Remove the negative setup.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Mystical assertions:** Rashbi’s soul being “rooted in the Kodesh Kodashim” and his ability to see every Jew’s inner potential need named sources and should be framed as teachings, not observable biography.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Attribution:** **ואהבת לרעך כמוך, זה כלל גדול בתורה** is Rabbi Akiva’s statement in Sifra, Kedoshim. Cite it, then explain its relevance to Rashbi as his student.
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Repetition:** “See that penimiyus and the good in everyone” repeats the same object. Use **“recognize the inner good in every person.”**
- **Printed p. 349 (archive p. 369), Yahrzeit/source:** Verify and source the tradition that the Rema died on Lag BaOmer, as well as the identities and succession of R’ Shimon Sofer and R’ Bunim in Krakow.
- **Printed p. 350 (archive p. 370), Calendar claim:** “Lag BaOmer always falls out on either Parshas Emor, Behar, or Bechukosai” needs calendar verification and clarification of whether it refers to the preceding/following Shabbos and to Israel or the diaspora.
- **Printed p. 350 (archive p. 370), Wordplay clarity:** The reversal of **אמר** to **רמ״א** is clear, but the links from *Behar* and *Bechukosai* to “his rulings and statutes became holy like Har Sinai” are hard to follow. Quote the original Hebrew vort and translate each step.
- **Printed p. 350 (archive p. 370), Ambiguous title:** Calling the Rema “our Rav” is devotional but imprecise in a general anthology. Use his name or the traditional epithet being cited.
- **Printed p. 350 (archive p. 370), Filler:** “I think it’s a worthwhile point to bring out” can be deleted.
- **Printed p. 350 (archive p. 370), Pagination:** Two short paragraphs occupy an almost entirely blank page. Pull them back to p. 349.
- **Printed pp. 349-350 (archive pp. 369-370), Bidi:** Names, the Rabbi Akiva quotation, yahrzeit terminology, parshah names, and the אמר/רמ״א reversal need a final manual RTL pass.

### Shavuos 5783

- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Source identification:** Identify the opening midrash and the exact *Chanukas HaTorah HaChadash* passage. The biblical/Talmudic core is Shabbos 88b-89a on Moshe’s ascent and the angels’ objection.
- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Duplicated words:** “They’ll burn me up **with the with their** hevel” contains a clear duplication. Use **“with the breath of their mouths.”**
- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Major mistranslation:** **אחוז בכסא כבודי** means “grasp My Throne of Glory.” The gloss “like a segulah, a token, a trinket, a talisman” is not a translation and appears to confuse the Hebrew. Delete it and translate the Gemara accurately.
- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Punctuation:** Hashem’s words, Moshe’s response, and the angels’ quotation run together. Break the long retelling into short quotations with citations.
- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Redundancy:** “His chesronos, his yetzer tov and yetzer hara, and all of his various defects” repeats “defects.” A person’s yetzer tov is not itself a defect.
- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Grammar:** “The malachim’s ta’anah” should use a plural possessive consistently, and “would only be mekayem those parts ... that they are able to” should be **“could fulfill only the portions applicable to them.”**
- **Printed p. 351 (archive p. 371), Sweeping anthropology:** “A person’s netiyah is to do aveiros and not to keep the Torah” is needlessly negative and not required by the source. Say that human observance is fallible and includes the possibility of teshuvah.
- **Printed pp. 351-352 (archive pp. 371-372), Source/theology:** The argument that teshuvah makes a person superior to sinless angels needs a precise source and careful qualification. Yoma 86a says teshuvah reaches the Throne of Glory; the stronger hierarchy is the author’s inference.
- **Printed p. 352 (archive p. 372), Unverifiable spatial claim:** “A little space” beside the Divine Throne is said to be inaccessible to angels but reachable by a hardworking person. Attribute this exact image to Rabbi Oelbaum and give the recording/text; do not present heavenly geography as fact.
- **Printed p. 352 (archive p. 372), Narrative invention:** “The malachim did not agree with this argument” is not part of the cited Gemara as quoted. Mark it as a constructed objection.
- **Printed p. 352 (archive p. 372), Repetition:** “Spotless, flawless, whose record is clean as a whistle, had no aveiros at all” gives four versions of the same idea. Retain one.
- **Printed p. 352 (archive p. 372), Overbroad use of source:** The “breath of schoolchildren without sin” teaching concerns *tinokos shel beis rabban* engaged in Torah learning. It does not establish that every child is automatically on a higher spiritual level than every adult or angel.
- **Printed p. 352 (archive p. 372), Unsupported exclusivity:** “It was only in the zechus of those little children,” not in the merit of the Avos, mitzvos, Torah, or chesed, is an unnecessary absolute. Quote the source’s claim without negating other traditional merits.
- **Printed p. 352 (archive p. 372), Private blessing:** “All of our kinder, they should all live and be well and gezunt” sounds addressed to a family audience. Generalize or move it to a dedication.
- **Printed pp. 351-352 (archive pp. 371-372), Bidi:** The Shabbos narrative, Throne phrase, Yoma quotation, Psalm 8 verse, and many Hebrew possessives require a full RTL rebuild.

### Shavuos 5784

- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Source:** Cite the midrash behind the claim that Israel slept before Sinai, and cite the source connecting the all-night learning custom to repairing that lapse. Do not call it merely “the famous Midrash.”
- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Awkward repetition:** “They didn’t stay up all night then. They didn’t prepare themselves that particular night” repeats the point, as do “work on it” and “work on ourselves.” Tighten.
- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Colloquialism:** “It’s no kuntz,” “the real trick,” “great hustle and bustle,” and “we got the message” read like an unedited talk. Translate or replace them with formal prose.
- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Self-correction:** “Everybody, or many people, stay up...” preserves an oral correction. Use **“Many communities have the custom...”**
- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Overpromise:** “No matter our circumstances ... we each have the ability to learn Torah” should acknowledge disability, workload, caregiving, and differing access. The message can be that meaningful engagement remains possible in varied forms.
- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Source precision:** The land-access case and **הנותן בעין יפה נותן** need the correct Bava Basra citation, and the Chiddushei HaRim application should be sourced to a specific work/page.
- **Printed p. 353 (archive p. 373), Metaphor:** “The Torah ... comes with the access road” is effective, but explain what the “road” represents rather than leaving the practical conclusion implicit.
- **Printed p. 354 (archive p. 374), Duplication:** “Every single Yid, every person” is redundant. Choose one inclusive noun.
- **Printed p. 354 (archive p. 374), Simplification:** “All we need to do is get on the road and begin walking” understates sustained study and support. Use **“The gift includes the capacity to begin; growth still requires effort and help.”**
- **Printed p. 354 (archive p. 374), Othering:** “Being the chosen people separates us from all the goyim in the entire world” is triumphalist and redundant. Explain chosenness as responsibility and covenant, not superiority over outsiders.
- **Printed p. 354 (archive p. 374), Layout:** A short conclusion occupies a separate, mostly blank page. Reflow with p. 353.
- **Printed pp. 353-354 (archive pp. 373-374), Bidi:** The midrash citation, Yisro paragraph, Bava Basra principle, and final blessing need manual RTL and punctuation proofing.

### Shavuos 5785

- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Citation:** The opening is Mishnah Ta’anis 4:8; 1 Kings 5:12 states that Shlomo spoke three thousand proverbs. Give both citations in a uniform format.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Flippant comparison:** “The Dubno Maggid might have had almost as many, if not more” is unsourced and distracts from the verse. Delete it.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Major source query:** The claim that Shlomo created a descending chain of three thousand mashalim and that the three-thousandth was Shir HaShirim is not the plain meaning of 1 Kings. Cite the exact midrash or R’ Moshe Wolfson passage and distinguish it from the biblical statement.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Repetition:** “So lofty, so far beyond,” followed by one, second, third, fourth, and three-thousandth mashal, is overextended. Condense the ladder image.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Historical/theological precision:** “The union between Hashem and Klal Yisrael came into being on Shavuos” should be presented as the marriage metaphor used by the Mishnah and later commentators, not literal cosmology.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Wedding-custom claims:** Walking directions, candles as lightning, the chuppah as the suspended mountain, kiddushin, luchos as a contract, and keruvim as a “cosmic couple” each need sources. Some are customs, some midrash, and some later symbolism; do not merge them into one historical account.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Grammar:** “The luchos ... are also two - corresponding...” needs spacing and a complete explanation of why two tablets correspond to bride and groom.
- **Printed p. 355 (archive p. 375), Register:** “Cosmic couple” is jarring beside traditional terminology. Explain the keruvim symbolism in sourced, respectful language.
- **Printed p. 356 (archive p. 376), Vague theology:** “Hashem finished the creation ... with Shavuos” and “the briyah found its tranquility” need the source about creation’s dependence on Torah acceptance.
- **Printed p. 356 (archive p. 376), Source/wording:** “Klal Yisrael died hearing the voice of Hashem” should cite Shabbos 88b and say their souls departed and were restored, according to the midrashic account.
- **Printed p. 356 (archive p. 376), Generalization:** “The great chassidish gedolim and even the litvish gedolim” is broad, factional, and unsupported. Give named examples or omit it.
- **Printed p. 356 (archive p. 376), Spoken closing:** “I think it will have been worth the time I spent relaying it to you” is transcript residue. End with the substantive insight.
- **Printed p. 356 (archive p. 376), Layout:** The conclusion occupies a mostly blank page. Reflow with p. 355.
- **Printed pp. 355-356 (archive pp. 375-376), Bidi:** The Mishnah, Kings verse, Sinai verses, and wedding analogies contain long mixed Hebrew-English runs that need isolated RTL display.

### 17 Tamuz 5784

- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), House style:** Standardize the English spelling as **Tammuz** or **Tamuz** throughout the title, headings, URLs, and prose.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Source attribution:** The month/organ correspondences are commonly discussed through Sefer Yetzirah and kabbalistic sources. Cite the exact Arizal source for Tammuz as the right eye and Av as the left.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Translation:** Eicha 1:16 uses a singular verb with “my eye, my eye.” Explain the poetic construction rather than inserting “[referring to both eyes]” without a source.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Moral causation:** “Our job is to dry those tears ... by keeping our eyes pure” should be framed as a homily, not a claim that communal tragedy is remedied by one narrowly defined behavior.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Vague euphemism:** “Maintaining Torah standards” conceals what conduct is meant. Name the practice in neutral, non-shaming language.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Genealogical/source query:** The statements that Mashiach ben Yosef “comes from Yehoshua” and Mashiach ben David “comes from Kalev,” and that these two spies are therefore the roots of Mashiach, need exact genealogical and rabbinic sources.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Causal guarantee:** “We can also bring Mashiach by overcoming the challenges of Tammuz and Av” should be **“the teaching presents such growth as contributing to redemption.”**
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Repetition:** “Learning Torah now is critical. Raising our standards is critical” can be one sentence.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Layout:** The text occupies less than half the page. Consider a consistent short-piece design or combine it with the adjacent annual entry.
- **Printed p. 357 (archive p. 377), Bidi:** The Eicha verse, messianic titles, and closing prayer require separate RTL lines.

### 17 Tamuz 5785

- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Source accuracy:** Cite Mishnah Ta’anis 4:6 for the five calamities. Clarify the chronology of the tamid’s cessation and the wall breach rather than assigning each event casually to “the first” or “the second” Temple.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Name/spelling:** Standardize **Apostomus/Apostomos** and cite the source for the additional claim that the Torah scroll he burned was written by Ezra.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Imprecision:** The Mishnah says an idol was placed in the Sanctuary. “They put a sheketz” is slangy, changes singular agency to plural, and avoids the actual event.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Fragment:** “A day of tragedies” is a fragment and adds nothing after the list.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Source:** Cite the Chasam Sofer/R’ Moshe Wolfson source for the tradition that Boaz married Ruth on 17 Tammuz and explain its status.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Dated calendar claim:** “The Shabbos before ... is almost always Parshas Balak, which this year ... is true” needs calendar verification and will not age well in a permanent volume.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Grammar:** “What was Balak’s ta’anah to Bilaam why he was afraid...” should be **“Why, according to this teaching, was Balak afraid of Israel?”**
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Tone:** “Who are you kidding?” and “This is brilliant, the guy was brilliant” are flippant and oddly admiring of a hostile plan. Replace with neutral exposition.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Hebrew-order error:** The letters **רב** can abbreviate **רות ובועז** in that order. The English says “Boaz and Rus,” reversing the initials and obscuring the wordplay.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Major halachic/textual error:** Deuteronomy 23 does not state a general rule that “whoever curses Klal Yisrael will be forbidden to enter the congregation.” The passage concerns Ammon and Moab and gives specific reasons. Reconstruct the entire argument from the cited source.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Missing legal distinction:** The later line “Moavi, but Moavis, yes” alludes to the male/female interpretation. Explain it and cite Yevamos 76b-77a rather than relying on a slogan.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Degrading mystical phrasing:** Ruth is described as a soul “trapped in the tumah of Balak.” This requires a source and should not characterize a biblical convert or an ethnic group as filth.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Gematria:** Verify the claimed value 771 for **וירא בלק בן צפור** and **חודש תמוז**, including spelling and whether spaces/collective letters are counted.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Historical claim:** Cite the tradition that 17 Tammuz would have become a festival when Moshe descended with the tablets; do not say it was “supposed to be” a holiday without attribution.
- **Printed p. 358 (archive p. 378), Bidi:** The five events, abbreviations, Deuteronomy verse, Moavi/Moavis distinction, and gematria line need complete RTL reconstruction.

### 9 Av 5783

- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Attribution:** Give the exact Rav Pesach Krohn book, lecture, date, or recording for the Rav Mordechai Gifter story. “In his typical derech, very beautiful, very meaningful, and very real” is praise, not a citation.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Name verification:** Confirm the spelling and identity of Rav Yirachmiel Chasid and all honorifics.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Redundancy:** “Yungerman, you’re a young fellow” translates the same address twice.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Gender stereotype:** The explanation assumes a young man relates to a mother because she cries while lighting candles and serves her children, while only a yeshivah survivor can relate to the Mikdash. Preserve the personal testimony without turning maternal care or spiritual understanding into rigid categories.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Repetition:** “Majesty,” “splendor,” “grandeur,” and “what ... was all about” recur throughout the central quotation. A substantial tightening will make the testimony more powerful.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Historical context:** Explain what Rav Gifter witnessed in Telz and what “its destruction” refers to. The Holocaust context should not remain implicit.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Universal claim:** “None of us” in America has witnessed comparable Torah grandeur is both unprovable and dismissive. Attribute the limitation to the speaker’s own experience.
- **Printed p. 359 (archive p. 379), Dismissive aside:** Calling visual reconstructions “primitive” and saying none “do any justice whatsoever” adds contempt without helping readers. Recommend reliable models/resources instead.
- **Printed p. 360 (archive p. 380), Garbled sentence:** The Yom Kippur/Maseches Yoma sentence tries to contrast analytical learning with imaginative experience but becomes syntactically incoherent. Rewrite it from scratch.
- **Printed p. 360 (archive p. 380), Trivializing diction:** “Get a little feel, and get a little sad” is too casual for the subject. Use **“develop some sense of the loss.”**
- **Printed p. 360 (archive p. 380), Layout:** A single paragraph is stranded on an otherwise blank page. Reflow onto p. 359.
- **Printed pp. 359-360 (archive pp. 379-380), Bidi:** Place names, titles, Mikdash terms, and the final Yoma reference need a manual RTL pass.

### 9 Av 5784

- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Terminology:** When Rosh Chodesh falls on Sunday, the special haftarah is *Machar Chodesh*. Say that it **replaces the regular haftarah**, not that “we skip a haftarah.”
- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Incorrect generalization:** “We never skip a haftarah on Shabbos unless there’s a Yom Tov” overlooks Rosh Chodesh, the Four Parshiyos, special seasonal haftaros, and other rules. Delete the absolute.
- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Source hierarchy:** The link between *Machar Chodesh*, the missing korban, and sinas chinam is attributed through Rabbi Frand to Rav Schwab. Do not say “Chazal are reminding us” unless Chazal explicitly make this interpretation.
- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Translation:** **מזבח חדש בציון תכין** asks for a new altar in Zion, not directly “a new Beis Hamikdash.” Translate accurately, then explain the implication.
- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Homily versus context:** “Why has the son of Yishai not come?” in 1 Samuel 20:27 asks why David is absent from Saul’s table. The messianic reading is a homily and should be labeled as such.
- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Overreach:** Sinas chinam is traditionally associated with the Second Temple’s destruction, but “is a cause of all our tzaros today” is an unbounded causal assertion. Moderate and source it.
- **Printed p. 361 (archive p. 381), Structural break:** “The following story has nothing to do with what’s been said until now” announces that the piece lacks cohesion. Give the story its own heading or connect it thematically.
- **Printed pp. 361-362 (archive pp. 381-382), Story source:** Cite the R’ Aryeh Levin ice-cream-shop story. Interior thoughts and exact dialogue should not be presented as verbatim without a source.
- **Printed p. 362 (archive p. 382), Quotation-mark typo:** “The words **“Shabbos is Shabbos’**” opens with a double mark and closes with a single mark. Correct the nested quotation.
- **Printed p. 362 (archive p. 382), Repetition:** The store owner’s explanation repeats that the test was hard, R’ Aryeh understood, and he did not yell. Condense while preserving the compassion.
- **Printed p. 362 (archive p. 382), Attribution:** “What comes from the heart will go into the heart” is a known maxim. Attribute it or set it as a conventional saying rather than the author’s conclusion.
- **Printed pp. 361-362 (archive pp. 381-382), Bidi:** The haftarah, Mussaf text, Saul-David verse, and Hebrew labels in the shop story require RTL and quote-mark proofing.

### 9 Av 5785

- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Source:** Identify Rav Yechiel Spiro’s work and the specific Sanzer Rebbe for the hand-over-eyes interpretation. The standard practice also has concentration-based explanations, which should not be displaced.
- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Redundancy:** “We can only see a limited vision. Our ability to see is made specifically limited so we should understand our limitations” repeats “limited” and makes an unsupported teleological claim.
- **Printed pp. 363-364 (archive pp. 383-384), Pastoral/ethical concern:** The Holocaust survivor is told that his murdered family and shattered life only *appeared* broken, like tablets that were “really not” broken. This can deny actual death, trauma, and grief. The passage requires substantial reconsideration, expert pastoral review, and a reliable source; it should not be offered as a universal consolation.
- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Dehumanizing anonymity:** The survivor is described only as “completely alone and completely broken.” Name him if the source permits, or preserve his dignity with a less objectifying description.
- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Biblical citation:** Cite Genesis 42:24 and Rashi for Shimon’s imprisonment “before their eyes,” and Deuteronomy 9:17 for the tablets. Make clear that the Rebbe is drawing a word association, not denying the physical events.
- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Factual error:** At the burning bush, the bush really was aflame; the miracle was that it was not consumed. “It wasn’t burning” and “only to our eyes did it appear to be burning” contradict Exodus 3:2.
- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Logical contradiction:** “The broken luchos only looked broken” contradicts the Torah’s explicit account and the manuscript’s own wording “I smashed them.” Explain any symbolic claim without erasing literal breakage.
- **Printed p. 363 (archive p. 383), Source:** Give the source for R’ Moshe Sherer’s statement that closed eyes create unlimited vision.
- **Printed pp. 363-364 (archive pp. 383-384), Grief invalidation:** “The only reason we cry is because our eyes are open” frames mourning as a failure of perspective. Replace it with language allowing grief and faith to coexist.
- **Printed p. 364 (archive p. 384), Grammar:** “We’ll be able to see all that Hashem does is really for the good” should be **“we will come to understand how all that Hashem does is for the good.”**
- **Printed p. 364 (archive p. 384), Layout:** The conclusion occupies a mostly blank page. Reflow with p. 363.
- **Printed pp. 363-364 (archive pp. 383-384), Bidi:** Shema, divine attributes, the two **לעיניכם** verses, and the closing blessing need isolated RTL formatting.

### 15 Av 5784

- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Word choice:** “This day has a number of significances” should be **“The Gemara gives several reasons for the day’s significance.”**
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Historical nuance:** The wilderness generation’s deaths are traditionally said to have ceased on 9 Av and the fact was recognized on 15 Av. Check the cited account before saying they “stopped dying on 15 Av.”
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Source/technical accuracy:** Ta’anis 31a connects Tu B’Av with ending the cutting of wood for the altar as the sun weakened. The explanation about heat, worms, and exact timing should be checked against the Gemara and halachic sources.
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Awkward code-switch:** “You would not want to, chas v’shalom, bring wormy, infested wood” is conversational and repetitive. Use **“worm-infested wood was unfit for the altar.”**
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Unsupported hierarchy:** The wood-cutting episode does not by itself prove that Torah study is “even higher” than preparing Temple needs. Attribute that conclusion to the cited teacher.
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Health/longevity claim:** “Learn two mishnayos before sleep; it’ll add years to your life” must not be presented as a reliable health promise. Quote it as an inspirational saying and avoid guaranteed longevity.
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Preposition:** “Add another 60 seconds **on** my learning” should be **“to my learning.”**
- **Printed p. 365 (archive p. 385), Causation:** Tu B’Av has multiple stated reasons. Do not imply that a barely perceptible change in daylight alone “made it a day of celebration.”
- **Printed pp. 365-366 (archive pp. 385-386), Date/count:** Explain whether the “45 days” from Tu B’Av to Rosh Hashanah is inclusive and cite the source for reading **מה** as 45.
- **Printed p. 366 (archive p. 386), Private attribution:** “As my sister ... said” introduces an unexplained family source. Identify her and why her comment is authoritative, or delete it.
- **Printed p. 366 (archive p. 386), Talmud citation:** The statement that fear of Heaven was a “small matter” for Moshe is Berachos 33b. Cite it and clarify that it is not small for ordinary people.
- **Printed p. 366 (archive p. 386), Singular/plural:** “Give Him one more minute on that day” should explain whether the practice is one extra minute **each of the 45 days**.
- **Printed p. 366 (archive p. 386), Layout:** The short continuation should be pulled back to p. 365.
- **Printed pp. 365-366 (archive pp. 385-386), Bidi:** Dates, tractate quotations, the Deuteronomy verse, **מה/45**, and the High Holiday phrases need manual RTL proofing.

### 15 Av 5785

- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Bibliography:** Identify the *Chaim Sheyesh Bahem* volume/page, the Bnei Yissaschar passage, and the exact Ta’anis references rather than “a few blatt later.”
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Sentence fragment:** “A few blatt later” is attached awkwardly to the Hebrew quotation. Incorporate the citation before the quote.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Garbled sentence:** “Hashem will make it that every one of the chalakim of the tzaddikim will be appropriate to them” is not intelligible English. Translate the source anew.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Inference:** A circle having no beginning or end does not automatically establish that every righteous person will have the identical spiritual level. Distinguish the Gemara’s image from the later homily about equality and unity.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Historical scope:** The Mishnah describes the daughters of Jerusalem borrowing white garments so no one would be embarrassed. “All the Bnos Yisrael” and “poor girls” broaden and coarsen the text.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Unsupported idealization:** “There was no jealousy, only love...” is not stated by the Mishnah. Present it as the author’s symbolic reading.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Hebrew-letter explanation:** Spell out the wordplay cleanly: **אב** is alef-bet, and the fifteenth Hebrew letter is samekh, whose closed form resembles a circle. The current questions are hard to follow.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Grammar:** “The machol that Hashem **will be create**” should be **“will create.”**
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Source:** Give a reliable source for the long R’ Baruch Ber Leibowitz quotation and translate the Yiddish word *keppel*.
- **Printed p. 367 (archive p. 387), Grammar:** “Be melamed zechus on myself” should be **“be melamed zechus for myself,”** and “a Yid comes and passes me” should be **“when a Yid passes me.”**
- **Printed p. 368 (archive p. 388), Conditional:** “If we would have such an attitude” should be **“If we had such an attitude.”**
- **Printed p. 368 (archive p. 388), Awkward wording:** “Someone that we can share and be joyful with” is incomplete. Say what is being shared.
- **Printed p. 368 (archive p. 388), Severe pagination:** One sentence is stranded on an otherwise blank page. Reflow it onto p. 367.
- **Printed pp. 367-368 (archive pp. 387-388), Bidi:** Mishnah text, circular-dance quotation, letter names, and the long R’ Baruch Ber quotation need RTL/quotation-mark reconstruction.

### Elul 5784

- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Grammar:** “My vort for Elul was inspired by a number of people, including Rabbi Oelbaum, **is as follows**” has two predicates. Use **“My Elul vort, inspired in part by Rabbi Oelbaum, is as follows.”**
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Custom/source:** Daily recitation of Psalm 27 in Elul varies by community and continues for different lengths. State the custom followed here rather than “every day we say.”
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Digression:** The fresh roll and scrambled egg are unnecessary staging. Move directly from Psalm 27 to Birkat Hamazon.
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Irreverent paraphrase:** “In case I left anything out, please throw in all the other good stuff” trivializes the closing petition. Translate **ומכל טוב לעולם אל יחסרנו** accurately.
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), False immediacy:** “Ten seconds ago” assumes Psalm 27 and bentching occur consecutively. Use **“Yet elsewhere in our prayers...”**
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Homily versus literal meaning:** Gifts and loans from parents are still literally *matnas basar vadam*. The family analogy can illustrate dependence without claiming the words cease to apply.
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Gender/consumer stereotypes:** Mother supplies dinner; father supplies a Borsalino, car keys, and a car. Use one neutral example and avoid brand promotion and escalating consumption.
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Quotation marks:** “What’s for supper?‘”, “‘Daddy...,” and the car-key/car examples use mismatched single and double quotation marks. Re-punctuate all dialogue.
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Anthropomorphic claim:** “I want to live in Your house and be part of Your mishpachah” is a homiletic reading of Psalm 27. Label it as such and capitalize divine pronouns consistently.
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Grammar:** “May we be zocheh that Hashem **give** us” should be **“may Hashem give us”** or **“may we merit receiving.”**
- **Printed p. 369 (archive p. 389), Bidi:** The long Birkat Hamazon text is visually fragile and should be a separate RTL block with an exact translation.

### Elul 5785

- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Source:** Give the exact *Ohr Gedalyahu* and Sefer Yetzirah references for interpreting bodily openings as gates governed by *shoftim v’shotrim*.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Vague moral language:** “Improper achilah, or otherwise” leaves readers with an undefined moral judgment about eating. State the source’s actual examples and avoid food shame.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Simplistic psychology:** “Use his head and seichel to control his lev” and “when he loses control, the shotrim come in” need explanation. Define judges as discernment and guards as habits/boundaries; do not imply punitive self-control.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Repetition:** “Not able to control himself ... loses control ... unable ... restrain himself” repeats the same condition four times.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Personal aside:** “A pasuk which I’ve learned many times with my mishpachah” is private context. Remove it unless the family setting matters.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Awkward phrasing:** “Whether it’s light, whether it’s darkness, chas v’shalom” should be **“in favorable and difficult circumstances.”**
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Unsupported superclaim:** “Which pasuk is kolel every other pasuk in the Torah?” requires a named source and should be presented as a homiletic summary, not an objective ranking.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Ageist assumption:** “The second half of all our lives, when we can’t do what we used to” treats later life as uniform decline. Say **“when abilities or circumstances change.”**
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Unrealistic equivalence:** People may serve with equal devotion but not “exactly the same intensity” or “the same success” after capacities change. Emphasize continuity of purpose rather than identical output.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Duplicated word:** “Then we can then be successful” contains an unnecessary second **then**.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Repetition:** The final three “towards” phrases can be compressed.
- **Printed p. 370 (archive p. 390), Bidi:** Deuteronomy, Psalms, Numbers, and the shoftim/shotrim wordplay need isolated RTL lines and source labels.

### Rosh Hashana 5784

- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Source:** Identify the weekly sheet as *Divrei Siach* (or correct the title if a different sheet is meant), and give the issue/date/page. “A weekly sheet by Rav Chaim Kanievsky” is not enough for readers to locate the story.
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Hype:** “One of the most amazing and important articles I’ve ever read” is promotional rather than editorial. Begin with the story and let it establish its own importance.
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Attribution:** “The moral of the story, which they don’t bring down” admits that the following lesson is the author’s addition. Label it explicitly as **“My takeaway is...”**
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Vague theology:** “Every little step ... is counted as a very important item up in Shamayim” sounds childlike and makes an unsourced claim about heavenly accounting. Use restrained language such as **“Even a small step toward teshuvah can matter.”**
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Source chain:** The story passes from an unnamed “chashuv Yid” to R’ Yitzchak Zilberstein to R’ Chaim Kanievsky. Give the exact printed source and explain why the central witness remains unnamed.
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Dating:** “A recent trip to America” will become misleading in a permanent collection. Give the year or omit “recent.”
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Repetition/tone:** “Far away from Yiddishkeit and shemiras haTorah” says essentially the same thing twice and labels a relative judgmentally. A neutral description, such as **“not observant at the time,”** is sufficient.
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Characterization:** The narrator’s astonishment at a “big yarmulke” and a house full of Jewish objects risks equating observance with costume and décor. Condense the inventory and focus on the cousin’s changed practice.
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Irrelevant detail:** “Beautiful villa on the ocean shore” foregrounds wealth without advancing the story.
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Word choice:** “Hordes of people” has a hostile or dehumanizing connotation. Use **“a large group of people.”**
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Typo:** “I’m going to with these people” is missing **go**: **“I’m going to go with these people.”**
- **Printed p. 371 (archive p. 391), Repetition:** “I’m going to go down and join them to go to the shore” repeats **go**. Use **“I decided to join them at the shore.”**
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Duplicated adjective:** “A little small shofar” should be **“a small shofar.”**
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Quotation mark:** `My name is Moshe Katz ”` has an extra space and a displaced closing quotation mark.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Halachic/source query:** A private set of thirty shofar blasts is central to the story, but the time of day, blessings, and circumstances are omitted. Have a knowledgeable reviewer verify that the account is being represented accurately.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Tone:** “Through her wailing and sobbing” is melodramatic. **“She began to cry”** conveys the scene without overstatement.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Historical clarity:** If “killed in the war” means murdered in the Holocaust, say so accurately and sensitively; otherwise identify the war.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Genre/source:** The deceased father-in-law’s appearance and R’ Chaim’s conclusion that it was a *giluy mishamayim* are supernatural claims. Preserve them as a reported tradition, not as independently verified history, and cite the precise wording.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Vague support:** “These things are mentioned in Chazal many times” needs examples or should be removed.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Hype:** “A beautiful story, a classic story ... lema’alah min hateva” repeats praise instead of explaining the point.
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Idiom:** “The rest of the story followed through” should be **“the rest of the story followed.”**
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Spelling:** “No matter what ever we do” should be **“No matter whatever we do”** or, better, **“Whatever we do.”**
- **Printed p. 372 (archive p. 392), Awkward conclusion:** “Whatever we do, it should be successful” does not follow grammatically. Consider **“May every sincere step toward teshuvah be accepted.”**
- **Printed pp. 371-372 (archive pp. 391-392), Bidi:** Rebuild the mixed Hebrew-English lines for *Rosh Hashanah*, *Tashlich*, *shofar*, *teshuvah*, *giluy mishamayim*, and the quoted source. Several Hebrew runs have punctuation and quotation marks attached on the wrong visual side.

### Rosh Hashana 5785

- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Repetition:** “A number of little vignettes, little stories” repeats the same idea. Use **“three short stories.”**
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Source:** Give the exact source for R’ Tzvi Hersh HaKohen of Rimanov’s account of R’ Mendel of Rimanov, including the correct spelling of the narrator’s name.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Generic praise:** “An outstanding chassidishe Rebbe and tzaddik” adds no verifiable information. Identify the person and proceed.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Judgmental claim:** “It takes at least an hour if you do it fast and two hours if you do it right” treats duration as the measure of proper Selichos. State the approximate length without declaring one pace “right.”
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Quotation/source:** Identify the Selichos passage, reproduce the Hebrew accurately in an RTL block, and provide a complete translation.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Source/theology:** The claim that R’ Mendel would not continue until he had implanted teshuvah in every congregant’s heart needs a source and should be framed as the tradition’s description, not as observable psychology.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Mind reading:** “He wasn’t thinking about the Shemos of Hashem at all” is absolute. Attribute the interpretation to the three cited disciples.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Awkward sentence:** “The concept of just hearing about the fact that...” is bulky. Use **“Even hearing this demand can prompt reflection.”**
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Garbled thought:** “Let’s give a thought for at least one second, maybe I would like...” needs rewriting. For example: **“Let us pause and ask whether we truly want to return with our whole heart.”**
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Transcript residue:** “That’s the first story of the three that I’m going to tell you” sounds spoken and should be removed from a finished essay.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Unclear attribution:** “A great Rebbe was asked by the Gerrer Rebbe no less” leaves both speakers vague and adds hype. Name each person and clarify who later thanked whom.
- **Printed p. 373 (archive p. 393), Overstatement:** “That’s what shofar blowing is all about” reduces a multivalent mitzvah to requests for a good year. Use **“That is one aspect of the prayers surrounding shofar.”**
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Copyright/source:** The wine-merchant story reads like polished published prose rather than an oral paraphrase. Identify the source and secure permission if the wording is reproduced substantially.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Overwritten detail:** “The middle barrel of the third row” is needlessly specific unless it is part of a quoted source.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Run-on sentence:** “He begged to be excused, raced down to find out what was amiss” needs **and** or a sentence break.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Characterization:** The servant is presented as a lazy drunk caricature. Since the story is allegorical, shorten the degrading physical description and preserve the moral point.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Speaker confusion:** “The Gerrer thanked the Rebbe” is unclear after several unnamed rebbes. Replace titles with names.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Source:** Give the source for the Berditchever shofar-blower story.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Tone:** “Many righteous folks ... vying with each other” and “abstruse kabbalistic secrets” sound mocking. Use respectful, neutral language.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Sensitive phrasing:** “Four daughters of marriageable age” reduces the daughters to a marital problem. Retain only what the sourced story requires and avoid treating marriage as their sole defining need.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Quotation marks:** The shofar blower’s prayer ends with a mismatched opening single quote after **“daughters.”** Rebuild the whole quotation.
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Grammar:** “Please You do what I want” should be **“Please do what I ask.”**
- **Printed p. 374 (archive p. 394), Oversimplification:** “That’s the bottom line of all stories” forces three different teachings into one slogan. Give each story its distinct takeaway.
- **Printed p. 375 (archive p. 395), Idiom:** “Not just rattle off” needs an object: **“not just rattle the prayers off.”**
- **Printed p. 375 (archive p. 395), Repetition:** The conclusion repeats **“We’re davening...”** several times. Combine the requests into one focused paragraph.
- **Printed p. 375 (archive p. 395), Pagination:** A single continuation line sits on an otherwise blank page. Reflow it onto p. 374.
- **Printed pp. 373-375 (archive pp. 393-395), Bidi:** Selichos, shofar, the three stories’ Hebrew phrases, and their quotation marks need RTL reconstruction. Do not leave Hebrew as isolated visual fragments inside an English sentence.

### Rosh Hashana 5786

- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Source:** Give the volume/page in *Chaim Sheyesh Bahem*, the original R’ Sholom Schwadron source, and any source given for the anonymous witness from Radin.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Generic label:** “The famous maggid” can be replaced by the person’s full name and dates or role.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Historical query:** Verify the Friday-night timing, the 3:00 a.m. scene, and the witness’s presence in the Chafetz Chaim’s home. The story is presented with documentary precision but no citation.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Hyperbole:** “The whole town, the entire yeshivah, everybody” repeats and universalizes. State who is reported to have been asleep.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Hebrew quotation:** Set **Elokai, neshamah... atah asid litlah mimeni ule’hachazirah bi le’asid lavo** as an accurate RTL block with a source and translation.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Theological claim:** The conclusion that the identical damaged soul will be returned at resurrection needs the Chafetz Chaim’s exact sourced wording. Distinguish the standard prayer text from the sermon built on it.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Repetition:** “Exactly the same...” recurs several times in quick succession.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Shaming imagery:** Describing a soul as “torn up,” “in poor condition,” and needing a “cleanup job” can sound contemptuous toward a person in spiritual distress. Use moral language without objectifying the soul.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Transcript residue:** Repeated **“Oy, oy, oy”** and dramatic exclamations should be reduced.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Speculation:** The scene of all the Avos and Imahos arriving with “glorious, shining, brilliant neshamos” is imaginative. Label it as the speaker’s image, not a sourced description.
- **Printed p. 376 (archive p. 396), Tone:** “How beautiful this is” is jarring immediately after a fear-driven image of a damaged soul.
- **Printed p. 377 (archive p. 397), Translation:** Translate the list of spiritual faults rather than leaving readers to decode a long Hebrew/Yiddish chain.
- **Printed p. 377 (archive p. 397), Conditional grammar:** “If we would stop” should be **“If we stopped.”**
- **Printed p. 377 (archive p. 397), Accusatory generalization:** “We rattle [it] off every day because [we are] in a hurry to get to Berachos” assumes insincerity. Invite attention without assigning motives to every reader.
- **Printed p. 377 (archive p. 397), Duplicated word/phrase:** The ending repeats **gezunt** four times in a row. Retain one blessing.
- **Printed pp. 376-377 (archive pp. 396-397), Bidi:** The prayer, cleansing list, and closing blessings are interwoven with English in unstable order. Rebuild them as separate Hebrew lines followed by translations.

### How to do Teshuva

- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Title consistency:** Use the book’s chosen spelling and capitalization consistently, for example **How to Do Teshuvah**.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Grammar/source:** “I came across from Rav Tzadka’s weekly sheet” should be **“I found the following in Rav Tzadka’s weekly sheet.”** Give the sheet’s exact title, issue, date, and page.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Unresolved note:** The bracketed identifier for Rav Tzadka and his institution is an editorial placeholder. Integrate the full identification into the prose and remove the brackets.
- **Printed pp. 378-379 (archive pp. 398-399), Halachic/theological review:** Calling these the “easiest things” and saying they are “guaranteed” to produce kapparah turns nuanced rabbinic teachings into a mechanical checklist. Each claim needs exact sourcing, stated conditions, and review by a qualified halachic editor.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Source-title query:** Verify the title *Tziporen Shamir* and explain it accurately. The gloss “little worm that could that could break stones” contains a duplicated phrase and may not accurately explain the title.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Duplicated phrase:** “Could that could” should be **“could.”**
- **Printed pp. 378-379 (archive pp. 398-399), List formatting:** The numbering is visually broken across the spread, with floating numerals and inconsistent punctuation. Rebuild a single 1-11 list with parallel syntax and enough spacing between entries.
- **Printed pp. 378-379 (archive pp. 398-399), Source audit:** Verify and cite every item individually, including *Hashem Hu HaElokim* with kavannah, being *ma’avir al middosav*, attending a shiur/responding to Kaddish, reciting *Az Yashir* joyfully, praying for a sick person, Friday-night *Vayechulu*, accepting suffering, learning *lishmah*, tefillin with proper kavannah, the Yom Kippur passage about Aharon’s sons, and reciting Tehillim/requesting forgiveness. Several are stated more broadly than their likely sources warrant.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Absolute statement:** “No one ever got ahead in life by always being right” is neither demonstrable nor precise.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Safety/tone:** “Just say, ‘You’re right’” can encourage appeasement in abusive, coercive, or legally consequential situations. Distinguish waiving honor from lying, accepting mistreatment, or surrendering legitimate rights.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Unclear comparison:** “Which are bigger than aveiros” lacks a clear antecedent and needs a source.
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Idiom:** “We all once in a while have difficulties” should be **“We all have difficulties from time to time.”**
- **Printed p. 378 (archive p. 398), Harmful promise:** “If ... we’re mekabel them b’ahavah, we’ll live long life” may blame ill or bereaved readers and overpromises a physical outcome. Quote and contextualize the source rather than turning it into a guarantee.
- **Printed p. 379 (archive p. 399), Accusatory voice:** “Sometimes [we] learn just so we can show off” assigns a motive to readers. Use a first-person invitation to examine intention.
- **Printed p. 379 (archive p. 399), Redundancy:** “All-time, always favorite” should be one expression.
- **Printed p. 379 (archive p. 399), Gender/mechanical framing:** “If he does all of these...” should be gender-neutral and should not imply that performing eleven acts automatically completes teshuvah.
- **Printed p. 379 (archive p. 399), Condescension:** “Things people really don’t know how to do” belittles readers. Say **“practices that are sometimes overlooked.”**
- **Printed p. 379 (archive p. 399), False dichotomy:** “They think yelling during Ne’ilah is teshuvah. That’s not...” dismisses a potentially sincere form of prayer. Contrast emotion with sustained change without invalidating either.
- **Printed p. 379 (archive p. 399), Minimization:** “They’re not hard things” ignores the emotional, interpersonal, and spiritual difficulty of the acts listed.
- **Printed pp. 378-379 (archive pp. 398-399), Bidi:** The numbered Hebrew sources, numerals, punctuation, and translations are not reliably ordered. Typeset each item with a separate Hebrew source line and English explanation.

### Rosh Hashana in Berditchev

- **Printed pp. 380-382 (archive pp. 400-402), Copyright/source:** Nearly three pages appear to reproduce or closely adapt *The Complete Story of Tishrei* by Nissan Mindel. Give full bibliographic details, distinguish quotation from paraphrase, and secure permission if the wording is substantially copied.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Private/transcript framing:** “One of the family’s favorite stories” and “asked to repeat it ... for posterity” belong to an oral family recording, not necessarily a published essay. Condense or move this to a prefatory note.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Filler:** “The central figure of course” can be replaced by the person’s name.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Piyyut identification:** Name the piyyut, reproduce each cited line accurately, and give a complete translation. “The famous piyyut that we say” is not a citation.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Grammar:** “The congregation repeated him” should be **“the congregation repeated after him.”**
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Melodrama/universal claims:** “Many a sob burst forth,” “hardly anyone’s eyes were dry,” “every heart,” and “everyone pictured...” narrate the inner state of an entire congregation. Attribute the imagery to the source or reduce it.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Redundancy:** “Presided to dispense judgment and pronounce the verdict” repeats the judicial idea.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Genre:** The trance and heavenly-court sequence is a hagiographic or mystical story. Label the genre rather than narrating “a critical situation must have arisen” as a verified report.
- **Printed p. 380 (archive p. 400), Sensational language:** “The prosecution ... on the verge of triumph,” “held its breath with palpitations,” and “ecstasy and triumph” overdramatize an already intense scene.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Supernatural narration:** “I saw the Satan carrying a heavy load” and the ensuing heavenly episode should remain a quotation or attributed vision, not merge into the editor’s factual voice.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Patronizing term:** “Hapless Jew” should be replaced by neutral language.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Translation:** Translate the long catalogue of sins and set it as a readable list if it is retained.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Shame imagery:** “Ugly creatures of aveiros” turns wrongdoing into grotesque bodies. Consider **“the accumulated wrongs”** unless the exact source language is essential.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Overgeneralization:** “No Jew is really bad” is pastoral, but the passage then excuses nearly all wrongdoing as carelessness, poverty, hardship, or frayed nerves. Preserve compassion without erasing intent, accountability, or harm to victims.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Grotesque/comic portrayal:** The “one with a thousand eyes,” gleeful collection of “precious sins,” beard-and-payos grabbing, and slavery threat need exact sourcing and careful genre framing.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Typo:** “Theard a terrible cry” should be **“I heard a terrible cry.”**
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Punctuation:** “My sins may be many, but not that many?” should end with a period or exclamation point, not a question mark, unless it is meant as an actual question.
- **Printed p. 381 (archive p. 401), Legal/source explanation:** The double-payment and sale-into-servitude analogy relies on Torah theft law. Cite the law and make clear that this is the story’s allegory.
- **Printed p. 382 (archive p. 402), Biblical quotation:** Cite Isaiah 46:4 accurately and remove the bracketed aside “It’s the famous...” from the body text.
- **Printed p. 382 (archive p. 402), Character error:** “I have made him, | will bear him” uses a vertical bar in place of capital **I**.
- **Printed p. 382 (archive p. 402), Missing word:** “We are the servants Hashem” should be **“We are the servants of Hashem.”**
- **Printed p. 382 (archive p. 402), Grammar:** “Write us all in the sefer chaim” should be **“inscribe us all in the Sefer Chaim”** or an accurate translation.
- **Printed p. 382 (archive p. 402), Pagination:** The page contains only a short conclusion. Reflow it onto p. 381 if design permits.
- **Printed pp. 380-382 (archive pp. 400-402), Bidi:** The piyyut, sin list, Satan dialogue, Isaiah verse, and closing prayer have reversed punctuation and fragmented Hebrew. Reconstruct from the cited original, not from the current visual order.

### Yom Kippur 5784

- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Hebrew/definition:** The opening appears to invoke *lifnim mishuras hadin*. Reproduce the phrase correctly and define it before applying it.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Repetition:** “Stand us in good stead” appears twice in the opening paragraph.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Ethical qualification:** Saying “You’re right” while believing the opposite can be dishonest or unsafe. Distinguish voluntarily yielding from lying, appeasing abuse, or surrendering legal and financial rights.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Bibliography:** Give the author, publisher, date, and page for *Regards from Our Upstairs Neighbors* and confirm permission for any quoted wording.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Undermining disclaimer:** “Stories, which you may or may not want to believe” makes the editor sound unsure of the source. Identify the genre and evidentiary status directly.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Irrelevant joke:** The opening politician-lie joke is unrelated, dated, and needlessly abrasive. Delete it.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Persuasion by character:** “I get that,” “definitely not the ... dream type,” and “upright and down-to-earth guy” do not verify a supernatural event. Keep the witness description brief and factual.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Privacy/fairness:** The business, estate, and family details may identify living private people. Obtain consent, anonymize more fully, and make clear that the manuscript gives only one participant’s perspective.
- **Printed p. 383 (archive p. 403), Loaded framing:** “Courageously decided to remain silent and swallow the loss” praises nonconfrontation without considering whether clarification, mediation, or legal advice would have been appropriate.
- **Printed p. 384 (archive p. 404), Supernatural claim:** The deceased mother’s appearance should be reported as Yaakov’s personal experience. The book should not independently certify it as proof of a spiritual reward.
- **Printed p. 384 (archive p. 404), Medical claim:** “I was not hallucinating” is not something the narrator or editor can clinically establish. Quote it only as the witness’s own conviction.
- **Printed p. 384 (archive p. 404), Causation:** The sequence implies that financial concession caused the apparition and divine approval. Present the moral interpretation as the teller’s, not as demonstrated causality.
- **Printed p. 384 (archive p. 404), Harmful promise:** The conclusion links yielding in disputes with children and family being well. Avoid implying that illness or family trouble results from a reader’s failure to give in.
- **Printed pp. 383-384 (archive pp. 403-404), Bidi:** The opening Hebrew principle and closing blessing need clean RTL setting and translation.

### Yom Kippur 5785

- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Source:** Verify and cite the Gemara for **olam hafuch ra’isi** and the exact R’ Levi Yitzchok tradition used here.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Duplicated words:** “In the in the old days” contains a repeated **in the**.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Duplicated/garbled phrase:** “That the in the shul...” needs reconstruction; the current wording is not grammatical.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Historical stereotype:** The claim that people in the past were uniformly honest while people today “cheat and lie and rob and steal as much as possible” is false, sweeping, and defamatory toward the present generation.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Repetition/absolute:** “Dealt honestly ... did everything properly ... never let out a lie” repeats the same praise and makes an impossible universal claim.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Offensive generalization:** The contrast in which old worshippers supposedly lied during *Al Chet* while modern worshippers tell the truth depends on insulting both groups. Present the paradox as a sharply limited homily, not social history.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Unsupported claim:** “Absolute out-and-out sheker all around the world” and “emes is a rare commodity” are polemical assertions, not evidence.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Letter teaching/source:** Cite the source for the observation that the letters of *emes* span the alphabet while the letters of *sheker* cluster together. Typeset the letters clearly so the reader can see the argument.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Redundancy:** “A moral obligation, our achrayus” states the same idea twice.
- **Printed p. 385 (archive p. 405), Talmud citation:** The angels’ question about not reciting Hallel on Rosh Hashanah and Yom Kippur is in Rosh Hashanah 32b. Check the current citation and quote the passage accurately.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Insensitive framing:** “We made it since last year ... those of us who are here” can be painful for bereaved readers. Do not use survival as a rhetorical contrast with those who died.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Transcript residue:** Repeated **“Oy, oy, oy”** should be reduced.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Harmful inference:** “Learn the lesson from those who are not here” can weaponize death as evidence that survivors behaved better or received a favorable verdict.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Logic/theology:** Surviving one year does not prove acquittal in a future judgment. Present hope and gratitude without claiming certainty.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Article:** “He gives a interesting answer” should be **“He gives an interesting answer.”**
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Source:** The final letters of the Torah’s first three words spelling **אמת** and “the seal of Hashem is truth” need sources; the latter is associated with Shabbos 55a.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Overconfidence:** Repeated assurances that the reader will “certainly” be found innocent conflict with the essay’s own discussion of fear and judgment.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Nuance:** “You can’t have simchah ... on a day of fear” is too absolute. Rosh Hashanah includes both awe and festival observance; state the tension more carefully.
- **Printed p. 386 (archive p. 406), Unintelligible phrase:** The phrase resembling **“just ‘o and, a happenstance”** cannot be edited reliably as printed. Return to the recording or manuscript and reconstruct it.
- **Printed pp. 385-386 (archive pp. 405-406), Bidi:** *Olam hafuch*, *Al Chet*, the letter sequences, Gemara passage, and closing Hebrew are visually scrambled. Build the English around stable RTL blocks.

### Yom Kippur 5786

- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Cliché/transcript:** “A simple chassidish story ... all simple ... profound. Listen carefully” sounds like an unedited talk. Begin with the source and story.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Source:** Give the volume/page in *Torah Tavlin* and the original source for the encounter between the two rebbes.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Opening Hebrew:** The initial Hebrew line appears detached from the English paragraph. Reconstruct it, translate it, and explain its connection to the story.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Hype:** “Two gigantic rebbes” should be replaced by their names and roles.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Ableist premise:** The rich man assumes that a frail person deserves help but a robust person should “get a job.” The story rebukes this judgment, but the degrading premise still needs careful quotation and context.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Awkward dialogue:** “If you want to go out and if you want to eat” should be rewritten from the source or recording.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Impracticable directive:** Asking forgiveness from “every simple man” for the rest of one’s life is impossible if read literally and can foster unhealthy guilt. Verify the source and explain it as moral hyperbole if that is the intent.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Hierarchical language:** “The lowest of the low to the highest of the high” demeans people while arguing for their dignity. Say **“every person, regardless of status.”**
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Scope/consistency:** The essay alternates between “Every Yid is a holy neshamah” and the broader teaching that every human bears *tzelem Elokim*. Clarify the intended universal principle.
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Transactional promise:** “Hashem will notice and react accordingly” guarantees a response. Use aspirational language such as **“May that awareness shape the way we are judged.”**
- **Printed p. 387 (archive p. 407), Bidi/layout:** The orphaned Hebrew/Yiddish lines at the bottom are untranslated and appear in unstable order. Rebuild or remove them; the page also has substantial unused space.

### Sukkos 5784

- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Core source:** The piece asks why the festival is called *Sukkos* in the plural while the tractate is *Sukkah* in the singular, but it does not clearly identify who asks or answers. Give the exact sefer, author, volume, and page, including the correct citation to R’ Dovid Meisels if that is the source.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Weak bibliography:** “A nice sefer” is not useful identification.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Aggadah/source:** The explanation involving the present sukkah and the future sukkah made from the Leviathan needs its Talmudic or midrashic source and a brief note that this is aggadic imagery.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Certainty:** “Moshiach is going to come...” should not be used to paper over an unsupported textual derivation. Quote the source’s actual claim.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Oversimplification:** “Maseches Sukkah talks about only [the] current [sukkah]” is imprecise. The tractate discusses the laws and observance of the mitzvah, not merely one physical structure.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Colloquial wording:** “Hashem will take care of the future one” is too casual for the subject.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Weak analogy:** The comparison with *Pesach* admits multiple sacrifices or observances yet still has a singular title. As written, the analogy undermines rather than proves the proposed distinction.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Unclear sentence:** “So it’s not two ..., but there actually are two...” is not intelligible in the mixed Hebrew-English setting. Return to the source and rewrite the logic in English before reinserting Hebrew terms.
- **Printed p. 388 (archive p. 408), Bidi:** Most Hebrew examples are too scrambled to proofread confidently. Reconstruct them from the cited source, then have a Hebrew reader check spelling, gender, and order.

### Sukkos 5785

- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Stereotypes:** The opening assigns children’s favorite holidays according to food and fun, then says a man did not have to prepare or clean the house. This both trivializes observance and implies that domestic labor belongs to women. Rewrite with neutral examples or remove the setup.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Tone:** “Pesach is fun, lots of food” and “Shavuos isn’t bad either ... blintzes and milchigs” read as stand-up patter rather than a polished introduction.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Colloquialism:** “The Torah puts us on our heads” and “You guys think...” should be recast in formal prose.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Source:** Give the exact *Chaim Sheyesh Bahem* citation and the original *Meshech Chochmah* passage by R’ Meir Simcha of Dvinsk.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Conceptual clarity:** The distinction between mitzvos “against nature” and “according to nature” needs definitions. Explain whether “nature” means ordinary human inclination, agricultural rhythm, physical material, or something else.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Context/gender:** The agricultural example assumes a male farmer and an ancient subsistence economy. State that this is the biblical agricultural setting rather than a universal description of every reader.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Repetition:** Work, sweat, effort, harvest, and “the whole summer exhausting experience” repeat the same setup.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Word order:** “Take his all of his efforts” should be **“take all of his efforts.”**
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Awkward wording:** “Swing it over” and “drop all that” are too colloquial and may imply contempt for necessary physical work. Explain the temporary shift in attention rather than denigrating labor.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Source/metaphor:** Calling sukkah walls “pure ruchniyus” and devoid of physicality is a homiletic claim, not a literal description. Cite and explain it.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Source/grammar:** The tradition that Yaakov recited Shema when meeting Yosef needs its source. “As Yaakov Avinu took the simchah when he met his son Yosef” is not grammatical.
- **Printed p. 389 (archive p. 409), Overstatement:** “Leaving olam hazeh and going into olam haba” makes the sukkah sound like literal departure from this world. Mark it as metaphor.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Conceptual challenge:** The daled minim are not effortless or simply “natural”; they require acquisition, selection, handling, and halachic care. Clarify what the source means by the category.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Flippancy:** “You have to pay for them of course” is an unnecessary commercial aside.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Biblical citation:** Quote and cite Leviticus 23:40 accurately.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Unclear Hebrew claim:** The statement that the species are “not mechubar...” needs a complete phrase, translation, and source.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Awkward terminology:** “Mitzvos which are teva” should be rewritten as a complete English explanation.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Incomplete phrase:** “Raise ourselves out” needs an object, for example **“raise our attention above material preoccupation.”**
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Repetition:** “Using your mind to propel yourself” repeats the surrounding language about mental effort.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Halachic accuracy:** *Ta’aseh velo min ha’asui* requires active construction rather than a sukkah that comes into being passively; it does not necessarily require a completely “fresh” sukkah every year. Correct the explanation and cite the law.
- **Printed p. 390 (archive p. 410), Obvious conclusion:** “Simultaneously both sukkah and arba minim in the same Yom Tov” can be stated once without emphasis.
- **Printed pp. 389-390 (archive pp. 409-410), Bidi:** The Meshech Chochmah terminology, biblical verse, *ta’aseh velo min ha’asui*, and closing Hebrew need stable RTL blocks and translations.

### Sukkos 5786

- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Opening/Bidi:** The opening Hebrew fragments are grammatical only when read in RTL order. Set the blessing and theme as a complete Hebrew line, then translate it before continuing in English.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Missing words:** “What’s going happen when Moshiach comes?” should be **“What is going to happen when Moshiach comes?”**
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Repetition:** “When Moshiach comes” appears twice in consecutive sentences.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Talmud source:** Cite Avodah Zarah 3a for the nations’ future sukkah test and explain that the passage is aggadah. The Gemara describes participants kicking the sukkah and leaving; “proceed to demolish” may overstate the text.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Mind reading:** “They’re not interested in the mitzvah of sukkah” interprets the characters’ motives. Attribute the conclusion to the cited exposition.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Bibliography:** Give R’ Kormornick’s full name, the exact title/volume of *A Short Vort*, and a page number. “His second sefer” is not a durable citation.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Source:** The seventy Sukkos offerings corresponding to the seventy nations should be cited, commonly to Sukkah 55b and Numbers 29.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Demeaning self-reference:** “We, little Yidden” is unnecessary. Use **“The Jewish people.”**
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Serious othering:** “They don’t know what it means to have gratitude. That is a Yiddish trait; it’s not a goyish trait” assigns a moral defect to non-Jews as a group. Remove this blanket ethnic/religious stereotype.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Triumphalism:** “They will fail miserably” and “we are passing magnificently” turn a complex aggadah into group boasting. State the teaching without scoring entire populations.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Source:** Give the source for the R’ Moshe Schwab *shalom zachar* story and identify the people involved sufficiently to make the chain understandable.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Pronoun agreement:** “A distant acquaintance; he really did not know **them**” shifts from singular to plural. Use **him**, **the family**, or recast the sentence.
- **Printed p. 391 (archive p. 411), Age wording:** “He was an old man” is blunt. **“He was elderly, and the walk was difficult”** is more respectful and relevant.
- **Printed pp. 391-392 (archive pp. 411-412), Clarity:** Explain plainly that the acquaintance’s great-grandfather had been the only guest at R’ Moshe Schwab’s own *shalom zachar* on the first night of Pesach. The current pronouns force the reader to reconstruct this relationship.
- **Printed p. 392 (archive p. 412), Repetition:** “That is gratitude. That is sensitivity. That is something...” can be one concise concluding sentence.
- **Printed pp. 391-392 (archive pp. 411-412), Bidi:** Rebuild all terms for Sukkos, gratitude, the offerings, nations, mitzvah, shalom zachar, and Pesach. The current visual order makes some English clauses attach to the wrong Hebrew term.

### Hoshana Raba 5784

- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Title consistency:** Choose one spelling, such as **Hoshana Rabbah**, and use it consistently in titles, URLs, headings, and prose.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Source:** Identify which Satmar Rebbe gave the parable and cite the original discourse.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Shaming labels:** The parable divides people into “clever,” “simple-minded,” and “fools.” If those labels are in the source, quote them with context; otherwise use neutral descriptions based on when each group approached the ruler.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Universal motive claim:** “Everyone had reason to be ashamed because they came ... seeking their own benefit” assigns a selfish motive to all worshippers. Attribute it to the parable rather than stating it as fact.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Liturgy:** Reproduce and identify the opening phrase translated as “For Your sake, Hashem...” and check that the English matches the actual Hoshana Rabbah text.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Source/accuracy:** The familiar symbolism of the four species, including the aravah as having neither taste nor fragrance, needs a source and an accurate explanation. Avoid describing people represented by the aravah as having “no Torah and no mitzvos” without the source’s nuance.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Unclear attribution:** Give the exact title, author, and page of the sefer that interprets the aravos as the lips of heavenly accusers. The title is not legible enough in the current mixed-script sentence.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Illogical wording:** “Please instead listen to our davening, not to their davening” is confusing because the accusers have been described as speaking accusations, not praying. Use **“not to their accusations.”**
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Redundancy:** “A very excellent, good year” should be **“a good year.”**
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Talmudic distinction:** The treatment of teshuvah from fear and teshuvah from love should be checked against Yoma 86b. The manuscript appears to blur whether intentional sins become errors or merits.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Custom/halachic query:** Placing beaten aravos on top of the aron kodesh is not a universal practice. Name the custom and community, and have a halachic editor verify the description.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Contradictory image:** “We beat them,” then “we’re not going to mistreat them,” needs explanation. The transition from symbolic striking to elevating the aravos is not self-explanatory.
- **Printed p. 393 (archive p. 413), Bidi:** The liturgical phrase, species symbolism, teshuvah categories, and closing request are visually scrambled. Set each source in a discrete RTL line with an English gloss.

### Hoshana Raba 5785

- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Digression:** The opening discussion of private family practices and never running across a street does not prepare the Hoshana Rabbah teaching. Begin with the relevant practice or remove the aside.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Safety/precision:** “You never run across the street, you only walk” is not a universal safety rule; circumstances and traffic controls matter. It should not be elevated into a religious practice.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Transcript residue:** “Which I’ve never divulged to anybody, but I do it every year” is conversational suspense, not edited exposition.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Privacy/clarity:** Identify who “Bobbi” is or replace the private nickname. Explain why a shortened responsive text is being used.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Liturgical/halachic query:** “We leave out all the little words ... [and finish] under three minutes” needs review.
