Do not imply that portions of the service may simply be omitted without identifying the person’s needs, the communal practice, and any guidance received.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Subject-verb agreement:** “One of the phrases we says” should be **“one of the phrases we say.”**
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Source/biography:** Give a reliable source for the R’ Yeshaya Bardaki shipwreck story and verify the biographical claims, including “one of the founders of the Old Yishuv” and “rav of Pinsk.”
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Word choice:** “Approached the holy soil” is geographically odd while the family is still at sea; **“the coast of Eretz Yisrael”** is clearer.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Melodrama:** “Thrashed about,” “heart-rending call,” and “a surge of strength” can be tightened, especially in a story involving children in mortal danger.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Sentimentality:** “Both of his wonderful children” adds praise unrelated to the rescue.
- **Printed p. 394 (archive p. 414), Guaranteed response:** The inference that if a human father responded, God “would certainly respond” turns a metaphor into a promise. Preserve the story’s hope without guaranteeing a particular answer to prayer.
- **Printed p. 395 (archive p. 415), Assumption/shame:** “Deep inside, our teshuvah was lacking” and “we might well be unworthy” assign spiritual failure to every reader. Use first-person reflection or conditional language.
- **Printed p. 395 (archive p. 415), Idiom:** “Whether or not our request is in place” should be **“whether or not our request is justified”** or **“however imperfect our prayer may be.”**
- **Printed p. 395 (archive p. 415), Word choice:** “Emit that final, heartfelt cry” is mechanical. Use **“offer”** or **“voice.”**
- **Printed p. 395 (archive p. 415), Colloquial/exclusionary tone:** “A tough Yom Tov for all of us regular guys” is informal and gendered.
- **Printed p. 395 (archive p. 415), Irreverent shorthand:** “Running to catch up ... and bang those aravos” trivializes the ritual. Describe the practical distraction respectfully.
- **Printed p. 395 (archive p. 415), Repetition:** “Give it a moment’s thought, at least one moment” repeats **moment**.
- **Printed pp. 394-395 (archive pp. 414-415), Bidi:** The central cry, Hoshana Rabbah text, names, and closing plea need reconstruction from the source; punctuation currently lands on the wrong side of Hebrew phrases.

### Hoshana Raba 5786

- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Spelling:** “Shoomzen” should follow a chosen transliteration, such as **shmuessen** or **shmuzen**.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Source:** Identify the translated R’ Moshe Wolfson discourse, volume/page, and the exact *Imrei Emes* passage from the Gerrer Rebbe.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Source/genre:** The description of Rosh Hashanah as writing, Yom Kippur as sealing, and Hoshana Rabbah as a further/final seal needs its kabbalistic or liturgical source and should not be presented as an uncontested chronological mechanism.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Halachic analogy:** The “double seal” rule applies in specified kashrut situations, not simply whenever “food [is] left unsupervised.” Identify the actual law and explain *chosam b’toch chosam* accurately.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Serious generalization:** “The vast majority of Yidden are far away from the tzibbur” is unsupported and alienating. Define the intended spiritual category without judging most Jews.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Source:** Cite the order of the ushpizin and explain why David is associated with the seventh day in the tradition being used.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Colloquial repetition:** “Sort of at the end ... sort of climbing down” should be rewritten directly.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Theological sensitivity:** The treatment of David’s sin and teshuvah needs exact sourcing and nuance, including the relevant Talmudic debate. Do not casually state that he committed an enormous sin and then use it as a simple ladder metaphor.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Demeaning phrase:** “The lowest of the low” conflicts with the message that no person is beyond return.
- **Printed pp. 396-397 (archive pp. 416-417), Disrespectful imagery:** “Beat it up,” “threw it on the floor,” and “fling it on top of the aron kodesh” describe a ritual object carelessly. Use the ritual’s established verbs and explain the custom.
- **Printed p. 396 (archive p. 416), Self-abasement:** “We are nothing and we have nothing” and “battered and broken aravos” may intensify shame rather than teshuvah. Attribute the metaphor and temper it.
- **Printed p. 397 (archive p. 417), Calendar/source:** It is true in the fixed calendar that Hoshana Rabbah does not occur on Shabbos, but the manuscript must cite the calendar rule rather than infer that it was arranged solely to preserve the aravah ritual.
- **Printed p. 397 (archive p. 417), Halachic inaccuracy:** “Shabbos supersedes” shofar and lulav is too broad. Their nonperformance on specified Shabbos days is a rabbinic safeguard and varies by Temple-era/location context; have the passage checked carefully.
- **Printed p. 397 (archive p. 417), Unsupported hierarchy:** “Shabbos ... cannot help the aravah that is stretched out on the ground” invents a competition between sacred observances. Quote the source’s actual homily and label it as such.
- **Printed p. 397 (archive p. 417), Historical overclaim:** “The chachamim made it come out that way” suggests the fixed calendar was designed for this one ritual. Correct or source the causal assertion.
- **Printed p. 397 (archive p. 417), Garbled noun:** The line that appears as “many will never be abandoned” likely contains a damaged Hebrew/English transition. Reconstruct it from the recording or source.
- **Printed pp. 396-397 (archive pp. 416-417), Bidi:** This piece is especially dependent on Hebrew wordplay. Rebuild the seals, ushpizin, David, aravah, Shabbos, shofar, and lulav passages from authoritative text before English proofreading.

### Shmini Atzeres 5784

- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Transcript opening:** “Good Yom Tov everybody. I have two divrei Torah...” should be recast as a written introduction.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Bibliography:** The sefer title, its two-volume status, R’ Avraham Landau of Ciechanów, and R’ Menachem Mendel Chaim Landau of Zawiercie need standardized spellings and full publication details.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Filler:** “After you hear it you say, why didn’t I think of that?” can be deleted.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Biblical quotation:** Cite and translate Psalms 145:19-20 accurately. Make clear where the verse ends and where the sefer’s distinction between those who fear and those who love begins.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Ranking language:** “A much higher madreigah” should be attributed to the cited interpretation rather than asserted as a measurable ranking of people.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Talmud citation:** The Hillel story is Shabbos 31a. Quote **“What is hateful to you, do not do to your fellow”** accurately and identify the rest as the Ruzhiner Rebbe’s homiletic rereading.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Accuracy:** Shemini Atzeres is explicitly commanded in the Torah. If the point is that the Torah does not state its reason, say that; do not imply that the festival itself lacks a Torah basis.
- **Printed p. 398 (archive p. 418), Speculation:** “This ger apparently was already fairly well-versed” is not in the Gemara and exists only to support the later wordplay. Label it as part of the homily.
- **Printed pp. 398-399 (archive pp. 418-419), Source:** Give the original source for interpreting Hillel’s “one foot” as one festival and Shemini Atzeres as a day for removing separation between Jews.
- **Printed p. 399 (archive p. 419), Source/genre:** Cite the R’ Meir of Premishlan story and present the daughter’s recovery as a reported miracle, not verified medical causation.
- **Printed p. 399 (archive p. 419), Pastoral concern:** “As if there was nothing wrong” can make suppressing fear or grief sound spiritually superior. Acknowledge that joy and anguish can coexist.
- **Printed p. 399 (archive p. 419), Rabbinic accuracy:** The teaching that one blesses for the bad as for the good does not necessarily demand the identical emotional joy. Check Berachos 54a and its commentaries before translating it as “exactly the same simchah.”
- **Printed p. 399 (archive p. 419), Medical detail:** “Immediately began perspiring profusely” should not be used as proof that the rhetorical argument cured her illness.
- **Printed p. 399 (archive p. 419), Garbled phrase:** “Work very hard to be ... on ourselves” is not intelligible in the mixed script and must be reconstructed.
- **Printed p. 399 (archive p. 419), Awkward ending:** “Treat Hashem with the awesomeness He is worthy of” should be rewritten, for example **“approach Hashem with fitting awe.”**
- **Printed pp. 398-399 (archive pp. 418-419), Bidi:** Psalms, the Hillel story, the festival wordplay, the Mishnah/Gemara quotation, and the miracle-story dialogue need separate RTL blocks.

### Shmini Atzeres 5785

- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Source:** Cite Sukkah 55b and Numbers 29 for the seventy bulls and their association with the nations.
- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Hostile generalization:** “When the goyim destroyed the Beis Hamikdash, they were ... hurting themselves” should be tied to the Talmudic lament and its historical actors, not projected onto all non-Jews.
- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Othering:** “We’ve finished with all the goyim” makes the festival sound like relief from other peoples. Replace it with the source’s positive focus on Israel’s additional day with God.
- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Source:** Give the precise *A Short Vort* reference and the source of **kasheh alai preidatchem**.
- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Repetition:** The car and milk examples both make the same point about a purchase. Keep one.
- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Gendered analogy:** The marriage example should be explained as a specific halachic act rather than a universal model in which a man gives and a woman passively receives.
- **Printed p. 400 (archive p. 420), Typo/grammar:** “The relationship between the buyer and a seller” should be **“the buyer and the seller.”**
- **Printed p. 401 (archive p. 421), Theological/othering claim:** “They rejected the Torah. They only want the means ... not a lasting relationship with Hashem” treats all nations and individuals as a single spiritually deficient group. Cite the midrashic frame and avoid applying it to contemporary people wholesale.
- **Printed p. 401 (archive p. 421), Patronizing aside:** “Believe it or not, we do daven for their existence” assumes readers find concern for non-Jews surprising. Remove it.
- **Printed p. 401 (archive p. 421), Hostile wish:** “We wish that they be happy with their lot and leave us alone” is condescending and adversarial. State the universal prayer for peace and sustenance positively.
- **Printed p. 401 (archive p. 421), Weak contrast:** The claim that Pesach and Shavuos are “strictly for us” ignores their universal themes and needs a source if retained.
- **Printed p. 401 (archive p. 421), Redundancy:** “Forever lasting relationship” should be **“lasting relationship”** or **“everlasting relationship.”**
- **Printed pp. 400-401 (archive pp. 420-421), Bidi:** Festival names, offerings, the midrashic quotation, and relationship terminology need stable RTL formatting and exact translations.

### Shmini Atzeres (1) 5786

- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Source:** Identify the exact Chizkuni passage. “One of the major mefarshim on Rashi” is a description, not a citation.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Colloquial dialogue:** “Have a nice day, see you then” and “Do me a favor and stick around” sound modern and casual for a sourced royal parable. Paraphrase consistently or quote the source more faithfully.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Calendar check:** Verify the stated intervals of fifty days, four months, and seven months. Sukkos to Pesach is ordinarily about six lunar months, not seven.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Accuracy:** “There is only atzeres after Sukkos” is too broad. The seventh day of Pesach is called an *atzeres* in Deuteronomy 16:8, and Shavuos is called *Atzeres* in rabbinic literature. Say that only Sukkos has an additional festival day named Shemini Atzeres.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Pilgrimage context:** The rainy season does not mean Jews “can’t go back” to God or Jerusalem at all. Explain that the parable concerns the long interval until the next pilgrimage festival.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Quotation mark:** The divine quotation beginning “Please wait another day...” lacks its closing quotation mark.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Missing relative pronoun:** “Showing the chibah Hashem has for us” should be **“the chibah that Hashem has for us.”**
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Pagination:** A short one-page piece occupies only the upper half of the page. Consider combining it with part (2), especially because both pieces share the same festival and year.
- **Printed p. 402 (archive p. 422), Bidi:** The Chizkuni, pilgrimage festivals, rain, and *chibah* need clean RTL ordering.

### Shmini Atzeres (2) 5786

- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Redundancy:** “Extraordinarily unusual” needs only one intensifier.
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Factual nuance:** The day is not devoid of mitzvos; it has festival sanctity, offerings, prayer, kiddush, simchah, and other obligations. The intended point may be that it lacks a unique physical symbol like matzah, shofar, sukkah, or lulav.
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Contradiction:** “Devoted to one thing amongst others, but the main concept...” is self-canceling. State the thesis directly.
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Grammar:** “Deveikus is a word that **was is** bandied about” contains two competing verbs. Use **“is often used.”**
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Sweeping claim:** “We really don’t know what deveikus is” speaks for all readers. Use **“It can be difficult to define.”**
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Demeaning self-reference:** “Show us little people” should be **“make the concept accessible.”**
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Source:** Give the exact *Torah Tavlin* issue/page and its source for the Dubno Maggid parable.
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Exact duplication:** “He mulled over his options and ultimately decided...” appears twice consecutively. Delete one occurrence.
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Hyphenation:** “A far away land” should be **“a faraway land.”**
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Blended-family language:** “His wife’s children” versus “his own kids” stigmatizes stepchildren and makes their status the basis for unequal affection. Preserve only what the original parable requires, and explain the metaphor without endorsing the family hierarchy.
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Wrong word:** “A surge of exuberance **capitulated** the air” should likely be **“filled”** or **“permeated the air.”**
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Redundancy/word choice:** “A crowd of young little faces jumped at him” should be **“the children ran to greet him.”**
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Anatomy:** Tears stream from eyes or down cheeks, not “from her face.”
- **Printed p. 403 (archive p. 423), Usage:** “Befitting for” should be **“befitting”** or **“appropriate for.”**
- **Printed p. 404 (archive p. 424), Pronoun confusion:** “Her children” and “their children” are hard to track. Use **stepchildren** and **their shared children** only if the distinction must remain.
- **Printed p. 404 (archive p. 424), Factual error:** Numbers 29:36-38 prescribes one bull on Shemini Atzeres, but also one ram, seven lambs, and a goat. Do not say “only one korban was brought”; say **“only one bull was brought.”**
- **Printed p. 404 (archive p. 424), Colloquialism:** “We are lucky to be home with our Father” can be expressed more formally.
- **Printed p. 404 (archive p. 424), Transactional undertone:** “Hashem has much to offer to those close and intimate with Him” makes closeness sound like access to benefits. Emphasize relationship rather than reward.
- **Printed p. 404 (archive p. 424), Gendered/repetitive analogy:** “The intimate relationship a man has with his family, children, wife, whole family” is male-centered and repeats **family**.
- **Printed p. 404 (archive p. 424), Vague source:** “As is well known, brought down by many sefarim and rebbishe...” is not a citation for calling Shemini Atzeres the year’s spiritual peak. Name at least one exact source.
- **Printed pp. 403-404 (archive pp. 423-424), Bidi:** Festival terms, offerings, *deveikus*, and the closing source list need RTL reconstruction.

### Simchas Torah 5784

- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Hype/repetition:** “A splendid and very wonderful story ... so beautiful” praises the story three times before it begins.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Source:** Give the exact *Torah Tavlin* issue/page and R’ Chaim Dahan’s source or permission status.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Disability style:** Contemporary English generally uses **Down syndrome**, not “Down’s syndrome.” Consider asking the family’s preference for person-first or identity-first language.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Pronoun error:** “Nota Shloima was as happy as **they** could be” should be **“as happy as he could be.”**
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Infantilization:** “Young,” “little,” “beautiful little,” and “to little Nota Shloima” recur. Retain age-relevant description without making disability synonymous with childlikeness.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Repetition:** “Numerous sifrei Torah” followed by “all the many sifrei Torah” can be condensed.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Clumsy contrast:** The gabbai is described as “knowing what his answer was but not knowing or not having the heart...” Rewrite as **“The gabbai hesitated, unsure how to explain that the paper scroll could not be used for the reading.”**
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Patronizing adjective:** “Soft, compliant reply” should be **“quiet reply.”**
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Mind reading:** “The perfect words to ease Nota Shloima” is narrator inference. Say that Rav Pam’s words reassured him.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Sweeping timing claim:** “Close to two ... as is usual on Simchas Torah” varies by congregation and should be localized.
- **Printed p. 405 (archive p. 425), Terminology:** “Make-believe aliyah” can sound demeaning. **“A symbolic aliyah with his paper sefer Torah”** is more respectful, while still making clear it was not a halachic Torah reading.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Garbled quote:** Rav Pam’s explanation contains a broken clause around “we’ll use your sefer Torah ... will receive an aliyah.” Reconstruct the quotation from the source.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Absolute conclusion:** “Such is the true joy ... and the mark of a great Torah leader” can be softened to **“The episode illustrates...”**
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Dating/source:** “This last Rosh Hashanah” will date the collection. Give the year and identify R’ Oelbaum’s source for the hijacking story.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Factual/privacy query:** Identify the hijacking, verify that all passengers were released safely, and protect the privacy of the survivor, bride, and families.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Trauma ethics:** Advising a newly released hostage not to attend her best friend’s wedding may isolate a survivor and removes the bride’s ability to decide. Present this as one mother’s judgment, not an unquestionable model of sensitivity.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Repetition:** “Center of attention” appears four times in one short exchange.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Gender/focus:** “This is the bride’s night” ignores the groom and other participants. The intended point can be made without defining the event as one person’s spotlight.
- **Printed p. 406 (archive p. 426), Odd contrast:** “Relatives and non-relatives alike” is unnecessary.
- **Printed pp. 405-406 (archive pp. 425-426), Bidi:** Torah-scroll terms, aliyah, hakafos, wedding, prayers, and Rosh Hashanah need stable RTL setting.

### Simchas Torah 5785

- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Source:** Identify the “famous sefer,” give its exact wording and page, and avoid turning a spiritual teaching into a guaranteed promise that Torah will remain in every descendant.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Source chain:** Give exact references for R’ Elimelech Biderman, R’ Chaim Vital’s report about the Arizal, the additional sefer, and the Satmar Rebbe anecdote.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Physical safety:** Dancing “until the point of collapse” is not an example readers should be urged to imitate without qualification.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Wordplay:** Explain the “fresh set of legs” joke only after reproducing the Hebrew/Yiddish correctly; the current visual line is inaccessible.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Colloquialism:** “Having fantastic hakafos” should be recast in the essay’s formal register.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Biblical/source accuracy:** Cite II Samuel 6:16-23 and the relevant commentators concerning Michal. The text’s meaning and whether she had previously borne children are interpreted in more than one way.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Harmful fertility inference:** The manuscript treats Michal’s childlessness as punishment for disdaining dance, then reverses that into a promise that dancing produces children. This is not a safe or logically sufficient basis for counsel about infertility.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Flippant transition:** “Good to know” is especially inappropriate after discussing childlessness as punishment.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Privacy:** The piece names a couple and discloses years of infertility and birth timing. Obtain explicit consent or anonymize the account.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Demeaning language:** “He became a fool” and “putting in ... his entire dignity” equate expressive worship with humiliation. Describe the dancing without insulting the person.
- **Printed p. 407 (archive p. 427), Causal overclaim:** A child’s birth nine months later and another after a second year are presented as proof that dancing caused conception. Label this as the teller’s interpretation and explicitly avoid promising the same outcome.
- **Printed p. 408 (archive p. 428), Privacy/source:** The second man’s ten-year infertility history and impending bris also require consent and a traceable source.
- **Printed p. 408 (archive p. 428), Stigma:** “I couldn’t make a fool of myself in my shul” and using a home for older adults as a less embarrassing venue demeans both the worshipper and the residents.
- **Printed p. 408 (archive p. 428), Terminology:** “Old age home” should be replaced by the institution’s accurate, respectful description.
- **Printed p. 408 (archive p. 428), Quotation punctuation:** The nested dialogue has mismatched apostrophes, quote marks, and a final degree-like symbol after the bris statement.
- **Printed p. 408 (archive p. 428), Pastoral impact:** “What a nice story” fails to acknowledge how painful this causal framing may be for readers who dance, pray, and remain childless.
- **Printed pp. 407-408 (archive pp. 427-428), Bidi:** Names of sefarim, verses, hakafos, fertility stories, and blessings need exact RTL reconstruction.

### Simchas Torah 5786 (1)

- **Printed pp. 409-410 (archive pp. 429-430), Source/privacy:** No source is identified for a detailed story naming a city, rabbi, congregation, terminally ill mother, and children. Obtain permission, verify the event, and anonymize private medical information unless the family has consented.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Halachic nuance:** “The Torah stays in shul” is too absolute; a sefer Torah may be transported under specified circumstances and with proper honor. Have the procession described by a halachic editor.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Awkward adverb:** “The father replied factually” should be **“the father replied.”**
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Melodrama:** “Without warning ... uncontrollable sobs” can be simplified to **“the boy began to sob.”**
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Harmful description:** “Sick and dying,” “hopeless situation,” “bed sores,” and detailed IV imagery expose a person’s suffering and imply that her life was without hope. Use only the medical detail necessary to understand why she could not attend.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Medical claim:** “She sank into deeper and deeper depression” is a diagnosis and progression claim. Attribute it to a source or describe observable sadness without clinical certainty.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Normative stereotype:** Contrasting her holidays with synagogue, new clothes, and “good food” suggests that there is one correct visible form of celebration and that illness excludes a person from it.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Halachic/logistical check:** Verify the route, carrying conditions, Torah protection, and outdoor hakafos on Yom Tov in San Diego. The moving scene should not obscure practical accuracy.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Grammar:** “Dancing the hakafos ... right there” should be **“holding hakafos”** or **“dancing during hakafos.”**
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Stereotyped tableau:** “Men in [talleisim], children with flags, women pushing babies” sorts the congregation by gendered roles. Condense to **“the congregation came together.”**
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Cliché/word choice:** “It was priceless” and “deep joyousness” are imprecise.
- **Printed p. 409 (archive p. 429), Garbled sentence:** The passage about “the biggest and most honored get-well wishes ... of endless cards” has no clear grammatical structure and introduces cards not previously mentioned. Reconstruct it from the source.
- **Printed p. 410 (archive p. 430), Timeline:** She dies three months later, but the newfound joy is said to carry her through “the last month.” Verify whether this should be **“last months.”**
- **Printed p. 410 (archive p. 430), Euphemism/foreign phrase:** The Hebrew abbreviation after “succumbed to her illness” should be expanded or translated for readers, and its tone checked against the surrounding sentence.
- **Printed p. 410 (archive p. 430), Tone:** “May we all hear good things” immediately after reporting her death feels formulaic. End with the community’s act of compassion and the family’s memory of it.
- **Printed pp. 409-410 (archive pp. 429-430), Bidi:** Simchas Torah, Torah procession, medical blessing, mitzvah language, and the closing blessing need clean RTL ordering.
